## Session Notes


*Add dated notes here as the project evolves:*

- **June 2026** — Completed full setup: hub live at theorganicalanguage.vercel.app, Genesis Creator integrated with 55 forms, Strata with Smart+ at /strata/, Vercel team renamed to studio_rann, docs/ folder created with VISION.md, ROADMAP.md, ANIMATION-SYSTEM.md. CLAUDE.md added to root.
- **June 9, 2026** — Shipped **Spore** (`/spore/`, generative stippling) and **Pollen** (`/pollen/`, advanced stippling). Pollen: variable-radius blue-noise engine, Circle/Polygon/Line points with Size/Angle Range + Random + Warping, Gamma/Contrast/Overpaint/Hide Zone, Rotation/Flip/Invert, Solid/Adaptive colour with RGBA + Random, presets, and WYSIWYG PNG/JPG/SVG/Figma export (export serialises the exact preview points). Both are single-file vanilla HTML/CSS/JS. Hub nav + `/spore/` + `/pollen/` routes added.
- **July 23, 2026** — Prototyped **Halide** (`/halide/`), a single-file dither-portrait tool inspired by evaluating ditherface.com (a manual $150-per-slot commission service, not a tool). Real Floyd–Steinberg + Atkinson error diffusion and recursive-Bayer ordered dithering (verified: no prior dithering code existed in the repo — algorithms written from scratch), plus a flat-threshold baseline. Reuses Pollen's shell verbatim where it fit: drop-zone, zoom/pan, tone pipeline (rotation/flip/invert/gamma/contrast, + a new Bias shift), PNG/JPG/SVG export, and the `organica-svg` → Figma postMessage protocol. Square-crop toggle for avatar framing. One `runs` (row run-length-encoded ink cells) feeds preview canvas, raster export, and true-vector SVG output alike — same shared-function WYSIWYG discipline as Pollen/Living Path. 8 built-in presets + localStorage custom presets. Not yet added to `docs/` as a dedicated manual — do that once the prototype's shape is confirmed with Diego.
- **July 24, 2026** — Closed out the first full arc on **Halide**, validated against a real test photo (`halide/test-photos/`, deliberately kept out of git). Shipped: **background removal** (colour-key, global threshold — not connected flood-fill, which let lighting gradients bleed the flood into the subject; **Pick…**/Auto reference, since border-average contaminates the moment the subject touches the frame edge); a **sticker Outline** (largest-connected-component + enclosed-hole-fill for the silhouette, diagonal-corner-touch bridging so the ring always reads solid); **Background fill** outside the outline (pure subtraction, independent of Paper); an **interactive drag-to-position square crop** (replaced Crop X/Y sliders entirely, per direct feedback that a slider divorced from the visual is worse than dragging the frame on the photo); a **true colour swap** (Swap Colors now only trades hex values — verified byte-identical shapes before/after — it no longer silently toggles Invert, which is a real image change, not a recolour); the **Ditherface** preset (Atkinson + square + background-removed + Outline + Background fill, the validated reference-matching look, replacing the now-redundant "Studio Rann Ink"); and **"Simplify shapes"**, an opt-in SVG-export toggle that traces each region into one rectilinear `<path>` (evenodd fill-rule for holes) instead of a pile of `<rect>` tiles — verified pixel-exact against the tiled version on every tested case, 4,789 rects → 4 paths on the reference photo. Evaluated and **explicitly declined**: swapping the square/pixel mark for Genesis organic forms (would duplicate Pollen, and would undo the Simplify win since organic marks don't merge like rectilinear cells do); autonomous/hover-reactive pixel animation (autonomous fits the existing Motion vocabulary and is worth revisiting as an animated-SVG export; mouse-hover reactivity needs real JS and can't be a static export file — different deliverable, not built). Full manual: `docs/HALIDE.md`.
- **July 26, 2026 (accessible names + contrast)** — Two fixes, both found by measuring rather than assuming. **(1) Accessible names:** audited all six panels and found **121 of ~210 form controls with no accessible name** — a slider sat next to a `.ctrl-label` reading "Grid width" with nothing tying them together, so a screen reader announced "slider" with no name. Same markup pattern (row → label + control, no `for`/`aria-labelledby`) in every tool, so fixed once as `Organica.autoLabelPanel(document)` in `core.js` instead of 121 hand-edits. **Two bugs caught only by re-measuring after the first pass, not by reading the diff:** the first edit deleted `global.Organica = Organica;` while adding the new function, which would have broken *every* tool silently (no console error until something called `Organica.*`); and the `hasName()` check treated any `el.closest('label')` as "already named", so Strata's toggle switches (checkbox wrapped in an empty `<label class="toggle">` styled as a slider knob, no text) were skipped as false-positives — fixed by requiring the wrapping label to have non-empty text. Final: **0 of ~210 unnamed**, segmented buttons keep their own visible text (the function skips buttons that already have text — `aria-labelledby` replaces a name, it doesn't add to one), zero runtime errors. **(2) Contrast:** requested a "just a little more margin" on `panel/value`/`panel/sub-label`; measuring first showed it was already a **real AA failure**, not a margin problem — `--mid` was **three different values** across tools (Strata's own `--muted: #888` at 3.17:1, Spore's `--mid: #c8c0b0` at **1.62:1**, everyone else's `#726a5e` at 4.43:1 *on panel background* — all below the 4.5:1 floor). Unified to **`#696256`** everywhere (5.4:1 paper / ≥5.0:1 panel, real headroom, computed the same way as `--border-strong`). Also separately answered a **px vs rem** question: kept px — browser zoom at 200% already scales the panel correctly with px (verified: no overflow, no clipping), rem's only real benefit is respecting an OS-level font-size override (rarer path than zoom), and the panel is pixel-grid-locked (26px rows, 1px borders, a hierarchy resting on a single px of difference) so a partial rem conversion would break alignment — documented as a conscious choice, not silently decided. Docs: `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §5 (`--mid`) and §6 (accessible names).
- **July 25, 2026** — Shipped **Komorebi** (`/komorebi/`), the first **WebGL2** tool in Organica (a browser API, not a framework — holds the vanilla/single-file rule; a Canvas2D radial blur would be ~9M ops/frame, dead). Replicates 木漏れ日 — dappled sunlight through a canopy — via the three real graphics mechanics Diego named: **(1) light cookie / gobo** — a canopy mask (procedural 2-layer fBm foliage combined with `max()` so gaps only exist where both layers open; OR an uploaded silhouette, so a Halide 1-bit export / Strata trace / photo drops straight in as the canopy) projected onto a ground plane in perspective, two layers domain-warped at different rates = wind; **(2) penumbra + pinhole** — the detail nobody implements and the thing that *makes* it komorebi: the sun subtends ~0.53°, so a gap at height h blurs its shadow edge by ≈ h/108, and once a gap is smaller than that blur it becomes a pinhole projecting a round disc of the sun instead of the leaf shape. Implemented as ONE "Height" control = a disc-kernel sample radius over the cookie (golden-angle taps, per-sample rotation), so small gaps naturally wash into soft coins of light — verified visibly in Gobo mode; **(3) god rays** — post-process radial blur of an occlusion buffer (Mitchell, GPU Gems 3 ch.13), *no* shadow map / *no* 3D scene — the occluder is the same 2D canopy, so pass A (occlusion, half-res) and pass B (composite) share the CANOPY_LIB + PROJECT_LIB GLSL chunks and can never disagree. Two modes: **Scene** (horizon + canopy ceiling + rays + dappled floor — the picture) and **Gobo** (orthographic flat fill — the tileable *pattern*, the Phase-4 feed). **Wind: Sway | Drift** — Sway is built from a single phase angle so the field is exactly periodic → the **WebM export is a genuinely seamless loop** (Phase-6 installation deliverable); Drift travels and deliberately doesn't loop. WYSIWYG discipline (same as Pollen/Halide/Living Path): one `renderFrame(w,h)` serves preview, PNG/JPG (`toDataURL`, sync — `toBlob` raced the canvas resize-back), WebM frames, and the pixel source the SVG reads. **SVG export = posterised tone-band separation** — a volumetric field has no honest 1:1 vector form, but N flat luminance bands traced into one path each *is* honest and is a real print/riso separation (bands stacked as "≥ level" regions so no hairline seams); reuses **Halide's rectilinear contour tracer verbatim** (evenodd fill). 8 built-in presets (Forest Floor / High Noon / Cathedral / Shoji / Undergrowth / Riso Two-Tone / Dusk Ember / Bamboo), each a complete deterministic state layered over the Forest-Floor baseline; custom presets via `localStorage`. Drag on canvas = move the sun (rays + light pools both read `uSunUV`). Hub nav + `/komorebi/` route added. Manual: `docs/KOMOREBI.md`.
- **July 25, 2026 (panel layout)** — Consolidated to **one control panel, always on the right**. Strata's only panel was on the left (grid flipped to `1fr var(--panel-w)`, aside moved to `grid-column: 2 / grid-row: 2` — the first attempt used `grid-row: 1/-1` and the aside fought the header for row 1, pushing it below the panel). Living Path had **two** panels; merged into one, ordered *what you load → what you apply → what you inspect* (Input · Presets · Effect stack · Source view), giving the stage the full width. Genesis Library keeps its left column on purpose — it lists **sets**, which is navigation, not controls. Verified: all six tools now report exactly one panel, on the right, 248px, zero uppercase, zero runtime errors.
- **July 25, 2026 (panel component)** — Audited the six control sidebars (Strata, Spore, Pollen, Living Path, Halide, Komorebi): **four different section-heading styles** (9/400, 10/500, 12/600×3), **three widths** (240/244/280), **two slider tracks** (2px/3px), two label sizes, **76 uppercase elements**, and two markup vocabularies (`.ctrl-row` vs `.row`). New **`shared/panel.css`**, modelled on Figma's Design panel: **sentence case everywhere, no tracking on labels**, three levels of hierarchy (section → sub-label → row), one row height so controls align down the column, tabular values so numbers don't jitter under a dragging slider. Section title went 12px/600 uppercase+0.1em → **11px/500 sentence** — smaller on the page *and* faster to scan, which is the whole argument for dropping caps. Class names match what the tools already used, so adoption was "link the file, delete the local copy"; `.sec h3` / `.row` / `.group-label` are aliased so Strata's and Living Path's JS is untouched. Verified: **0 uppercase** across all six, one width (248px), one slider (2px), one heading (11px/500), nothing below 9px, all Manrope, zero runtime errors. Two things worth knowing: the tool's `<style>` loads *after* the component, so a leftover local rule silently wins — migrating means deleting, not just linking (Living Path's `.sec h3` survived my first pass and its headings fell back to the browser's default `h3` size); and Strata deliberately keeps its full-width slider with min/max captions ("Clean → Raw") — the slider's visual style is unified but that layout does real work for its audience, so it's a documented variant, not drift. Also lifted 6px index numerals and 8px captions in Spore/Pollen to `--fs-micro`.
- **July 25, 2026 (header component)** — Audited every header outside the hub: **five different structures for ten pages** (`#topbar` in Spore/Pollen/Halide/Komorebi, `.top` in Living Path, `<header>`+`.toolbar` in Strata, an editorial `<header>` in Genesis index/Indicators, `.toolbar` in Library/Creator), plus a stale `#organica-banner` migration notice on three pages. The four main tools had **8–9 interactive controls in a 40px bar** and, measured: no `<header>`/`<nav>`/`<main>` landmark at all, **zero `aria-*` attributes**, a `#status-text` mutated from JS with no live region (so export progress was never announced), `:focus` defined on only 3 of 10 pages, `title=`-only explanations (unreachable by keyboard), and `--border` #d0c8b8 at **1.49:1** — failing even the 3:1 UI minimum. New **`shared/header.css`** + `Organica.status()` / `Organica.popover()`: one component, five slots (identity · context · status · spacer · actions), three variants (Tool / Catalog / Editor — only the Context slot differs). **The rule: identity, context, status, at most three actions.** Everything else moved to where it acts — Export Scale + Simplify + PNG/JPG/SVG into an **Export popover** (with `aria-expanded`, Escape, click-outside and focus-return, verified step by step), Komorebi's Play/Pause/Reset onto the **canvas HUD** next to the clock they control (they were split: time read bottom-left, controlled top-right), and REC into the popover's Motion group since it is an export, not a transport. Applied to Halide (8→4 controls) and Komorebi (9→3). `--border-strong` is **computed, not eyeballed**: a first pass at #b5a992 *looked* right and measured 2.08:1; the shipped #958462 is 3.27:1 on paper and 3.03:1 on panel. Logo link target went 14px→26px (WCAG 2.2's inline-link exception doesn't cover a nav control). Responsive fixed too — the old bar never overflowed, flex just squeezed until logo/"EXPORT SCALE"/"→ FIGMA" each wrapped onto two lines inside 40px; the component sheds the least-critical text instead (verified at 700px: no wrap, no clip). Template updated so new tools inherit it. **Dev gotcha, third time:** `python3 -m http.server` cached `core.js` so hard the page ran a stale copy and threw `Organica.status is not a function` with an empty console — replaced with a `no-store` dev server; when a change seems not to apply, suspect cache before code. Still to migrate: Spore, Pollen, Living Path, Strata, Genesis pages.
- **July 25, 2026 (system pass)** — Three cleanups that turn "coherence by copy-paste" into an actual system, done ahead of the Phase-4 Pattern Engine. **(1) `shared/core.js`** — the copy-shared JS is now one module: download, preset storage + legacy migration, Figma postMessage, hex validation, the rectilinear contour tracer, zoom/pan. The copies had **already drifted**, which is the whole argument: Living Path revoked its download URL after a delay (correct) while three tools revoked immediately (can cancel the download); Komorebi's tracer emitted `.toFixed(2)` coordinates while Halide emitted integers; Komorebi validated colour hex while **Halide did not** (typing junk in a hex field set an invalid colour). Each merged routine is the better of the variants — the unified tracer emits the shortest correct form, so it is byte-identical to Halide's *and* **43% smaller than Komorebi's**, proven on 48 equivalence cases incl. checkerboard saddles, ring-with-hole, solid and empty masks (geometry identical everywhere). Wired into Halide, Komorebi, Pollen, Spore. **(2) Typography unified to Manrope** via `shared/tokens.css`. Killed four families (Syne, Syne Mono, DM Sans, DM Mono, Georgia, ui-monospace) and four variable names for one idea (`--font`/`--sans`/`--mono`/`--display` → **`--font`**, old names kept as aliases). **The real find: six of eleven pages declared `'DM Mono'` and never loaded it** — Spore, Pollen, Halide, Komorebi, Genesis Library and Creator had been silently rendering system monospace, so the design was never what the CSS claimed. Verified: all 11 pages carry the tokens, zero old families, zero stale imports, Manrope loads as a variable font (200–800). **(3) `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` + `docs/UI-SHELL.md` + `shared/_template.html`** — type scale (1.2 ratio anchored at 11px), weights, tracking, spacing, per-tool accents, each with the **Figma text-style mapping** (Figma tracking is a *percentage*, CSS is em — they map 1:1, don't type 0.08 into Figma). The template is a working page, verified rendering with zoom/pan already wired from the core. **Known drift left honest, not hidden** (documented in UI-SHELL §7): panel widths still 240/244/260, Genesis pages use a different shell entirely, Strata and Living Path weren't retro-fitted, and Spore/Pollen/Halide still run their own inline zoom/pan — migrating those is safe but untested, so it's a follow-up rather than done blind.
- **July 25, 2026 (housekeeping)** — Two architectural cleanups ahead of the Phase-4 Pattern Engine, which will be the 8th consumer of the shared library. **(1) Split `organic-library.css`** — it mixed the 77 keyframes with the Genesis *catalog page* layout (`body{padding}`, `header`, `.grid`, `.cell`), so any tool wanting the animations inherited page chrome. Now `animations.css` (pure, reusable, append-only rule moved here) + `page.css` (chrome), with `organic-library.css` kept as an `@import` shim so every existing link is untouched — page chrome imports first, animations second, order load-bearing. **Verified rule-for-rule** on index/library/creator: identical computed vars, body metrics, 77 reachable keyframes, exact flattened rule counts (372/350/353 = lib+inline as before), and forms 1/29/36/48 computing identical fill/stroke/animation. **The load-bearing discovery:** the animations need `--bg-cell`, but *none* of the three Genesis pages define it — they inherited it only from the `:root` inside the old bundle, and only forms 29 (iris) and 36 (moon mask) use it to punch holes. A naive split would have turned exactly two forms into black blobs and left the other 53 fine — near-invisible. That contract is now documented at the top of `animations.css` and in `docs/SHARED-LIBRARY.md`. **(2) Standardised localStorage** to `organica.<tool>.<thing>` (was three different separators: `pollen-presets`, `halide-presets`, `organica_komorebi_presets`, `organica_library`). Each tool migrates forward on first read and **deliberately does not delete the legacy key** — presets and custom forms are real user work, and leaving the old copy means a rollback still finds them. Genesis is the delicate one: `organica.library.forms` is read/written by *both* `library.html` and `creator.html`, so they carry an identical `readLibraryRaw()` that must stay in sync. Verified end-to-end per tool (seed legacy → read → migrate → save new → legacy stays frozen) and cross-file for Genesis. **Dev gotcha that cost a cycle:** the Genesis migration looked broken on reload and was just the dev-server cache — `?v=N` fixed it, exactly as `docs/LIVINGPATH.md` already warned. New doc: `docs/SHARED-LIBRARY.md`.
- **July 25, 2026 (later same day)** — Komorebi: **Scene mode removed from the UI** per direct feedback — Diego wants pattern-making (Riso Two-Tone, Shoji direction), not a landscape picture; the Scene shader code stays in place but inert (`uMode` now hardcoded to gobo in JS) so nothing downstream (occlusion pass, rays, SVG separation) had to change. Canopy got two new controls for organic realism: **Clumping** (a slow low-frequency field that varies local leaf density across the frame — denser thickets, wider gaps, instead of one uniform threshold) and **Leaf detail** (a fine high-frequency layer that scallops the leaf edges and flecks pinholes through the interior, instead of smooth blob edges). Rebuilt the preset set around Gobo-only patterns: **Forest Floor / Riso Two-Tone / Shoji / Dense Canopy / Sparse Grove / Fine Foliage** (the old Scene-tuned presets — High Noon, Cathedral, Undergrowth, Dusk Ember, Bamboo — were dropped, not just hidden). Caught in verification: the first "Dense Canopy" tuning (density 0.74 + leaf-detail 0.55) rendered near-solid black — Leaf detail's nibble subtracts as often as it adds, so on an already-high-density field it closed off the few remaining gaps instead of texturing them; fixed by lowering density to 0.60 and leaf-detail to 0.35. Lesson for tuning future presets: Leaf detail and Density fight each other at the high end, verify visually rather than reasoning from slider values alone.
- **July 2026** — Shipped **Living Path** (`/livingpath/`), a full web port of ivangrozny/LivingPath (single-file vanilla). Two engines (Vector node-effects | Raster "glyph hydrography"), layer **groups + blend modes**, 9 raster algorithms (dilate/erode, blur, threshold, noise, particles, center-line, polygonize, seam-carve, reaction-diffusion), 24 presets mapped to the author's example sheets, **Vector→Raster chaining**, live multi-language **text specimen**, and installable **OTF export** (charset/name/HTML specimen/`.lvp` projects/**randomised alternates** via `rand`+`aalt`) run off a **Web Worker**. Manual: `docs/LIVINGPATH.md` (incl. §12 Development notes). Key invariant: preview = specimen = export share one normalised 1000-box scale (WYSIWYG). **Dev gotchas** (also in the manual): the dev server caches aggressively — hard-refresh / `?v=` when testing; `<svg>` and `display:flex/grid` both defeat the `hidden` attribute (toggle a class instead); verify visibility via `getComputedStyle().display`, not the attribute. **Still open:** Genesis Creator Bézier tangent-handle drag/edit (flagged since setup, unresolved).

- **July 27, 2026** — Komorebi: widened Canopy from a single foliage generator into a **6-pattern gobo library** (Foliage / Cellular / Veins / Drop mark / Blinds / Blob), per direct steer — "pattern naturali o meno su diverse superfici e diverse luci... concentriamoci prima sui pattern, poi le animazioni" (wind/animation is deliberately parked for a later pass). All six are `float mask(vec2 p)` functions sharing the same six sliders (Scale/Density/Edge/Layer2/Clumping/Leaf detail), each repurposed to fit — the panel relabels the rows per pattern (`applyPatternLabels()`), so no new UI real estate per type. **Drop mark** uses Organica's own gesture (a jittered grid of gravity-tailed circles + a smaller satellite-droplet layer) as a gobo, not just foliage — the most on-brand of the five new masks. **Blinds** is the one deliberately non-natural pattern (venetian slats / grid), per the brief's own "natural or not." Added a **Canvas Bloom** preset (Blob pattern) built from two of Diego's reference photos (painted canvas studies — soft organic two-tone blobs, blurred edge). **Real bug caught by verifying the vector export, not just the PNG preview:** the reference's sage green measured luminance ≈0.64 — the *same* posterize band as the cream background at Bands=2 — so `buildSVG()` silently produced one full-canvas rectangle instead of the traced blob shape, even though the live preview looked correct. Diego's requirement that **export must always be a genuine vector file** is what caught this (checked by calling `buildSVG()` directly and inspecting path count/geometry, not just eyeballing the PNG). Fixed by darkening the shadow colour to luminance ≈0.45 and documented the rule for future two-tone presets in `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §16: colours must straddle the band threshold in *luminance*, matching on screen isn't sufficient. All six patterns + the "Foliage" default + existing built-in presets (Riso Two-Tone etc.) verified regression-free after the `canopy()` refactor. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §16. **Not yet done:** wind/animation tuning per-pattern (Sway/Drift currently apply uniformly; some patterns like Blinds or Drop mark may want different wind semantics — parked per Diego's own sequencing).

- **July 27, 2026 (later same day) — Komorebi off WebGL/GLSL onto Canvas2D/JS.** Direct feedback on the 6-pattern work above: the raw GLSL was suspected as "the problem" — it wasn't a library (Komorebi never depended on one; the §16 bug was a luminance-math oversight, not a library fault) but it *is* genuinely hard to read/debug (no breakpoints, no console.log). Once the priority was stated plainly — best possible pattern **shapes**, always exportable as real vector SVG; wind/animation explicitly deferred — the GPU stopped earning its complexity cost. Checked `shared/core.js` first per instruction: nothing pattern/noise-specific there to reuse (it's UI/utility — download, presets, Figma, the rectilinear contour tracer, zoom/pan, header, sliders), so it was untouched and still fully reused (contour tracer, hex validation, preset storage). Rewrote Komorebi's entire render pipeline: no WebGL, no GLSL, no shader compilation — every mask/noise/compositing routine is a direct, checkable port to plain JS functions on scalar (x,y). Biggest architectural change: **no continuous animation loop** — wind/time is frozen (Sway/Drift are now two different *static* warp shapes, not motions), and rendering is on-demand (`scheduleRender()`, one rAF-coalesced call wired via a single input/change listener on the panel). Added occlusion caching so colour/grade/ray tweaks (~10ms) don't pay the noise-recompute cost that shape changes do (~120ms preview, ~480ms full export with rays — all measured, all comfortably interactive for an on-demand model). Penumbra went from 14-tap per-pixel disc sampling to a separable box blur — cheaper and reads as exactly what it does. SVG export got *simpler*, not just ported: `ctx.getImageData()` off a real Canvas2D context is top-down already, so the old WebGL readPixels y-flip code is just gone. **Verified the thing that matters most first**: re-ran the exact Canvas Bloom luminance check from §16 (`buildSVG()` → 2 bands, 2 real contour paths, ~4.5 KB) to confirm the rewrite didn't regress the one bug that was actually found and fixed there. All 6 patterns + all 6 built-in presets re-verified visually against their pre-rewrite look; WebM recording disabled (not deleted) since recording a static frame isn't a real feature until animation returns. Full detail incl. the one disclosed simplification (dropped sun-parallax "sunShift", re-addable later): `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §17.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Komorebi quality fix + control audit.** Immediate feedback on the Canvas2D rewrite: "il risultato è pessimo e ci sono tanti controlli della precedente libreria che non servono." Two real, separate findings, not vague dissatisfaction. **(1) A genuine bug, not a taste call**: every pattern rendered as a muddy wash. Isolated by setting Dapple Height to 0 and comparing — the box blur standing in for the GPU's penumbra sampling was ~5× too strong, because a dense box average over a radius is a much stronger low-pass than the old **14-tap sparse Monte-Carlo disc sample** at that same nominal radius (sparse sampling under-samples fine noise and leaves visible texture; a full average erases it). Fixed with an empirically-verified ×⅕ correction, verified against Foliage, Cellular, Veins, Drop mark, and Canvas Bloom before/after. **Canvas Bloom barely moved** (Height≈0.02 already) — exactly why the bug survived the §16 verification pass, which leaned on that preset. **(2) A real control audit, not just this bug**: confirmed **Colour → Canopy** swatch is never read anywhere in gobo-mode rendering (grep'd before deleting, not assumed) — pure Scene-mode leftover — removed. Confirmed **Wind → Speed/Loop** are not just visually inert but literally unread by `windOffset()` since time is frozen — removed. The one genuinely debatable cut — the whole **Rays + Sun position** cluster (god rays, Density/Weight/Decay/Exposure, Azimuth/Altitude/Size) — was real working code but the single biggest thing still pulling Komorebi toward "photoreal light sim" against the stated shape/vector priority, and the single most expensive render pass (O(w·h·32) ray march); asked Diego rather than deciding unilaterally, since it's a bigger scope cut than dead-code removal. **Answer: remove both.** Colour → Sun (the tint swatch, unrelated to the deleted Sun position) stays. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §18.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Komorebi tree/foliage variety.** Ask: "ho bisogno di komorebi che richiamino diversi tipi di alberi e fogliame." Asked which species first (broadleaf, conifer, sparse birch/poplar, palm/bamboo all named) rather than guessing, since each implies genuinely different mask maths. Shipped: **Foliage renamed "Broadleaf"** (label only, same code — its existing dense rounded look already reads as oak/beech). **Conifer** (new pattern) — needle tufts via a single cosine angular-modulation "star" SDF per grid cell, no per-needle loop. **Fronds** (new pattern) — covers palm *and* bamboo as one mask; a repurposed "Spread" control (angular window width) is narrow for near-parallel upward canes, wide for a full radiating palm burst. **Sparse birch/poplar needed no new mask** — just a **Birch Grove** preset tuning existing Broadleaf (low density, small scale, high clumping/leaf-detail) — a case of "this is parameter tuning, not a new shape generator," decided rather than defaulted into a 7th pattern type. 4 new tree-themed presets total (Birch Grove/Conifer Grove/Palm Shade/Bamboo Grove), each own palette. All verified visually (Palm Shade's radiating stars in particular look strong) and via `buildSVG()` producing real multi-path vector geometry, not degenerate rectangles. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §19.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — reference-photo workflow.** Created `komorebi/reference-photos/` (kept out of git, same convention as `halide/test-photos/`) so Diego can teach the target look via real photos instead of describing it. 18 photos uploaded on the first pass (dappled-shadow photography, painted canvas studies, riso/grain prints) — read all of them and reported back the recurring themes not yet covered (leaf silhouettes, pinhole-dot bursts, density gradients, heavy grain/riso texture, chromatic-aberration edge fringing, visible surface material under the light) rather than building blind. Two actioned: **(1) Blinds retuned** to match a specific reference (2×2 window projected on a wall) — pure parameter tuning, no new code: the fix was **Spread down to 0.03** (not Scale, whose slider floor of 0.4 can't reach few-enough repeats given Blinds' hardcoded ×6/×5.4 internal frequency), Slat width at its floor, Cross grid maxed — shipped as **Window Light** preset, verified against the photo and via `buildSVG()`. **(2) Leaves (new pattern)** — first "recognizable silhouette" request: a lens/vesica leaf shape (width tapers to zero at both base and tip, unlike every other pattern's shape), Elongation controls round-vs-slender, Serration adds a toothed margin. Caught and fixed a real correctness bug before shipping: the width formula is only valid for t∈[0,1], so a naive "distance = |x| − width(t)" reads as false-positive "inside" far outside the leaf along its own centreline — fixed with a capsule-style fallback outside that range. Shipped as **Leaf Litter** preset. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §20.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Komorebi "Cluster": the organic-randomness technique.** Diego's critique was about **method**, not subject: "non focalizzarti su 'cosa' (leaves) ma sul come giocare sulle forme in maniera totalmente casuale." **Diagnosis:** every shape pattern so far (Drop mark/Conifer/Fronds/Leaves) places one same-size shape per jittered-grid cell — and jitter can't hide a lattice, because equal shape-count-per-cell and equal size are the statistics the eye actually reads. **Three attempts failed and were each isolated and rendered ALONE before judging** (the discipline that mattered here): a thresholded ridged-fbm "bold branch" layer gave cow-spot patches, not a flowing branch; continuous spatial modulation of the sampling *frequency* gave wood-grain/fingerprint rings (classic FM artifact — scaling coordinates by a spatially varying factor makes that factor's iso-contours visible); blending two fixed-frequency fields' *values* was artifact-free but no better than the plain mask, so it was reverted rather than kept as noise. `foliageMask` was restored byte-for-byte after all three. **The fix came from re-reading the reference photos for MECHANISM instead of subject** — the two painted studies make it unambiguous: everything is a **union of round blobs with enormously varying radii, merged smoothly where they overlap** (sparse → isolated dots of wildly different size; dense → one flowing mass with bulging joins). That's a placement+combination property, which is precisely why every noise-thresholding attempt missed it. **Shipped as `clusterField`:** (1) 3-octave scatter with each octave in its **own rotated frame** (irrational-ish angle → no two lattices can align, something a single jittered grid can never do); (2) **heavy-tailed radii** `r = 0.10 + 0.75·u^p`, p = 1+Layer2, so mostly tiny with a rare few huge; (3) **`smin` smooth union** instead of `min()` — and this is where branch-like continuity comes from: it *emerges* from chains of overlapping discs rather than being drawn, exactly what attempt (1) failed to draw explicitly. Two presets at the range's ends (Cluster Bloom / Cluster Scatter), both verified for real vector SVG output; cold render ≈200ms, comparable to Fronds. **Not done:** retro-fitting the same three ingredients into Drop mark/Conifer/Fronds/Leaves — proven now, but it changes four shipped patterns so it wants its own pass. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §21.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — scatter engine retro-fitted into every shape pattern.** The §21 follow-up: **Drop mark, Conifer, Fronds, Leaves now all run on one shared `scatterField(x,y,P,shapeFn,cfg)`**, supplying only their own shape SDF (`shapeDrop`/`shapeNeedle`/`shapeFrond`/`shapeLeaf`/`shapeDisc`). The shape does its **own rotation**, because orientation isn't uniform — a drop may only tilt (gravity has a direction), a frond points mostly up, the rest are free 360°. **Two new controls, and they had to be new**: Size range and Merge belong to the *scatter*, not to any shape, and all six existing sliders already carried shape meanings — reusing one would make a control mean different things per pattern. Shown only for the five scatter patterns, hidden for the noise-field ones (dead controls are a bug here, §18). **Real discovery while retuning:** Size range had to gate the **octave count** too, not just the radius tail — extra octaves *are* a size difference (a whole population at a smaller scale), so at the slider's floor Bamboo Grove kept sprouting tiny specks. One control, one meaning. **Two controls freed by the refactor were given real jobs rather than left dead**: Drop mark's Layer 2 (its hand-rolled satellite-droplet pass is now the engine's finer octave) → **Splat**; Cluster's Layer 2/Leaf detail (they were standing in for Size range/Merge) → **Elongation**/**Irregularity**, so its blobs read as hand-made lumps not geometric circles. **Presets re-checked visually one by one, not assumed** — Palm Shade and Leaf Litter improved on defaults; **Bamboo Grove is the useful counter-example: bamboo canes are genuinely near-uniform, so the heavy tail that flatters palm actively hurt** (ships at Size range 0.30). All 14 presets + 10 patterns verified, real vector SVG throughout. **Design note:** the engine is now the default way to place shapes — a new shape pattern is a `shapeFn` + `cfg`, not a new grid loop. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §22.
- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Raking light.** Asked for a preset matching a reference photo of low sun on leather: soft light pools all elongated along **one shared diagonal**. **No existing control could do it** — Cluster's Elongation rotates every blob independently, so raising it gives ellipses pointing every which way; per-shape elongation and *shared* directionality are different things. Took the mechanism from the physics instead: glancing light foreshortens the whole projection along the direction of incidence, so every shape stretches the same way because the *projection* is anisotropic, not the shapes. Implemented in `groundPoint()` as an anisotropic squash of the sampling coordinates (rotate into the rake frame → scale that axis → rotate back), with two controls in Format: **Raking** + **Rake angle**. Because it acts on the coordinates feeding the mask it applies to **all 10 patterns for free**, noise-field ones included — Broadleaf/Veins/Blinds/Blob all gained a directional variant a per-shape control could never have given them. **Verified as a true no-op at 0 rather than assumed**: sampled 500 random pixels, ground coords bit-identical to the pre-Raking formula (max delta 0), and all 14 pre-existing presets confirmed at Raking = 0, so nothing shipped changed. Shipped as the **Raking Leather** preset. Full detail: `docs/KOMOREBI.md` §23.

- **July 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Strata "Refine paths" verified, fixed and shipped.** A Paper.js post-trace editor existed uncommitted in the working tree; Diego asked for it to be verified rather than deployed blind. **It had been written but never once executed** — running it found **five defects, all fixed**: (1) `paper.view.fitBounds is not a function` — fatal, `fitBounds` is an `Item` method not a `View` one, threw inside `importSVG`'s `onLoad` so the editor never loaded and clicking Refine only logged an error; (2) a **layout feedback runaway** (flex item with default `min-height:auto` + in-flow canvas → canvas grows wrap grows canvas) measured reaching **16,777,215 px**; (3) **device pixel ratio applied twice**, canvas coming out 2× its container on HiDPI — root cause was `fitToView` measuring *the canvas*, which paper.js resizes via inline style, making it a one-way ratchet; now measures the container, which is also what the existing `ResizeObserver` watches so re-fits happen automatically; (4) **style silently rewritten** — `styleTraceItem()` forced fill/no-stroke on every path and export reads from those same items, so touching Refine turned a "stroke only" trace into filled shapes and discarded Strata's own Fill mode; (5) **units dropped on export** — the header was rebuilt from parsed numbers, turning `570.000000pt` into `570`, a **1.33× size change** on Figma import. (4) and (5) mattered most: they silently altered output for a tool whose whole job is print-ready vectors. **Two false alarms I caught by testing instead of reporting**: "Unite all loses geometry" (no — overlaps merging *is* union; verified with disjoint squares, area preserved exactly) and "anchor drag is broken" (no — my own screenshot-px vs CSS-px error; verified the moved distance matches the drag exactly through `view.zoom`). Also a **deliberate behaviour change kept**: export now follows the selected region tab instead of always emitting the full SVG — the same WYSIWYG rule as Komorebi/Halide/Pollen; cross-region contamination verified impossible. **First third-party runtime library in Organica**: `shared/paper-full.min.js` (Paper.js 0.12.17, MIT, sha256 recorded in the manual) vendored rather than CDN — no build step exists, and a CDN would be the system's only external runtime dependency and would break the offline/local-backend flow. Load order `core → paper → organica-paper` is load-bearing (organica-paper merges into the namespace, so loading it before core lets core clobber it). Shipped to production at Diego's call to test on real cases and iterate. Manual: `docs/STRATA-REFINE.md`. **Known sharp edge, not a bug:** anchors need a precise (~8px) click and have no persistent visual indicator — clicking the stroke selects but does not drag.

- **July 31, 2026 — Strata now traces in production (Vercel Python functions).** Strata had never worked on the deployed site: `BACKEND_URL` was `null` on any non-localhost host, so the whole tool was local-only while sitting in the hub next to 8 tools that work everywhere. Now `api/trace.py` + `api/health.py` run the **same pipeline** as Vercel Python functions; `backend/process.py` stays the single source of truth and is shared verbatim (`vercel.json` → `functions.includeFiles: backend/**`), with `api/_shared.py` supplying only the serverless plumbing. Frontend picks the endpoint automatically — `localhost:5050` locally, `/api` deployed, identical response shapes. **Three read-only-filesystem blockers, all found by measuring rather than guessing:** (1) `cv2.imwrite('output/debug_trace_input.png', …)` was an unconditional **relative-path** write depending on cwd — raises on any read-only FS; now conditional on a real `output_dir`. (2) All server writes routed through `writable_dir()`, honouring `STRATA_WORK_DIR` (`/tmp` on Vercel, `backend/output/` locally). (3) **potrace is an external binary** that doesn't exist on Vercel — the fallback now catches the missing executable and returns a valid empty SVG instead of a 500. Per-invocation scratch dirs are removed in a `finally`, verified clean after repeated calls since **Fluid Compute reuses instances** and a leak would accumulate. **Bundle size was the real go/no-go and I measured it from the actual manylinux wheels** rather than estimating: opencv-headless 143 MB + numpy 57 + pillow 19 + vtracer 3 = **222 MB against Vercel's 500 MB limit** (not the 250 MB I first assumed — checking the docs changed the decision from "too tight, don't" to "comfortable"). `opencv-python-headless` replaces `opencv-python`; the GUI bindings can't load there anyway. **A real bug this uncovered in shipped work:** `handle_send_to_figma` preferred `output/full.svg` whenever it existed and only then fell back to the POSTed SVG — so the Refine editor's edited geometry, and any region other than "full", **never reached Figma**. Verified with a marker SVG that the POSTed body was discarded. My earlier Refine verification had stopped at the frontend boundary (I confirmed the payload carried the refined SVG, never that the backend honoured it) — a gap in my own testing, not inherited code. **Deliberately still local-only: the Figma push.** It works by the plugin fetching a URL the local server keeps on disk, which serverless has nowhere to put; the plugin is shared by every Organica tool so it gets one unified pass rather than a Strata-specific workaround. Deployed, → Figma downloads the SVG and says so. Verified end to end: functions run under a **read-only cwd** (the real serverless condition), local backend unchanged (byte-identical trace output), and the full browser flow trace → Refine → export with style/units preserved. Manual: `docs/STRATA-REFINE.md` §6.

- **August 1, 2026 — Strata Refine/Preview state-sync + production 413 fixed.** Diego reported five bugs from live use of the Refine editor shipped July 31. All five verified live (real trace, real sketch upload) and fixed: **(1) Refine edits silently reverted** — `state.svgData` was written once right after trace and never updated again, so reopening Refine or switching to Preview reloaded that stale snapshot and discarded any edit (delete/colour/smooth); fixed by baking every Paper.js `onChange` straight back into `state.svgData`, and only reimporting into the editor on an actual new trace, not every tab switch. **(2) Preview showed stale content** — same root cause; Preview's `#svg-embed` was only ever painted at trace time. **(3) sketch/trace offset residual** — a *second*, independent cause from the one fixed July 27: the sketch underlay was scaled to the trace's full padded viewBox, but the backend pads 50px on every side (`copyMakeBorder`), so the sketch stretched into that padding; fixed by subtracting the padding from the scale calculation (position was already correct since padding is symmetric). **(4) 37 shapes but 36 exported paths** — `simplify(4)` could reduce a shape to zero segments, which `exportSVG()` silently drops from the markup while the shape stayed in the live model/pills as a "ghost"; fixed by pruning degenerate shapes after simplify. **(5) Smooth ignored selection** (unlike Fill/Border/Simplify) — fixed to scope to the selected shape, same rule as the others; this also fixed a related minor bug where `collectPaths` recursed into a CompoundPath's own children after already matching the CompoundPath itself, double-processing it. Also hardened `backend/server.py`'s `Content-Length` parsing (missing/non-numeric/negative/oversized header now returns a real error instead of an uncaught exception or unbounded read) — verified in isolation since the shared local dev server was mid-use by another session. **Separately, a production-only bug**: tracing a full-resolution phone-photo crop returned an opaque "Server error 413" on the deployed site only. Root cause: Vercel Serverless Functions hard-cap the request body at 4.5MB (platform-level, confirmed via Vercel's own docs, not app-configurable) — measured a real crop's PNG at **12MB**. Fixed by re-encoding the crop as JPEG before upload (cv2.imread is format-agnostic) with progressive quality/downscale fallback — same photo now uploads at **1.76MB**, verified end-to-end (200 OK, real trace). Also fixed `showServerGuidance()`, which picked its message off `BACKEND_URL` truthiness — true both locally AND in production (`/api` fallback) — so a production trace error always showed the stale "Strata runs locally, can't run on this static site" text even though tracing has run in production since July 31; now branches on `isLocal` and on whether the server actually responded, with a real "Trace failed" message for the reached-but-errored case. Manual: `docs/STRATA-REFINE.md` §6 (Refine bugs), inline comments in `backend/server.py` (Content-Length).

- **August 1, 2026 (later same day) — Shipped Camouflage (`/camouflage/`), Gray-Scott reaction-diffusion patterns.** Scoped alongside Komorebi at Diego's request, then deliberately kept as a **separate standalone tool** rather than a Komorebi pattern or a Komorebi feature addition — confirmed with Diego before building (asked, didn't assume): Gray-Scott is an iterative PDE that has to *converge* over many steps, a different interaction model from Komorebi's instant on-demand masks, and folding both in one file would repeat Living Path's own two-engine complexity. Two other asks were explicitly declined by Diego rather than silently reopened: reintroducing Komorebi's wind/animation loop (removed July 27, §17/§18) and Komorebi's Sun-position/disc-ellipse controls (removed July 27, §18) both stay removed. Built on the **same shell as Komorebi/Halide** (`tokens.css`/`header.css`/`panel.css`, `core.js` — no new shared code needed, every building block already existed) — genuinely new work is only the simulation core. **The one place in Organica where a continuous `requestAnimationFrame` loop is correct** rather than the on-demand scheduling every other tool uses: the loop *is* the algorithm (Gray-Scott has no single-frame result to schedule), same as Living Path's own CPU reaction-diffusion. Auto-pauses once the field's mean per-cell change stays below a threshold for 40 consecutive frames ("Simmering…" → "Settled · f=… k=…"), so it doesn't spin forever once converged. **Toroidal (wrap-around) grid from the start**, not retrofitted — makes the Zoom control (repeats the same seamless field, no reseed, no artefacts) and every export's seamless tiling free instead of two separate features. **Spatial gradient** blends a second f/k target across the canvas (Horizontal/Vertical/Diagonal/Radial) — e.g. leopard spots dissolving into labyrinth on one canvas — verified by sampling the live field (left-third vs right-third mean A−B measurably different, 0.56 vs 0.41) not just eyeballing it. SVG export traces the **reaction-diffusion field directly** (not a re-rasterised image) using the same posterised band-separation tracer Halide/Komorebi already share. **Honesty finding, disclosed in the manual rather than hidden**: isotropic Gray-Scott cannot produce true parallel stripes from random seeding — verified by testing the brief's own suggested "stripes" f/k and a transition zone, both gave labyrinth/worm patterns, not straight lines; shipped as an honestly-labelled "Stripes / worms" regime instead of a promise the algorithm can't keep, with an anisotropic-diffusion follow-up documented for later. **Real bug caught during verification, not before it**: init-order bug where `syncColor()` (painting the Ink/Paper swatches on load) triggered a render before the simulation grid was allocated — threw on first load, canvas stayed blank; fixed by allocating/seeding the grid first. Manual: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md`.

- **August 3, 2026 — Camouflage reframed as a 3-mode creative disruption engine (Phase 1 of the `docs/organica-camouflage-tool-brief.md` scope).** Diego supplied the missing research docs (`docs/Research_Report_Camouflage.md`, `docs/Research_Report_GenerativeArt.md`) and the brief itself, reframing the tool explicitly away from "match this environment" concealment toward Disruptive Pattern Material (Hardy Blechman's term) — camouflage as design language (dazzle ships, adaptive brand identity, Thayer's countershading), judged on aesthetic merit only, with an explicit non-goal (no adversarial/ML-detection-evasion functionality — noted, held to). Scoped via three rounds of clarifying questions before writing code (Structural mode: shared palette layer, not a 4th engine — confirmed; naming: kept "Camouflage" — confirmed; physical-output pipeline: both export-tuning AND a JS algorithm replica — confirmed, deferred to its own pass; "Concealed Motion" 7th animation category: deferred to its own pass, since it touches `docs/ANIMATION-SYSTEM.md`/`animations.css`, a file the other 55 forms depend on). Also evaluated and **declined** p5.js as a runtime dependency for this work — it would be Organica's first creative-coding framework, breaking the vanilla-Canvas2D consistency every tool (including Komorebi's own deliberate move *off* WebGL/GLSL) has held; used only as personal prototyping reference, same as the research report's own recommendation to study Shiffman's p5.js Coding Challenge #13 rather than embed it. **Shipped**: header rearchitected onto `.org-header--editor` + `.org-tabs` (a shared component defined in `header.css` since before Camouflage existed but never adopted by any tool — confirmed by grep before using it) for Disruptive/Dazzle/Countershade mode-switching. **Dazzle** (new): recursive polygon fragmentation, cut angles snapped toward 0/45/90/135° + jitter for a bold zigzag rather than generic-irregular tessellation, sibling facets from one split always differently coloured, pure vector end-to-end (canvas preview and SVG export share one facet list, no tracing). **Countershade** (new): Thayer-principle tonal gradient (dark-light-dark = fake volume), Linear (cylinder) or Radial (sphere), seeded Grain overlay for the "same design, every physical unit unique" packaging case; exports as a genuine SVG gradient def, not a raster. **Structural palette** (new, shared across all three modes, not a 4th mode — decided explicitly to avoid re-deriving Disruptive/Dazzle's geometry a second time just to recolour it): hue from local orientation (field gradient direction / facet cut angle), lightness from a local scalar — genuinely iridescent, Morpho-butterfly-adjacent result verified visually on Disruptive (rainbow rings around each spot). **Reproducible numeric Seed** (new, shared across modes, one `mulberry32` PRNG each generator resets itself from at the start of its run): verified directly, not assumed — regenerating Dazzle at the same seed twice (with a different seed run in between) produced byte-identical SVG both times. **Drop-mark seeding** (new, Disruptive only): seeds the simulation from Organica's own gravity-tailed teardrop gesture (a simplified port of Komorebi's `shapeDrop` SDF) instead of plain circles — the concrete link the research report draws between the drop mark and Thayer/Cott's disruptive coloration. **A real dead-control bug caught during verification, not before it**: the shared Palette's Threshold row stayed visible in Dazzle/Countershade under Duotone even though neither mode's render/export ever reads it — same class of bug as Komorebi §18 ("a control with no effect is a bug"); fixed by gating its visibility on `mode === 'disruptive'`. Per-mode preset stores (three separate `localStorage` keys, since the three modes' control sets don't overlap) rather than one shared list that would need per-mode filtering. Verified end-to-end in a live browser session: all three modes' render + SVG export in both Duotone and Structural (6 combinations, no exceptions), PNG export at ×2 scale with correct canvas restore, presets save/apply/delete per mode, 0 of 38 controls unaccessibly-named. **Deferred to their own passes, not built this round**: physical-output pipeline (Ink/Stitch density maps, vpype-flow-imager-style flow hatching, k-means/Lab screen-print separation) and the "Concealed Motion" animation category. Manual: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md`.

- **August 3, 2026 (later same day) — Camouflage output-quality audit: four measured defects, all fixed.** Diego's verdict on the three-mode build was that output quality was "limitata e mediocre". Rather than tune by eye, each mode was **measured**, and four distinct root causes came out — all the same underlying mistake in different clothes: **an internal representation leaking into the output**. **(1) Render resolution.** 54% of a settled field's cells hold an *intermediate* value — more than half the field is edge information — and the build threw all of it away by thresholding per CELL, writing an N×N ImageData and letting `drawImage` upscale: on a 128 grid at 640px, each of ~1,717 boundary cells became a hard **5-pixel stair-step**. Now the field is sampled **bilinearly at output-pixel resolution** with the threshold applied there; the simulation grid is untouched, this is purely how it's drawn (9.2ms/frame, 74fps with the solver — an affordable trade for the most visible defect in the tool). **(2) Dazzle facet distribution.** Measured: largest facet **29.6%** of canvas, top three **65.2%**, largest:smallest **1162×**, two thin slivers (perimeter²/area 689 vs 16 for a square) — i.e. "three blobs", not dazzle. Two causes: the cut point was **uniformly random in the bbox** (near an edge half the time = one huge piece + one sliver) and recursion was **depth-driven**, so a facet that won the big half stayed big regardless of size. Now cuts pass near the polygon **centroid** (±18%) and recursion is **area-driven**. After: largest **4.1%**, top three **12.1%**, ratio **3×**, worst sliver **55**, zero over 100. `Fragments` changed meaning from recursion depth (2–8) to approximate **facet count** (4–80) — what its label always implied. **(3) Structural colour.** The build mapped angle onto the **full 0–360° hue wheel** at fixed saturation: the classic rainbow colormap, ignoring the user's own Ink/Paper — and backwards from the phenomenon it cites, since `Research_Report_Camouflage.md` §1 is explicit that a Morpho's blue is a Bragg stack reflecting *"una banda stretta di lunghezze d'onda (~460nm)"*. Now a **76° arc anchored on the user's Ink hue** (falling back to Morpho blue 220° when Ink is achromatic, as the default is). A second defect found alongside: the gradient was sampled over a **sub-cell step**, and bilinear interpolation has a constant derivative inside a cell — so every pixel in a cell got the same hue, producing visible cell-sized facets; the step is now a full cell. **(4) SVG geometry.** The shared `Organica.contoursToPathD` walks CELL edges and emits axis-aligned segments — exactly right for Halide (where the subject *is* dithered square pixels), exactly wrong for organic blobs: measured **26 right-angle vertices per spot**, 1,220 straight segments, **zero curves**. Camouflage now reuses the shared tracer for **topology** (loop/hole detection, which is correct and worth sharing) and replaces only the **geometry** — decimate, low-pass filter, emit Catmull-Rom **cubic Béziers**: 1,209 curves, 0 straight lines, verified by overlaying the exported SVG on the canvas. The shared tracer is deliberately **not** modified (Halide depends on it), and Dazzle correctly still exports straight lines only since its facets are meant to be sharp. **(5) Countershade shading curve** — a physics error rather than a measurement: it ramped **linearly** from a flat plateau, which is why it read as "a flat area with dark edges" instead of a rounded form. Real self-shading has no plateau — a cylinder's Lambertian brightness is **√(1−x²)**, the rounded shoulder that *is* Thayer's countershading. Now 17 sampled stops shared by canvas and SVG so the two can't drift, with Falloff as the power. Verified end to end: all 7 mode×palette combinations export valid SVG, seed reproducibility intact, canvas restore after export correct, 0 unnamed controls, no console errors. Full before/after numbers: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md` §11b.

- **August 3, 2026 (still later same day) — Disruptive enrichment: anisotropic stripes + seed-from-shape.** Diego asked for four Disruptive enrichments (anisotropic stripes, seed-from-shape, cephalopod-style layered noise, Web Worker for higher resolution); sequenced the architectural one (Worker) last on purpose, so it ports one stable set of logic instead of being built into. Two landed this pass. **Anisotropic diffusion — three attempts, in order, the first two real failures caught by measuring, not eyeballing:** (1) a rotated diffusion tensor through axis-aligned finite differences (textbook formula) worked at θ=0°/90° but produced a **maze, not stripes** at θ=50° — the 4-point corner estimate of the mixed ∂²/∂x∂y term is too coarse once D⊥ gets small. (2) Rotating the *sampling* instead, with **bilinear** taps, fixed the topology at 50° but a direct ratio measurement (mean-squared step along vs across the stripe) explained why it still read soft: **9.24× at θ=0°** (exact grid lookup) collapsed to **2.15× at θ=50°** (bilinear blends in neighbours, leaking diffusion into the axis meant to be suppressed) — Catmull-Rom made it *worse*, confirming the leak scales with interpolation smoothness. (3) **Shipped: nearest-neighbour taps** — no interpolation, no blur to leak, clean directional split verified at every angle tested; the trade (faint lattice-aliasing stipple) is minor at Grid=Fine and part of why the new "True stripes (zebra)" preset sets it. A separate, earlier bug in the same feature: the unscaled tensor Laplacian diverged (field oscillating at the ±1 clamp bounds, `lastDiff` stuck ~2.0) because a plain second-difference stencil is ~5× the magnitude of the existing hand-tuned 9-point kernel at the same nominal coefficient — fixed by matching scale (×0.2), not by guessing a smaller number. New controls: Anisotropy › Amount (0 = original kernel, unchanged — every existing preset stays on it) and Direction. At high Anisotropy, `seed()` also switches from random blobs to seeds **laid out along the stripe direction and repeated across the perpendicular axis** — the first version kept random placement and produced clean stripes near the few seed points with blank canvas everywhere else, since suppressed perpendicular diffusion can no longer carry information sideways to unseeded regions. **Seed from shape**: a third Seed shape option opens a file picker (Halide/Strata output drops straight in) and seeds B only inside the dark region via the same **masked reaction-diffusion** Living Path already uses on glyphs — cells outside the mask are pinned to the resting state every step, not just at t=0, so the pattern can't leak past the silhouette no matter how long it runs. Verified directly: seeded from a synthetic star, ran to settlement, confirmed **0 of ~24,957 outside cells** had moved — 0.00% leak, not "looks contained." Full regression pass (all 6 Disruptive presets + Dazzle + Countershade) confirmed no breakage. Deferred to their own passes: cephalopod-style layered noise (a genuine new noise primitive, bigger scope) and the Web Worker. Full detail: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md` §11c.

- **August 3, 2026 (still later same day) — Camouflage preset rebuild + three new Disruptive parameters.** Ask was to verify the presets actually make sense, build better ones, and survey the other tools for reusable parameters/functions. Ran a cross-tool inventory first (Komorebi's noise/scatter/raking stack; Living Path's 9 raster algorithms, group/blend architecture and marching-squares contours; Pollen's blue-noise + flow-field streamlines; Halide's dithering) before writing any code. **Presets were measured, not eyeballed** — each run to settlement from a fixed seed and fingerprinted (ink %, connected components, mean component area, local-density spread). Two of seven failed on their own terms: **`Drop-mark disruption` was a straight duplicate of `Labyrinth / coral`** (identical f/k, settled to 42.9% vs 40.8% ink with the same topology — a seed *shape* leaves no trace once a Gray-Scott field converges, since the seed decides *where* features land, never *what kind*), and **`Stripes / worms` settled at 63.7% ink**, so dark it read as solid with light cracks. Now **15 presets**, verified by signature check to have **0 identical field-generating parameter pairs**. **Useful caveat about the metric itself**: it is rotation-invariant, so `True stripes` and `Tiger diagonal` fingerprinted identically (52.8/11/1487 vs 52.7/11/1485) — a separate directional measurement confirmed stripe angles 0° vs 65°, anisotropy ratios 9.4 vs 12.0. The metric was wrong for that pair, not the presets. **Three new parameters, each fixing something the tool couldn't do:** **(1) Feature size** — `dA`/`dB` had been hardcoded at 1.0/0.5 forever, leaving no way to change how *big* features are (Grid changes cost, Zoom repeats a tile), even though `Research_Report_Camouflage.md` §1 states outright that diffusion coefficients are the scale control. The obstacle was stability: explicit Euler holds only while dt·D ≲ 1 and dA was **already exactly at that limit**, so Scale raises D and lowers dt together (dt·dA pinned at 1) — dt changes only how fast a reaction-diffusion system reaches its steady state, not what that state is. Verified bit-identical at s=1 (no shipped preset moved), and mean component area came out **49 → 101 → 164 → 215** for s=0.5/1.0/1.6/2.4, proportional to s exactly as area ∝ λ² ∝ D predicts. **A real physical bug surfaced doing this**: at s=2 the pattern died out entirely (0 blobs) because nucleation sites sized for D=1 disperse below critical density once D doubles — seed size now scales with √s. **(2) Modulation** (the cephalopod-skin layer): a slow noise field varies f/k per cell so one canvas carries several regimes at once; `hash2`/`vnoise`/`fbm` ported verbatim from Komorebi rather than reinvented. **Defect caught by measuring the field itself, not the output**: plain fbm's *mean drifts with frequency*, so `fbm*2−1` measured entirely negative at low Region size (−0.29…−0.53) and pushed f/k consistently one way — quietly changing regime instead of texturing it; fixed with `fbmSigned` (accumulates `vnoise−0.5`, centred at zero by construction), and the slider floor set to 1.8 after measuring residual bias across 6 seeds (0.188 at 1.2 → 0.076 at 2.0), since below that less than one noise cycle fits the canvas and it degenerates into the ramp Gradient already does better. **(3) Persistence** — re-injects B at the original sites every step instead of only at t=0, so the drop-mark shape survives into the settled pattern; verified by correlating the result against the injection map: **−0.02 → 0.35 → 0.45** for 0/0.35/0.8, where the −0.02 restates the duplicate-preset diagnosis numerically. Also exposed **Sites** (nucleation count, previously hardcoded `5 + rng()*4`). Full regression: 15/15 presets apply, 7/7 mode×palette SVG exports valid, canvas restore correct, seed reproducibility intact, 0 unnamed controls, no console errors. **Deferred, evaluated but not built** (Diego's call this round): Living Path's `blendField`/`rasterFieldFromGroups` group+blend architecture, its `skeleton` and marching-squares contours, Pollen's flow-field streamlines, Halide's dithering. Full detail: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md` §11d.

- **August 3, 2026 (still later same day) — Camouflage: two real bugs + convergence readout.** Asked to re-explain the Disruptive algorithm and all its presets; documenting it against the actual code surfaced two bugs the UI was hiding. **(1) `Sites` was dead whenever Anisotropy > 0.3** — the anisotropic branch of `seed()` lays nucleation points out as rows along the stripe direction and never read the control; measured at Anisotropy 0.85, Sites 5/20/40 all seeded exactly **675 cells**. Sites now drives the row spacing, but over a deliberately narrower range than on the isotropic path, and that is physics rather than a UI compromise: suppressed perpendicular diffusion can't carry the pattern sideways into an unseeded band, so sparse rows leave bare canvas. **My first attempt did exactly that** — mapping Sites straight onto a row count seeded 3 rows on a 176 grid at the default and left most of the frame empty; caught by looking at the render, not at the numbers, and fixed by interpolating between "just dense enough to fill" and "tightly packed" with the default landing on the previously-hardcoded spacing. **(2) The canonical f/k values were literally unreachable** — a range input snaps to `min + n·step`, and at `step="0.0005"` the Pearson/Munafo spots-mitosis pair fell between stops: f 0.0367 → **0.0365**, k 0.0649 → **0.0650**. So the `Leopard spots` preset declared one pair and the simulation ran another (visible in the status bar all along). Step on f/k/f2/k2 is now 0.0001 and both are exact; Coral labyrinth's 0.0545/0.0620 happened to land on a stop and were always right. **(3) The simulation stopped too early** — the symptom Diego actually reported ("l'animazione a un certo punto si ferma"). The stop is deliberate (Gray-Scott converges), but the test was "mean per-cell change per step under 4e-5", and measuring showed three things wrong with it: `lastDiff` **never reaches zero** (it settles on a noise plateau at ~2.4e-5, barely under the cutoff, so the cutoff fired mid-evolution — and lowering it would mean never stopping); it is **not monotonic** (traced `f19 6.2e-5 ↓ · f31 5.1e-5 ↑ · f55 1.6e-4 ↑`, because Gray-Scott has an *expansion* phase where spots multiply and change climbs again); and at the old stop point the structure was still forming (47 blobs / 25.8% ink, versus 49 / 26.7% when left to run — it was stopping mid-mitosis). **Two replacement criteria failed before the third worked**, each caught by measurement: "stopped improving" on `lastDiff` fires during the expansion climb and was *worse* (stopped at 1.2s with **30.7%** of cells still to flip); "drift stopped improving" never fires at all, since drift decays smoothly and indefinitely (Giant cell 35% → 11% → 8% → 4% → 1% of peak, no plateau) so it ran to the 30s cap while ink% had been flat since 7.5s. Shipped: measure the **field** the way a viewer would — snapshot, wait a window, see how far the picture moved — and stop when drift falls to **12% of that run's own peak**, counted cumulatively because drift spikes back up occasionally (7.4% → 15.0% → 8.0%) and a consecutive counter resets on every blip. Also scaled **steps per frame by 1/dt**, since Feature size lowers dt and `Giant cell` was overrunning the cap purely because each frame advanced less simulated time. Result: Leopard spots 6.6s/11.0%-still-to-flip → 8.5s/8.5%; Giant cell 30s-cap → 6.0s/4.0%. **Disclosed honestly**: the residual never reaches zero — once the structure is fixed the features still migrate slowly forever (16.3% → 7.9% → 5.2% flip at 6k/12k/20k steps); the criterion guarantees the *structure* has settled, not every pixel. **(4) Convergence readout** — "Simmering…" now carries a percentage. Two refinements from watching a real run: the signal **rises** during growth, pinning a first-sample baseline at 0% for half the run (fixed by baselining on the peak), and it fluctuates near the end, making the readout go **85% → 81% → 99%** (fixed by making it monotonic). Full detail: `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md` §11e. Full regression: 15/15 presets, 7/7 mode×palette SVG exports, seed reproducibility, canvas restore, 0 unnamed controls, no console errors. Also: the 23 generated preset previews were pulled back out of git (`camouflage/previews/`, 1.6MB of tool *output*, regenerable on demand) and added to .gitignore alongside the existing reference/test-imagery block.

- **August 10, 2026 — Shipped Warping (`/warping/`), plus a shared noise module and a bug fix to Camo Turing's committed anisotropic-diffusion work.** Session started as a Camo Turing follow-up (domain-warping the anisotropic stripe direction with `uWarpAmount`/`uWarpScale`, using `hash2`/`vnoise`/`fbm`); Diego stopped it mid-implementation and redirected to a standalone tool instead, so those in-progress Camo Turing edits were reverted (`git checkout`) and Camo Turing itself was **not modified** this session. Asked for a reuse analysis before writing code, then three explicit decisions: **Canvas2D/JS** over WebGL (same reasoning as Komorebi's own July move off GLSL — the pattern family is a pure per-pixel function of (x,y), no iterative convergence, and Canvas2D stays debuggable); **extract `hash2`/`vnoise`/`fbm`/`ridgedFbm`/`voronoiF1F2` into `shared/noise.js`** now that a third consumer exists (Warping), rather than a third private copy — ported verbatim from Komorebi's own JS (itself a port of older GLSL), then **Komorebi switched to consume the shared module** (one-line alias `const { hash2, hash2b, vnoise, fbm, ridgedFbm, voronoiF1F2 } = Organica.noise`) and verified regression-clean: Cellular pattern (its own `voronoiF1F2` user) renders identically, Canvas Bloom's `buildSVG()` still produces the exact real 2-loop evenodd path the §16 fix required (not the single full-canvas rectangle that bug produced), zero console errors. Load order is load-bearing and documented in the new file's own header: core → noise → tool script, since core's own last line (`global.Organica = Organica`) would clobber a `.noise` attached before it. **All three pattern families in v1**: Wood grain / Marble veining (fbm turbulence — `sin` of radius or position plus fbm-driven phase), Cellular network (Worley F1/F2, `edge = f2−f1` thresholded — the direct "giraffe reticulation" case from the original ask, reusing Komorebi's own Cellular-mask technique verbatim), Level curves (fbm field sliced into N threshold bands — topographic contours). **Warp is the tool's namesake control**: bends the *sampled coordinates* (two decorrelated fbm taps, same `(5.2, 1.3)` offset trick as Komorebi's `windOffset`) before any pattern function runs, so it's one shared mechanism across all four patterns rather than a per-pattern reimplementation. **Two real bugs caught by looking at the render, not by reasoning about the numbers**: (1) the first version's world-coordinate scale made every slider's own stated meaning wrong — "Rings: 6" produced ~25 stripes, because `rings` multiplied raw un-normalised coordinates directly; fixed by normalising every pattern's frequency input by a shared `NORM` constant, so a slider now reads as "cycles across the canvas half-width" consistently across all four patterns. (2) Seed was baked into the *base* coordinate before the geometric radius/direction math, which for Wood/Marble silently shoved the pattern's own centre far off-frame at the default seed — wood rings read as near-straight parallel lines instead of concentric rings, since far from a circle's centre the local curvature is negligible; fixed by keeping Seed's effect confined to each pattern's own *noise* sampling (grain/turbulence/warp), never the geometric position term. Verified visually after the fix: Oak plank reads as genuine concentric rings, Burl wood (high Warp) as visibly twisted grain, Giraffe hide as a real polygonal reticulation, Topographic map as real closed contour lines. Export reuses Komorebi's own `buildSVG()` shape verbatim (posterised N-band luminance separation via the shared rectilinear tracer, "≥ level" stacking so bands can't gap) — confirmed real multi-path geometry (5 paths / 6 bands on Giraffe hide, ~245 KB at scale ×2), not a degenerate rectangle. 8 built-in presets across the four patterns, `Organica.presetStore('warping', …)`, 0 of 23 controls unaccessibly-named (`Organica.autoLabelPanel`), zero console errors across every preset switch tested. Hub nav + `/warping/` route (`vercel.json`) added. **Not yet done**: the marble preset reads more like directional brushed grain than classic branching veins and could use a further tuning pass.
- **August 10, 2026 (later same day) — Wrote `docs/CAMO-TURING.md` and `docs/WARPING.md`, the two manuals missing for the repo's two newest tools.** Camo Turing had never had a manual at all (only the stale `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md`, describing the removed predecessor tool); read the actual shipped file end to end — Seeds' six modes, Anisotropy, Layers' two independent blend systems (field-space shape vs. per-layer tint colour), the Transformers raster stack ported from Living Path, Compare/diff, Palette's three colour modes, Paper background image/stencil's real `<clipPath>`+`<image>` SVG embedding, and the 23 built-in presets (mrob/Munafo catalogue + animal/plant) — rather than writing from the session summary alone, since that only covered a fraction of what the file actually does. Corrected the tools table's Camo Turing row in the process: it had accumulated a claim (domain-warped stripe direction) from the reverted false-start earlier this same session that was never actually shipped.

- **August 10, 2026 (still later same day) — Shipped Loom (`/loom/`) Phase 1 MVP, from a Notion brief (`Grid Definition`) Diego shared and asked for analysis-only first.** Read the full brief before writing anything, then presented an architecture analysis rather than starting to build (per the brief's own instruction: "Always explain architectural decisions before writing code"), covering the pipeline (Canvas → Grid Definition → Constraint Engine → Universal JSON Model → renderers), the elementary-generators-vs-named-presets distinction the brief's own two lists imply, and three explicit forks put to Diego: Kiwi.js (Cassowary constraint solver) vs. a hand-rolled parametric solver for the Constraint Engine, single-file vs. native ES modules for the file structure, and math.js vs. hand-rolled math. **Decisions**: hybrid solver (Kiwi for modular/column/row/Bento-family grids where equal-by-default-but-overridable sizing is the real behaviour wanted — verified feasible by fetching and inspecting the actual npm package before committing to it; parametric direct math for continuous-function generators like Sinusoidal, since a sine curve isn't a linear constraint); native ES modules (the one deliberate deviation from every other Organica tool's single-file convention — a real module boundary exists here that a single huge file would obscure); math hand-rolled for the MVP's two generators, math.js deferred until a generator actually needs it. **Vendored `shared/vendor/kiwi.min.js`** (Cassowary solver, MIT/BSD-3-Clause, 14.5KB, fetched from the npm registry tarball and inspected — not npm-installed, since this repo has no build step) — same vendoring convention as Paper.js in Strata: a real file, not a CDN, license recorded. **Shipped**: Canvas Manager (size/unit/margin/safe area/bleed, 7 industry presets), two generators — **Bento** (Kiwi-solved: seeded random cell-merge spans on a lattice, equal-by-default track sizing with a hard minimum and a hard canvas-fill sum) and **Sinusoidal** (parametric: sine-modulated track *sizes*, disclosed honestly as not wavy *boundaries* since neither CSS Grid nor an axis-aligned SVG grid can represent that) — the Universal JSON Model (`tracks` + cell spans as the canonical source, pixel rects always derived, never stored redundantly), and three renderers: a live preview that is a genuine `display:grid` (not simulated with absolute positioning), SVG (outlined cells — grid guides, not filled shapes), and PNG; Figma export reuses the existing `figma-plugin/`'s SVG-import path verbatim, honestly labelled as not-yet-native-Auto-Layout (that's Phase 5). **One real bug caught during Bento's own build, before it shipped**: an initial track-weighting scheme (wider cells should pull their tracks wider) divided each cell's contribution by its own span, which made wide-cell tracks *thinner* — the opposite of the intended look; reverted to plain equal-by-default sizing, since Bento's visual variety already comes entirely from span topology. Hub nav link added directly after Creator (Diego's own placement request) + `/loom/` route in `vercel.json`. Verified live: both generators render correctly, `variety=0` is an exact no-op (16 plain 1×1 cells), Kiwi-solved equal columns sum exactly to the inner canvas width net of margin and gaps (checked numerically, not just visually), all four exports (PNG/SVG/HTML/JSON) and the Figma send run without throwing, 0 of 20 controls unaccessibly-named, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md`, including the full Phase 2–6 roadmap (remaining ~23 elementary/preset generators, WYSIWYG cell editing, native Figma Auto Layout frames, tests).

- **August 11, 2026 — Loom: Export moved from the header to the shared bottom-centre floating toolbar.** Diego caught that Loom's header still had its own `Export ▾` + `→ Figma` buttons — a leftover from `shared/_template.html`'s own layout, which predates the product-wide move (documented in `camo-turing/index.html`'s own comment, and mirrored verbatim in `strata/index.html`) of Export into `shared/floatbar.css` and the outright removal of the Figma button pending its return as an Export-popover tab. Every other Organica tool (Strata, Spore, Pollen, Halide, Komorebi, Living Path, Genesis Creator, Camo Turing) already follows this; Loom now matches: header is logo + status only, the Export popover (PNG/SVG/HTML/JSON, PNG scale) lives in the floatbar fixed bottom-centre over the canvas, and `sendToFigma()` stays defined as a plain, unwired function — same "ready to reuse once it's a popover tab" convention Strata's own comment states. One real markup bug caught in the move, not before it: a stray `</path>` left over from editing the Export icon's SVG, which is invalid but silently tolerated by browsers (closing tags on void/self-closed elements are ignored) — found by re-reading the diff, not by any visible symptom. Verified live: floatbar renders bottom-centre offset for the right panel exactly like Komorebi/Camo Turing, popover opens upward, all four export functions run without throwing, zero console errors.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: mouse-wheel zoom/pan, via the shared `Organica.createZoomPan` rather than another private copy.** Checked first: Halide/Spore/Pollen/Strata each still carry their OWN private zoom/pan implementation, and only `shared/_template.html` actually calls the shared `Organica.createZoomPan` — a real, already-known drift (flagged back in the July 25 system-pass session notes). Rather than add a fifth private copy, wired Loom directly to the shared function, on `#canvas-frame` (a pure CSS transform — never touches `canvas.width/height` or the JSON model, so it can't affect any export; verified live that all four exports still run correctly mid-zoom). **A real UX gap found during verification, not assumed**: `createZoomPan`'s default zoom range is `[1, 12]` — zoom-OUT below 100% isn't possible by default, and Loom's canvas is rendered at its own real declared size (anywhere from an A4's 297px to a 1920px social preset), unlike Halide/Komorebi's own capped ~900px preview resolution — so a large canvas landed clipped by `#canvas-wrap`'s `overflow:hidden` with no way back except a keyboard shortcut.
