Fixed two ways: `min:0.1` so the user can always zoom out manually, and a `fitToViewIfNeeded()` that runs once whenever the canvas's own physical size changes (not on every unrelated param tweak) so a freshly picked preset lands fully visible by default instead of clipped. Verified: 1080×1080 auto-fits to ~63–72% depending on viewport, A4 portrait (small) stays at a true 100% (fit-scale correctly clamps at 1, never enlarges), zoom toward cursor confirmed via a dispatched `WheelEvent`, zero console errors.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: right-panel slider readouts were silently frozen, fixed once for the whole panel.** Asked to debug the right column and specifically verify whether the Grid sliders update their displayed numbers. They didn't: `Organica.enhanceSliders`'s own delegated listener (`core.js`) only maintains each range input's `--fill` CSS custom property (the coloured track) and a click-to-edit affordance on the value span — it never syncs the span's TEXT from a drag, that's always been each tool's own job via a hand-typed `oninput="syncVal(this,'v-x')"` per control. Loom's Canvas (Margin, Safe area) and Grid (all 13 Bento/Sinusoidal params) sliders never got that wired, so dragging any of them left the number frozen at its initial HTML value while the grid itself rebuilt correctly underneath — confirmed live: `rg-bento-cols` set to 8 rebuilt the JSON model to 8 real column tracks, while `v-bento-cols` kept reading "4". Fixed with one delegated listener on `#panel` instead of 13 hand-typed attributes (the exact failure mode that caused the bug — easy to add a 14th control later and forget it again): reads any `input[type=range]`'s own `.ctrl-row`, finds its `.ctrl-val`, writes the value. Verified across Bento and Sinusoidal, including a value that got step-snapped by the browser (0.77 → 0.78 at `step=0.02`) — confirms the fix reads the slider's actual post-snap value, not a stale JS-side number.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: grid wasn't centred in its own canvas, root cause in the CSS Grid renderer.** Diego caught it visually after the slider-readout fix. Measured before touching anything: `#grid-preview`'s inline style sized the container to the FULL canvas (`width/height: 1080px`) while `grid.tracks` (Kiwi/parametric-solved) only sum to the INNER rect after margin (993.6px at 4% margin) — a plain `display:grid` container with no padding places its tracks starting at the top-left content edge by default, so all 86.4px of margin space landed entirely at the bottom-right instead of split across all four sides. Fixed in `html-renderer.js`'s `buildGridCSS`: container stays sized to the full canvas but now gets the margin as real `padding` (`box-sizing:border-box`), so the already-correct track sizes centre themselves naturally — no separate inner wrapper needed, and the fix applies identically to the live preview and the exported HTML snippet since both come from the same function. Verified numerically, not just visually: left/top/right/bottom gaps from canvas edge to the outermost cells now measure 27px/27px/27px/27px (scaled by the current fit-to-view zoom) on both Bento and Sinusoidal, where before the bottom/right gaps were visibly larger than top/left. SVG/PNG/JSON exports unaffected (they read `resolveCellRects`, a separate code path that was never wrong), reconfirmed running clean after the fix.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: grid line colour changed to Photoshop's default guide blue (`#3399ff`), everywhere at once.** Added `--guide-blue` to Loom's own `:root` (a content colour — the line the tool draws — not shared UI chrome, same exception CLAUDE.md already carves out for Halide's ink/paper), applied to `.loom-cell`'s border. SVG/PNG renderers took a `lineColor` param instead of a second hardcoded hex, and `main.js` reads the CSS variable once via `getComputedStyle` and passes it to both — so the live preview and every export read the exact same value rather than two hand-matched literals that could quietly drift apart later. Verified: preview renders visibly blue; captured the actual exported SVG blob (temporarily intercepting `Organica.download`) and confirmed it contains `#3399ff` and zero remaining `#0a0a0a` cell strokes; PNG/HTML/JSON exports still run clean.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: overlapping cell borders now draw as one line, not two stacked.** Asked to fix "when two lines overlap, show it as one line." Measured first rather than assuming a positioning bug: at Gap 0, adjacent cells' touching edges land at a 0.0000076px difference — geometrically exact, not misaligned. The real cause was every cell independently stroking all 4 of its own sides, so a shared boundary between two adjacent cells got drawn TWICE, exactly coincident; two overlapping anti-aliased 1px strokes compound into a visibly bolder/darker line than a genuine single border, in both the vector exports and (potentially) the CSS preview. Fixed at the source in three places sharing one idea — draw each unique boundary once: **(1)** `json-model.js` gained `collectEdges(rects)`, a de-duplicated segment list (canonical-keyed, coordinates rounded first so solver float noise can't split one edge into two keys). **(2)** `svg-renderer.js`/`raster-renderer.js` now stroke that edge list (`<line>` / one `ctx.stroke()` path) instead of one `<rect>`/`strokeRect()` per cell — verified the exported SVG for a 10-cell Bento at Gap 0 contains 32 `<line>`s and only the 2 unrelated background/guide `<rect>`s, zero per-cell rects. **(3)** The live CSS preview needed a different technique (a DOM grid can't draw an independent line) — every `.loom-cell` now sets only `border-right`/`border-bottom` inline, plus `border-left`/`border-top` on col-0/row-0 cells only, so a shared edge is always drawn by exactly one of the two neighbouring cells. Caught a related latent bug while touching this: the exported standalone HTML snippet referenced `var(--guide-blue)` with no fallback, which resolves to nothing (invisible border) once pasted into a page that doesn't load Loom's own `:root` — added the `, #3399ff` fallback. Verified across Bento (Gap 0 and Gap 12) and Sinusoidal, PNG/SVG/HTML/JSON exports all clean, zero console errors.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: Shuffle numbers.** New `Numbering` panel section (`Shuffle` / `Sequential`). Each cell gained a `number` field, independent of its `col`/`row`/`span` — shuffling relabels which block shows which number via a Fisher–Yates permutation (not the classic biased `sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5)`) without touching any actual layout geometry. `paintGridDOM`/`buildHTMLSnippet` read `c.number` (falling back to array index for safety). Sequential numbering is the default on every rebuild — deliberately not persisted as a param: any panel change re-runs `build()`, which regenerates `cells` from scratch and resets to sequential, since a shuffle is a one-off action on the current grid, not part of the reproducible generator pipeline. Verified: shuffled output is a true permutation of 1..N (same set, new order), JSON model reflects it, all four exports still run clean, 0 console errors.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: cell Padding, plus a Canvas Manager extraction note.** Evaluated (not built) turning Loom's Canvas Manager into a shared component: split it in two on the call — the zoom/pan + fit-to-view shell is genuinely universal and already duplicated privately in Halide/Spore/Pollen (only `shared/_template.html` and now Loom call the existing `Organica.createZoomPan`), but deferred per Diego's own call; the physical-space data model (unit/margin/safe-area/bleed/presets) stays Loom-only for now — every prior Organica `shared/` extraction happened at a second real consumer, never speculatively, even though it's a plausible seed for `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own Phase 6 print/mural output pipeline. Documented the trigger condition in `docs/LOOM.md` §8 rather than just dropping the idea. **Shipped**: a `Padding` control (Grid section) that insets each cell's own visible box inward from its track allocation — independent of `Gap` (which changes the track sizes the Kiwi/parametric solvers actually resolve). Implemented as `grid.padding` on the Universal JSON Model (a persisted render parameter, not ephemeral UI state, so every renderer reads the same value): `resolveCellRects` shrinks each SVG/PNG rect symmetrically (clamped at 0, verified no throw at max Padding on the smallest cells), and the CSS/HTML renderer applies it as `margin` rather than literal `padding` — a literal CSS padding would only push the already-centred number label further from an unmoved border, a no-op, where `margin` is what actually shrinks the bordered box within its grid area. A real side effect, not a bug to special-case: once Padding pulls two adjacent cells apart, `collectEdges` naturally stops finding a shared edge to dedupe — it doesn't need to know Padding exists. Verified: live readout syncs (reuses the existing delegated panel listener), 0 is an exact no-op (no `margin` written to the DOM at all), Padding persists in the exported JSON, all four exports run clean at both 0 and the slider's max.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: cm and m added as canvas units, via a canonical-mm conversion layer, not a label-only add.** Analysed first, presented the fork explicitly: mm/cm/m convert cleanly with decimal factors, px does not (no fixed physical size, converting it would assume an unstated DPI) — Diego confirmed the real conversion layer over the faster label-only option, since the latter leaves Gap/Padding meaningless at metre scale (a mural's Gap slider would range 0–40 *metres*). **Found and fixed alongside** (Diego's own call, same code path anyway): SVG/CSS export never actually emitted a unit suffix — an A4 canvas exported `width="210"` (210px per the SVG/CSS spec's own bare-number default), not 210mm; `docs/LOOM.md`'s prior claim that this worked was wrong, never verified. **Shipped**: `canvas-manager.js` canonicalises any mm/cm/m input to an internal mm-equivalent (`canvas.width`/`height`), which every generator and the on-screen sizing already read directly — `canvas.displayWidth`/`displayHeight`/`unit` keep the raw typed number, unconverted, for labels only. Gap/Padding (absolute numbers, unlike percentage-based Margin/Safe area) route through the same canonicalisation (`unitVal()`), and their own slider range/step now rescales on unit change (`rescaleUnitField`) to preserve the physical size rather than leaving the raw number meaning something completely different — verified live end to end: 12mm Gap → 1.2cm → 0.02m (step-snapped, expected) switching units in sequence, then back to px correctly restored the 0–40 range. Export unit suffix: neither SVG nor CSS has a native metre unit, so any non-px unit exports as literal `mm` off the already-canonical number (a 3m×2m mural canvas verified exporting `width="3000mm" height="2000mm"`, dimensionally exact) — `viewBox` and the grid's own internal CSS stay bare canonical numbers rendered as px, only the outer declared size carries physical meaning. **A real bug caught by testing preset switches, not assumed away**: `applyCanvasPreset()` set `sel-unit` and a bare `lastUnit` tracking variable directly, bypassing the rescale entirely — picking a px preset right after an mm one left Gap's slider stuck at the mm range while the Unit field said px. Fixed by routing the preset's own unit change through the same `onUnitChange()` path a manual switch uses. All four exports verified clean at px/mm/cm/m, zero console errors.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: cm/m made the canvas disappear — three real bugs, all found by reproducing before fixing.** Diego reported the canvas vanishing under cm/m; reproduced first rather than guessing. **(1) Zoom clamp edge case**: switching Unit to cm with the default "1080" left unchanged (a real px→cm value has no conversion to preserve, see the cm/m session note above) reinterprets it as 1080cm = 10.8m — `fitToViewIfNeeded` needed ~5% zoom to fit, but `Organica.createZoomPan`'s own `min: 0.1` clamped it to 10%, and the shared library zeroes pan outright whenever zoom clamps to exactly its own minimum — reproduced via `getBoundingClientRect()` landing at `x:-5087, y:-4908`, entirely off-screen. Fixed by lowering `min` to 0.01 (covers real canvases up to ~50–100m before the same edge could recur). **(2) Double conversion**: made Width/Height rescale between physical units too (mm↔cm↔m preserve real size, same as Gap/Padding already did) — but `applyCanvasPreset()` still routed through the same `onUnitChange()`, which reconverted a value the preset had ALREADY written correctly (A4 landed at "2100" instead of "210" after switching units by hand, then picking the preset). Fixed by splitting Gap/Padding rescaling (`rescaleGapPadding`, used by both paths) from Width/Height rescaling (only in the manual `onUnitChange()`, never in `applyCanvasPreset()`, which supplies its own already-correct numbers). **(3) Uncaught solver crash**: a physically tiny canvas (typed while working in m, then Unit switched to px with — correctly — no conversion, since px has no physical equivalent to convert to) crashed the whole app with Kiwi's own uncaught "unsatisfiable constraint" exception. A fixed canonical-size floor was tried first and rejected: the real minimum depends on whatever Columns/Gap/Margin happen to be dialled in, not a constant. Fixed properly with a try/catch around `generator.generate()` in `build()`, degrading to a clear status message ("Canvas too small for this grid") for ANY unreachable parameter combination, not just this one path into it. All three verified live after the fix: the original repro (px→cm, unchanged number) now renders and stays centred, the preset-after-manual-unit-change sequence no longer double-converts, and the tiny-canvas sequence shows the status message with zero console errors instead of crashing.

- **August 11, 2026 (same day) — Loom: canvas size capped at 50 000 canonical units (50m at any physical unit).** Diego's own call after the cm/m debugging above. Applied as a ceiling alongside the existing floor (`MIN_CANONICAL`) at `createCanvas`'s single choke point — `clampCanonical()`. 50m chosen to stay well inside the range already verified working this session (`createZoomPan`'s `min:0.01` fits up to ~50–100m before its own zoom-clamp/pan-zero edge case could resurface) while covering every realistic Studio Rann mural. Verified live: 500m clamps to exactly 50 000 canonical units in the JSON model, canvas still renders centred and fit-to-view, zero console errors — `displayWidth` (what Diego actually typed) is deliberately left unclamped, only the geometry-driving canonical value is capped.

- **August 11, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Voronoi, the first polygon-shaped generator.** Asked to pick the next generator off the brief's own list; recommended Voronoi (direct reuse of `shared/noise.js`) and Diego confirmed — but building it surfaced that the "direct reuse" plan was wrong: `voronoiF1F2` is a per-pixel RASTER query (nearest/second-nearest distance at one point, Komorebi's own Cellular-pattern technique), not something that produces cell polygon VERTICES — a grid-definition tool needs the actual boundary geometry, not a distance field. **Real architecture fork, surfaced and handled rather than faked**: Voronoi cells are arbitrary convex polygons with no honest fit onto Bento/Sinusoidal's shared `tracks + col/row/span` model (a bounding-box approximation would be exactly the kind of degenerate output this project's conventions already refuse elsewhere — the Padding no-op discipline, the real-vector-SVG rule). Extended the Universal JSON Model with `grid.cellShape: 'rect' | 'polygon'` as the fork every renderer branches on. **Cell construction**: seed points scattered (seeded RNG) in the inner rect, each cell built by half-plane intersection — Sutherland–Hodgman clip of the inner rect against every other seed's perpendicular bisector, O(N²), far simpler to get right than Fortune's algorithm at the cell counts this tool actually uses. `solver: 'geometric'` — a genuine third category alongside Kiwi/parametric, since there's no track lattice and no linear constraint system involved at all. **Renderers**: `svg-renderer.js`/`raster-renderer.js` now branch internally (moved the rect-vs-polygon resolve INSIDE each renderer, so main.js's export call sites don't need to know or care which kind of grid they're exporting); a new `collectPolygonEdges` (json-model.js) generalises the existing `collectEdges` rect-dedup to arbitrary polygon edges — two adjacent Voronoi cells share the exact bisector segment bounding them both, so stroking each cell's own full outline independently would have reintroduced the identical doubled-line bug `collectEdges` fixed for rect grids earlier this session. **No honest CSS Grid form exists for a polygon cell**, so `paintGridDOM`/`buildHTMLSnippet` branch to a new `buildPolygonSVG` (embedded SVG, built from the same de-duplicated edges the SVG *export* uses — about as close to byte-identical preview/export as this tool gets) instead of forcing one. Numbers are drawn at each polygon's centroid in that preview SVG — disclosed as staying consistent with the pre-existing convention that numbers were always a preview/HTML-only feature, never in the SVG/PNG export, for rect grids too. Gap/Padding hidden (not left inert) for Voronoi, since neither has a defined meaning on a polygon cell yet — same "a dead control is a bug" rule Komorebi's own audit established. Verified live: real polygon diagram renders (not a raster field), SVG export contains 55 deduplicated `<line>`s and zero `<rect>`/`<polygon>` cell shapes for an 18-point diagram, Shuffle produces a true permutation of the SVG `<text>` labels, switching back to Bento/Sinusoidal shows zero regression (live CSS Grid preview, Padding row reappears), 0 of 23 controls unaccessibly-named, zero console errors throughout.

- **August 12, 2026 (later same day) — Loom: fixed a focused-slider/wheel-zoom conflict, not a Hexagonal geometry bug.** Diego reported specific hexagons (39, 44) looking wrong after using the new generator. Investigated the geometry first rather than assuming the report was right: fetched the actual JSON model and SVG DOM for both cells — regular hexagons, all 6 sides equal length, all 6 boundary edges present exactly once in the rendered SVG, full correct 6-neighbour tessellation (44's missing neighbours were legitimately off-canvas, the rightmost column). Cell-level geometry was never wrong. **The real bug, found by reproducing my own testing steps**: after dragging a panel slider (e.g. Seed), that `<input type=range>` keeps keyboard focus — and Chromium routes mouse-wheel scroll to a focused range/number input *regardless of where the cursor actually is on the page*, independent of the `wheel` listener Loom's own zoom/pan already had on the canvas (which does call `preventDefault()`, but that has no effect on this separate focus-driven browser behaviour). So scrolling over the canvas to zoom in for a closer look — the exact workflow used to inspect the grid — silently also nudged whichever slider was last touched (reproduced live: Gap drifted 0.06→0.1, Seed 7→0 purely from scrolling near the canvas with Seed focused), regenerating the grid under different params and making specific cells look different from what was seen a moment before. This is a shared-module bug, not a Loom-only or Hexagonal-only one: `shared/core.js`'s `createZoomPan` (used directly by Loom and `shared/_template.html`; other tools still run their own private zoom/pan, per the July 25 system-pass notes) now blurs `document.activeElement` at the start of its `wheel` handler whenever focus sits on a control outside the zoom/pan surface itself — wheel-over-canvas now means "zoom," never "zoom and also adjust whatever slider I used last." **Verified the fix directly reproduces the exact failure and resolves it**: focused the Seed slider, scrolled over the canvas at the same coordinates as the original repro — before the fix, Gap/Seed drifted and focus stayed on the slider; after, both values held exactly (0.06/7) and focus moved to `<body>`. Zoom itself still works (tracked 65% after the same scroll), keyboard zoom shortcuts and drag-pan untouched. Only Loom is a live consumer of `createZoomPan` today, so blast radius is contained to this one tool. Zero console errors.

- **August 12, 2026 — Loom: Hexagonal, the second polygon-shaped generator.** Continued the generator roadmap after Voronoi; picked Hexagonal because it's the cheapest possible proof that Voronoi's `grid.cellShape: 'polygon'` fork actually generalises — every renderer (SVG/PNG export, the embedded-SVG live preview, `collectPolygonEdges` dedup) already branches on that one flag, so the new generator only had to emit cells shaped `{ points, centroid }`; nothing outside `hexagonal.js`/`registry.js`/the panel block changed. Real regular-hexagon tessellation (standard redblobgames axial spacing — flat-top: 1.5r horizontal/√3·r vertical with odd columns offset half a row; pointy-top swaps the axes), not a distorted approximation. `Columns` sets target density (derives hex radius from `inner.width / cols`) rather than a literal count, so it behaves consistently on any aspect ratio. `Gap` shrinks each hexagon toward its own centre pre-clip (the polygon analogue of rect-cell Padding) — **verified 0 is an exact no-op**, edge-to-edge tessellation with a single de-duplicated boundary line, no doubled-stroke artefact. `Jitter` offsets each centre by a seeded random amount, breaking the lattice into an organic hand-set-tile look — verified visually at 0 (perfect grid) vs. 0.7 (overlapping, organic). Clipping to the inner rect reuses Voronoi's own Sutherland–Hodgman technique, generalised from one half-plane per neighbour seed to 4 fixed half-planes. **Dev-environment gotcha hit again** (documented since the Genesis-migration session, still true): the running `python3 -m http.server` served a browser-cached pre-edit copy of `registry.js` even after a hard navigate with a new query string — confirmed via `fetch(url, {cache:'no-store'})` that the disk file was correct all along; verification only succeeded after spinning up a throwaway `Cache-Control: no-store` Python server on a different port. Verified end to end: both orientations render correctly, all four generators (Bento/Sinusoidal/Voronoi/Hexagonal) regression-tested together with zero console errors, real de-duplicated polygon SVG export (276 `<line>`s on a 95-cell canvas), 0 of the new block's controls unaccessibly-named. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Hexagonal's Flat-top/Pointy-top toggle replaced with a continuous Rotation slider.** Diego's own follow-up request: the two-option segmented control was never a real binary — "pointy-top" is just "flat-top" rotated 30°, and a rigid hex tiling tiles at any angle, not only those two. The naive implementation (rotate each hexagon's own shape, leave the lattice centres where they were) is wrong and was avoided rather than shipped-then-caught: a hexagon's edges only meet its neighbours' edges when its orientation matches the lattice's, so rotating the shape alone opens visible gaps/overlaps at any angle that isn't a multiple of 60°. Fixed by rotating the CENTRES and the SHAPE together — a rigid rotation of the whole pattern, which a honeycomb's own rotational symmetry guarantees still tiles everywhere. Building this needs the lattice generated in its own unrotated frame first, sized from the inner rect's own **diagonal** (not width/height — a rotated lattice needs to cover every corner of the fixed, unrotated inner rect it'll be clipped against), then rotated as a whole around the inner rect's centre, then clipped. `Rotation`'s range is exactly `[0, 60)` since that's the hexagon's true rotational period — 0 is the old "Flat-top", 30 the old "Pointy-top", anything past 60 just repeats. Verified live: 0° and 30° reproduce the two old named orientations exactly; 15° (a genuinely in-between angle) tiles the full canvas edge-to-edge with zero gaps or overlaps, confirmed both visually and via a real de-duplicated SVG export (220 `<line>`s on a 73-cell canvas); Gap 0 stays an exact no-op at every rotation tested; all four generators (Bento/Sinusoidal/Voronoi/Hexagonal) regression-clean, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Hexagonal gained per-cell "Spin", distinct from the whole-lattice "Rotation" shipped minutes earlier.** Diego's follow-up: "what if we rotate each individual hexagon?" Flagged the real consequence before building rather than after: rotating each hexagon's OWN orientation, independent of its neighbours, necessarily breaks edge-to-edge tiling (gaps/overlaps) the same way Jitter already trades off for position — this is a genuinely different control from `Rotation`, not a finer-grained version of it. "Rotate each hexagon" doesn't name one specific effect, so rather than guess, asked which flavour was meant; Diego said all three, so shipped all three as one `Spin mode` selector: **Random** (seeded, uniform per-cell offset — fractured mosaic look), **Checkerboard** (alternates by lattice row+col parity — regular woven pattern, not random), **Radial** (scales with distance from canvas centre — smooth centre-to-edge unfurling gradient). Applied to each hexagon's local vertex angles *before* the rigid whole-pattern `Rotation` step, so the two compose correctly instead of fighting. `Off` (default) is an exact no-op, verified pixel-for-pixel against the pre-Spin render. **One UI correction caught live, before shipping**: the first pass used a 4-way segmented control (matching Loom's existing `seg-ctrl` pattern for Orientation/Axis), but "Random"/"Checkerboard"/"Radial" don't fit a 4-way segmented control in a 248px panel without truncating ("Ra..", "Ch.." on screen) — switched to a dropdown, the same pattern already used for Grid type. All three modes verified visually distinct and correct; SVG export still real de-duplicated vector geometry (mostly no-longer-deduped once spun, correctly, since neighbouring edges stop aligning); 0 unaccessibly-named controls; all four generators regression-clean. **Correction to this entry, found in the very next round of work** (see the Spiral/Noise entry below): the "stale console errors, not a real bug" call made during this pass was wrong — `window.syncHexSpinRow` was genuinely never exported, so every real spin-mode switch threw; it went undetected here only because the specific verification pass that checked a fresh tab's console never actually exercised a spin-mode switch in that tab. Lesson: a clean console after switching *tabs* doesn't confirm a clean console after switching *modes* — verify the exact interaction, not a proxy for it. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: two more Spin modes (Spiral, Noise) + two real bugs caught by properly verifying this time.** Asked Diego what other per-cell Spin flavours might be worth adding rather than guessing; recommended Noise (organic, on-brand, reuses shared code) and Spiral (near-free, reuses Radial's own distance math swapped for angle) and Diego said both. **Spiral**: angle around the centre (`atan2`), not distance — a true pinwheel/vortex twist, with an honest, disclosed seam at ±π (a real spiral has a seam; smoothing it would make it a wave, not a vortex — a different mode). **Noise**: `Organica.noise.fbm` sampled at each cell's own centre — Loom's first use of `shared/noise.js`, ported rather than becoming a fourth private copy after Komorebi/Camo Turing/Warping (load order added: core → noise → kiwi → the tool's own modules, documented same as the other two). Needed its own second control, `Noise scale` (field cycles across canvas), since chaos-amount and swirl-size are genuinely independent knobs — shown only when Noise is selected. **Caught two real bugs this round, properly this time**: (1) `window.syncHexSpinRow` was never exported, so every actual Spin-mode switch threw — invisible in the *previous* round's verification because that pass checked a fresh tab's console without ever actually switching modes in it, a proxy check standing in for the real one; fixed the missing export line, then re-verified by switching through all 6 modes in a single pass with the console open throughout, catching it live before calling it done a second time. (2) A pre-existing bug, unrelated to Spin, found purely by re-testing carefully: a *fresh Loom load with zero interaction* — plain default Bento — was landing at 1% zoom, fully off-screen, every single time, because `fitToViewIfNeeded`'s first call ran before `canvas-wrap`'s own layout had settled, read a `clientWidth` small enough that the 64px-margin subtraction went negative, and that negative "available space" fed a negative scale straight into the zoom-pan module's own floor clamp. Fixed by retrying on the next animation frame instead of fitting to garbage dimensions the moment the measured space isn't yet positive — verified a fresh load now lands at a sane fit (57% in the tested viewport) automatically, no manual Reset needed. **The actual lesson from both bugs, not just the fixes**: "the console was clean" only proves something if the interaction that would trigger the bug was actually exercised in that exact check — a passing check on a *different* action, or on page-load alone, isn't evidence about the action actually being verified. Final state: 4 generators × 6 Spin modes all regression-clean, SVG export real vector geometry in every mode, 0 unaccessibly-named controls, zero console errors confirmed by actually flipping every switch. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Radial, a genuinely new structure, not another variant.** Continued the generator roadmap; recommended Radial (rings × sectors, dartboard/sunburst/mandala) over the other remaining Phase 2 options as the most structurally distinct pick — Bento/Sinusoidal are a rect track lattice, Voronoi/Hexagonal are a tessellation of one repeated cell shape, Radial is true polar coordinates, a third real category, not a fourth flavour of an existing one. Third `cellShape: 'polygon'` generator; each wedge's two curved edges are approximated as short arc segments (~1 vertex per 10°) rather than the straight edges every prior polygon generator emits — a real arc has no exact polygon form, so it's subdivided finely enough to read as smooth at any Rings×Sectors count; `collectPolygonEdges`'s existing dedup needed no changes, since two adjacent wedges still share one exact straight radial edge. Genuinely simpler than Voronoi/Hexagonal in one respect: no inner-rect clipping needed at all, since the whole field is built inside a circle already inscribed in the inner rect by construction. `Inner radius` (0–0.8) is the donut-hole control — 0 gives true converging pie slices at the centre, handled through the *same* arc-point code as every other ring (a near-zero-radius arc just lands every point at the centre) rather than a special case. `Gap` insets each wedge on both its radial and angular sides; `Start angle` rotates the whole field. Verified live, properly this time (every mode/param switch exercised in the same tab the console was watched in, the discipline learned the hard way in the previous Spin round): Inner radius 0 converges cleanly with no glitch, Gap 0 is an exact edge-to-edge no-op (single de-duplicated lines throughout — concentric circles + spokes), both extremes tested without error (1 ring×3 sectors and 12 rings×36 sectors=432 cells), SVG export real vector geometry (213 `<line>`s on a 48-cell canvas), 0 unaccessibly-named controls, all five generators regression-clean. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Triangular, the fourth polygon generator, plus a design mistake caught before shipping.** Continued the roadmap with the equilateral-triangle tessellation (alternating up/down triangles via the standard row-height construction, not a rectangle split diagonally — the cheap version only tiles at 90°/45° and reads as a cut rectangle, not a real triangular mesh). **Wrote the header comment first, as usual, then caught it was wrong before writing the code to match**: the draft claimed "no inner-rect clipping needed, same as Radial" by analogy — but Radial's field is bounded by construction (built inside a circle inscribed in the inner rect), while a plain triangle row/column loop has no such built-in boundary and would visibly overflow the canvas edges with nothing to trim it. Caught by re-reading my own reasoning rather than by a failed test, corrected before ever running the code: added the same Sutherland–Hodgman `clipToRect` Hexagonal already uses (each polygon generator keeps its own private copy). `Columns` sets density (triangle side length), `Gap` shrinks each triangle toward its own centroid, `Jitter` offsets each triangle independently (same accepted "breaks the exact shared edge" trade-off as Hexagonal's own Jitter, not the more involved shared-lattice-vertex jitter a fully "correct" irregular mesh would use — flagged as a future refinement, not built this round). Verified live: Gap 0 is an exact edge-to-edge no-op with correct clipping on all four sides (no overflow, confirmed visually after the fix), Jitter 0.5 gives a genuinely distinct scattered texture, SVG export real vector geometry (576 `<line>`s on a 180-cell canvas), 0 unaccessibly-named controls, all six generators regression-clean with the console watched through every actual mode switch. **Same round, before any commit — Diego asked for Hexagonal's Rotation, then Spin, ported onto Triangular too**: `Rotation` (0–60°) is the identical rigid whole-lattice rotation (the triangular lattice is the hexagonal lattice's own dual, same 6-fold symmetry, so the same "rotate centres and shapes together, not the shape alone" construction applies unchanged); `Spin mode` is the identical five modes (Random/Checkerboard/Radial/Spiral/Noise), rotating each triangle's own local orientation around its jittered centroid before the rigid rotation. **Remembered the exact bug from the previous Hexagonal-Spin round and avoided repeating it**: exported `syncTriSpinRow` on `window` in the same edit that defined it, rather than only after the console caught the omission a second time. Verified live: Rotation 0 reproduces the pre-Rotation render exactly, Rotation 30 still tiles perfectly at a visibly different angle, all six Spin modes show correct row visibility and render distinctly, SVG export real vector geometry (544 `<line>`s on a 172-cell Noise-spun canvas), 0 unaccessibly-named controls, all six generators regression-clean with the console watched live through every actual switch in the same tab. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Diamond, and a real geometry bug caught before ever running the code.** Fifth polygon generator: squares on a plain uniform lattice, rigidly rotated as a whole field — Rotation 0 reads as a plain axis-aligned square grid, Rotation 45 reads as the classic diamond/argyle pattern, one continuous `[0,90)` control (a square's point-group order is 4, not the 6 every other polygon generator here uses) morphing between what would otherwise be two separate named generators; ships with Rotation defaulting to 45 so it reads as "Diamond" out of the box. **The first draft was wrong, and caught by hand-deriving the geometry rather than by running it**: it built diamond-oriented squares directly on an offset row/column lattice, guessing spacing by analogy to Hexagonal's own offset-row construction — checking where same-row diamonds' vertices actually landed showed they only touched at a single VERTEX, not a shared EDGE, meaning real gaps between rows, not a valid tiling. Rebuilt from a different, safer starting point: a plain (non-offset, trivially edge-to-edge) square grid, each square's own vertices placed at its corners so Rotation 0 gives axis-aligned squares, then the exact same rigid whole-field rotation mechanism already proven correct by Hexagonal and Triangular — a rigid rotation of a tiling that already tiles is still a tiling at any angle, with zero new spacing math to get wrong. Spin mode/amount/Noise scale ported identically from Hexagonal/Triangular. **Remembered to export the new sync function on `window` in the same edit that defined it** (third generator in a row to get this right, after catching the omission live on Hexagonal's own Spin). Verified live: Gap 0 is a true edge-to-edge no-op (the exact property the first draft got wrong, confirmed fixed by continuous zig-zag lines with no gaps); Rotation 0 renders a correctly-counted plain square grid (81 cells, 9×9); Rotation 45 renders diamonds; an in-between angle (20°) tiles perfectly; Noise Spin shows the same organic wobble as the other generators; SVG export real vector geometry (340 `<line>`s on an 85-cell canvas); 0 unaccessibly-named controls; all seven generators regression-clean, console watched live through every actual switch. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Seed had zero effect on Spin's Noise mode across all three generators, fixed.** Diego asked for a targeted check of Diamond's Seed control specifically rather than a general review — the right instinct, since it surfaced a real gap a broad pass might have glossed over. Tested every Spin-mode combination by diffing the exported JSON model (not eyeballing): Seed correctly matters for Random and for Jitter, correctly does nothing for Checkerboard/Radial/Spiral (genuinely deterministic by design, not seeded randomness at all), but ALSO did nothing for Noise — a real dead-control case, unlike the other three. Root cause: `Organica.noise.fbm` (`shared/noise.js`) has no seed parameter of its own; it's a pure hash of `(x,y)`. Fixed by sampling `fbm` at a per-generation coordinate offset drawn from a SECOND, independent `mulberry32` stream — seeded from `seed ^ 0x9E3779B9`, deliberately never sharing draws with the main `rng` that feeds Jitter and Random spin, so the fix changes only Noise's own output and leaves Random/Jitter identical at any given seed. Same bug, same fix, applied to all three generators that share the copied `spinFor` (Hexagonal, Triangular, Diamond) — confirmed it wasn't Diamond-specific before starting, then fixed all three in one pass rather than three separate rounds. Verified by re-running the exact diffing test that found the bug: Noise now genuinely differs across Seed values on all three generators, Random's own seed-dependence confirmed unaffected, all seven generators regression-clean, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 12, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Circular, and a design mistake caught by Diego rather than by testing.** First draft clipped a plain rect lattice inside a circular boundary — a grid of squares that happens to sit inside a circle. Diego caught it immediately: the base CELL shape has to actually be a circle, matching every sibling generator's own naming convention (Hexagonal's cells are hexagons, Triangular's are triangles, Diamond's are squares). Rebuilt as a genuine circle-packing grid: centres on the identical triangular/hex-packing lattice as Hexagonal's own offset-row construction (a hexagonal tiling's centre lattice and a circle-packing's centre lattice are the same lattice, just different spacing constants — circles pack point-tangent, not edge-to-edge: same-row spacing = full diameter, row spacing = `d·√3⁄2`, odd rows offset `d/2`). Disclosed honestly, same as Sinusoidal's own wavy-boundary note: circles can't truly tessellate, so at Gap 0 they touch at a single point per neighbour and real triangular voids remain between any three mutually-touching circles — correct circle-packing geometry, not a rendering bug, and why the edge dedup finds nothing to merge here (no circle shares an actual edge with another, unlike every rect/hex/triangle/diamond pair). Inner-rect clipping reused the same `clipToRect` every polygon generator already carries. Verified live: Gap 0 shows circles genuinely touching (visible triangular voids, no overlap, no gap); Gap > 0 opens clean space between them; SVG export real vector geometry (3579 `<line>`s on a 95-cell canvas — a high count is correct here, not a regression, since there's nothing to dedup); 0 unaccessibly-named controls; all eight generators regression-clean, console watched live through every switch. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 14, 2026 — Loom: Circular gained Rotation + Jitter/Seed, per Diego's own follow-up ask.** Asked what other parameters would suit Circular before building anything (exploratory question, 2-3 sentence answer per house style); recommended Rotation and Jitter+Seed as the low-risk reuses of proven infrastructure, and a per-circle radius variation ("pebble" look, Komorebi-Cluster-style) as a bigger, deferred idea. Diego confirmed the first two. `Rotation` (0–60°, same period as Hexagonal) rigidly rotates the whole lattice of centres, built the identical way as every other polygon generator this session (unrotated frame sized from the inner rect's diagonal, rotated as a whole, then clipped) — the one thing worth stating plainly: since a circle's own outline is rotationally symmetric, Rotation never changes an individual cell's shape, only how rows/columns align and therefore how the boundary clips (confirmed visibly different diagonal edge-cuts at 30°, circles themselves unchanged). `Jitter`/`Seed` offset each circle's centre before clipping, same accepted trade-off as every sibling's own Jitter — verified a genuinely different scattered "pebble/bubble" look at Jitter 0.4 (circles overlapping/unevenly spaced) versus a perfect packing at 0. Verified live: Rotation 0 reproduces the pre-change render exactly, SVG export stays real vector geometry throughout, all eight generators regression-clean, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 14, 2026 (later same day) — Loom: every SVG/PNG/HTML export replaced with real closed, fillable per-cell shapes.** Diego reported opening an exported SVG and finding each cell was actually a pile of independent `<line>` segments that only *looked* like one closed shape on screen — no path a design tool could select and fill with a colour, across every generator. Root cause traced to a deliberate original design choice: `svg-renderer.js`/`raster-renderer.js`/`html-renderer.js`'s `buildPolygonSVG` all drew a de-duplicated edge list (`json-model.js`'s `collectEdges`/`collectPolygonEdges`) instead of one shape per cell, specifically to avoid two adjacent cells each stroking their own copy of a shared boundary (a real, documented cosmetic issue from earlier in the project — "two lines look like one, but bolder"). That trade-off was wrong once weighed against the actual requirement: closed, fillable geometry matters more than avoiding a faint doubled stroke on shared edges. Fixed by switching all three renderers to draw one real closed shape per cell — `<rect>` for rect-lattice grids (Bento/Sinusoidal), `<polygon>` for every polygon-cellShape grid (Voronoi/Hexagonal/Radial/Triangular/Diamond/Circular) — reading `resolveCellRects`/`cell.points` directly instead of the edge-dedup functions, which were then deleted from `json-model.js` as dead code (nothing called them any more). Updated every stale doc/comment reference to the removed dedup mechanism across `docs/LOOM.md` and the generator files' own headers, rather than leaving them describing code that no longer exists. **Verified thoroughly, not just visually**: exported SVG for all 8 generators — zero `<line>` elements in any of them, real `<rect>`/`<polygon>` counts matching each generator's own cell count exactly; parsed an exported Hexagonal SVG, applied real fill colours to each polygon programmatically, rendered it standalone in the browser and confirmed the fills render as clean, correctly closed regions (screenshot-verified, not assumed); PNG export still produces valid non-empty files for every generator; HTML export's embedded polygon SVG also fixed and verified (93 real closed polygons on Circular, zero lines); live preview (both the real CSS Grid path for rect cells and the embedded-SVG path for polygon cells) unaffected and correct; 0 unaccessibly-named controls; zero console errors throughout. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §6.

- **August 14, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: three canvas verification toggles + bigger numbers.** Diego's own follow-up, straight after the closed-shape export fix: (1) a Background grid overlay, density derived from the canvas's own width rather than a fixed pixel count; (2) a Preview fill toggle, tinting every cell so a real closed/fillable shape is obvious at a glance — the direct visual counterpart to the export fix that made those shapes actually fillable in the first place; (3) bigger numbers (9→13px on both preview paths) plus a toggle to hide them. All three are pure `#canvas-frame` CSS state (`syncCanvasOverlays()`), never touch the model, and were verified never to leak into any export (checked directly: an SVG exported with all three toggles on still comes out `fill="none"` with zero grid-overlay artefacts, since the export functions read the model, never the DOM). **One real bug caught immediately by looking at the render, not by reasoning about the CSS**: the background grid was invisible on first try — every renderer's own opaque white background rect (there so a standalone exported file has one) fully covered a reticle placed behind it; fixed by drawing the overlay *above* the grid content instead, the same way a guide layer sits above content in a real design tool. Preview fill needed no renderer changes at all — a single CSS rule overriding the polygon/rect `fill="none"` presentation attribute (CSS beats SVG presentation attributes) plus the equivalent `background-color` for the CSS-Grid rect-cell path. Verified live across all 8 generators: background grid renders correctly on both the SVG-preview and CSS-Grid-preview paths, preview fill visibly fills every cell with no gaps (confirming the earlier closed-shape fix), numbers hide/show correctly, toggle state persists across generator switches, 0 unaccessibly-named controls, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §3.

- **August 14, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: cross-tool grid import, the first real step of the "define once in Loom, reuse everywhere" workflow.** Diego described the target workflow (save a grid in Loom, import it into any other Organica tool — or Figma/Affinity — with different behaviours once there, e.g. slicing an image into the cells vs. giving each cell its own independent structure) and asked how to test/verify/build it. Recommended building the shared import path first (needed either way) then proving the simpler of the two behaviours — Slice & Reposition, tool-agnostic pure compositing — before touching any host tool's own generative core; deferred Mix & Restructure (deeper per-tool integration, already proven separately by the Bento×Camo-Turing reaction-diffusion concept test) to its own pass. Shipped: **`Organica.loadLoomGrid(json)`** in `shared/core.js` — reads Loom's own JSON export and resolves it into absolute per-cell geometry (rects or polygons) any other Organica tool can consume with zero knowledge of tracks/spans/Kiwi, ported (not imported — every other tool is a plain script, no bundler) rather than pulling Loom's own ES module in. **Save grid** in Loom itself — named presets via the same shared `Organica.presetStore` every other tool's own presets already use, with a REAL round-trip load (`applyGridParamsToUI`, the mirror image of `readGridParams`'s own explicit per-generator branches), not just a re-display. **Verified live**: saved a Hexagonal grid (Rotation 30°, Spin Noise) mid-edit, switched to a totally different generator (Bento), loaded the save back — every control re-synced exactly, render matched pixel-for-pixel; `loadLoomGrid` round-tripped real exports of both a rect grid (Bento) and a polygon grid (Hexagonal, 67 cells) correctly, and threw a clean user-safe error on invalid input rather than crashing. Built and verified (not shipped into any tool yet) a standalone concept-test page, `loom/_test-slice-reposition.html`: exported a real grid from Loom, loaded it via `loadLoomGrid` in a separate page, sliced a synthetic test image into the grid's own cells and re-rendered it both as a faithful slice (image stays continuous across cell boundaries — confirms the geometry is exact) and shuffled (a genuine "puzzle remix," confirmed visually with a numbered test image) — proven on both rect and polygon cellShapes. All 8 Loom generators regression-clean throughout, 0 unaccessibly-named controls, zero real console errors (one unrelated stray 404 to Strata's own backend port, confirmed not from either test page). Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §3.

- **August 14, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: Mix & Restructure concept-proven, the second of the two brief-named cross-tool behaviours.** Followed the plan from the loader/Slice-&-Reposition round: prototype the deeper per-tool integration on ONE host tool (Spore, the simplest canvas-based one) before touching any product code. Researched Spore's own real algorithm first rather than guessing: `doRender()` (`spore/index.html`) is a hex-jittered lattice (rows at 0.866× spacing, alternate rows offset half a step — not blue-noise/Poisson-disc), mark size linearly mapped from local darkness (40–100% of a base size), points above `luma 0.92` skipped, marks drawn as full replayed Genesis SVG symbols. Ported the lattice/sizing math faithfully into a standalone test page, simplified marks to plain filled circles (disclosed — full Genesis-symbol replay needs `forms.js`'s mark data, more than a concept test needs). Each grid cell gets its OWN independent stippling pass over its own source-image sub-region, with its own spacing/size/rotation (a small fixed preset cycled by cell index — reproducible, not random-every-render) — the genuine "different structure per container" the whole thread of work set out to test, not Slice & Reposition's shared-crop version. **Verified live on both a 3×3 Bento grid and a 45-cell Hexagonal grid**: "Vary structure" shows clearly distinct textures per cell while each cell still faithfully tracks the source image's own luminance (the dark vignette/ellipse test shapes read correctly through every different stipple density); "uniform structure" (unchecked) reads as one continuous pattern only interrupted by the clip shape — the correct contrast baseline, confirming the varied version's difference is real structure, not a rendering artefact. Both prototypes (`loom/_test-slice-reposition.html`, `loom/_test-mix-restructure.html`) share one architecture: `Organica.loadLoomGrid` for the grid, an offscreen canvas per cell for the source-image sub-region, `ctx.clip()` to the cell's own rect/polygon shape. Zero console errors throughout. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §3.

- **August 14, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom: the three concept-test pages committed for reuse, plus a ninth generator, Noise.** Diego asked to document and keep the three prototypes (`_test-camo-cells.html`, `_test-slice-reposition.html`, `_test-mix-restructure.html`) since they'll seed later feature work, rather than leave them as throwaway uncommitted files — committed as-is, `docs/LOOM.md` §3 updated to describe each rather than just point at it. Then continued the generator roadmap: **Noise**, Sinusoidal's own direct sibling — identical `solveTracksParametric` architecture (Columns×Rows lattice, rhythm in track SIZE not topology), a 1D slice of `Organica.noise.fbm` in place of a sine wave. Not a new mechanism: `constraint-engine.js`'s own header already named "sine, noise, radial, polar…" as the intended extension points back when Sinusoidal shipped. Two DECORRELATED noise curves for Columns vs Rows from one seed (fixed, different y-offsets sampled from the shared 2D field — the same trick Warping's own domain-warp taps use), verified genuinely different (not the same curve twice) by diffing the exported track arrays, not just eyeballing the render. Verified: Amount 0 is an exact uniform no-op; Amount 0.6 gives a visibly irregular, non-periodic rhythm distinct from any Sinusoidal wave; Seed changes the pattern (confirmed via diff); Save-grid round-trip works (including the segmented Axis control restoring correctly); SVG export real closed rects, zero stray lines; the "Colu.." truncation on the Axis control is a pre-existing cosmetic quirk shared with Sinusoidal's own identical control (confirmed by direct comparison before assuming it was a new bug); all nine generators regression-clean, 0 unaccessibly-named controls, zero console errors. Manual: `docs/LOOM.md` §4.

- **August 14, 2026 (still later same day) — Loom Phase 2/3/4 completed in sequence (18 new/combined generators + Phase 4 editing), plus a real cross-tool bug found and deliberately parked.** Continued the roadmap straight through: **Phase 2** (8 elementary generators — Column, Row, Modular, Rectangular, Diagonal, Angular, Polar, Elliptical), then on request Column+Row were combined into one **Linear** generator (Axis: Columns/Rows/Both, plus Rotation/Jitter/Seed matching Hexagonal/Triangular/Diamond) after Diego flagged via a screenshot annotation that rotated Linear strips left two canvas corners uncovered — root cause was a fixed-count strip span that only covers the canvas at Rotation 0 (where the division axis IS the canvas axis); fixed with the same oversized-lattice-then-clip technique Diamond/Hexagonal already use, verified corners now covered at any angle with Rotation-0 cell counts unchanged. **Phase 3** (Masonry/Fractal/Recursive/Organic/Spiral — five real new generators; Swiss/Golden Ratio/Rule of Thirds/Timeline/Isometric turned out to be pure parameter presets on generators that already existed, shipped as read-only `BUILTIN_GRID_PRESETS` instead of duplicated code). **Phase 4** (per-side margins with a Link toggle; real Kiwi edit-constraint use via a new `solveTracksKiwiWithEdit`; WYSIWYG drag-to-resize scoped to the 5 `cellShape:'rect'` generators). Two real bugs caught by testing rather than assumed away: the drag-handle visibility check was backwards (`cellShape !== 'rect'`, when no rect generator ever sets that flag explicitly — inverted from every other cellShape check in the codebase), and Save grid silently discarded a drag on Load (the generic rebuild regenerates fresh equal tracks from params, the same reset Shuffle Numbers already relies on) — fixed by restoring the saved model's own tracks after rebuild. Loom now has **21 generators**, every item from the original Grid Definition brief is live. Full detail: `docs/LOOM.md` §4/§7a.
  **Separately, before starting what would have been Phase 5** (native Figma Auto Layout frames): discovered `Organica.sendToFigma()` — the shared function behind the "→ Figma" button on every Organica tool except Strata (Loom, Komorebi, Camo Turing, Warping, …) — posts a `postMessage` of type `'organica-svg'` that `figma-plugin/code.js` **never listens for** (its handler only checks `'reload'`/`'import-svg'`/`'import-regions'`, confirmed via grep — zero matches for `'organica-svg'` anywhere in the plugin). Strata's own Figma integration works today only because it uses a completely different mechanism (the plugin UI polls a local Python backend on port 5050), not `sendToFigma()` at all. Net effect: **"→ Figma" has been silently doing nothing on every tool but Strata**, not just something newly broken by this session's work. Given this changes the shape of any Figma-frame-generation work (Phase 5 can't produce anything if the channel itself is dead), asked Diego how to scope the fix rather than deciding unilaterally; his call was to flag it and stop, not fix it this session — recorded here and as a background task suggestion (`task_8f56904e`) rather than touched.

- **August 15, 2026 — Shipped Soul (`/soul/`), the animation engine, from a scoping conversation through a working first vertical slice.** Diego's own framing: Genesis is animated but the animation is hand-typed CSS per form; the real need is a general engine — input (SVG/text/image) → decomposed into primitives → placed on a Canvas+Grid (already Loom's job) → brought to life by a motion engine (the new part). Scoped across several rounds before writing code: evaluated a general multi-grid layer system for Loom first (declined as a Phase-7-sized rebuild, not needed — the actual ask was two grids compared visually, shipped instead as a much smaller **Overlay grid** feature, reusing the existing preset store, `cellsMarkup`/`drawCells` extracted from the SVG/raster renderers so a second grid's shapes bake into the same export without a second background wiping out the first); evaluated p5.js (declined a second time, same reasoning as Camouflage's own August 3 call — duplicates `noise.js`, wants to own its own canvas — kept as algorithm-reference-only) and Three.js (accepted for future large-particle-count rendering, since it's already vendored for Camo Turing — zero new cost, and GPU is justified there the same way it's justified for Camo Turing's own Gray-Scott, a genuinely parallel workload) against GSAP (accepted — free "no charge" license confirmed live from gsap.com, not assumed; timeline/stagger/easing/morph/draw all map directly onto documented needs). Diego's own direct request mid-scoping — compare p5's noise() against `noise.js`'s own value-noise — led to a real finding (p5's `noise()` isn't gradient/Perlin noise despite the name, same value-noise family as ours; the genuine gaps were p5's native 3rd time-axis and non-repeating hash) and a concrete deliverable: **Simplex noise** (`simplex2`/`simplex3`/`simplexFbm2`/`simplexFbm3`, Ken Perlin's real 2001 algorithm) added to `noise.js`, additive only, verified over 200k samples (range ≈[-1,1], deterministic, time axis genuinely continuous — max delta 0.004 at a 0.001-step in t). **Named "Soul"** (Diego's own choice, offered alongside "Anima"/"Moto"/"Palpito") — hub link + `/soul/` route added, rose/pulse identity colour. **Shipped**: `shared/motion.js` — `parsePrimitives(svgString)`, one parser for every tool's SVG export (no per-tool adapter, since every tool already vectorises — Halide/Komorebi/Camo Turing/Warping's own raster output already goes through the shared contour tracer first); the 6 Genesis physics patterns rebuilt parametric on GSAP (`PATTERNS.pressure/gravity/growth/environmental/rotation` — "Collective behaviour" isn't a 7th pattern, it's any of the 5 plus a `staggerDelay` formula, exactly Genesis's own documented relationship); GSAP + MorphSVG + DrawSVG vendored (`shared/gsap*.min.js`). **Two real bugs caught by testing against actual Loom exports, not synthetic cases**: `getBBox()` returns an all-zero bbox for a detached SVG host in Chromium (fixed by attaching it off-screen, not `display:none`, which also breaks measurement); SVG fill/stroke inheritance from an ancestor `<g>` (Loom's own convention: style set once on the wrapping group, not per-shape) wasn't propagated, so every Loom export rendered in Soul's own fallback colour instead of the real one — fixed by threading an `inherited` style context through the parse recursion, verified before/after on a real Hexagonal export. Soul's own stage moved from Canvas2D to real SVG DOM once animation entered the picture — GSAP/DrawSVG/MorphSVG need actual elements, not canvas draw calls; `buildElement()` reconstructs each primitive's real tag from its own preserved `sourceTag`/`sourceAttrs`, the same technique Pollen's own `drawSvgEl()` already uses for canvas replay. **Verified thoroughly, not just "no console error"**: all 5 patterns confirmed via live `gsap.getProperty()` reads showing real values changing over time (not just visual glancing); stagger confirmed two ways (different elements at different phases in one snapshot; a longer-delayed element still at rest while an earlier one had started); scale-tested on a real 32-cell Hexagonal Loom export — `pressure`+distance-stagger produces a genuinely organic phase-shifted "breathing" field, `growth`+index-stagger produces a real staggered self-drawing effect across the grid (both screenshot-confirmed); counts cross-checked against Loom's own regression test exactly (11/68 primitives matching Bento/Hexagonal's own known cell counts); 0 of 5 new Motion controls unaccessibly-named; zero console errors across every load/pattern/play/stop tested. **Not built yet** (see `docs/SOUL.md` §7): export pipeline (playback only today), MorphSVG actually invoked by a feature (vendored/registered, unused), large-particle-count rendering (the Three.js path), a real layer/group concept (`rotation`'s per-element parity split is a stand-in), loading a Loom grid directly via `loadLoomGrid` instead of through an SVG round-trip. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md`.

- **August 15, 2026 (later same day) — Soul: direct feedback on the first vertical slice, two timing fixes + two genuinely new patterns.** Diego's own critique after using it: "ci vuole sempre un po' ad iniziare l'immagine" (a real delay before motion becomes visible) and "le animazioni sono davvero basiche" (the animations feel basic). Neither was tuned by eye — both measured before deciding what to change. **Timing**: `growth`'s `power1.inOut` ease measured genuinely flat at the start (via the live `strokeDasharray` segment length) — only 1.85% of the path drawn 300ms into a 3s duration; switched to `power1.out`, verified 18.4% at the same point after. `gravity`'s own slow start (`power1.in`) is physically correct — a falling object starts at rest — but spanned 40% of total duration, so 1.2s of near-invisible motion at the default 3s; recalibrated the keyframe percentages (0/40/55/70/100 → 0/18/32/50/100) to keep the exact same physical shape while reaching each stage sooner (0.19px → 1.1px moved at 300ms, ~5.8× improvement, measured both ways). **"Basic" led to two new pattern types**, both built from infrastructure that already existed but had never been used by a real feature: **Organic wobble** — continuous, non-repeating motion, each primitive sampling `simplex3(x,y,t)`'s own genuinely continuous time axis (built earlier the same day, verified but unused until now) at its own `(cx,cy)` every `gsap.ticker` frame, so neighbours drift correlated but never identical; implemented as a `{kill(),delay()}` object driven by `gsap.ticker.add()` rather than a real GSAP tween, since there's no fixed duration to hand back — a second legitimate strategy behind `animate()`'s own same two-method contract. **Morph** — MorphSVGPlugin (vendored since Soul's first commit, registered, never invoked), morphing a circle into a noise-perturbed organic blob built from its own radius/seed and back; the first pattern that changes a primitive's own SHAPE, not just position/scale. **A real bug caught by testing immediately, not assumed from GSAP's own docs**: a bare `morphSVG` tween on a `<circle>` does nothing at all — no error, `d` never appears, `r` unchanged — MorphSVGPlugin does NOT auto-convert a non-`<path>` target; isolated with a 2-line repro (identical tween on a real `<path>` worked immediately) before touching the real code. Fixed with `MorphSVGPlugin.convertToPath(el, true)`, which converts AND swaps the new `<path>` into the live DOM — the primitive's own `.el` reference updated to match, since a second bug would otherwise follow (anything reading the primitive afterward would target a detached node). A related gap: `d` is an SVG attribute GSAP's `clearProps` can't restore, so Stop left morphed shapes frozen mid-blob — fixed by stashing the pre-morph circle-as-path `d` on the primitive the moment `convertToPath` produces it, restored explicitly in `stopMotion()`. **The "test every option, not a sample" lesson from earlier the same day held**: writing `morph`'s own blob-path generator, the exact same bare-`noise.` mistake (vs. `Organica.noise.`) got typed again — caught by re-reading the new code against the lesson already recorded, before ever running it. Verified: all 7 patterns × 4 stagger formulas (28 combinations) run without error; wobble confirmed via `gsap.getProperty` showing 3 elements at the same timestamp genuinely NOT identical (each samples its own noise position) and continuing to change over 3 consecutive samples; morph confirmed via the real `d` attribute genuinely distorting mid-animation and reverting byte-exact to the captured rest value after Stop; 15 wobble play/stop cycles leave zero leaked `gsap.ticker` listeners. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md`.

- **August 15, 2026 (still later same day) — Soul: real-world stress test against a Pollen "Hatch Flow" export, a real performance bug found and fixed, and a new scanline-relief prototype.** Diego pointed at a reference (generative-gestaltung.de's `P_4_3_1_01`, p5.js — studied for the technique only, no code imported, same "reference not runtime" rule already applied to p5 during scoping). Checked it against real Pollen code before assuming a gap: modes 1 (line angle from brightness) and 3 (variable-size dots) are already covered by Pollen's own Line marks + `Angle Range`, and Pollen's **Flow** mode (align to image isophotes) is actually more sophisticated than the reference's flat brightness ramp. Verified end to end with a real photo: Pollen's built-in **"Hatch Flow"** preset → real SVG export (5748 line-mark paths) → loaded into Soul. **Found a real, serious bug at this scale**: `wobble` measured **1fps** on 5748 primitives — isolated by testing a real GSAP tween (`pressure`) on the identical element count at a smooth 51fps, ruling out "too many SVG elements" and pointing at wobble's own architecture (5748 independent `gsap.ticker.add()` callbacks). Fixed by batching into ONE ticker loop writing `el.style.transform` directly. Re-profiling after the fix found the remaining cost isn't JS at all (17,244 `simplex3` calls = 2ms, 5748 style writes = 10ms, both isolated) — it's the browser's own SVG paint/composite cost; binary-searched (after a first sequential sweep gave nonsense numbers across every scale including n=100, caught as a harness bug — not a real per-scale finding — by re-testing n=100 alone and getting a normal 62fps) to a real, sharp cliff: **smooth to ~3000 elements, 1-3fps by ~4000**. Added a UI warning in Soul (Wobble, >3000 primitives) rather than letting the tab silently freeze — matches the Three.js/WebGL follow-up already flagged during scoping. **An open finding, disclosed rather than chased to ground or hidden**: `growth` (DrawSVG) showed inconsistent behaviour on a new scanline-relief prototype's own many-segment polyline paths — `strokeDasharray` stuck at zero for a disproportionate fraction of the tween in repeated isolated tests (reproduced in a fresh tab, ruling out session degradation), independent of segment count. `wobble` on the identical content works correctly and looks genuinely good. Not root-caused tonight — flagged for a focused pass rather than either claimed-working or silently dropped. **New prototype**: `/_test-scanline-relief.html` (Loom's own "test before deciding where it lives" discipline) — the reference's mode 5 (brightness as vertical displacement along horizontal scanlines, a topographic/relief look), no equivalent anywhere in Organica today, real luminance sampling and real SVG path export (125 verified real `<path>` elements). Where it belongs architecturally is still open (closest kin: Halide, or a small new tool — not Soul itself, which animates primitives rather than decomposing raster images). Manual: `docs/SOUL.md` §6a.

- **August 15, 2026 (still later same day) — Soul: major UI overhaul (Seeds panel, floatbar consolidation, zoom/pan, video export) then a design-system audit that found four real cascade/token bugs.** Diego drove a rapid sequence of UI corrections: Source's own bespoke paste-textarea block replaced by a real centralised **Seeds panel** matching Camo Turing's own tabbed component exactly (Genesis/SVG/Text — Image deliberately excluded per instruction, corrected after an initial wrong interpretation of "seeds centralizzato" that had instead built a floatbar Open button); Primitives panel removed (Motion kept); Play/Stop, Loop (to a configurable duration), and Export (PNG/SVG/**Video**) all consolidated into the shared `org-floatbar` bottom-centre component (Halide/Komorebi/Loom/Camo Turing's own convention), replacing separate header controls entirely; the header's own "N primitives loaded" status text moved into the same floatbar pill (`Organica.status()` works anywhere via class selector, not literal `<header>` placement, so this needed zero JS changes); mouse-wheel zoom + drag-pan added via the shared `Organica.createZoomPan`, same wiring as Loom's own. Verified thoroughly: all 7 patterns × 4 stagger formulas (28 combinations) re-checked clean after the restructuring, video export produces a genuinely decodable file (loaded into a real `<video>`, correct duration/dimensions), 0 unaccessibly-named controls, zero console errors across every tab/source/pattern/zoom/export combination tested. **Then, per Diego's own explicit follow-up ask ("verifica che sia tutto centralizzato e che riusi i componenti giusti") — a direct design-system audit, not a visual scan**: diffed Soul's local `<style>` block's selector list against every shared file it links, and grepped for hardcoded values that should be tokens. Found **four real defects, same root mistake four times**: Soul's own local `<style>` loads AFTER the shared `<link>`s, so any selector it redefined silently WON the cascade even though the correct shared rule was already linked. **(1)** `.panel-select`/`.ctrl-row`/`input[type=range]` local copies blocked the shared filled-track slider system entirely — fixed by deleting them and wiring `Organica.enhanceSliders(document)`. **(2)** `.mini-btn` local copy reintroduced 9px uppercase tracked text on the "Apply text" button, the exact style the July 25 panel-component pass moved every tool away from — deleted. **(3)** `#panel` local copy redeclared width at a stale 260px against the shared unified `--panel-w` (248px) — deleted, panel now visibly narrower and correct. **(4)** `--mid` shipped as `#726a5e`, the pre-July-26-fix value that failed AA at 3.17:1 — every sibling tool already carries the unified `#696256` (5.4:1); Soul, built after that fix, should have inherited it and hadn't. Also checked and explicitly left alone: `#stage-frame`'s white background (the canvas/artwork surface, the same content-colour exception already carved out for Halide's ink/paper — not chrome) and a couple of raw-px values (`#zoom-hud` position, `#drop-hint` gap/icon size) that were copied verbatim from Loom's/`_template.html`'s own identical components, not local drift. One value-identical tokenisation made in passing (`#canvas-wrap` padding 24px → `var(--space-7)`, exact match, no visual change). Four separate commits, each independently revertable; each verified live (screenshot + zero console errors + functional replay) before moving to the next. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md` §6b (UI overhaul), §6c (audit).

- **August 15, 2026 (still later same day) — Soul: floatbar icon parity + real Play/Pause toggle.** Two follow-ups on the design-system audit, both prompted by Diego catching things a first pass had wrongly called clean. First: "non è vero le icone sono diverse" — I'd claimed the floatbar icons matched Camo Turing's without ever diffing the actual SVG path data; two genuinely didn't (Play was a solid filled triangle with an arc-based path vs. Camo Turing's stroked-outline straight-line triangle; Loop's reused-Reset-arrow icon had its arrowhead coordinates altered, not byte-identical) — both fixed to match exactly. Second: "manca il controllo su pause e l'icona" — Soul only had Play/Stop, no Pause; Camo Turing's floatbar is a real Play/Pause toggle (freeze in place, resume) plus a separate Reset, and Soul's Stop only covered the reset half. Replaced the two buttons with one `#btn-playpause` toggle, Camo Turing's own id/icon-swap/aria-label technique verbatim. GSAP tweens (every pattern but wobble) already had `.pause()`/`.resume()` built in; wobble's own batched ticker handle didn't, so `pause()`/`resume()` were added to `animateWobbleBatch` in `motion.js`. **First version had a real bug, caught by a controlled test, not assumed correct from the icon/status changing**: it shifted `startTime` using `gsap.ticker.time` deltas, but `ticker.time` only advances when the ticker actually ticks — with wobble as the ticker's sole listener, pausing it can let GSAP's ticker go idle for the whole pause, so `ticker.time` reads the same stale value at pause and resume, computing a zero shift. A same-script busy-wait test proved it: continuous play across a 300ms real gap moved the shape ~26px on the next tick (expected); the paused case produced the same size jump instead of a small one. Fixed by measuring the pause duration with `performance.now()` instead, independent of whether the ticker itself is running — re-verified with the identical test: 0px jump on resume. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md` §6c.

- **August 15, 2026 (still later same day) — Soul gains an 8th pattern, Flow field, imported from the standalone `explorations/flow-field/` p5.js tool built earlier the same day.** Two real architecture questions settled with Diego before writing code, not assumed: how a free-particle flow field maps onto Soul's "pattern applied to existing primitives" model (decided: each loaded primitive drifts through the field from its own position — the field is the only piece ported unchanged, the particle concept doesn't carry over, a primitive IS what moves), and whether to keep p5.js or rewrite to vanilla JS/Canvas2D per CLAUDE.md's own production rule (Diego's call: keep p5.js — loaded in **instance mode**, `new p5((sk)=>{sk.setup=()=>{sk.noCanvas();...}})`, so none of p5's own globals touch Soul's script scope and no visible `<canvas>` is ever created, verified live). `animateFlowBatch()` follows `wobble`'s own batched-`gsap.ticker` architecture in `motion.js`, but stays Soul-local rather than joining the shared pattern registry, since it's the one pattern that needs p5.js — no other Organica tool loads it. Own params (Cell size/Noise scale/Angle mult/Speed) replace Amount/Duration, which hide for this pattern rather than sitting there inert. **A real bug from the exploration was already fixed at its source before this port**: an earlier version wrap-teleported particles to the exact opposite canvas edge, which could trap one oscillating forever at a noise-field seam discontinuity (39 of 40 stuck in one test) — fixed there by respawning instead of teleporting; Soul's own version resets a primitive's drift to 0 (home) past a distance threshold, the same principle adapted since primitives don't need a literal edge-wrap. Verified end to end: full Play/Pause/Resume/Stop lifecycle confirmed via live transform inspection (paused position byte-identical across forced ticks, resume continues with no jump — no elapsed-time compensation even needed, since drift accumulates incrementally rather than from an absolute time coordinate), ~60fps at 24 primitives, zero console errors, zero regression on `pressure`/`wobble` re-tested after the change. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md` §6d.

- **August 15, 2026 (still later same day) — Soul's Flow field pattern gains free particles, the piece the primitive-only port had left out.** Diego caught it directly: the Flow field port only moved primitives already loaded on the stage; the exploration's own free-roaming marks (independent particles drawing their own accumulating trails) weren't there. Added as a "Free particles" toggle inside Flow field's own panel, layered onto the same field/speed the primitive-drift side already reads — not a separate mode. Rendered as real SVG `<path>` elements on the stage (one per particle, its whole trail folded into a single `d` string per tick, not one element per trail point), so N particles cost exactly N DOM elements and export automatically alongside everything else already on the stage — verified: 60 particles → 60 `<path>`s, both live and in the serialised export. Mark styles (Line/Dots/Drop marks, the exploration's own three) reimplemented as SVG path data instead of canvas draw calls, with one disclosed simplification — SVG has no per-vertex opacity, so the true per-point fade became a single opacity per path, with Dots/Drop marks still tapering in size toward the tail so the trail visibly narrows regardless. Respawn-not-teleport on exit reuses the same fix already applied to primitive drift. Verified live: all three styles visually distinct and screenshot-confirmed, Pause freezes a sampled particle's `d` byte-identical across forced ticks, Stop removes every particle `<path>` (0 remaining, counted not assumed), 60fps holding at 600 particles (the slider's own max, Drop marks, the priciest style), 0 of 9 new controls unaccessibly-named, zero console errors across every combination tested. Manual: `docs/SOUL.md` §6d.

- **August 25, 2026 — Shipped Vortex (`/vortex/`), migrated from `explorations/vortex/` the same way Membrane was — and backfilled Membrane's own missing CLAUDE.md documentation while at it (its migration had shipped without ever getting a Tools-table row or Repo Structure entry, a real gap found while adding Vortex's own). Three decisions Diego made directly before any code: **(1) Palette** — Membrane's own RMX chip UI (up to 8 colours now, not 5 — Membrane's cap was a Camo Turing GLSL-array leftover that doesn't apply to Vortex's plain `i % n` cycle), **plus** a new reproducible numeric **Seed**. **(2) Zoom/pan** — keep Vortex's own real 3D camera (drag-orbit, Shift-drag-roll, Camera distance) as the ONLY zoom mechanism; explicitly do NOT layer `Organica.createZoomPan` on top of a 3D scene. **(3) Export** — PNG+SVG+Video all available whether paused or running, pausing being the natural (not enforced) way to frame a shot. One fact verified directly before designing around it: the exploration's own `draw()` starts with an unconditional `background(6)` — **Vortex redraws from scratch every frame, it never accumulates** — which is why SVG export needed none of Membrane's `shapeHistory`/throttle/cap machinery: `state.lastItems` (the current frame's already depth-sorted segment list) is complete on its own. Seed reproducibility hinges on `segConstCache` invalidation (`applySeed()` clears it on every seed change, plus `p.noiseSeed()` and a `mulberry32`-derived `seedOffset` for belt-and-braces decorrelation) — verified directly, not assumed: set seed 42 → visit a different seed → set 42 again → `state.lastItems` came back byte-identical (`JSON.stringify` equality), while a genuinely different seed produced genuinely different geometry. PNG export re-renders at scale for real (`renderFrame(g, {scale})`, one `g.scale(s)` wrapping the whole draw — item positions always computed in unscaled `state.W/H` space) rather than upsampling a bitmap, which Vortex can afford precisely because it already redraws from scratch regardless.
