SVG export verified structurally, not just "opens without erroring": real `<line>` count matching the active segment count exactly, 142 distinct stroke-widths and 71 distinct segment-lengths on one 260-segment export (confirms `scaleAmt` is applied to both, per-depth, not just one), zero `<image>` tags, non-empty at rest and mid-transition. Camera drag-while-paused (the export-framing workflow) verified working; Shift+drag-roll verified correct at the **logic** level (`state.shiftDown` branch rolls only, confirmed by injecting `state.shiftDown=true` directly and redrag — roll changed, yaw/pitch didn't) — the browser-automation test harness's own modifier-drag doesn't fire real `keydown`/`keyup` events for the held key, a test-tool limitation disclosed rather than glossed over, not an app bug (real browsers fire genuine keyboard events for held modifiers). 60fps confirmed holding at 800 segments with Spin+Orbit active, matching the original exploration's own measured perf-fix result. Canvas resize deliberately does **not** reset the running animation (divergence from Membrane, which loses its accumulated trail on resize) — Vortex reconstructs every pixel from `(state, animClock, camera)` each frame regardless, confirmed live: resizing through several presets mid-run caused no jump, no reset, vortex stayed centred (`cx,cy = state.W/2, state.H/2` recomputed every frame, no stored centre to go stale). Accent colour `#6d4bd8` (deep indigo) chosen after checking the full `--tool:` registry — the exploration's own `#e94f37` collides with both Strata and Membrane's warm-red band. Hub nav link added after Membrane; the original `/explorations/vortex/` link deliberately left in place, same as Membrane's own exploration survived its migration. 0 of ~50 panel/floatbar controls unaccessibly-named (the 4 that show as "unlabeled" by attribute alone all carry real visible button text — PNG/SVG/Start recording/Reset camera angle — which is a valid accessible name on its own).

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS ↔ Genesis Creator: seeds from Creator's library, Loom grid variety, and a ported transform-preview strip (Phases A + C of a 3-phase evaluation; Phase B deferred).** Diego asked to evaluate linking FVS's own Element/Seed definition to "the Creator tool," using Paper.js where it fits. Two Explore agents reframed the question before any code: **Genesis Creator (`genesis/creator.html`) is already a full shape-authoring tool** (Draw/Import/Generate modes + a live Appearance transform + its own `organica.library.forms` library), not just a composer over the 55 built-in forms — a much closer fit than assumed. **Paper.js's only proven use in the repo (Strata's Refine editor) is shape *correction*** (anchor-drag/smooth/simplify/unite) — zero rotate/scale/flip/transform-preview usage anywhere, which is Canvas2D/SVG territory everywhere it's actually needed, including FVS's own preview strip. Diego confirmed the architecture directly: **Creator** authors/stores/(future) animates Seeds; **FVS** builds Components and Symbols from them. Scoped to 3 phases, then EXPANDED Phase A on his own follow-up ("includes already the grid 2x2 and other type of grids... include also some basic geometric and organic shapes") before any code was written. **Phase A shipped**: `fvs/index.html` gained a "Creator library…" picker reading `organica.library.forms` and feeding a form's own `.svg` through the *already-built* `extractElementFromSVG` (the exact upload code path, different source) — caught and fixed a **real bug before it ever ran**: the plan's own research had the wrong data shape (`set.forms` as full objects); reading `creator.html`'s actual `saveForm()` showed `set.forms` is really just an array of ID strings referencing a **top-level** `lib.forms` array — a naive implementation would have silently produced `undefined` id/name/svg for every real Creator-saved shape while "working" against hand-built test data that happened to share the same wrong assumption. Grid variety: `getGrid()`/`resolveGridCells()`/`frameSize()` generalized to Square 2×2/3×3/4×4 plus a genuine `Organica.loadLoomGrid` JSON import (rect *and* polygon cellShape, verified with real fabricated exports of both), with the 7 four-cell-only symmetry rules (Pinwheel/Mirror/Diagonal/Checkerboard/Row/Column/Radial) greyed out via `syncRuleAvailability()` whenever the active grid isn't 4 cells, bouncing off an invalidated selection rather than leaving it silently stuck; Random/Exhaustive fall back to a per-cell-independent draw at any other cell count (an honest, uncurated fallback, not a fake generalization of rules hand-derived for exactly 4 cells); Manual mode's mini-editor and its `Organica.autoLabelPanel` call now rebuild for the real cell count instead of a hardcoded 4. **A second real bug caught by testing the actual sequence, not just the destination**: Exhaustive's own over-cap `Generate` disable never got cleared when switching to any OTHER rule (only re-checked on returning to Exhaustive) — Pinwheel silently stayed disabled after visiting an over-cap Exhaustive; fixed in `syncRuleUI()`. **A third gap caught by a live accessibility check**: `buildManualEditor()` rebuilds its DOM on every grid change, but `Organica.autoLabelPanel` had only ever run once at page load — rebuilt rows went unlabeled; fixed by calling it again at the end of `buildManualEditor()` itself (confirmed idempotent — skips rows that already have a name). Shipped a **"Basic Shapes" seed set** (Square/Circle/Triangle/Arc/Hexagon/Star, real SVG markup reusing FVS's own path formulas) seeded once into `organica.library.forms` from both `genesis/creator.html` and `genesis/library.html` (kept in sync per that file's own existing comment) — deliberately NOT hooked into the more special `forms.js`-driven "Organic Forms" built-in-set machinery, which doesn't accept plain `.svg` entries; shipped as a normal (non-builtIn) set instead, the same shape `saveForm()` itself produces, verified rendering correctly in both Creator's own picker and `library.html`. **Phase C shipped**: ported FVS's own 7-state rotation/flip preview strip into Creator's Appearance panel (`renderTransformPreview()`, hooked into the existing `render()` so it updates for free on every mode switch or geometry change) — built from Creator's *own* `geometry()`/`formTransform()` primitives rather than FVS's data model, verified as genuinely complementary to (not a duplicate of) the existing continuous Rotation/Scale sliders: changing those sliders correctly leaves the strip's own fixed reference states unchanged, while an actual geometry change (switching Circle/Poly/Star, adding draw points) correctly updates it. **Phase B (Paper.js-powered drawing in Creator) deferred** — the plan's own largest, riskiest piece, explicitly flagged as needing an isolated prototype before touching Creator's real Draw mode; not started this pass. Verified end to end: real (not hand-assumed) Creator library data round-trips into FVS including a custom-uploaded shape's own path data; a real 6-cell rect Loom export and a 3-cell polygon Loom export both resolve and render correctly; 0 unlabeled controls confirmed via a live accessibility check that actually exercised Manual mode at a rebuilt cell count, not a stale snapshot; zero console errors on fresh tabs throughout (distinguishing real errors from this browser tool's own known stale-console-buffer artifact, re-confirmed several times this session).

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: "Save to library" debugged and found genuinely broken, not a test artifact.** Diego asked to debug Save to Library directly. Reproduced it for real — a real click through the actual UI, no stubbed `window.prompt` this time (the earlier session's own automated tests had all stubbed `window.prompt` to make them deterministic, which silently hid this) — and it threw `Error: prompt() is not supported` the instant Save was clicked, an uncaught exception that aborted the whole save with zero visible feedback to the user, exactly the "doesn't work" symptom reported. Root cause: `window.prompt()` isn't just occasionally cancelled (returns `null`) in some embedding contexts, it can be entirely disabled and throw outright — a real risk for any Organica tool, which can run embedded in varied contexts. **Checked whether this is FVS-only or systemic before fixing**: grepped for `prompt(` across every tool and found the identical pattern in **five others** — Camo Turing, Komorebi, Halide, Warping, and Pollen all name their own preset via a bare `prompt()` call, meaning this exact failure mode is very likely latent product-wide, not an FVS-specific bug. Fixed FVS's own instance now (in scope); the other five are a separate, flagged background-task candidate rather than fixed blind here. **Fix**: replaced the blocking native dialog with a plain inline row (a labelled text input pre-filled with a sensible default name, plus Save/Cancel buttons and Enter-to-confirm/Escape-to-cancel) that depends on nothing but the DOM — works in any embedding context. Verified via real UI clicks end-to-end: Save opens the row with the input pre-selected, clicking Save adds the real thumbnail to the Library and closes the row, Cancel and Escape both close it without saving anything. One thing worth naming precisely for future debugging in this environment: a stale, already-fixed error can keep reappearing in `read_console_messages` checks on the SAME tab after a fix — traced twice now to the tool buffering console history across the tab's lifetime rather than resetting per navigation; a genuinely fresh tab is the reliable way to confirm a fix actually landed, not re-checking the same tab. 0 of 46 controls unaccessibly-named (added a real `aria-label` to the new name input rather than leaning on its placeholder, which isn't a reliable accessible name), zero real console errors on a clean tab.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: Element preview strip + a local Component Library, from a "valuta due cose" evaluation ask.** Diego asked to evaluate (not just build) two things: a step working exclusively on Element definition before Component generation, and a saveable/reusable Component library feeding a future Symbols tier and Figma export. Researched before designing: confirmed `Organica.presetStore` is a fully generic JSON-blob store (Loom's own "Save grid" already persists its whole Universal JSON Model this same way — the right precedent to follow), and confirmed via direct code check that `Organica.sendToFigma`'s `'organica-svg'` postMessage type is **still** never listened for by `figma-plugin/code.js` — the same product-wide bug found during Loom's own work in August, still unfixed. Presented both forks to Diego before building: **(1)** a literal wizard/step flow vs. an always-visible preview strip in the current single-page panel — Diego chose the strip, keeping FVS consistent with every other Organica tool's own "everything visible, live" convention (no tool in the product has steps/tabs). **(2)** fix the shared Figma-plugin bug now vs. wire FVS into the same broken channel anyway vs. skip Figma this pass — Diego chose to skip it entirely rather than ship a button that silently no-ops. **Shipped**: an Element preview strip (`buildElementPreviewSVG`/`renderElementPreview`) showing the current Element at 7 fixed reference states (0/90/180/270° + flipH/flipV/both) — genuinely useful for an asymmetric or uploaded shape, verified with a deliberately asymmetric L-shaped upload where all 7 tiles read as visibly distinct (a symmetric shape like the triangle only shows the vocabulary correctly, not the value — confirmed both). Wired into every Element-affecting change (type switch, Base/Height/Thickness, upload success, Ink colour, Paper colour) — same trigger points `renderGallery()` already used. **Component Library**: `Organica.presetStore('fvs')` holding full self-contained snapshots (Element incl. an upload's own `customElement` geometry — not just its type string, Grid, Palette, Paper, and the exact generated cells, not a re-derivable reference) — Save/Load/Remove, library thumbnails rendered from each entry's OWN saved state via a `buildComponentSVGWithPaper` wrapper (temporarily swaps `state.paperColor` so a saved thumbnail can never drift just because the live panel keeps changing after saving). **A real bug caught by testing, not assumed working**: the first `buildLibraryEntry()` only saved `{cellSize, gap}` for Grid, omitting `cols`/`rows` that `gridCellCenters()` needs — `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'cx')` on the very first save+render. Fixed by storing `getGrid()`'s own full return value instead of hand-picking fields. **A separate false alarm, caught and correctly dismissed rather than "fixed" blind**: the same error kept reappearing in console checks even after the fix and after clearing `localStorage` — traced to `read_console_messages` buffering across the tab's whole lifetime rather than resetting per-navigation; confirmed by opening a genuinely fresh tab, which loaded clean, proving the bug was already fixed and the persistent-looking error was a tool artifact, not live app state. (A related, already-known artifact resurfaced too: `<input>` values bleeding across tabs on the same origin — same phenomenon documented earlier in this session for Vortex/Loom, not an FVS bug.) Verified end to end on a clean tab: full save→edit-everything→load round-trip restores every control and the exact saved cells (not a re-generated arrangement); a saved custom-uploaded element's own path `d` string round-trips byte-identical; Remove drops the entry from both the DOM and `localStorage`. 0 of 44 controls unaccessibly-named, zero real console errors.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: Small/Medium/Large scale values converted from number inputs to sliders, debugged end-to-end.** The three Scale-axis values (used by Random/Exhaustive's own family parameter spaces) were `<input type="number">` — swapped for `<input type="range">` (`rg-scale-small/medium/large`, 10–400%) with real `<span class="ctrl-val">` readouts, matching every other slider in the panel and picking up `Organica.enhanceSliders`'s filled-track styling for free (a plain number input never had that). Debugged as asked, not assumed working: dragged each slider and confirmed three things independently — the readout text updates live; `scaleValues()` (which `axisValues()`/`FAMILIES` read) returns the exact dragged percentages as `[0.4, 1, 2]` after setting Small=40/Large=200; and those exact values actually reach generation — Random mode at those settings only ever produced cells scaled `0.4`, `1`, or `2` (checked by collecting every distinct scale value across 60 generated components), and Exhaustive's own live "N possible" count recomputed correctly from the new values too. Screenshotted the resulting gallery to confirm the scale spread is visually real, including the expected overflow at 200% (a triangle scaled past its own cell is supposed to spill over — not a bug, Base/Height=100 already fills the cell at 100%). Zero console errors, 0 of 41 controls unaccessibly-named.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: the 4 fixed arc-thickness elements collapsed into 1 Arc element with a live Thickness slider.** Direct follow-up: the 4 preset elements (`arc25/50/75/100`) already shared one underlying `arcGeometry(thicknessPct)` generator, so exposing thickness as a real control rather than 4 fixed dropdown entries was a UI simplification, not new geometry work — `ELEMENT_TYPES.arc.geometry(p) → arcGeometry(p.thickness)`, `getElement()` now also reads `thickness` from a new `rg-thickness` slider (range 1–100, default 100 — the solid quarter-circle, same default the standalone presets converged on). `syncElementUI()` extended to show the Thickness row only for `arc` (mirroring how Base/Height already only show for `triangle`). Element dropdown now just Triangle/Arc/Custom instead of 5 entries. Verified: dragging Thickness live-updates the gallery (path `d`'s inner-radius term matches `100*(1-thickness/100)` exactly, checked directly against the rendered `d` string), and Radial at Thickness=100 still produces the exact same clean full circle as before — no regression from the refactor. 0 of 41 controls unaccessibly-named, zero console errors.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: 5 real elements (triangle + 4 arc thicknesses), SVG upload, two rules derived directly from a second Figma reference, plus Ink/Paper.** Diego shared a second reference (a real Figma component library: Elements→Components) and asked for the 5 shown elements built for real, SVG upload as a general way to add new ones, and the shown Components' construction rules derived — "Analizza" again, so two genuinely ambiguous reads were confirmed with him before building rather than guessed: **(1)** the dashed-box "4 arc icons" are 4 different **thicknesses** of one arc shape (not 4 rotations of one arc, which the existing Rotation axis would already produce for free) — confirmed directly, changed the whole element plan. **(2)** Components 4/5 ("S curve"/"bowtie") aren't full independent-4-cell arrangements; Diego specified them precisely — component 4: top row (cells 1,2) as-drawn, bottom row (cells 3,4) reflected on X (flipV); component 5: cell 1 reflected on Y (flipH), cell 2 as-drawn, repeated for cells 3/4 — two genuinely new **row-grouped** and **column-grouped** 2-state families, distinct from the existing diagonal-grouped Checkerboard. **Element model upgraded from polygon point-lists to real SVG path `d` strings** (`ELEMENT_TYPES[type].geometry(params) → {d, normTx, normTy, normScale}`) — needed because arcs have real curves; SVG render now emits `<path d>` and Canvas2D draws via `ctx.fill(new Path2D(d))` (replacing a manual `moveTo/lineTo` loop, and natively supporting the arc `A` command). The `norm*` fields are a **second, separate transform layer** (nested `<g>`/`ctx.translate+scale`, nothing baked into `d` itself) — deliberately chosen over writing a general path-data coordinate transformer, since arbitrary uploaded paths (arcs/beziers/whatever) have too many token shapes to safely regex-rewrite; one more transform nesting handles any path losslessly with zero parsing. **4 arc presets** (`arc25/50/75/100`, apex anchored at the box's own corner, outer radius fixed at 100, inner radius derived from thickness%) share one `arcPathD(thicknessPct)` generator — verified visually on first render that the sweep direction was already correct, no flag-flip needed. **SVG upload**: hidden `accept=".svg"` input + button (Membrane's own convention), `DOMParser`, first drawable shape only (`path/circle/rect/ellipse/polygon/polyline` — confirmed via explore agent that no ready-made "SVG→one flat path" helper exists anywhere in the repo, this is genuinely new code), measured via a real off-DOM `<svg>` host's `getBBox()` (Pollen's own technique) to derive the `norm*` fit. Verified directly, not just "no throw": a `<path>`, a `<circle>` (manually converted to a 2-arc path), and a non-square `<polygon>` (200×50 triangle) all extracted with correct `normScale`/centering math, a multi-shape SVG correctly used only the first element, and a shape-less SVG threw a clean readable error. **The two derived rules verified against Diego's own spec by diffing the actual generated cells, not eyeballing**: Row mirror's first render matched `[-,-,V,V]` exactly; Column mirror's first render came out `[-,H,-,H]` — backwards from Diego's own "cell 1 reflected, cell 2 original" — caught by the diff, fixed by swapping which column carries the flip, re-verified `[H,-,H,-]`. **Radial** (the harder derived piece, for the reference's circle/rosette/star): realized Pinwheel's own rotation stepping follows ROW-MAJOR cell order (TL,TR,BL,BR), which is not the grid's actual rotational adjacency (TL,TR,BR,BL, walking the square's real corners) — built a second cell-order constant (`RADIAL_ORDER`) for true rotational tiling. **Worked on the first attempt, no iteration needed**: `arc100` (solid quarter-disc) through Radial produced a pixel-clean full circle, and the same rule with `arc25` (thin arc) produced a genuine ring/donut and, at other base/chirality combinations in the same 8-component set, both a 4-pointed star and a 4-petal rosette — directly matching all three of the reference's otherwise-unspecified Components 1–3 in one generation. **Ink/Paper**: audited where colour was actually used before adding anything (per Diego's own "fai un check") and found a real gap — no background concept existed anywhere (hardcoded CSS white on the thumbnail, no `<rect>` in the SVG export at all, PNG export started from a transparent `clearRect`). Added a `Paper` swatch (`Organica.createColorSwatch`, same convention as every other tool's Background swatch) wired into all three render paths at once, then deleted the now-redundant hardcoded `.fvs-thumb` CSS background so the thumbnail shows exactly what exports, not a CSS approximation — verified by sampling an actual exported PNG corner pixel against the swatch's own hex, exact match. **Gap now defaults to 0.** **Cell size "doesn't work"**: traced end-to-end rather than assumed — it was never broken, `rg-cellsize` correctly threads through to the exported SVG/PNG's own pixel dimensions (verified: 120px at Cell size 60 vs 520px at Cell size 260, exact doubling+ratio match); the reported symptom is real but was a legibility gap — `.fvs-thumb`'s fixed 96×96 CSS box always renormalizes the SVG's own viewBox to fill it, so Cell size's only remaining on-screen effect is a gap-to-cell ratio too small to perceive, made totally invisible once Gap defaults to 0 in this same pass. Fixed with a clarifying panel hint rather than inventing a fake preview-scaling feature that would make the gallery unpredictable at high component counts. 0 of 40 controls unaccessibly-named (added a missing `aria-label` on the hidden upload `<input>`, caught by the same accessibility check every pass here runs), zero console errors across every element/rule/upload/colour combination tested.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: Base/Height default to 100 (perfect cell fit), Fill replaced by a centralised Palette, and Random/Exhaustive rebuilt around named symmetry families instead of independent per-cell combinatorics.** Three direct asks. **(1)** Base/Height now default to 100/100 — already geometrically correct (the triangle's own point set already spans the full 0–100 box at those values, `apexY=50-height/2` etc.), so this was a default-value change only, verified: cells now tile edge-to-edge with zero gap at Gap=0. **(2)** Fill moved out of Element (now pure shape geometry, no colour) into a new **Palette** panel section — the RMX chip UI Membrane/Vortex already established, min 1 (not their own min 2, since a single flat colour is a legitimate baseline here) max 8, `colorAt(i)` cycling by cell index into `buildComponentItems`'s own per-item `color` field, which both `drawComponentCanvas` and `buildComponentSVG` now read instead of one shared `element.fill`. **(3)** The substantial one — analysed the existing 6 rule modes before touching code and found a real split: Identity/Pinwheel/Mirror always look designed because they encode actual symmetry, while Random/Exhaustive chose each of the 4 cells fully independently, which mostly reads as noise once more than one axis is active (confirmed the reasoning wasn't just aesthetic guessing: verified numerically that the old exhaustive space with Rotation+Flip active was 65,536 — almost entirely "unrelated cells," with the handful of genuinely symmetric arrangements buried in it being exactly what Pinwheel/Mirror already produce directly). Presented this analysis and three concrete directions via AskUserQuestion rather than picking one; Diego chose all three, which turned out to compose into one coherent fix rather than three separate ones. **Shipped**: two new named symmetry rules — **Diagonal mirror** (on-diagonal pair TL/BR shares rotation R, off-diagonal pair TR/BL shares R's 90°-reflection `(90−R) mod 360`) and **Checkerboard** (TL/BR = state A, TR/BL = state B, exactly 2 distinct states) — alongside the existing Identity/Pinwheel/Mirror. Then rebuilt **Random and Exhaustive to draw from a `FAMILIES` registry** of these same five named rules instead of choosing 4 cells independently: each family exposes `paramSpace(activeAxes)` + `build(params)`, gated by `requiresAxis` (Pinwheel/Diagonal need Rotation, Mirror needs Flip, Checkerboard needs any one active axis, Identity has no requirement so `activeFamilies()` can never return empty). This directly answers all three requested directions at once: every Random/Exhaustive result is now a member of a real symmetry family (direction 1), the two new named rules expand the "always good" set rather than patching the "sometimes bad" one (direction 2), and low entropy falls out structurally — every family caps distinct-states-across-4-cells at 1–4 rather than allowing full independence — so no separate probability-weighting hack was needed (direction 3). Verified directly: Random at 16 components showed 1–4 distinct states per component, never fully independent, and every thumbnail read as a genuine composition (pinwheels/diagonals/checkerboards), a stark visual improvement over the prior noise-heavy gallery. Exhaustive's own total is now computed per-family and is dramatically smaller in the common case — Rotation+Flip active dropped from the old design's 65,536 (hard-refused) to a real, generatable 288 — while the same honest 512-cap-refuses-cleanly behaviour still holds at the extreme (all 3 axes active → 2,400, correctly refused with the gallery left untouched). Same-seed Random reproducibility re-verified after the rewrite (byte-identical). All geometry factored into shared `build*Cells()` functions so the standalone dropdown rules and the `FAMILIES` registry can't drift apart from each other. 0 of 35 controls unaccessibly-named, zero console errors throughout.

- **August 25, 2026 (later same day) — Shipped FVS (`/fvs/`), a new tool modelled on Figma's own component system, from a reference screenshot Diego shared (Elements → Components → Symbols/Assets → Applications). Scoped to the first stage only per his own ask ("iniziamo con un test"): Elements→Components, verified with one authored test Element (a black isosceles triangle) in a square 2×2 grid — Symbols/Pattern-tiling/Applications are later phases, not built this pass.** Checked the codebase for reuse before writing anything: **no "generate N transformed shape copies into a grid" engine exists anywhere in Organica** (Loom/Genesis/Soul all checked) — genuinely new work. Loom's own grid model has no cheap "just give me a plain 2×2" call (`json-model.js` needs a generator or hand-built tracks), so FVS builds its own trivial even-division grid directly rather than routing through Loom's Kiwi solver for a case that doesn't need one. The real reuse point is `Organica.loadLoomGrid` (`shared/core.js`) — already resolves any Loom JSON export into absolute per-cell geometry with zero ES-module imports; FVS's own internal grid shape was deliberately kept compatible with its return value so "import a Loom-designed grid" is a future data swap, not a rewrite. Architecture pattern reused directly from Vortex: **one item-descriptor array** (`cx,cy,rotation,flipH,flipV,scale`) feeds both the Canvas2D PNG export and the SVG string, identical transform math in both — same discipline as `vortex/js/render.js`/`svgexport.js`. Confirmed the full transform vocabulary and rule-engine scope with Diego via AskUserQuestion before building (all three transform axes; all four generation modes plus manual) rather than assuming a narrower v1. **Shipped**: Rotation (4 values) / Flip (4) / Scale (3, editable %) as independently-toggleable "active axes"; **Identity** (1 component), **Pinwheel** (up to 8 — 4 rotation offsets × 2 chirality, deduped), **Mirror/Kaleidoscope** (up to 4, one per "seed corner"), **Random seeded** (configurable count, `mulberry32` — verified reproducible: same seed after visiting a different one produced byte-identical output), **Exhaustive** (literal per-cell-independent cross-product of active axes, **hard-capped at 512** — refuses cleanly with the exact computed total above cap, e.g. "65,536 possible — exceeds the cap," rather than silently truncating or fake-sampling; verified the refused click leaves the existing gallery untouched), and a **Manual** 2×2 mini-editor (12 labelled selects) that appends one hand-built component without disturbing the rest of the gallery. A `dedupeComponents()` pass (JSON-stringify-keyed) runs after every named-rule generation so Pinwheel/Mirror can never present two combinatorially-identical arrangements as distinct — Random and Exhaustive skip it deliberately (Random because the requested count should be the count delivered; Exhaustive because independent-cell cross-products can't collide by construction). **A real bug caught by diffing the actual generated cell data, not by eyeballing the render**: `ruleMirror`'s first implementation (a generic "clockwise cycle then re-index into a fixed flip-pattern list") put the diagonal cell's "both flips" state on the wrong corner — invisible on screen because the isosceles triangle is itself left-right symmetric, so a wrong `flipH` produces no visible difference on this particular test shape. Rewrote as a direct adjacency table (`MIRROR_RELATIONS`: for each possible seed corner, its actual horizontal/vertical/diagonal neighbours by grid position) instead of the generic cycle, then re-verified numerically — `[TL,TR,BL,BR] = [-, H, V, HV]` for the TL-seed case, exactly the spec. **Accent colour `#3fa876`** (emerald) chosen after auditing all 12 tools' `--tool:` hues — a genuinely open gap between Komorebi's olive (83°) and Camo Turing's teal (192°). Verified end to end: all 6 generation modes produce correct transform data (checked numerically, not just visually, throughout); gallery selection/highlight correct; PNG and SVG export both run clean through the real floatbar UI; exported SVG structurally verified (exactly 4 `<polygon>` elements per component, each `rotate()` matching the component's own stored per-cell value exactly, zero `<image>` tags); Fill colour swatch live-repaints the whole gallery and propagates into export; 0 of 36 panel/floatbar controls unaccessibly-named (the 4 that show as "unlabeled" by bare attribute check all carry real visible button text — PNG/SVG/Generate/Clear); hub nav link renders in the new accent colour after Vortex; zero console errors throughout. Manual/docs not yet written beyond this note — a dedicated `docs/FVS.md` can follow once the Symbols/Pattern/Applications phases give it more to document.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — Phase B shipped: Paper.js-powered drawing in Genesis Creator, replacing its hand-rolled bezier anchor editor.** Completes the 3-phase plan from earlier the same day (Phase A: FVS reads Creator's library + grid variety + Basic Shapes set; Phase C: transform-preview strip ported into Creator) — Phase B was deliberately deferred until last since the plan itself flagged it as the largest, most novel piece needing an isolated prototype first. New `Organica.createPaperDrawEditor(canvasEl, opts)` in `shared/paper.js`, additive alongside Strata's own `createTraceEditor` (untouched, unaffected): click-to-place anchors on a single `paper.Path`, drag a segment's point, drag its mirrored handleIn/handleOut for manual tangent control, toggle Closed/Smooth, undo, simplify, `getPathData()`. **Smooth is "live"** the way Creator's own hand-rolled `tangentOf()` was — every edit re-derives ALL auto segments' handles via real `path.smooth({type:'continuous'})` (paper.js has no live-recompute mode of its own), while a `manualSegments` Set remembers which segments were hand-tangented and restores them after each re-smooth, so a manual edit survives editing elsewhere on the path (double-click a segment releases it back to auto — same convention Creator's own hand-rolled version used). **Prototyped first in isolation** (`genesis/_test-paper-draw.html`, matching Loom's own `_test-*.html` convention) before touching Creator itself, per the plan's own instruction — caught and fixed two real bugs there before they could reach the real tool: (1) toggling Closed never recomputed smoothing for the new wraparound segment, so it rendered as a straight line even with Smooth checked — fixed by calling `resmooth()` inside `setClosed()`, not just setting the flag; (2) `simplify()` was destructively cumulative like Strata's own original `simplifyAll` — dragging the tolerance slider back down couldn't recover discarded detail — fixed with the same `simplifyLive` pattern Strata already established (a `baselineJSON` snapshot via `path.exportJSON()`, re-imported before every `simplify()` call, updated after every real draw action but never after simplify itself). Verified live in the prototype: draw/drag/close/smooth/undo/simplify all correct, plus the harder-to-verify parts confirmed directly via console inspection rather than fighting pixel-perfect drag targeting — manual handle drag mirrors correctly (`handleIn = -handleOut`), a manually-tangented segment's handle survives byte-identical after dragging a different anchor elsewhere, and double-click correctly releases it back to the auto Catmull-Rom-equivalent value. **Integration into `creator.html`**: a `#draw-canvas` overlay sits exactly over `#art` (both fixed 460×460 CSS px, confirmed from the existing CSS rather than assumed responsive) with a `fitBox: {x:0,y:0,width:200,height:200}` option (new on the factory) mapping Paper's project space 1:1 onto the artboard's own 0–200 box, so `getPathData()` drops straight into `geometry()`'s draw branch with zero coordinate conversion — replacing `tangentOf()`/`bezierPathD()`/`S.points` entirely (deleted, along with the old hand-rolled pointerdown/pointermove/pointerup anchor-drag logic in `bindArtboard()`, the old SVG-rendered `.handle`/`.thandle`/`.thline`/`.guide` markers in `render()`'s handles-layer block, and their now-dead CSS — Draw mode's anchor/handle overlay is Paper's own `fullySelected` rendering on the canvas now, not hand-drawn SVG). The canvas's own path is stroked `transparent` — the SVG form-layer stays the shape's one visual source of truth (styled per Fill/Stroke mode exactly as before), the canvas contributes only the edit affordances on top. **One disclosed simplification, not silently dropped**: new points land in raw 0–200 space regardless of the live Appearance transform, where the old version ran clicks through `invTF()` so a new point landed under the cursor even mid-rotation (`applyTF`/`invTF` deleted as genuinely dead code once nothing else called them) — drawing new points while a non-default rotation/scale/move is already dialled in is a rare workflow against the natural "draw first, then transform" order, so this wasn't carried over. **A real crash caught immediately on Creator's first real load** (not the prototype, which never happened to toggle Closed at 0 segments): `path.smooth()` throws on a path with under 2 segments, and Creator's own init calls `drawEditor.setClosed(true)` right away to match `#ck-close`'s default-checked state — fixed with a length guard in `resmooth()`. **A second false alarm, correctly dismissed rather than chased**: the exact same stale-console-buffer artifact documented repeatedly earlier this project (a tab's `read_console_messages` keeps showing an already-fixed error) reappeared here too — resolved by opening a genuinely fresh tab, which loaded clean, before concluding the fix had actually landed. **A real, pre-existing Phase A bug found only by this phase's own end-to-end verification**: saving a drawn shape and loading it back through FVS's "Creator library" picker (Phase A, shipped hours earlier) threw `clone.getBBox is not a function` — traced to `extractElementFromSVG`'s `DOMParser(...,'image/svg+xml')` call silently parsing every element into the null namespace (no `getBBox`, no `SVGElement`) whenever the source string has no explicit `xmlns` attribute on its root `<svg>` tag. Real uploaded SVG FILES always carry `xmlns` (why file upload "worked" when Phase A was verified earlier), but **every single Creator-authored library string does not** — inline markup dropped into `innerHTML` never needs the attribute, so nobody had written it, and Phase A's own earlier verification never happened to run a saved Creator form through this exact `getBBox` code path (only through thumbnail rendering, a more forgiving path). This means Phase A's whole "Creator library" picker — the 6 built-in Basic Shapes AND every user-drawn form, not just the one drawn this session — was silently broken for every entry until now. Fixed once at the top of `extractElementFromSVG` with `ensureSvgNamespace()` (injects `xmlns` only if missing, verified as a true no-op on an already-namespaced string via direct comparison). **Verified end to end after both fixes**: drew a real shape in Creator, confirmed it renders through the existing `formMarkup()`/Appearance pipeline (rotation applied and reset correctly, edit-canvas overlay staying in its own raw space exactly as documented), saved it, round-tripped it through FVS's Creator-library picker (previously the exact broken path), generated a real 16-component gallery from it with zero errors, then separately re-tested one of the 6 Basic Shapes (Triangle) through the same previously-broken path to confirm the fix isn't specific to freshly-drawn shapes. Also regression-checked Import/Generate modes still work post-refactor and that `#draw-canvas` correctly stops/resumes intercepting clicks when switching modes away from and back to Draw. Zero console errors throughout, confirmed on fresh tabs. Not yet done: retrofitting `docs/` with a Phase A/B/C summary (this note is the only record so far) and re-running the full accessibility-label check across Creator's changed markup.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — Creator: full Draw + Generate debug pass, 4 real bugs found and fixed.** Diego asked for a complete debug of Draw and Generate. Worked through both systematically (0/1/2/N points, Close/Smooth toggles, Style Fill↔Stroke, Stroke W, Rounded caps, Scale/Width/Length/Rotation/Move, Fit to Grid, anchor drag, manual tangent-handle drag, double-click reset, Undo/Ctrl+Z/Clear including past-empty, Save with each Type pill, mode-switch round-trips; Generate's Circle/Poly/Star, Sides, Radius at both extremes) plus a regression pass on Import, since shared code (`formMarkup`/`styleAttrs`) was touched. **(1)** The Phase C preview strip (shipped earlier today) hardcoded `fill="var(--ink)"` on every tile regardless of the real Style toggle — switching to Stroke changed the main artboard but the 7 preview tiles kept showing solid filled silhouettes. Fixed by factoring the fill/stroke logic `formMarkup()` already had into a shared `styleAttrs()`, used by both — the same "one function so two call sites can't drift" fix already applied once this session (FVS's `xmlns` bug), now applied to a second, unrelated drift of the identical shape. **(2)** Generate mode's Sides field: typing "99" correctly clamped the actual rendered polygon to a 20-gon internally, but the input's own displayed value stayed "99" forever — nothing ever wrote the clamped value back. Fixed with a `blur` handler that syncs the field to the true `S.gen.n` once the user leaves it (deliberately not on every keystroke, which was tried first and rejected: clamping live while typing "20" would clamp "2" to "3" mid-keystroke, making "20" untypeable). **(3)** `#status-text`/`#status-dot` in the header were pure static markup — grepped and confirmed zero JS ever touched them, so the header always read "Draw a shape to begin" regardless of mode or whether a shape existed (Generate mode always has a shape; Draw mode kept saying "begin" after drawing one). `Organica.status()` already exists in `shared/core.js` for exactly this and is already used by Loom/Komorebi — Creator had just never called it. Wired via a new `updateStatus()` in `render()`: Draw shows point count once ≥2 (`"3 points · shape ready"`), Generate shows the shape+sides (`"star (5-sided) generated"`), Import shows imported/not-yet-imported. **(4)** Caught while verifying (3) — switching from Draw (with a shape mid-edit) to Generate left the Paper.js draw-canvas's own selection overlay (blue path outline + anchor squares) visibly stacked on top of Generate's circle, because `#draw-canvas.active`'s CSS only toggled `pointer-events`, and Paper.js keeps rendering its canvas regardless of whether it's receiving events. Fixed by also toggling `opacity` (0 when inactive) — verified the overlay no longer bleeds through, and that switching back to Draw still shows the exact preserved in-progress shape (Paper.js state isn't tied to visibility, only the CSS was hiding it late). All four verified via before/after screenshots + live JS state inspection (not just visual glancing), zero console errors across the full pass on fresh-tab checks.

- **August 25, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: Symbols, a second tier, added alongside Components.** Diego shared a Figma-style Assets reference (Elements → Components → **Symbols** → Applications) and asked for the Symbols tier: a Loom grid whose cells each hold either a raw Element or a whole saved Component, nested and scaled into the cell, filled manually or via seeded Generate. Scoped via Plan Mode with two confirmed decisions: **built directly into `fvs/index.html`** (a new `Components`/`Symbols` tab pair, `.org-tabs`/`org-header--editor`, the exact component Genesis Creator's own Draw/Import/Generate tabs already use — not a throwaway prototype), and **nested Components render as real vector**, not a flattened snapshot — a Symbol cell referencing a saved Component re-reads that Component's own `{element, grid, cells}` and redraws its actual geometry scaled into the cell, a second transform layer nested around the existing per-item `normTx/normScale` layer. **Two real Loom grids**, captured live from `/loom/` (not hand-built): a small **Bento** (Columns/Rows 3×3, Variety 0.35, Gap 8, Seed 7 → 7 merged rect cells) and a **Hexagonal** (Columns 3, Gap 0.08, Seed 7 → 17 polygon cells, the "more complex" case) — read directly off Loom's own "View JSON" panel via the live app rather than guessing generator output, since `generator.generate()` returns fully-resolved geometry (absolute rects or polygon points+centroid), not just params. Both embedded as `SYMBOL_GRID_PRESETS` constants, one click each. **Reused, unmodified**: `Organica.loadLoomGrid`, `resolveGridCells`/`frameSize` (already grid-kind-agnostic), `ELEMENT_TYPES`, `buildComponentItems`, and `mulberry32` (the same reproducible-seed discipline `ruleRandom()` already established). New: `state.symbolGrid`/`state.symbolCells` (independent of Components' own `state.loomGrid`/`state.components`), `getSymbolGrid()` (wraps the raw `loadLoomGrid` output into the `{kind:'loom',...}` shape `resolveGridCells` expects — found and fixed before it ever ran, by re-reading `getGrid()`'s own equivalent wrapping step rather than assuming `state.symbolGrid` could be passed straight through), `buildSymbolItems`/`buildSymbolSVG`/`drawSymbolCanvas` (one more transform layer around a nested Component's own per-item markup — `translate(cx,cy) rotate scale(flip×scale×fit) translate(-half,-half)` wrapping the *entire* nested per-item loop, `fit = symbolCell.cellSize / frameSize(nestedGrid)`), a per-cell Manual editor (mirrors `buildManualEditor()`'s own one-row-per-cell shape; a Source select toggles Element ↔ Component fields, and only THAT control rebuilds the editor DOM — per-cell Rotation/Flip/Scale/Type/Component selects re-render the canvas only, so editing one row never resets scroll/focus on the others), seeded Generate (50/50 element/component per cell, 100% element if the Component Library is empty), and a **separate** `Organica.presetStore('fvs-symbols')` library (matching the project's own established "per-mode preset stores, not one shared list" convention) — `state.symbolGrid` is stored verbatim as `gridModel` and fed straight back into `Organica.loadLoomGrid()` on reload, since it's already exactly the shape that function accepts. **A real, easy-to-miss bug caught by testing the nested-Component nesting itself, not just the top-level render**: `buildComponentItems()`'s own `color: colorAt(i)` always reads the CURRENT live Palette, never a saved entry's own colours — Components' own `renderLibrary()` thumbnails already work around this with a `savedColorAt` remap; `buildSymbolItems()` needed the identical remap for nested nested-Component cells, or a Symbol's own colours would silently drift every time the live Palette changed after saving. Verified end to end in a live browser session: both presets render their correct default all-triangle fill immediately; Manual mode edits (Element↔Component switch, Type/Rotation/Flip/Scale) apply live with zero editor-rebuild flicker on non-shape changes; Generate at seed 1 → seed 42 → back to seed 1 produced byte-identical `state.symbolCells` (checked via direct JSON diff, not eyeballing); SVG export of a Symbol containing nested Components produced 47 real `<path>` elements and zero `<image>` tags; Save/Load round-tripped a 17-cell Hexagonal Symbol exactly (cross-checked the restored `state.symbolCells[0]` against the saved value); switching Components ↔ Symbols repeatedly left each tier's own state untouched, Palette shared correctly; 0 of 153 controls unaccessibly-named. **Deliberately out of scope for this pass** (disclosed in the plan, not silently dropped): per-cell custom-uploaded Elements in Symbol cells (Triangle/Arc only — a custom shape is still reachable indirectly via a saved Component that itself uses one), a multi-result gallery for Symbols (one seeded composition + Regenerate, not Components' own Random/Exhaustive breadth), and recursive Symbol-inside-Symbol nesting.

- **August 26, 2026 — FVS Symbols: grid section reordered above Palette, a "Show loaded grid" outline toggle, and both presets re-captured at 0 gap/0 canvas margin.** Three quick follow-ups on the Symbols tier shipped the day before. **(1)** "Symbol grid" moved to the top of the Symbols panel, above the shared Palette section — Palette itself is one DOM block shared by both tiers (Components' own layout is untouched), so the fix was moving "Symbol grid" into its own `tier-block[data-tier=symbols]` positioned BEFORE Palette in the DOM, while Fill/Symbol library stayed in a second `tier-block` after it — no CSS reordering tricks needed. **(2)** New "Show loaded grid" checkbox draws a wireframe outline of the grid's own cell boundaries (rect or polygon) on top of the live preview — `buildGridOutlineSVG()` reuses the *exact* same origin/half offset math `resolveGridCells()`/`buildSymbolItems()` already use, so the outline lines up exactly with whatever is actually rendered per cell; verified pixel-aligned on both Bento and Hexagonal. Deliberately preview-only, same rule as Loom's own "Background grid" toggle — appended to the live SVG string inside `renderSymbolCanvasOnly()` only, never inside `buildSymbolSVG()`/`drawSymbolCanvas()` (the functions PNG/SVG export actually calls) — confirmed directly by checking the exported SVG string contains zero occurrences of the outline's own stroke colour. **(3)** Diego asked to re-check and re-import both `SYMBOL_GRID_PRESETS` grids with 0 gap and 0 canvas margin (they'd shipped at Gap 8/Canvas margin 4 for Bento, Gap 0.08 for Hexagonal) — re-captured both live from `/loom/` the same way as the original capture (View JSON panel, not hand-edited), confirming the seed/topology stays identical (same 7-cell Bento layout, same 17-cell Hexagonal layout) while the geometry shifts to genuinely edge-to-edge cells and a canvas with no border — verified visually (Hexagonal's cells now share exact edges with no gap-inset, unlike the visible gap in the original capture) and via the grid outline overlay from fix (2), zero console errors on a fresh tab for both presets.

- **August 26, 2026 (later same day) — FVS Symbols: per-cell Fit/Scale/Anchor/Padding controls, plus a real click-to-select Manual workflow.** Diego asked for a full evaluation of how a Symbol cell's content is fit/scaled/positioned, then a redesign of Manual mode: select one or more cells directly and assign a Component/Element with those properties applied to the whole selection at once. Scoped via Plan Mode with two confirmed decisions: **click-to-select on the canvas itself** (Shift-click for multi-select — the same convention Strata's own Refine editor already uses), and **three Fit modes**: Contain (kept, the old only behaviour), Fill/cover (new — independent X/Y scale to touch all 4 edges), Fixed size (new — an absolute size ignoring the cell's own dimensions). Also shipped: a continuous Scale slider (replacing the old 3-value dropdown), a 9-way Anchor grid (`object-position`-style), and a per-cell Padding percentage — all four apply uniformly to both source types (Element and nested Component), since they already shared the same transform fields. **Data model**: `state.symbolCells[i]` gained `fitMode`/`fixedSize`/`anchorX`/`anchorY`/`padding`; old saved Symbols (missing these fields entirely) are backfilled via a new `withPlacementDefaults()` at load time, and `resolveCellPlacement()`'s own fallback logic (`cell.padding || 0` etc.) makes the render math itself tolerant of the old shape too — verified by hand-constructing a pre-feature Symbol entry and confirming it renders pixel-identical to before. `resolveGridCells()` gained `cellW`/`cellH` alongside the pre-existing `cellSize = min(w,h)` (which Fill mode needs the real asymmetry Components never needed — added, not replaced, so Components' own call sites are untouched). One new shared function, `resolveCellPlacement(cellW, cellH, natural, cell)`, computes `{scaleX, scaleY, offsetX, offsetY}` from fit mode + anchor + padding + scale/fixedSize — used identically by both source types and both render paths (SVG string, Canvas2D), reducing to exactly the old centered/uniform-fit math at the defaults. **A real bug caught immediately during manual testing, not found by inspection**: clicking anywhere in a cell's own empty margin (the space a Contain-fit shape leaves inside a non-square Bento cell) selected nothing at all — a bare `<g data-cell-index>` wrapper has no hit-test area of its own in SVG, only the visible content inside it does. Fixed by adding an invisible `fill="transparent"` (not `"none"`, which has no hit area either) rect/polygon covering each cell's own full boundary, drawn first inside that same `<g>` — confirmed harmless in the exported file (zero visual effect, `transparent` paints nothing) but a real, disclosed departure from "export contains only meaningful geometry": every exported cell now also carries one invisible hit-shape, verified present in `buildSymbolSVG()`'s own output (7 `fill="transparent"` elements on the 7-cell Bento) alongside the real content paths. Selection highlight (`buildSelectionOutlineSVG()`) reuses the exact technique the "Show loaded grid" outline already established — a second, differently-coloured preview-only `<g>`, confirmed absent from `buildSymbolSVG()`'s own export output (checked directly: zero occurrences of its stroke colour). The old one-row-per-cell Manual list (`buildSymbolManualEditor()`) is gone entirely, replaced by one static "Cell properties" panel (`renderCellPropertiesPanel()`) that reflects/edits whatever `state.symbolSelection` currently holds — a batch edit, verified directly: Shift-clicking two cells then changing Rotation set both cells' `rotation` to the new value in one action, not just the last-clicked one. Verified end to end: Contain-default renders pixel-identical to before; switching a wide Bento cell to Fill visibly touches all 4 edges; Fixed size renders the same absolute size regardless of the cell's own dimensions; all 9 anchor positions visibly relocate content within a cell; a Component-sourced cell selects and edits correctly through the same panel (nested geometry, 10 real `<path>` elements on export, 0 rasterized); 0 of the new controls unaccessibly-named; zero console errors throughout on fresh-tab checks.

- **August 26, 2026 (later same day) — FVS Symbols: full regression/debug pass on the Fit/Scale/Anchor/Padding + click-to-select work, 2 real bugs found and fixed.** Diego asked for organized regression and debug testing on the feature shipped earlier the same day. Worked through it systematically — default-render regression, all 3 fit modes on both rect (Bento) and polygon (Hexagonal) cells, the 9-way anchor grid, multi-select batch-edit, Save/Load round-trip of the new fields, Generate reproducibility, export sanity (no NaN/Infinity, no selection overlay leak), tab-switch state isolation, and edge cases (empty Component library, a deleted/orphaned Component reference, extreme values). **(1)** Selecting "Component" as Source while the Component Library is empty silently produced an invisible cell — `componentName` ended up `undefined`, `buildSymbolItems()`'s existing `if (!entry) return null` swallowed it with zero console error, so the cell just vanished with no explanation. The old one-row-per-cell editor had guarded this (`disabled` on the option when the library was empty) but the new shared panel never carried that guard forward. Fixed two ways: the "Component" option is now disabled in `renderCellPropertiesPanel()` whenever the library is empty (blocks a real click), and the Source `change` handler also refuses the value programmatically (`.value` can still be set on a disabled option from code, so the click-blocking alone wasn't a complete fix — caught by deliberately bypassing the disabled state in a test to check). **(2)** Clicking the canvas *outside* any cell (the paper background) silently left an existing selection in place — a real gap against the plan's own stated reference convention (Strata's Refine editor deselects on an empty-space click). `bindSymbolCanvasSelection()`'s early-return on "no cell hit" never distinguished "nothing to do" from "should clear" — fixed by clearing the selection on a non-Shift click that misses every cell, verified the fix reproduces the Strata-like behaviour exactly (selection non-empty → click background → empty) without disturbing Shift-click's own additive semantics (a Shift-click on empty space now correctly leaves the selection untouched, tested separately). **Fixed on Diego's own follow-up**: the disclosed "deleted Component reference renders as nothing" gap above got a real fix rather than staying a known limitation — `buildSymbolItems()` now returns a `type:'missing'` placeholder (a dashed warning-red rect with a diagonal X, sized/positioned exactly where the real content would have been) instead of `null` for an orphaned `componentName`, rendered by both `buildSymbolSVG()` and `drawSymbolCanvas()` — deliberately visible in exported files too, since a silently missing chunk of a design is worse than a clearly-flagged one once the file leaves the tool. `renderCellPropertiesPanel()`'s own Component `<select>` also now surfaces the dangling reference explicitly (`⚠ name (missing)`) instead of silently mismatching whatever real option happened to render first — verified the normal case (a Component that still exists) shows no such option and renders unaffected (10 real paths, none dropped), while the orphaned case shows the placeholder in canvas/export and the flagged option in the panel, with zero console errors either way. Verified stable at deliberately extreme inputs (Scale 400%, Padding 40%, corner Anchor) — real overflow, no NaN/Infinity in the generated SVG. All fixes verified on fresh tabs after confirming a stray, unrelated `localhost:5050` 404 (Strata's own local backend, not running) was stale console-buffer noise, not a new regression — re-confirmed clean via a genuinely fresh tab per this project's own established discipline.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS Symbols: a visual "Choose content" overlay replacing the plain Source/Type/Component selects, plus click-and-drag marquee multi-select.** Diego's own framing: choosing a cell's content by picking a Component's name from a dropdown gives no visual sense of what it looks like, and selecting several cells to batch-edit had no faster path than repeated Shift-clicks. Asked directly whether Rotation/Flip still make sense for a Component cell (his own instinct going in): confirmed via AskUserQuestion — **removed entirely for Component-sourced cells** (a Component is already an internally composed arrangement, often symmetric by construction; re-rotating/flipping the whole thing as one more transform layer added a control with no clear use, not a real capability gap), **kept unchanged for Element cells**. Also confirmed the overlay's own trigger and the marquee's scope in one question: a plain click **immediately opens** the picker for that cell (no separate "select then open" step); Shift-click stays pure add/remove-from-selection with no overlay; a docked **"Choose…"** button reopens the overlay for whatever is currently selected, which is how a multi-selection (built via Shift-click or a drag) gets batch-assigned. **Docked panel restructure**: the Source/Type/Component `<select>` trio is gone from `#symbol-cell-props`, replaced by a one-line content readout + **Choose…** button; Rotation/Flip rows now hide (`display:none`, the same toggle pattern already used elsewhere in this panel) whenever the selection's first cell is Component-sourced. **The overlay** (`#symbol-content-overlay`, `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`) is genuinely new UI with no existing precedent to reuse — `Organica.popover` is anchored-to-a-trigger-button and doesn't fit "opens wherever a cell was clicked on the canvas," so this is a plain fixed-position centered modal instead, disclosed as such rather than forced into the wrong existing component. Two tile sections reuse the exact preview techniques already proven elsewhere: Elements render via the same geometry+fill the preview strip uses, Components via the identical `buildComponentSVGWithPaper` thumbnail technique the Library view already uses (a saved entry's own colours/paper, not the live palette — so a tile can't misrepresent what picking it will actually place). Picking any tile applies Diego's own stated defaults to every currently-selected cell in one shot: `scale:1, fitMode:'fill', padding:0, anchorX:0, anchorY:0`, plus `rotation:0/flipH:false/flipV:false` reset to identity even for a re-picked Element. **Marquee drag-select**: `bindSymbolCanvasSelection()` rewritten from a single `click` listener to `mousedown`/`mousemove`/`mouseup` with a 5px-movement drag threshold (Creator's own old draw-mode click handler used the identical pattern) — a plain click with no movement keeps the click-opens-overlay behaviour, movement past the threshold switches to a live marquee instead. New `svgPointFromEvent()` converts a mouse event into the SVG's own internal coordinate space via `getScreenCTM().inverse()` (re-queries the SVG element fresh on every call, not cached — matters because a render mid-drag replaces the SVG's own DOM node via `innerHTML`); new `symbolCellBounds()` reuses `resolveGridCells()`'s already-existing `cellW`/`cellH` for a per-cell axis-aligned box (a deliberate approximation for hexagon/polygon cells — their bounding box, not their true outline, same notion of "cell size" this feature already uses elsewhere); a plain AABB intersection test drives live selection updates as the drag proceeds, unioned with the selection already held at drag-start when Shift is down. The live marquee rectangle is appended in `renderSymbolCanvasOnly()` only (the same preview-only-overlay convention `buildGridOutlineSVG`/`buildSelectionOutlineSVG` already established) — never inside `buildSymbolSVG()`, which every export call reads directly, so it can't leak into a saved file. **Verification found the coordinate math correct and a first-pass test wrong, not the app**: an initial JS-simulated drag test produced an empty selection despite a large-looking marquee rect with a suspicious negative Y coordinate; traced to the test itself precomputing screen coordinates once from a stale CTM before the drag's own first `mousemove` triggered a re-render (which replaces the SVG DOM node) — recomputing the CTM fresh immediately before each dispatched event, exactly matching what a real continuous mouse drag does, produced correct results immediately. Verified properly from there: a full-canvas drag on the 7-cell Bento grid selected all 7 cells; a drag confined to the top-right selected only cell 1; a subsequent Shift+drag over the bottom-left added cell 5 without dropping cell 1; a drag over the left ~40% of the 17-cell Hexagonal (polygon) grid correctly selected the 10 cells whose bounding boxes actually intersect that band; no overlay ever opens from a drag release, matching the plan's own explicit rule that dragging builds a selection rather than committing content. `buildSymbolSVG()` (the real export path) confirmed to contain zero marquee/overlay markup after building up a selection and drag history in the same session. 0 of 4 overlay controls unaccessibly-named on a fresh tab (`Organica.autoLabelPanel`), zero console errors.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS Symbols: cell-overflow behavior defined — "Clip to cell" (default on), a bleed toggle, and a badge for the intentional-overflow case.** Diego asked to define what happens when a cell's content exceeds its own bounds, then to test it. Confirmed live first, before any code: a 720×360 Bento cell set to Fixed size 900 rendered a triangle covering three neighboring cells entirely — no clipping existed anywhere in `buildSymbolItems`/`resolveCellPlacement`/`buildSymbolSVG`/`drawSymbolCanvas`, in both preview and real export. Presented three options (clip always, allow-with-indicator, a toggle) via AskUserQuestion; Diego asked whether they could combine, and they do cleanly: **shipped all three as one design** — a per-Symbol **"Clip to cell"** checkbox, default **ON**, next to the existing "Show loaded grid" toggle. **Prototyped live in the browser first** (pure runtime JS against the running page, no file edits) before writing the plan — a `<clipPath>` per cell (`<rect>` for rect cells, `<polygon>` for polygon cells) reusing the *exact* geometry `buildSymbolSVG()` already computes for its invisible click hit-shape, confirmed clean on both the Bento (rect) and Hexagonal (polygon) grids with the same oversized-triangle repro — zero new geometry math needed, which is why the real implementation shipped in one pass with no surprises. **Shipped**: `state.symbolClipEnabled` (bool, Symbol-level, not per-cell); `buildSymbolSVG()` emits one `<clipPath id="clip-cell-N">` per cell into a new `<defs>` block and adds `clip-path="url(#clip-cell-N)"` to each cell's `<g>` when the toggle is on, omitting both when off (byte-for-byte pre-feature output); `drawSymbolCanvas()` mirrors it with `ctx.save()+ctx.clip()+ctx.restore()` wrapped around each cell's own draw calls, using the identical rect/polygon source data so preview/PNG and SVG export can't disagree. **The bleed case stays available and is now flagged, not silent**: `cellOverflowInfo(index)` reuses `resolveCellPlacement()`'s own already-computed `scaleX/scaleY` to check whether a cell's content footprint exceeds its own `cellW/cellH` (the same disclosed axis-aligned-footprint approximation `symbolCellBounds()` already uses elsewhere in this feature — rotation isn't accounted for precisely) — while the toggle is off, `renderCellPropertiesPanel()` shows a "⚠ Extends beyond cell bounds" row for a selected overflowing cell, hidden for a normal one, and not computed at all while clipping is on (the concept is moot once clipped). Persisted into saved Symbol snapshots (`buildSymbolLibraryEntry`/`applySymbolLibraryEntryToUI`/the library-thumbnail temporary-state-swap in `renderSymbolLibrary`) with a `clipEnabled !== false` backfill so Symbols saved before this feature still load and render exactly as they did (clip on, today's only real behaviour). `loadSymbolGrid()` resets the toggle to `true` for a freshly loaded/uploaded grid (a clean baseline, distinct from restoring a saved Symbol's own choice). Verified end to end: toggle-on crop confirmed identical to the live prototype on both grids, in both the DOM preview and the actual `buildSymbolSVG()` export string; toggle-off reproduces today's bleed exactly (regression-clean) with the badge showing only for the genuinely overflowing cell; toggling off→on→off leaves cell data byte-identical (only render/indicator behaviour changes); a save/load round-trip preserves `clipEnabled:false` exactly, and a hand-simulated pre-feature entry (field deleted entirely) backfills to `true` with no exception; 0 of 23 controls unaccessibly-named and zero console errors on a fresh tab.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — Genesis Creator: Mask/Container Type pills given real behavior via an attached "Underlying layer," plus a real browser rendering bug found and worked around.** Diego asked to work on Creator so an Element/Seed/Component could become a mask or a "total mask" (rendering the shape empty/transparent); scoped over several rounds of clarification down to a concrete, self-contained mechanism: "un element può essere una maschera di un altro element … lo stesso vale per i component." Explored first rather than assuming — found Creator's own **Type pills already had `mask`/`container` values** (alongside `asset`/`variant`) sitting in the UI since before this session, but **purely cosmetic**: read only by `genesis/library.html` to label tiles, with zero effect anywhere on render or save, confirmed by grepping the whole repo for `clipPath`/`mask`/`fill-rule` (the only real prior art found was FVS's own just-shipped "Clip to cell"). Confirmed design via several rounds: **Container** — the current shape gets an attached **underlying layer** (another shape picked from `organica.library.forms`, the same store/picker pattern as FVS's own Creator-library reader) shown **only inside** the shape's own outline; **Mask** — the same attached layer shown **everywhere except** inside the outline, a real hole, via an actual SVG `<mask>` element (not just `fill-rule`) — matching the original ask's "rendendo vuoto o trasparente la forma": the top shape's own flat fill disappears, it purely defines a boundary. The underlying layer inherits the shape's own transform (moves/scales/rotates together, no separate Appearance controls for it in this pass) and the **composited result bakes into the real saved SVG on Save** (`saveForm()` needed no changes at all — it already calls `formMarkup()`, which now returns the composite). Scoped explicitly to **Creator only this pass** — the confirmed Component-masks-Component analog lives in FVS's own Component/Symbol data model and is a distinct Phase 2, not built here. **A real, empirically-verified browser bug found mid-implementation, not assumed away**: the first working version rendered completely blank for Container — isolated with a minimal repro outside Creator's own code entirely (a bare `<clipPath><g><path/></g></clipPath>`, no transform at all) and confirmed **any `<g>` nested inside a `<clipPath>` renders the whole clip as empty** in this browser, even a transform-less wrapper — while the identical wrapping pattern inside a `<mask>` element rendered correctly. Fixed by moving `transform` onto the `<clipPath>` element itself instead of a nested `<g>` (geometrically identical, avoids the bug entirely) for the Container branch; the Mask branch was already safe since `<mask>` tolerates `<g>` wrapping fine, verified separately before relying on it. **Disclosed, not fixed, simplification**: this clipPath-transform-hoisting trick only works because Draw/Generate-mode geometry is a bare `<path>`/`<circle>` with no wrapping `<g>` of its own — an **Import-mode** shape's `geometry()` output IS itself a `<g>` (built by `importSVG()`), so picking an imported shape as a Container's own boundary would hit the identical browser bug; not fixed this pass, noted in the code as a known gap rather than silently broken. A second, separate disclosed simplification carried from the original design: the Mask case's forced `fill="#000"` on the boundary is a more-specific-attribute loser against an Import shape's own explicit `fill="var(--ink)"`, so an imported mask boundary isn't guaranteed a pure binary cutout (usually fine in practice since `--ink` is dark). New picker overlay (`#layer-picker-overlay`) reuses the exact tile-preview visual pattern FVS's own Choose-content overlay established, ported to Creator's own CSS tokens (`--ink-muted`/`--border` etc., not FVS's `--mid`/`--panel`). `renderTransformPreview()`'s 7-tile strip was refactored onto the same shared `compositeMarkup(tf, idSuffix)` function `formMarkup()` calls (an `idSuffix` per tile avoids duplicate `id`/`url(#...)` collisions across the 7 simultaneously-rendered mini-SVGs) — deliberately reusing the exact discipline the file's own `styleAttrs()` comment already documents ("one function means the two can't drift apart the same way again"), since the preview strip had silently ignored the Style toggle once before. Verified end to end: Container clips a picked layer exactly to a star's outline (screenshot-confirmed); Mask punches a real star-shaped hole through the same circle (screenshot-confirmed); Rotation 45°/Scale 0.6 moves the boundary and the underlying layer together as one rigid unit (both share the identical `rotate(45)` in the live markup); Remove reverts to plain flat fill with the Type pill itself untouched; a real Save produces a library entry whose own `.svg` string already contains the `<clipPath>` composite baked in, not just the bare shape; switching back to Asset hides the panel and reverts rendering to plain fill even with a layer still attached in state (dead until Mask/Container is reselected, not silently applied); 0 of 29 controls unaccessibly-named (including the new picker overlay's own tiles/buttons); zero console errors on a fresh tab.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — FVS: Phase 2 of the mask/container work — one saved Component can now mask/contain another.** Diego's own priority call: build the confirmed FVS analog ("lo stesso vale per i component") before committing anything from this session, rather than leaving it as a disclosed follow-up. New **Role** panel section in the Components tab (Normal/Container/Mask select, mirroring Creator's own pills) + "Pick underlying component…" (shown only for Container/Mask, the same dead-control rule this project applies everywhere) opening a tile picker of the shared Component Library. Applies uniformly to **every generated gallery candidate** (not just the selected one) and bakes into Save — `buildLibraryEntry()` gained `role`/`underlyingComponentName`, round-tripped through `applyLibraryEntryToUI()` with a `role || 'normal'` backfill for pre-feature saves. **A harder problem than Creator's own version**: a Component's cells are inherently multi-shape and always `<g>`-wrapped (per-cell rotation/scale), so the exact browser bug found earlier the same day (`<g>` nested inside `<clipPath>` renders empty) would hit basically every real Component, not just an Import-mode edge case. Fixed for Container by **flattening**: `componentBoundaryClipContent()` consolidates each cell's two transform levels into ONE `transform` attribute on a bare `<path>` (no `<g>` at all), and `<clipPath>` accepts multiple such direct-child paths, unioning them automatically per spec — geometrically identical to the normal per-cell markup, just restructured to dodge the bug. Mask's own boundary (`componentBoundaryMaskContent()`) keeps the normal nested-`<g>` structure unchanged, since `<mask>` already confirmed tolerant of it. **A second, real bug caught by testing, not assumed correct from the SVG structure alone**: the first Mask version painted this Component's own Paper rect *outside* the masked group (as the tool's usual unconditional background), so the "hole" only erased the underlying Component's own content and still showed opaque paper beneath — not a real hole. Fixed by moving the Paper rect *inside* the same masked `<g>` for the Mask case specifically (Container keeps the ordinary unconditional Paper rect, since its boundary only ever covers its own small item area) — verified directly by sampling raw pixel alpha, not just eyeballing: a point inside the boundary silhouette reads `[0,0,0,0]` (true alpha-zero) both in the live SVG markup structure and via a real `drawComponentCanvas()` render read back with `getImageData`, while a point outside reads the underlying Component's own opaque colour. **Canvas2D needed no such workaround** — it has no clipPath-style bug at all, since it never parses markup: `drawComponentCanvas()` builds ONE combined `Path2D` unioning every cell's own transformed outline (`Path2D.addPath` + a per-cell `DOMMatrix`), then either `ctx.clip()`s it (Container) or erases it via `globalCompositeOperation:'destination-out'` (Mask) — the same real-alpha-knockout mechanism confirmed for Creator, now proven consistent in both this tool's rendering backends. Underlying Component fits via a plain uniform `scale(size/under.size)` from the origin (both frames are square, so this covers corner-to-corner with no centring math needed). Deliberately **does not recurse** into the underlying entry's own role/underlying (even a literal self-reference just renders that entry's plain cells once) — makes runaway recursion structurally impossible rather than merely guarded, a disclosed simplification. `buildComponentSVGWithPaper()` (already the established "render a saved entry's own values, not the live ones" wrapper for Paper colour) extended to also swap `componentRole`/`underlyingComponentName`, so Library thumbnails and the Symbols tier's own "Choose content" tile previews render the correct composite automatically. **Explicitly still Phase 3, not built here**: a masked/container Component nested as a *Symbol cell's* own content — Symbols' `buildSymbolItems()`/`buildSymbolSVG()`/`drawSymbolCanvas()` use their own specialized nested-rendering path (`nestedItems`/`resolveCellPlacement`) distinct from `buildComponentSVG`/`drawComponentCanvas`, so wiring the same effect there is a separate, disclosed follow-up. Verified end to end: Container clips a real underlying Component (a distinctly-coloured Arc/Radial) to every one of 8 generated Pinwheel-triangle candidates uniformly (screenshot-confirmed, using a deliberately different colour after the first pass's same-default-black test data made the effect invisible by coincidence — a real lesson about picking diagnostic test data, not a bug); Mask shows a genuine alpha-zero hole confirmed by direct pixel sampling in both SVG and Canvas2D; Remove reverts cleanly with Role itself untouched; a real Save produces a Library entry whose own thumbnail already renders the composite; loading that entry back via `applyLibraryEntryToUI()` restores Role/underlying and the select/panel state exactly; 0 of 40 controls unaccessibly-named; zero console errors on a fresh tab.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — "Element" renamed to "Seed" product-wide, plus a real parametric-seed mechanism proven with Arc.** Diego wants the authored-shape building block — "Element" in FVS, "Form"/"shape" in Genesis Creator/Library — called only **Seed**, everywhere that concept appears. Audited the whole repo first: the concept is concentrated in three files (FVS's Element panel, Creator's "Form" panel + Type-pill system, Library's "Form" panel + "Basic Shapes" set), while OTHER "shape" mentions elsewhere are genuinely different concepts and were explicitly confirmed OUT of scope — Komorebi/Camo Turing's scatter-mask shapes, Halide/Strata's traced raster regions, Soul's parsed SVG primitives, Loom's grid-cell shape. **A real, deliberate exclusion found during implementation, not assumed away**: Membrane's own "Seed shape" control (Circle/Line/Point — the starting point-set shape for its particle sim) uses "seed" for a completely unrelated, pre-existing concept (a simulation's initial configuration, same idea as "random seed") that predates and has nothing to do with the Genesis-authored-shape concept — left untouched rather than blindly renamed just because the word matched. **Full rename, confirmed explicitly by Diego** (test phase, no real user data to protect): persisted JSON field names (`entry.element`→`entry.seed`, `cell.elementType`→`cell.seedType`, etc.) and internal identifiers (`ELEMENT_TYPES`→`SEED_TYPES`, `extractElementFromSVG`→`extractSeedFromSVG`, etc.) renamed too, not just UI labels — done via a Python find-replace on `fvs/index.html` (its own single biggest concentration of the concept) with the 4 real DOM API names that happen to contain "Element" (`createElement`, `createElementNS`, `getElementById`, `SVGPathElement`) protected first via placeholder swap. **A genuine implementation bug caught immediately, not shipped**: the first placeholder attempt used tokens like `@@CREATEELEMENTNS@@`, which themselves contain the substring "ELEMENT" — the very next replace pass (`'ELEMENT'→'SEED'`) mangled the protection tokens before they could be restored, silently corrupting every `createElementNS`/`createElement`/`getElementById` call into garbage (`ZZZ`-style placeholders left in the shipped file). Caught by grepping for the DOM API names afterward and finding zero matches instead of the expected ~20 — restored from a pre-edit backup and re-ran with placeholder tokens that share no substring with "element" in any case, verified clean on the second pass. Deliberately kept unchanged: the SHARED `organica.library.forms` wire format itself (its own `.svg`/`.forms`/`.sets`/`.type` property names and the storage key) — multiple already-shipped tools outside this pass's file list (Soul, Camo Turing, Loom) read that exact shape, and CLAUDE.md's own standing rule already treats localStorage keys as sacred; only `fvs/index.html`'s OWN component/symbol schema (which only FVS itself reads) got the full field rename. **A second real bug found while regression-testing the rename, not before**: `renderLibrary()` threw on the very first fresh load — traced to stale pre-rename Component Library data left in `localStorage` from earlier in this session (old entries still shaped `{element: ...}`, new code reading `entry.seed`); confirmed as a stale-test-data artifact rather than a real code bug by testing against a genuinely empty library first, then clearing the leftover keys per Diego's own "test phase, no migration needed" ruling. **Parametric seeds**: rather than inventing a separate parametric-entry schema, extended Creator's existing Generate mode (already "adjust live before saving") into "adjust live, saved, AND still adjustable after loading back" — Arc added as Generate mode's 4th type (`arcPathD(thicknessPct)` ported verbatim from `fvs/index.html`, 2×-scaled for Creator's 200-unit box vs. FVS's own 100-unit one,
