verified pixel-for-pixel identical at 25/50/100% thickness), `saveForm()` now persists `genType`/`genParams` alongside the baked `svg` (every `.svg`-only consumer elsewhere is unaffected, purely additive), and a new **"Load seed…"** picker in Creator's Source panel restores a saved seed's live Generate-mode controls exactly as saved (verified: reset to Circle, loaded a saved Arc-at-65%-thickness back, mode/type/slider-value/DOM-slider all restored correctly and still draggable). `genesis/library.html` got the matching live parameter slider inline for any selected seed carrying `genType` — dragging it regenerates that entry's own `svg`/`genParams` immediately (verified: tile updates live). **A wrong initial assumption, caught and corrected rather than left as a false claim**: expected Basic Seeds' entries to reset to their shipped defaults on reload (matching the plan's own "built-in protection" framing) — measured instead that edits persisted across a reload, then traced why: `basicShapeForms()`'s entries (now `basicSeedForms()`) never actually set `builtIn: true` at the per-form level in the ORIGINAL pre-existing design either (only the separate "Organic Forms" catalog's own entries are truly read-only) — so Basic Seeds were already meant to be a freely-editable starting-point set, and persisting Arc's own thickness edits in place is consistent with how Square/Circle/etc. could already have their Name/Type edited before this session, not a new gap. Renamed "Basic Shapes"→"**Basic Seeds**" (`BASIC_SHAPES_SET_ID`→`BASIC_SEEDS_SET_ID`) in both `creator.html` and `library.html` (kept in sync per this project's own documented dual-copy convention), with the set's own Arc entry now the parametric one instead of the old fixed quarter-disc. Verified end to end: fresh-tab loads clean (zero console errors) on FVS/Creator/Library/Camo Turing; FVS's full regression re-run post-rename (Components tier Normal/Container/Mask, Symbols tier Choose-content overlay's "Seeds"/"Components" tiles, Save/Load, `buildSymbolSVG()` export) all functionally unchanged; 0 unaccessibly-named controls on every new picker/slider. **Light touch, not a rewrite**: Camo Turing's existing "seed shape" hint phrasing (4 hint paragraphs + 2 aria-labels, all genuinely about a Genesis-sourced shape) tightened to plain "Seed"; nothing else in that file touched.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — Creator's Generate-mode "Kind" control became a thumbnail dropdown, reusing an existing shared component instead of building a new one.** Diego asked for the Circle/Poly/Star/Arc picker to become a dropdown with thumbnails, styled per the design system. Checked first rather than building blind: `shared/panel.css` + `shared/transformer.js`'s `Organica.Transformer.buildPicker` already implement exactly this — a `.preset-trigger`/`.preset-menu` thumbnail dropdown, built for Camo Turing/Living Path's own preset pickers and never yet used in Creator. Reused it wholesale rather than inventing a parallel component: linked `panel.css` (verified its shared class names collide with several of Creator's own local ones — `.panel-row`/`.panel-label`/`.seg`/etc. — but Creator's own `<style>` block loads after the `<link>`, so the existing look is unaffected, confirmed live) and `transformer.js` (after `core.js`, which it extends). Replaced `#gen-seg`'s 4 plain segmented buttons with an empty `.presets` host + `buildPicker(host, GEN_KIND_REGISTRY, {...})`, `GEN_KIND_REGISTRY` supplying one small representative SVG silhouette per kind (filled circle/pentagon/star/quarter-arc). **One real, disclosed extension to the shared component**: `buildPicker` always prepended an empty `''`/"None" entry, which makes no sense for a control that must always have a real value — added an opt-in `opts.noNone` flag (default `false`, so every OTHER existing caller of this shared function is unaffected) rather than special-casing it locally. Verified end to end: dropdown opens with all 4 thumbnails and no None option, selecting Star updates the trigger face + regenerates the shape + still correctly shows the Sides row (`syncGenerator()` untouched); the "Load seed…" picker's restore path now calls the new picker's own `setActive()` instead of hand-toggling `.seg-btn.active` classes, verified the trigger face updates correctly on a loaded Arc seed too; zero console errors, `aria-label="Generate kind"` reachable via `Organica.autoLabelPanel`.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — Creator's Generate mode: 3 enrichments to existing Kinds + 5 new Kinds, from an explicit "analyse first" ask.** Diego asked for an analysis of what parameters could enrich Circle/Poly/Star/Arc and what other shape types were worth adding, then confirmed a large, concrete scope across a few rounds (including a "fundamental geometric shapes" reference he shared mid-analysis, which settled Triangle/Square/Segment as real additions and Point as a deliberate non-addition, redundant with Circle at a small radius). **Enrichments**: Poly gained **Corner rounding** (0–50%, standard cut-corner-then-quadratic-bezier technique) and **Irregularity** (0–100%, seeded per-vertex radius jitter via a `mulberry32` PRNG — same reproducible-seed discipline as FVS/Vortex/Camo Turing); Star gained a real **Inner radius** slider (10–90%, replacing a hardcoded `r*0.45` — verified the new default of 45% reproduces the exact old geometry, distance-checked not just eyeballed); Arc was generalized from a fixed 90°-corner-anchored quarter-shape into a real **wedge/pie/ring generator** via **Sweep** (10–350°) + **Pivot** (Corner/Center), built as one `wedgePathD(cx,cy,outerR,startDeg,sweepDeg,innerR)` function — **verified algebraically and then empirically that the old fixed formula is an exact special case** (`cx=cy=0, outerR=200, startDeg=0, sweepDeg=90`), confirmed via a coordinate-array diff (not string equality, which failed only on decimal-formatting noise, correctly diagnosed as a test artifact rather than a regression) — so every already-saved Arc seed still renders identically. **5 new Kinds**: Triangle (Base/Height, the FVS Isosceles-triangle convention — genuinely distinct from Poly's forced-equilateral n=3), Square (Width/Height + the same Corner-rounding function as Poly, proving the shared-rounding design), Segment (an open stroked line — picking it auto-switches Style to Stroke as a helpful default, not a hard lock), Drop mark (an analytic teardrop — 2 cubic Béziers + a semicircle arc — disclosed as a faithful *visual* port of Komorebi's own `shapeDrop`, not a literal formula port, since that original is a per-pixel raster SDF and Generate mode needs real vector paths), Organic blob (adapted from `shared/motion.js`'s existing `organicBlobPath`, ported directly rather than adding a script dependency on Soul's own motion file — 32 points around a circle offset by `Organica.noise.simplex2` sampled on a unit circle around the seed, avoiding the polar-pole artifact a naive angle-based sampling would hit; needed `shared/noise.js` newly linked into Creator, after `core.js`). **Architecture stayed disciplined**: no new mechanism invented — every addition slots into the existing `S.gen`/`geometry()`/`syncGenerator()`/thumbnail-picker model already proven this session for Arc. Two real refactors done *because* the kind count grew to 9: `saveForm()`'s genParams serialization switched from a per-kind hand-picked field list to saving the whole `S.gen` bag (disclosed as a few harmless extra bytes traded for not needing a code change every time a future kind gains a param), and `loadSeedIntoWorkingShape()` now resets `S.gen` to a shared `DEFAULT_GEN` baseline before merging a saved recipe on top — **a real latent bug caught by design, not by testing**: without the reset, loading a plain Circle right after tuning Poly's Irregularity would have silently left the stale Irregularity value sitting in `S.gen`. Verified end to end: all 9 Kind tiles show distinct icons; Arc's three regimes (default quarter, Center+Sweep~350°+low Thickness = a real ring, Center+Sweep 45° = a clean pie wedge) all screenshot-confirmed; Poly's Corner rounding and Irregularity+Seed reproducibility (same seed twice → byte-identical path) verified; Drop mark degrades gracefully (no NaN) at Tail=0; Blob's Amount=0 baseline and seed-reproducibility confirmed (one earlier "not reproducible" result was traced to a test-script race reading the DOM before a slider's own `input` event had fully applied, not a real bug — confirmed by re-running the identical sequence cleanly); save→reset-to-Circle→"Load seed…" round-trip verified for a saved Drop mark, restoring the exact Tail value with a live, draggable slider; 0 of 28 controls unaccessibly-named after cycling through every Kind; zero console errors on a fresh tab.

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — Hub redesigned (light theme, single left-hand nav column) + the cream "paper" background swapped to white product-wide.** Two related but distinct asks. **Hub (`index.html`)**: rebuilt from a centred dark hero (giant "ORGANICA" wordmark, all-caps wrapped nav, `--bg:#0a0a08`) into a light shell — white background, the same organic-noise canvas animation kept but recoloured (darker, more saturated strokes at higher opacity) so it still reads against white instead of near-black; "Organica" now appears exactly once, small, top-left in a real header; every tool link moved into one vertical column, left-aligned, smaller font, natural Title Case (removed `text-transform:uppercase`, the HTML text was already correctly cased); the centre of the page is deliberately left empty — reserved for a future "displays" feature, not built this pass. Per-tool hover accent colours all carried over, individually darkened to hold contrast against the new white page (a color tuned to be visible on near-black doesn't survive the same brightness on white unchanged). **Product-wide background**: separately, replaced the cream/parchment "Pollen palette" tone (`#f5f2ec` and its handful of near-identical variants — `#f1f1ef` in `genesis/index.html`, `#f4f1ea` in `genesis/indicators.html`, a stray `#f5f2ed` second declaration in `strata/index.html`) with pure white (`#ffffff`) across every tool's own `--bg`/`--paper` token — 19 files total (all live tools, `shared/_template.html`, `design-system/index.html` including its own documentation code-sample, and the `genesis/_test-paper-draw.html` prototype). **Deliberately scoped narrowly, disclosed rather than silently expanded**: only the page/paper background token changed — `--surface`/`--panel` (the secondary panel/sidebar tone, a visually distinct grey-beige already) and `genesis/indicators.html`'s own dark/warm/cool theme-picker alternates were left untouched, since neither was what "questo giallino" referred to and changing them wasn't asked. Verified live across the hub, Creator, FVS, and Loom: white page background with the existing grey panel/canvas tones still providing visual hierarchy, zero console errors, no contrast regressions (dark text against a lighter background than before, if anything, gains contrast rather than losing it).

- **August 26, 2026 (still later same day) — Strata removed entirely; four-part maintenance pass (removal + bento bug fix + Design System refresh + cross-tool drift fixes).** Diego's call to remove Strata stood independent of a direct finding that its production pipeline actually worked (`/api/health` → 200, `/api/trace` → 200 with a real non-degenerate SVG on a repo test photo) — confirmed explicitly ("Non importa perché vogliamo comunque eliminarlo") rather than assumed. Deleted `strata/`, `api/` (the Vercel Python functions), `backend/` (the whole trace pipeline, its own `venv/`/`test-photos/`/`output/` — all gitignored, cleaned via `rm -rf`), root `requirements.txt`, `docs/STRATA-REFINE.md`; removed the hub nav link + its CSS, `vercel.json`'s `/strata/` rewrite and its now-orphaned `functions` block, and `.claude/launch.json`'s `strata-backend` entry. This file's own Tools-table row, Repo Structure entries (`strata/` and `backend/`, the latter now nonexistent too), and the `strata/`/`backend/` "do not touch" rules are removed here; Phase 5 of the Roadmap above (was "Strata AI") is retired. Passing mentions across `docs/VISION.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/UI-SHELL.md`, `docs/KOMOREBI.md`, `docs/LOOM.md`, `docs/SOUL.md`, `docs/CAMOUFLAGE.md` adjusted; dated historical session-note prose (including in this file) was deliberately left alone as record, not live instruction. **Bento bug**: forms 52/53 on the hub rendered with a zero-size bounding box (`getBBox()` confirmed `{w:0,h:0}`) because they `<use href="#cA">`-reference Genesis's own "terrazzo chip" defs, never copied into the hub's inline `<defs>` when form 35 was fixed earlier — fixed by copying the 7 `<path id="cA">`…`<path id="cG">` defs verbatim from `genesis/indicators.html`; both forms now report real non-zero bboxes. **Design System refresh**: `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`'s accent table gained the 9 tools shipped since July 25 (Camo Turing/Warping/Loom/Soul/Membrane/Vortex/FVS/TuneSutra/Mycel) and lost Strata's row; added a "Hub nav categories" table (the real functional groups, including "Coloring & palette" — previously undocumented); de-staled the §6 accessibility-audit language to name its July 26 date instead of reading as a current claim; date stamps updated. `design-system/index.html`'s three "Used in" component tags (Panel, header `--editor` variant, Floatbar) corrected to the current tool list. `shared/tokens.json` lost its `strata` accent entry and gained the same 9 new tools' hex values, verified as valid JSON after editing. **Cross-tool drift fixes** (confirmed explicitly — "Includili ora" — rather than deferred): **(D1)** `mulberry32` consolidated into `Organica.mulberry32` (`shared/core.js`), 6 independent copies (FVS, Living Path, Mycel, Camo Turing, Genesis Creator, Vortex) now delegate to it — Genesis Creator's own copy had a subtle correctness gap (`t += …` vs `t = (t + …) | 0`) fixed as a side effect; Loom's `js/generators/*.js` deliberately excluded, since their own documented zero-coupling architecture is intentional, not drift. **(D2)** The RMX/palette-chip pattern consolidated into `shared/organica-palette-chip.js` (`Organica.createPaletteChips`) — 6 tools retrofitted (Camo Turing, Membrane, Vortex, FVS, Pollen, Spore), each verified live via real add/remove/recolour DOM interaction, each tool's own `min`/`max` preserved. **(D3)** Inline hex-validation regexes replaced with `Organica.normalizeHex` in Spore/Pollen/Halide/Camo Turing — Halide and Pollen's hex text fields moved from a live per-keystroke `oninput` guard to `onchange` (matching Komorebi/Warping's own established convention: commit-time validation with fallback to the previous valid value, verified live in all 4 tools). **(D4)** Camo Turing's private scale-only zoom (`zoomLevel`, wheel + dblclick, no pan) migrated to the shared `Organica.createZoomPan` — gained drag-pan as a side effect of the shared implementation; the click-to-reseed handler needed no change since `getBoundingClientRect()` already reflects the transform either way; verified live (wheel-zoom, badge, drag-pan, dblclick-reset). **(D5)** Warping's Export UI moved from the header into the shared bottom-centre `.org-floatbar` (added the missing `floatbar.css` link), matching FVS/Loom/Camo Turing's own convention — no `→ Figma` header button (product-wide pattern: `sendToFigma()` stays defined, ready for a future Export-popover tab); all 3 export formats verified firing correctly from the new location. **(D6)** Removed the dead duplicate `.panel-section` CSS override in `warping/index.html` and `loom/index.html` (both silently redeclared the identical shared value, `--space-5` = `--panel-pad` = 14px — confirmed identical before deleting, zero visual diff); `docs/UI-SHELL.md` gained an `organica-palette-chip.js` mention alongside the other required/optional includes. Every touched tool re-verified on a fresh tab: zero console errors, correct behaviour exercised through actual DOM interaction (not just "no error"), consistent with this project's own repeated lesson that a clean console only proves something if the exact interaction was actually triggered.

- **August 27, 2026 — Shipped Rhizome (`/rhizome/`), a node-based visual workflow canvas chaining the other Organica tools as pipeline stages.** Preceded by a very detailed analysis (6 parallel research passes: what's already reusable — `Organica.loadLoomGrid` as the one genuine cross-tool JSON contract, `Organica.motion.parsePrimitives`, `Organica.createZoomPan`, `Organica.traceContours` — vs. what's genuinely absent — zero DAG, zero live wire-follows-drag, zero typed ports, the Figma bridge confirmed silently dead since at least two prior dated session notes; plus a full categorized catalog of every tool's own INPUT/ENGINE/TRANSFORM/EFFECT/MOTION/OUTPUT functions) written up as the project's approved plan before any code. Named **Rhizome** (Diego's own pick from a shortlist — botanical connective-network metaphor, distinct from Mycel's fungal-network domain). **Phase 0**: fixed the dead Figma bridge — `figma-plugin/code.js`'s `onmessage` never listened for `'organica-svg'` (only `'import-svg'`/`'import-regions'`), silently breaking "→ Figma" on every tool using `Organica.sendToFigma` (Spore/Pollen/Halide/Komorebi/Loom/Camo Turing/Warping) since it shipped; one-line alias fix, benefits all seven tools immediately. **Phase 1 MVP** (built after a standalone `_test-mvp.html` prototype validated the interaction mechanics first, same "prototype in isolation before real architecture" discipline as Loom's own `_test-*.html` pages): a two-tier node model — Tier 1 native (zero-porting wrappers on already-pure functions: Loom's `generateBento`/`generateHexagonal` imported as real ES modules, `Organica.loadLoomGrid`, `Organica.traceContours`) vs. Tier 2 bridge (a hidden sandboxed iframe loading the real tool page + a new `rhizome-set-input`/`rhizome-output-ready` postMessage protocol, ~20-30 lines added to each bridged tool's own file) — chosen over porting every tool's algorithm natively specifically to avoid a maintenance-drift risk (a ported copy silently diverging from the real tool over time) for a solo-designer tool where iframe round-trip latency doesn't matter. Typed ports (`svg`/`image`/`grid`/`color`/`number`/`points`) with exactly 3 disclosed auto-adapters (`svg→points`, `grid→points`, `grid→svg` — each a direct port of what a real tool already does by hand, no speculative conversion matrix). Execution engine: Kahn's-algorithm topological sort (throws a readable error on a cycle rather than hanging), per-node dirty-flag caching. Canvas: per-node drag (extends FVS's own threshold-drag technique, but is the first place in the repo that actually *writes* a dragged object's new position back to state), live wire-follow (extends Mycel's own curve math, made interactive), and a new `panAlways` opt-in added to `Organica.createZoomPan` itself (opt-in, zero behaviour change for the 6+ existing callers) since node-graph canvases need to pan at any zoom level, unlike every other tool's own image/canvas zoom. Shipped with 6 Tier-1 nodes (Loom Grid — 5 generators after Phase 2 below, Loom Grid→Geometry, Contour Trace, SVG→Points, Merge, Export with PNG/SVG/→Figma) + 1 Tier-2 proof of concept (Genesis Seed). **Real bugs found and fixed during live verification** (not just "no console error" — every mechanic exercised through actual dispatched DOM events): a leftover `position:absolute` CSS rule overlapping the floatbar's two buttons; the single most serious one — `bindPortInteractions` attached its connect-drag listeners by iterating `portEls` ONCE at boot, when the map was still empty, so **zero nodes added after page load ever had a working connect gesture at all** — fixed with event delegation on the graph container instead; a missing `stopImmediatePropagation` that let a port-drag also start a canvas pan simultaneously (both bound to the same element); the Genesis bridge sending `formMarkup()`'s bare `<g>` fragment instead of a wrapped standalone `<svg>` document, which silently rendered as escaped text instead of a shape. **Phase 2** (scoped to Camo Turing plus the lower-risk bridges per Diego's own sequencing, Membrane deferred): added 3 more Loom generators (triangular/diamond/circular — same param shape as hexagonal, zero-cost integration) to the Loom Grid node; shipped Komorebi Pattern and Warping Pattern bridges (both simple pattern-select + `buildSVG()`, no seed-loading complexity); shipped Soul (pass-through) — the first bridge node with a real upstream SVG *input*, not just a source; **found and fixed a second real race-condition bug**: two `recompute()` calls firing close together (e.g. add-node immediately followed by connect) each create/reuse the same per-node bridge iframe, and the second call's response-listener registration silently overwrote the first's in `bridge-iframe.js`'s `pending` map — the first call's `await` never resolved, hanging that whole `runGraph()` invocation forever with no error surfaced; reproduced deliberately in isolation before fixing, fixed by serializing `recompute()` (a later call while one is in flight just re-runs once more after, instead of running concurrently); re-verified against the exact original failing sequence. Also fixed a real `prompt()`-based Save button (same failure class already fixed in FVS in August — `prompt()` throws in some embedded contexts) with an inline text field, matching every other tool's own established convention. **Camo Turing bridge** (the flagged-risk one, tackled last, on its own per Diego's request): confirmed directly by reading the file that Camo Turing's Gray-Scott core has **no convergence/auto-pause detection at all** (its own comment: "no auto-stop — Camo Turing has no convergence/auto-pause detection to race against, unlike Camouflage," its removed predecessor) — so the bridge runs a disclosed, tunable fixed step count (`stepSim()` called directly in a synchronous loop, bypassing the rAF loop entirely so it can't be interleaved) rather than a true "wait until settled," exposed as the node's own `steps` param instead of a hidden guess. Reuses `applyPreset()` (all 23 built-in presets, including the 3 anisotropic ones) + `resetSim()` + the existing `exportSVG()`'s own pause-required guard. Bridge timeout raised from 8s to 15s specifically to give this one genuinely slow bridge room (every other bridge responds in well under a second). Verified live: Leopard spots and Tiger stripes (anisotropic) both render correctly and distinctly, a full Camo Turing→Export chain produces a real 354KB SVG with genuine path geometry and embedded `<metadata>` (f/k/engine info). **Phase 3**: added Rhizome to the hub nav (a new "Workflow & pipelines" category — it didn't fit any existing functional group) and `vercel.json`'s rewrite list, both real gaps (the tool was fully built but unreachable from the hub until now). Final state: **11 node types**, 0 of ~40 controls unaccessibly-named across all of them (checked per-type, not sampled), zero real console errors on every fresh-tab check throughout (only the expected iframe-sandbox and canvas-readback browser warnings). **Not yet done, disclosed rather than silently skipped**: Membrane bridge (generative accumulation over time, same class of complexity as Camo Turing, deferred past this session), graph preset picker UI beyond the raw localStorage save/load, adapter expansion (deliberately not speculative — only added when a real need shows up in use). Manual/docs beyond this session note and the plan file itself not yet written.

- **August 27, 2026 (later same day) — Rhizome Phase 4: 5 more bridges (Membrane, Living Path, Spore, Pollen, Halide), undo/redo, multi-select/marquee-select, variadic Merge inputs, and `docs/RHIZOME.md`.** Scoped precisely by Diego's own explicit list, deliberately excluding Vortex/TuneSutra/Mycel from the broader Phase-3-remainder options ("fai solo Living Path, Spore, Pollen, Halide"). **Membrane bridge**: the module-scoped (`<script type="module">`) case — unlike every classic-script tool, Membrane's `main.js` exposes nothing on `window` automatically, so its listener lives inside the module itself using already-imported bindings (`state`, `reseedLinear`, `buildExportSVGString`); sets `state.movementPattern`/resets `state.moveClock`/unfreezes, waits a real `seconds`-driven delay (up to 10s — the reason the shared bridge timeout was raised from 15s to 20s), then exports. Verified live: real `<metadata>{"tool":"Membrane"...}` SVG with a genuine visible orbit trail. **Living Path bridge**: 29 presets (5 vector + 24 raster) collapsed into one flat `tech:name`-prefixed dropdown, since Rhizome's param system has no cross-param-reactive dropdown support to show a second "which engine" selector; seeds from a Genesis form (id 38 default), switches engine, applies the preset, waits a flat 150ms (covers raster's real 110ms debounce, harmless no-op for vector's synchronous render). Verified live: `raster:Coral` and `vector:Shatter` both produced genuinely distinct, correct-looking output. **The critical bug, found once and fixed identically in 3 places**: Spore/Pollen/Halide all gate rendering on `loadImage(input)`, whose `img.onload` is asynchronous even for `data:` URLs — a poll loop checking the tool's own render-completion signal immediately after calling `loadImage()` sees the signal's still-pre-render state and exits on the very first check, returning stale/empty output with zero console error (found by reproducing, not assumed: Spore Stipple's first connect+wait test showed an empty `—` preview). Root-caused by reading all 3 tools' actual `loadImage` source rather than guessing. Fixed with an identical **two-phase wait** in all three: first poll `#drop-hint.classList.contains('hidden')` (each tool's own reliable "image genuinely finished decoding" signal, set inside its own `onload`), then poll the tool's own real render-completion flag (`#btn-save-svg.disabled` for Spore, `#btn-stop.disabled` for Pollen, the equivalent gate for Halide). Verified live post-fix, all three via a fake-file-upload test harness (constructed `File`→`DataTransfer`→dispatched `change`, since each tool's `sourceImage` is a private non-window-scoped `let`): **Spore** produced real stipple dots, denser in the darker regions of a synthetic gradient test image; **Pollen** produced a real Hatch-Flow line-stipple pattern correctly oriented/densified around a dark test circle; **Halide** (entirely untested before this pass) produced genuine Ditherface-preset output — background removed, outlined, visible Atkinson dither texture — confirmed via connect→wait→screenshot→fresh-tab-console-check, the same four-step discipline applied to every other bridge. **Variadic Merge**: `meta.inputs` was a static array on every node type; Merge now exports `getInputs(node)` (computing `in0..inN` from `params.inputCount`, 1–6), and a new `getNodeInputs(node)` dispatcher in `node-registry.js` is what every consumer (execution engine, `node-card.js`'s port-row builder, main.js's connect-time checks) reads instead of `meta.inputs` directly. `node-card.js`'s port rows became a standalone rebuildable `buildPortRows()` so changing `inputCount` rebuilds just the ports, not the whole card; edges pointing at a since-dropped port are pruned in main.js. Verified: dragging `inputCount` 2→4 live-rebuilt the port rows to `in0..in3`, connecting a real seed to `in0` produced correct composited SVG output. **Multi-select/marquee**: `canvas/selection.js`, deliberately gated on **Shift+drag** rather than plain drag, since plain-drag is already the canvas pan gesture (`Organica.createZoomPan`'s own `panAlways` option from Phase 1) — a capture-phase `mousedown` listener with `stopImmediatePropagation()` intercepts Shift+drag before the pan handler ever sees it. `bindNodeDrag` gained a `getDragGroup(node)` callback so dragging one selected node moves the whole multi-selection together. Verified live: Shift+drag over two spread-apart nodes selected both (dashed `--tool`-colour outlines, count confirmed at 2). **Undo/redo**: `history.js`, a plain JSON-snapshot stack over the canonical `{nodes,edges}` model — pushed only at real checkpoints (add/remove node, connect/disconnect, drag-**end** not every tick, param-**commit** via a slider's `change` event not its `input` event) so continuous interaction never floods the stack. **Real bug caught by testing the full cycle, not assumed working**: `updateHistoryButtons()` was only ever called from `commitHistory()` (new pushes), never from the Undo/Redo button click handlers or the keyboard-shortcut handler — so after undoing back to the empty baseline, the Undo button still read enabled; fixed by calling it from both button handlers and the keydown handler too, re-verified through a full add→undo→redo cycle toggling correctly at every step. A second real bug in the same area: Image Upload's preview rendered `[object Object]` for an `{dataURL}`-shaped value, since `guessValueType()` had no `image` branch — fixed by adding one to both the type-guesser and `node-card.js`'s `renderPreview` (a real `<img>` thumbnail). **Final verification**: 0 of 27 controls unaccessibly-named on the base 3-node graph, 0 of 3 on the Merge inspector (`inputCount`/`offsetXStep`/`offsetYStep`), zero console errors confirmed on a genuinely fresh tab (not just the working tab's own possibly-stale buffer, the discipline this project has needed repeatedly). Manual: `docs/RHIZOME.md`, covering the two-tier model, the full 17-node registry, the async-image-load bug writeup, the variadic-port dispatcher pattern, and what's still deferred (Vortex/TuneSutra/Mycel bridges, a real thumbnail preset picker, further adapter expansion, the still-open product-wide Figma-plugin gap for tools other than what Phase 0 already fixed).

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Hub bento showcase rebuilt as a masonry gallery, modelled on giuseppelaezza.com/playground per Diego's direct reference.** Inspected the reference live (screenshot + computed-style/DOM queries, not assumed from memory): CSS multi-column masonry (`columns:3`, `column-gap:10px`), full-bleed saturated cards (`border-radius:15px`), an always-visible top-left name pill, and a blur(8px)→none load reveal. Confirmed scope and two deliberate deviations with Diego via AskUserQuestion before touching code: **(1)** stay on Organica's own token scale (`--radius-lg` 8px, `--space-*`) rather than the reference's raw 15px — no new token invented; **(2)** cards go full-bleed (the SVG motif fills/bleeds the whole card on a saturated per-tool background) rather than staying a small centred icon; **(3)** scope is the bento section only, the left sidebar nav stays untouched. Replaced the old fixed 8×100px span-grid (`size-lg/wide/tall/sm` → `grid-column/row: span N`) with `columns: 4 220px` masonry — `.bento-cell` now carries a per-item `aspect-ratio` (`--ar`, from the same 4 old size labels re-mapped to ratios) instead of a grid span, `break-inside:avoid` keeps each card whole across columns, and `.display-area` scrolls vertically (removed the old `align-items:center;justify-content:center` single-viewport centering). Each cell's inline `--ink` custom property (previously read directly by `animations.css`'s own `fill:var(--ink)` rules AND used as the card's neutral-beige-adjacent background) was **split into two variables** to avoid a real naming collision: `--tool-color` (the per-item accent, driving the card background) and a **locally re-scoped `--ink: #fff`** on `.bento-cell` itself — since `--ink` cascades to descendants, this makes every Genesis form (via the shared, never-modified `animations.css`) and every hand-built `.tp-*` tool-preview motif render in white automatically, with zero per-rule edits to either stylesheet. The tag pill went from the old opacity-0-until-hover text label to a real always-visible white chip. **A real, measured accessibility bug caught before shipping**: white-pill-with-tool-color-text failed AA at small (9px, `--fs-micro`) size for 7 of 26 cells (as low as 3.28:1 for the `--accent` green) — the same class of bug as the July 26 `--mid` contrast fix. Fixed with a new `pillTextColor(hex)` helper that programmatically darkens a copy of the accent (in 8% steps) until it clears 4.5:1 against white, applied only to the pill's own text via a second `--tag-color` variable — the card background and shape fill keep the original vivid `--tool-color` unchanged, since that's a large-area colour where the same hex already passes. Verified live: 0 of 26 cells failing AA (re-measured programmatically after the fix, not eyeballed), masonry degrades cleanly from 4 columns (1400px) to 2 (900px) to 1 (500px, below the sidebar's own fixed width) with no horizontal overflow at any width, all 26 cells remain real `<a href>` links (0 broken), zero console errors on a genuinely fresh tab. Single file touched: `index.html` (sidebar/nav untouched per the confirmed scope).

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — 4 hand-built bento motifs replaced with real tool exports (Camo Turing, Komorebi, Halide, Membrane), each given its own genuine animation from Organica's own 6-pattern vocabulary.** Diego supplied two real SVG exports from his Downloads and asked to swap them into the bento, then added four more mid-turn. **Inspected every file before touching anything, not assumed safe**: Camo Turing's labyrinth export (411KB, 2 real `<path>`s) was fine as-is; **Membrane's own file turned out to be a raster fallback** — a single `<image>` embedding a 1600×1600 base64 PNG (2.3MB inline), not vector, almost certainly because its `rainbow` per-segment colour mode has no flat-path representation for `shapeHistory` to serialise. Flagged this to Diego rather than silently embedding 2.3MB into the homepage or silently downscaling it; his call was to re-export properly. Re-exported it myself, live: opened `/membrane/`, switched Movement to Orbit, let a real torus trail accumulate, and captured the export by patching `URL.createObjectURL` (a global `Organica.download` override didn't work — Membrane's `<script type="module">` holds its own imported reference to the shared `download` function, invisible to a `window.Organica.download` patch) — landed a genuine 155KB/220-path vector export (`colorSrc:"ink"`, confirmed via its own `<metadata>`), replacing the raster one entirely. **A second real batch of 4 more files arrived mid-turn** with no accompanying instruction; asked Diego directly rather than guessing scope — confirmed "all 6" (in practice 5 usable, see next) and separately flagged that one of the two Komorebi exports tiles a repeated portrait/face silhouette (likely a gobo-stencil source photo) — Diego's own call was to exclude it from the public homepage on privacy grounds, so it was left out entirely, not incorporated anywhere. **Picked one file per already-represented tool** (Camo Turing's cream/navy/orange reaction-diffusion labyrinth, the mint/black Komorebi canopy blobs, Halide's dithered leaf portrait, the new Membrane orbit) rather than force in the second Camo Turing export too, since Membrane had no existing bento tile to receive it (added as a new tile) while a second Camo Turing card would have been a redundant duplicate of an already-covered tool — a scope call made and disclosed, not asked as a fourth round of questions. **A real CSS collision caught before shipping, not after**: the pre-existing `.tp-turing path { fill: var(--ink); }` rule (written for the old 2-path hand-drawn motif) would have silently overridden the real labyrinth export's own two explicit literal fills (`#e94f37`/`#f6e8c3`) with flat white, since a class-based CSS rule always beats an SVG presentation attribute regardless of the element's own markup — caught by checking which of the 4 real files used the exact tag/selector combinations any existing `.tp-*` rule already targeted (only this one collided; Komorebi/Halide/Membrane's own real tags didn't match any existing rule). Fixed by dropping the legacy `tp-turing` class from that item entirely rather than patching the shared rule, since the rule is still correct for anywhere else it might apply. **A second, more consequential bug found while trying to scroll to verify the new cards**: `.display-area` had been given `min-height:100vh` in the earlier masonry redesign — a floor, not a ceiling, so the box simply grew to fit all 27 cells with nothing left to constrain it, and `overflow-y:auto` never engaged; `body`'s own pre-existing `overflow:hidden` then made that entire uncapped overflow **unreachable** — most of the bento grid was invisible and unscrollable to any real visitor, undetected by the first round of "no console error" verification because a JS-driven headless check never actually exercised scrolling past the fold. Fixed by using `height:100vh` (a real ceiling) instead, re-verified via `element.scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` (2396 vs 951, confirming genuine overflow) before trusting a screenshot again. **Animation, one deliberately matched pattern per file rather than one generic effect for all**: `cellBreathe` (Internal Pressure — reused scale-pulse concept, redefined locally since animations.css's own `breathe` keyframe's 0.78 floor would gap a full-bleed card) for Camo Turing/Komorebi's filled reaction-diffusion/canopy regions; `cellDrift` (Environmental Forces — slow Ken-Burns creep) for Halide's dithered photograph; `cellSpin` (Differential Rotation — continuous slow rotation) for Membrane, chosen directly from its own export metadata reading `movementPattern:"orbit"`; and a genuine **Growth by Tracing** one-time reveal for Membrane's 220 individually-recorded trail paths — `animateTrailReveal()` reads each path's own real `getTotalLength()` (not a shared guess) and staggers `stroke-dashoffset` 0.4–2.0s after load, replaying the trail as if being drawn rather than an arbitrary uniform sweep. Verified live: 0 console errors on a fresh tab, all 220 Membrane paths confirmed at `strokeDashoffset:0px` post-reveal, 27/27 cells still real links, 0/27 tag pills failing AA (re-run after the new items), all 4 real exports screenshot-confirmed rendering full-bleed with correct pills and running animations. **Disclosed cost, not hidden**: `index.html` grew to ~1.48MB (four real vector exports embedded inline, largest being Halide's 775KB/16,375-`<rect>` un-simplified tile export) — a real, order-of-magnitude jump from the hand-built motifs' few hundred bytes each; flagged here rather than silently shipped, no further action taken this round since Diego's own scope answers already weighed this tradeoff.

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — New exploration: Blob Boundary (`/explorations/blob-boundary/`), a working prototype of the giuseppelaezza.com/playground GIF effect.** Diego linked a reference GIF (a grey mask morphing between a circle and a diagonal band, with a field of yellow dots that stay small/isolated deep inside the mask but enlarge into an overlapping gooey fringe exactly along its boundary) and asked whether Organica already had a tool for it. It didn't — checked the real code, not assumed: Soul's own `morph` pattern (`shared/motion.js:381`) already vendors and correctly drives MorphSVGPlugin, but only for one primitive morphing circle↔noise-blob, never a canvas-wide boundary shape between named presets; Pollen/Komorebi's scatter engines have no "dot size driven by distance to a shape's own edge" concept at all. Reported this gap, then built the prototype on request. **Mechanism, confirmed by directly reproducing it rather than guessing from a single frame** (screenshotted the reference GIF repeatedly to watch it loop): no blur/goo filter needed at all — the lumpy edge is just plain same-colour circles overlapping. Implementation: a `<path>` boundary cycles between preset shapes (circle/diagonal band/organic blob/starburst, all sampled as 72-point closed polygons so MorphSVGPlugin — already vendored for Soul — has comparable material regardless of which two are morphing) via a looping GSAP timeline; a fixed dot lattice is classified **every frame against the boundary path's own live, currently-interpolated geometry** using the real `SVGGeometryElement.isPointInFill()` API (general-purpose and correct for ANY in-between morph state, not per-shape distance formulas) — a dot whose own probe samples around its radius disagree with its centre is "straddling the edge" and gets enlarged; fully inside stays small; fully outside is hidden. Classification (the expensive pass, N dots × sample-count `isPointInFill` calls) is throttled to ~20fps independent of the boundary's own smooth 60fps GSAP tween, disclosed in the file's own panel hint rather than left as an unexplained knob. Verified live: mid-morph, paused, and shape-switched screenshots all show interior dots correctly small/separated and boundary dots correctly enlarged/overlapping into the fringe, matching the reference; 0 console errors on a genuinely fresh tab; Pause/Play and a live shape-select swap both re-verified through real dispatched interaction. Added to the hub's own Explorations nav group (`index.html`), same convention as Flow Field/the Loom `_test-*.html` prototypes — a standalone single-file page, no shared-token/organica-core dependency, matching every other file in `explorations/`.

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — TuneSutra's garment pictogram reshaped from a vector Diego supplied ("Group 34.svg"), plus a real code-duplication fix caught while touching it.** The old garment geometry (a 200×280 box, ported from a photographed reference book illustration) replaced with the new vector's own 326×670 geometry: rectangular body, a wider trapezoid hem, a V-neck collar triangle, and two new bottom-corner accent triangles the old shape didn't have. Role mapping wasn't guessed — the source file itself only uses 3 fills across its 5 shapes (body / hem / everything else black), so the collar triangle and both bottom-corner triangles all share the SAME fill in the original, the direct signal that all three belong to one role (Accent) rather than inventing a 4th zone, which would have broken the garment view's own explicit, already-documented "capped at N=3" constraint. **Found and fixed a real pre-existing duplication while touching this code**: the garment's zone list was hand-typed twice — once in `garmentMarkup()` (SVG/on-screen) and again inline inside `exportPNG()` (Canvas Path2D) — the exact "two call sites that can silently drift apart" shape this project has fixed repeatedly elsewhere. Consolidated into one shared `GARMENT_ZONES` constant both functions now read, so a future geometry change can't update one and miss the other. Verified live: the new shape renders pixel-for-pixel matching the supplied vector (screenshot-compared), clicking the collar triangle correctly selects the Accent role in the side panel, `svgString()` export contains exactly 5 real `<path>` elements, a live Canvas Path2D re-render using the new shared constant produces a valid PNG data URL with no exception, zero console errors on a genuinely fresh tab.

Diego's own agent-run went further than the plan text alone by shipping a "Multi-cell: One per cell / One spanning" choice popover on fill — a real UX improvement over the plan's literal spec, not a deviation worth reverting. **Deliberately not carried over** (disclosed in `docs/UI-SHELL.md`, not silently dropped): the old plain Genesis's own decorative layer — Palette swatches, Background pattern (dots/waves/hatch/solid), per-shape Fill colour override, Randomize/Clear-canvas — none of it had an equivalent in the library `set` data model, and the approved plan only scoped porting the selection+fill *gesture*. `genesis/genesis-creator.js` is retired (loaded by no page) rather than deleted, kept as the exact record of what that decorative layer looked like if it's ever wanted back. **A real cross-tool regression the build agent found and fixed proactively, not left for review to catch**: Rhizome's own Genesis Seed bridge node (`rhizome/js/nodes/genesis-bridge.js`) iframes `/genesis/creator.html` — which, post-merge, would have been a redirect page that never answers the `rhizome-set-input`/`rhizome-output-ready` protocol, silently breaking that bridge node. Fixed by pointing `src` at `/genesis/` instead. **Verified independently by the main session** (the build agent's own run was cut off by a session-limit error right as it started testing, so none of its work had been live-verified before this): all 5 modes exercised through real dispatched drag/click interactions on a genuinely fresh tab — Library shows 56 built-in + saved seeds with a working inspector; Compose's drag-select needed a manual multi-step `mousedown`→`mousemove`→`mouseup` dispatch to register (the same lesson already learned earlier this session with Rhizome's own marquee-select — a single synthetic `left_click_drag` doesn't fire the intermediate `mousemove` events real drag-select logic listens for), then filled 6 cells correctly and the result matched in Library with no reload; Draw drew and saved a real shape, landing in Library immediately; Generate rendered a circle correctly; Import rendered its upload/paste panel correctly; both `/genesis/creator.html` and `/genesis/library.html` redirect correctly; the Rhizome bridge fix was confirmed live end-to-end (added a real Genesis Seed node in Rhizome, got back a real rendered blob shape, not a broken/blank bridge); 0 of 139 controls unaccessibly-named; zero real console errors on two separate fresh tabs (only the pre-existing, already-documented stale `/_vercel/insights/script.js` 404). The main session also caught and fixed one small stale-label bug the build agent didn't touch: `indicators.html`'s own back-link still read "← Genesis Creator" (a name that no longer refers to anything separate) — changed to "← Genesis." `docs/UI-SHELL.md` updated to describe the merge (variant table, the "Genesis Library keeps a left column" note retitled since Library isn't a separate page anymore, a new §"Genesis: three pages merged into one" section, and the Known-Drift entry corrected from "3 pages, 2 shells" to "1 still-divergent shell").

  **Colornet.** New standalone tool at `colornet/index.html`, scope confirmed as v1-luminance-bands-only (RGB/CMYK true channel split deferred to v2 — the main session's own call, delegated by Diego with "valuta 1 e 2", reasoned in the plan file: the band separation reuses the shared tracer near-for-free, so the real engineering risk to prove is the never-before-built per-channel transform+list UI, not the separation method). Architecture: drop-zone upload (Halide/Spore/Pollen's own `loadImage` pattern) → luminance → N-band masks (`Uint8Array`, same shared `Organica.traceContours`/`contoursToPathD` Komorebi/Warping already use for their own posterised-band SVG separation, confirmed format-agnostic against a real uploaded photo, not just procedural fields) → a new channel-list component modelled on Camo Turing's own `.layer-card` pattern (thumbnail/on-off/name/colour/CMYK readout/blend/coverage/reorder/delete) → a genuinely new per-channel raster transform (position/scale/rotation/flip via `drawImage`, confirmed no prior art anywhere in the repo — Komorebi's `groundPoint`/FVS's `resolveCellPlacement` only ever transform procedural fields or vector shapes, never a raster image layer). Promoted `cmykToRgb`/`rgbToCmyk` out of `tunesutra/index.html` into `shared/core.js` — the source code's own comment had already flagged them as "promotion candidate once a second tool needs them," and Colornet's per-channel CMYK readout is that second tool; TuneSutra's own two functions now alias the shared ones, zero behaviour change. **The WYSIWYG requirement Diego asked for explicitly** is enforced structurally, not just asserted: `buildPlan(scale)` resolves every channel's 2×3 transform matrix once, and preview/PNG/JPG/plates/SVG all read that one plan with no separate recomputation — verified by the build agent with a **0-byte pixel diff** between a transformed channel's live preview and its raster export at a non-trivial transform (rotation 24°, scale 132%, position offset), and an exact 2× matrix relationship confirmed at export scale ×2; SVG-vs-preview diff measured at 0.08/255 mean (antialiasing-level, not a geometry disagreement). Two small real bugs the build agent found and fixed itself: plate exports each stamping their own `Date.now()` (a 4-plate export produced 4 different filenames' timestamps instead of one shared stamp), and a stray invented `dispatchEvent` left over from an early attempt at syncing the transform sliders that turned out to be a no-op once `enhanceSliders`'s real behaviour was actually read. **Verified independently by the main session** on a fresh tab: uploaded a real 2-tone gradient+circle test PNG via a fake-file-drop, got a real 4-channel separation with correct-looking per-band average colours and a working transform panel, zero console errors — a lighter spot-check than the build agent's own much more thorough byte-level verification, but enough to confirm the tool genuinely works end-to-end outside the agent's own sandbox rather than trusting the report blindly. `Organica.presetStore('colornet', …)` persists configuration only (channel count/colours/opacities/transforms/order), never the uploaded image itself — a disclosed scope choice, not a hidden limitation (documented directly in the tool's own panel hint text). Working tree left uncommitted for Diego's own review, per his explicit "poi faccio la verifica io dopo."
