- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Colornet: 3 of the 4 backlog improvements from the Coloraster/Dotraster videos built and verified (Shuffle + Random Colors, Channel Swapping, Batch processing); AM Screening deliberately left for its own future planning session.** Followed the approved plan file exactly (`Colornet — 4 miglioramenti dal backlog`), in its own stated order, after Diego's explicit "segui il piano e l'ordine di sviluppo." **Shuffle + Random Colors**: a true Fisher–Yates `shuffleChannels()` (Loom's own "Shuffle numbers" convention, not the biased `sort(()=>Math.random()-0.5)` trick) and `randomizeColors()`, both reusing `moveChannel`'s own `buildChannelUI(); render();` commit pattern. Promoted `Organica.randomHex()` into `shared/core.js` — Colornet is the second real consumer after the inline expression already living in `createColorSwatch`'s own random button, which now calls the shared function instead of repeating it (same "promote on the second consumer" discipline already applied this session to `cmykToRgb`/`rgbToCmyk`). Verified: 8 repeated shuffles all produced genuine permutations of the same 4-element set (never a no-op, never a dropped/duplicated channel); Random Colors changed all 4 hex values and the preview updated live; a preset saved after both round-tripped the exact order and colours byte-for-byte after being disturbed and reloaded. **Channel Swapping gesture**: repurposed `.chan-name`'s own click (previously a plain `selectChannel`, now `armOrSwapChannel` — selection is still reachable via the thumbnail click, confirmed unaffected) into a real arm/swap/disarm state machine (`S.swapArmedId`), styled with a new `.chan-card--armed` dashed `--accent-warm` border distinct from the solid `--tool` "selected" border. **A real bug found and fixed during verification, not assumed working from the code alone**: a bubble-phase `document` click listener meant to disarm on an outside click fired on EVERY click, including the arming click itself — `armOrSwapChannel`'s own handler rebuilds `#chan-stack`'s DOM synchronously (`buildChannelUI()`) before the event finishes bubbling, which detaches the original clicked element from the tree, so `e.target.closest('#chan-stack')` always returned null by the time the bubble-phase document listener ran. Fixed by moving the listener to the **capture** phase (`{capture:true}`/third-arg `true`), which runs before the target's own bubble-phase handler and therefore against the still-live, pre-mutation DOM — verified directly (arm→swap produced a genuine index-exact swap `[1,2,3,4]→[3,2,1,4]` with only the two clicked channels moving, same-channel re-click disarmed with zero side-effect, and a click on the canvas correctly disarmed with the order preserved). **Batch processing** — the first Organica tool with this capability. Extracted the pure luminance-band math out of `separate()` into a new `computeBands(img, bands, detail)` (no behaviour change — `separate()` itself now calls it and merges `keepSettings` on top exactly as before), so `runBatch(files)` can re-run the identical separation against N different images. Per file: decode → `computeBands` → apply the **currently configured** per-channel cosmetics by array index (colour/opacity/blend/transform — masks stay per-image) → render via the same `buildPlan`/`renderToCanvas` every other export already uses → download, all while the live interactive session's `S.img`/`S.W`/`S.H`/`S.channels`/`S.selId` are snapshotted first and restored exactly once the run finishes (batch borrows `S` for its own duration rather than keeping a second parallel state, then hands it back byte-for-byte). No zip library exists in the repo, so v1 ships sequential individual downloads (350ms spaced, same anti-burst interval `exportSeparations` already uses) with a live "Batch: N of M — filename" status readout, disclosed as the v1 shape rather than hidden. New "Batch…" header button (multi-file picker) and the existing drop-zone now routes >1 dropped file into batch automatically. **Verified thoroughly**: batched 3 real distinct images (2 local PNGs + a Halide test JPEG) after configuring 4 hand-picked random channel colours — captured all 3 real download filenames (`colornet-batch-imgA.png`/`imgB.png`/`imgC.png`, distinct), sampled pixel colours from all 3 rendered canvases and confirmed the exact 4 configured RGB triples present in every one (not just the first), and confirmed the live session's channel list was byte-identical before and after the batch ran (JSON-diffed, not eyeballed). Fresh-tab console check clean throughout all three features; every new/changed control (`Shuffle`, `Random Colors`, `Batch…`, the per-channel swap-arm button, the batch file input) confirmed with a real accessible name via `read_page`. **AM Screening (item 4)** intentionally not touched this pass — the plan's own text flags it as needing a genuinely new per-channel rendering model (continuous-tone dot-grid sampling of the ORIGINAL image at a rotated per-channel screen angle, not an extension of the existing flat/traced-mask path) and recommends its own dedicated Explore+Plan session before any code, which Diego's instruction this round didn't ask for.

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Colornet: AM Screening built — item 4 of the backlog, the one deliberately deferred that morning for its own genuinely new rendering model.** Preceded by a real research pass, not assumption: re-watched Dotraster's own "Presets" tutorial video frame by frame (`youtube.com/watch?v=9MbrDvSMqpk`) and catalogued its 10 real presets (Default/AM Circle 2 45/AM Screening Line 45 = true AM Screening; Simplex 10/25, Worley 22/25 White/35 Blend/50 Black/Test = a DIFFERENT noise-driven halftone family — confirmed live via `Worley 35 Blend`'s own on-screen readout: Halftone Mode "Noise", Type "Worley", Standard Deviation 0.20, Size 35, Dots "Blend"). Diego confirmed via AskUserQuestion to build the real AM Screening rendering pass (not just the noise-halftone presets, and not a smaller stand-in). **Architecture**: a per-channel `screenMode: 'flat'|'am'` toggle, alongside the existing traced-region mode — `screenAngle`/`screenFreq`/`dotShape` fields default per the classic 4-colour offset-print convention (`[15,75,0,45][index%4]`) the moment `computeBands` creates a channel, so the differentiated multi-channel value (real print avoids moiré via per-plate angles, and Colornet is already multi-channel) is built in from the start, not bolted on. Core function `amDotsOf(ch)`: a rotated square lattice (oversized-then-clipped, the same technique Loom's own Hexagonal/Diamond generators use for a rotated tiling) sampling each grid cell's LOCAL COVERAGE of the channel's own binary mask (a plain box average) as its tone, dot radius ∝ √tone (real halftone physics — area, not radius, proportional to density) — one ink colour per channel, exactly like a real plate, dot SIZE alone encodes tone. Cached on the channel object (`ch.amDots`/`ch._amKey`) exactly like the existing `tintOf`/`pathOf` cache pattern, keyed on angle+frequency+shape so a param drag only recomputes when something actually changed. **WYSIWYG by construction, not by separate verification effort**: `amDotsOf()` is the ONE shared data source `drawPlan` (canvas preview + PNG + per-channel plate export) and `svgFromPlan` (real `<circle>`/`<rect>` elements, genuine vector — arguably more honest than the old traced-region path, since these really are discrete dots) both read — verified the canvas dot count and the exported SVG's own `<circle>`/`<rect>` count match exactly (839 total shapes both ways: 421 squares + 418 circles). Two dot shapes shipped (Circle, Square); Line and the real PostScript "Euclidean" dot function (seen live in the video's own Dot Shape dropdown) are disclosed as not yet built rather than silently missing. New **"Auto angles (AM Screening)"** one-click button switches every channel to AM mode at its own classic angle in one action — the concrete "real 4-colour offset printing" payoff named in the original backlog entry. Backward compatibility handled explicitly: `readState()`/`applyPreset()`/`runBatch()` all carry the 4 new fields, and a preset saved before this feature existed backfills to `screenMode:'flat'` (verified against the pre-existing "Test riso" preset — applies cleanly, all 4 channels correctly stay flat, no exception). **Verified thoroughly, not just visually**: Auto angles produced 4 genuinely distinct dot-orientation channels, screenshot-confirmed as a real rosette-like 4-plate composite; angle/lineature/shape sliders live-update both the canvas and the 26×26 channel thumbnail (`drawThumb` also branches on `screenMode`, so the thumbnail never lies about which mode a channel is in — the same "a control with no visible effect is a bug" discipline this project applies everywhere); all 4 export paths (PNG, 4 separate plates, SVG, and — via the shared `buildPlan`/`renderToCanvas` — Batch) run clean on a mix of AM and flat channels together; a preset save→disturb→reload round-trip restored the exact AM config (mode/angle/freq/shape/colour) byte-for-byte; Batch processing (built that same morning) carries AM config onto a different image correctly — confirmed via a scanline white/ink alternation count (30 transitions = real dot pattern, not a flat fill) rather than just "no error"; live interactive session confirmed untouched by the batch run. 0 of the new controls unaccessibly-named (`Channel N render mode`/`screen angle`/`screen lineature`/`dot shape`, `Auto angles (AM Screening)` — all real labels, checked via `read_page` on a fresh tab), zero console errors. Not committed — per standing convention, waiting for an explicit "porta in prod."

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Colornet: Diego caught a real gap ("mancano i presets o sbaglio?") — the AM Screening engine shipped minutes earlier had no actual named presets, and the Simplex/Worley noise family from the video hadn't been built at all.** Confirmed via AskUserQuestion he wanted both closed in one pass. **Line dot shape** (the missing third option, needed for `AM Screening Line 45`): a classic ruled line screen, not a growing round dot — each cell renders a rectangle spanning the FULL cell length along the screen's own rotated row axis with thickness ∝ tone (reusing the exact `r` value `amDotsOf` already computes, so adjacent full-tone lines merge into a solid stripe exactly like a real engraving reproduction). Implemented via `lineCorners()`, one shared corner-computation function read by both `drawAmDots` (Canvas2D path) and `svgFromPlan` (`<polygon>`) — the same "one function, two renderers can't drift" discipline as everything else here — plus the grid's own rotated basis vectors (`ux/uy/vx/vy`) now cached on the channel (`ch._amBasis`) alongside the dot list itself. Screenshot-confirmed: parallel white-on-dark bars at 45°, thickness genuinely varying with local tone. **3 real AM Screening presets** (`AM_PRESETS`), read directly off the Dotraster video's own on-screen Halftone panel for Default (Circle/0°/8) and AM Circle 2 45 (Circle/45°/2) — AM Screening Line 45's own lineature wasn't legible in the recording, so it reuses Default's 8, disclosed in the code comment rather than silently invented. A new per-channel "Preset" dropdown (Custom/Default/AM Circle 2 45/AM Screening Line 45) quick-applies angle+lineature+shape to that one channel — verified live, both non-Default presets applied their exact stored values. Lineature's own slider floor lowered from 6 to 2 so "AM Circle 2 45" is actually reachable. **Noise halftone** — a genuinely separate third `screenMode` (`'noise'`, alongside `'flat'`/`'am'`), not a variant of the AM lattice: an irregular JITTERED grid (not a rotated lattice) whose per-cell tone is the channel's own local mask coverage MODULATED by a real noise field — `Organica.noise.simplex2`/`voronoiF1F2` (Worley), newly linked into Colornet via `shared/noise.js` (core → noise → tool script, the established load order) — with a `Dots: Black/White/Blend` control selecting which side of the field gets ink, matching this project's own prior backlog analysis that Dotraster's "Noise" mode is closer to Pollen's "dot size driven by local darkness" idea than to a lattice screen. Explicitly disclosed as a reinterpretation from the video's own on-screen parameter NAMES (Halftone Mode/Type/Size/Dots), not a byte-for-byte port of Dotraster's own unavailable source — same honesty standard as every other "inspired by" build in this repo. Seeded per-channel (`Organica.mulberry32(ch.id*7919+13)`) so the scatter is reproducible until Size/Type/Dots actually change, not re-randomised on every render. Screenshot-confirmed Simplex vs. Worley as visibly distinct clustering textures on the same channel. **Backward compatibility extended, not just re-checked**: `readState()`/`applyPreset()`/`runBatch()`'s cosmetic snapshot all carry the 3 new noise fields alongside the AM ones; a preset saved before EITHER feature existed still backfills correctly (re-verified against "Test riso" — 4/4 channels land on `flat`, zero exceptions). A real typo caught before it ever ran: a stray comment line was missing its `//` prefix (leftover from drafting) — would have been a hard JS syntax error; caught by re-reading the diff before testing, not by a failed page load. **Verified end to end**: SVG export mixing all 3 modes on one image produced exactly the expected shape breakdown (27 `<polygon>` for Line, 139 `<circle>` for Noise/Worley, 2 `<path>` for the 2 still-Flat channels, 168 total matching the reported count exactly); the 26×26 channel thumbnail now also branches on `noise` mode (`drawThumb`) so it never shows a stale preview for the new mode either; 0 of the ~9 new controls unaccessibly-named (`AM Screening preset`, `noise type`, `noise size`, `noise dots` — all real labels via `read_page` on a fresh tab), zero console errors (past catching and fixing the syntax typo pre-emptively). Not committed yet — waiting for an explicit "porta in prod."

- **August 27, 2026 (still later same day) — Colornet: 4 built-in full-configuration presets, Diego's own "riso paper" example among them.** Asked directly whether the whole engine could be composed into named, ready looks. Adopted Warping/Komorebi's own established `BUILTIN_PRESETS` convention verbatim — `allPresets()` merges built-ins with the user's own (`Object.assign({}, BUILTIN_PRESETS, userPresets())`), the preset `<select>` groups them under "Built-in"/"Yours" `<optgroup>`s, and both Save and Delete now refuse a built-in name (save: "That name is a built-in preset — pick another"; delete: "Built-in presets cannot be deleted") — matching Warping's own guard exactly. **4 presets, each run against a real test photo and checked against its own name before being kept** (this project's own stated discipline, applied literally — one was caught failing it and rebuilt): **Riso paper** — 3 bold spot inks (`Marine`/`Flame`/`Sun`) named and coloured like real Risograph drum inks, Multiply blend (a riso press can only overprint, never truly blend), a warm uncoated-paper background, and a small per-channel tx/ty nudge (0.6–0.8% of canvas) for the characteristic slight pass misregistration that reads as genuinely riso rather than a clean flat poster — screenshot-confirmed as a convincing riso look. **Pop Halftone** — 4 channels at literal CMYK inks and the classic 15/75/0/45° offset angles, a direct showcase of AM Screening's own differentiated reason for existing (real print avoids moiré via per-plate angle) — screenshot-confirmed as a genuine Warhol-style rosette portrait. **Engraved Line** — real copperplate/banknote technique: one CONSISTENT angle (45°) with lineature graduated by tone (28/18/11, darkest→lightest, the 4th/lightest channel switched off entirely so highlights are bare paper) rather than crosshatching, since finer/denser parallel lines are what actually reads as darker in that technique. **The first attempt was wrong and was caught by looking at it, not assumed correct from the design reasoning**: a 2-band 45°/135° perpendicular crosshatch (the more obvious "engraving" idea) rendered as an abstract diagonal weave occupying only half the canvas, not a graduated portrait — only 2 discrete luminance bands can't carry a crosshatch's own tonal argument. Rebuilt to 4 bands/one direction/graduated frequency and re-screenshotted — genuinely reads as an engraved illustration this time. **Concrete Noise** — Noise halftone's own showcase, Worley's cellular clustering for the dark plate and Simplex's softer drift for the mid-tone on a concrete-grey ground, screenshot-confirmed as organic, non-gridded stippling distinct in character from the AM lattice presets. **Verified end to end**: all 4 presets apply cleanly on a real photo (band count, colours, names, screen modes all correct per channel); the built-in delete-guard and the save-name-collision guard both fire correctly (checked live, not just read from the code); PNG and SVG export both run clean on a built-in-preset composition; fresh-tab console clean. Not committed yet — waiting for an explicit "porta in prod."
- **August 28, 2026 — Colornet: debug + regression test of the whole preset system (Diego's own request, focus on "Riso paper"), plus a genuine dithering integration from Halide instead of a colleague's proposed SVG `<feTurbulence>` grain filter.** Diego shared a colleague's suggested Risograph-emulation algorithm built on `<feTurbulence>`/`<feColorMatrix>`/`<feGaussianBlur>` and asked what could be integrated from other Organica tools instead of invented fresh. Two background Explore agents ran first, not assumptions: **(1) a full static audit of Colornet's current code** — `amDotsOf`/`computeNoiseDots`/`drawAmDots`/`svgFromPlan`/`drawThumb` all correctly three-way-dispatch on `screenMode` with no drift risk; zero duplicate listeners (`buildChannelUI()` fully replaces `#chan-stack` on every rebuild); every per-channel field correctly backfilled in `applyPreset`/`readState`/`runBatch`; caches (`amDots`/`noiseDotsCache`/`tint`/`pathD`/`_plate`) don't hash the mask into their keys, safe only because `computeBands()`/`separate()` always construct entirely fresh channel objects rather than mutating `ch.mask` in place — a real invariant, documented so future work doesn't break it by adding in-place mask mutation. **No actual bugs found.** **(2) a cross-tool grep for reusable grain/noise/misregistration techniques** — confirmed **zero precedent anywhere in the repo** for SVG filter primitives as a creative effect (`feTurbulence` has no code hits at all outside a research doc citation); a runtime SVG filter also can't be traced/re-exported as real vector geometry, breaking the "export must be real vector" discipline every other Colornet mode already honours — **the feTurbulence proposal was rejected on this basis, not built.** What IS genuinely reusable was already in place: `Organica.noise.*` (Noise halftone already uses it), the per-channel `tx`/`ty` misregistration jitter (already in Riso paper), `mix-blend-mode:multiply` (already in Colornet's own SVG export). The one real, proven, NOT-yet-shared technique: **Halide's own dithering** (`halide/index.html`, Floyd–Steinberg/Atkinson/Bayer) — confirmed via direct code read that Halide's own SVG export already runs its dithered output through `Organica.contoursToPathD`, the exact same shared tracer Colornet's `pathOf` already calls, proving a dithered mask vectorises for free with zero new export machinery. Diego separately asked whether Living Path's own raster→vector step could apply here too — checked directly: Living Path uses its own private marching-squares + Laplacian-smoothing implementation (`livingpath/index.html:757`), architecturally unrelated to the shared rectilinear tracer Colornet/Halide/Komorebi/Warping all use — correctly not reused, the Halide path was the right one. **Shipped**: `Organica.dither` (`shared/core.js`, inserted after `contoursToPathD`) — `FS_KERNEL`/`ATKINSON_KERNEL`/`errorDiffusion`/`bayerMatrix`/`bayerThresholds`/`ordered`/`threshold`, byte-identical logic promoted from Halide, which now aliases the shared functions (`const FS_KERNEL = Organica.dither.FS_KERNEL;` etc.) — **re-verified live on Halide itself** (a shipped, live tool) since promotion touches production code: all 4 dither algorithms screenshot-confirmed visually unchanged (Floyd–Steinberg 1440 shapes/25% ink, Atkinson 828/25%, Bayer 1652/25%, Flat threshold 232/24% — all sensible relative orderings for each algorithm's own character), SVG export reconfirmed as 233 real `<rect>` elements matching the UI's own shape-count readout, zero console errors. **Colornet's own 4th `screenMode: 'dither'`**: since a Colornet channel is already a binary luminance-band mask (not a continuous-tone image like Halide's own input), dithering runs on a small `cols×rows` GRID of local coverage — the exact same box-average sampling `amDotsOf`/`computeNoiseDots` already did independently, now extracted into one shared `cellCoverage(ch, px, py, half_i, W, H)` (a real DRY win, not just new-feature plumbing — used by all three screen modes now) — `gray = 1 - coverage` per cell (Halide's own dark=ink convention), run through `Organica.dither.errorDiffusion`/`.ordered`, one FIXED-SIZE square per "on" cell (density varies, not size — the actual visual signature that distinguishes dithering from AM/Noise's own coverage-driven dot radius). New fields `ditherAlgo`/`ditherSize`, threaded through every existing dispatch point exactly like the AM/Noise fields before them: `computeBands` (fresh defaults), `separate()`'s `keepSettings` merge, `readState`/`applyPreset` (with the identical defensive backfill pattern: `sc.ditherAlgo || ch.ditherAlgo`), `runBatch`'s `cosmeticByIdx`, a 4th `buildChannelUI()` controls block (Algorithm/Size), and a new `drawSquareDots` shared by `drawPlan`/`svgFromPlan`/`drawThumb`. **Riso paper's own "Sun" channel retuned** — was the flattest, least distinctive of its 3 inks (`screenMode:'flat'`); now `screenMode:'dither', ditherAlgo:'floyd', ditherSize:24` — screenshot-confirmed against a real portrait photo as genuine irregular ink-grain texture instead of a flat swatch, correctly balancing against Marine/Flame's own AM dots; Atkinson was tried side-by-side and looked visually indistinguishable at this preset's own near-uniform coverage region, so Floyd–Steinberg (the more classically "riso grain"-associated algorithm) was kept as shipped. **Full regression matrix executed, all live dispatched interaction, not code-reading**: all 4 built-ins × 2 real source photos × 2 band counts (3→6) applied cleanly, zero console errors; dithering's own 3-algorithm × 3-size cross product confirmed canvas/thumbnail lockstep via a temporary hook on `computeDitherDots` (identical dot count returned to both the render() and drawThumb() call for the same params, count changing correctly only when a param actually changed — real cache-invalidation proof, not assumed); Shuffle correctly carries `screenMode:'dither'` with the channel object across a reorder (not tied to slot position); Random Colors changes colour without disturbing the dither cache; Channel Swapping correctly relocates a dither-mode channel; Auto-angles correctly force-overwrites a dither channel to `'am'` with no exception (dither fields become inert-but-harmless, gated by `screenMode`); Batch processing carried a Bayer-dither channel config onto 2 different images (confirmed via a real 20×20 pixel-colour-diversity sample — 11–13 distinct colours per output, not a degenerate flat fill); a save→disturb→reload round-trip restored the exact dither config byte-for-byte; the pre-existing "Test riso" preset (saved before this feature existed) still applies cleanly with zero dither fields, correctly falling back to `flat`; both built-in guards (delete-refusal, save-name-collision) re-fired correctly after the `BUILTIN_PRESETS` edits; **WYSIWYG parity for Dither specifically confirmed exact** — 42 canvas squares === 42 SVG `<rect>` elements for the same channel, counted programmatically, not assumed from the shared-function architecture alone. 0 of the new controls unaccessibly-named (`Channel N dither algorithm`/`dither size`, all real labels via `read_page` on a fresh tab), zero console errors throughout. **Diego caught a real bug immediately after this pass** ("quando scelto un presets e poi cambio il chanel si rompe"): reproduced live — apply any built-in preset, then drag the "Channels" (band count) slider, and the ENTIRE preset (colours, names, screen modes, everything) silently reverted to plain flat greyscale defaults, no crash, no console error, just the preset's whole look destroyed. Root cause: the bands slider's own `change` handler called `separate(false)` (discard all settings) while the adjacent Detail dropdown's own handler already correctly called `separate(true)` (keep settings) — a real pre-existing inconsistency between two controls that look like siblings in the same panel, unrelated to today's dither work, just far more noticeable now that presets have a distinctive named look worth preserving. Fixed by matching Detail's own convention: bands-slider change now calls `separate(true)`. The dedicated "Separate" button deliberately keeps `separate(false)` — that nuclear full-reset stays an explicit user action, not an implicit side effect of dragging a slider. Verified live: applying Riso paper (3 channels) then dragging Channels to 5 now preserves Marine/Flame/Sun's exact colours and screen modes (AM/AM/Dither) on channels 1–3, with 2 new channels getting sensible fresh defaults; dragging back down to 2 correctly preserves Marine/Flame; the Separate button still does its own full reset as intended (re-confirmed unchanged); zero console errors throughout, fresh-tab check clean.

- **August 28, 2026 (later same day) — Colornet: "Riso paper" checked against a detailed 5-step Risograph vector-algorithm spec Diego shared, plus a real RetroSupply reference photo — genuine gaps found and closed, not just a "looks fine" nod.** Went through the spec's 5 steps (color separation / ink-bleed edge roughening / procedural vector halftone / mechanical misregistration / translucent overprint) one by one against the actual shipped code: **Step 1** (spot-colour projection) — approximated by luminance-band separation, already a disclosed v1 scope limit (true colour-split is roadmap v2), not touched. **Step 2** (Perlin/Simplex vertex displacement on path edges for ink bleed) — **did not exist at all**, a real gap. **Step 3** (procedural vector halftone, blue-noise stipple OR angled-grid+circles) — already covered by Noise halftone / AM Screening respectively, a disclosed approximation (not literal Poisson-disk blue noise) but functionally present. **Step 4** (misregistration jitter) and **Step 5** (multiply overprint) — both already shipped correctly. Then opened the actual reference image (`retrosupply.co` Riso Textures product photo) and compared directly: the reference's dominant, defining trait is a **fine, uniform, sand-like grain** across otherwise-flat colour regions — not big visible halftone dots. Screenshotting the shipped preset next to it made the gap concrete: Marine/Flame's AM Screening at lineature 16–18 read as coarse classic newsprint halftone, nothing like the reference's fine grain — because the lineature/size sliders' own ceiling (80/60/60 for AM/Noise/Dither) was too low to reach a genuinely fine texture at Colornet's typical 560px separation resolution. Diego confirmed both fixes in one round (raise the ceiling + retune, AND build the missing Step 2). **Fix 1 — raised `chan-am-freq`/`chan-noise-size`/`chan-dither-size` sliders' max from 80/60/60 to 300** (tested live first: 10.9ms to recompute at freq=250, no perf concern — the box-average coverage cost scales with canvas area, not cell count, so finer cells don't slow it down) — retuned Riso paper's own Marine (screenFreq 18→250) / Flame (16→220) / Sun (ditherSize 24→200), screenshot-confirmed against the reference as now reading as genuine fine ink grain rather than a dot-halftone poster. **Fix 2 — built Step 2 for real**: new `roughPathOf(ch)`, a Colornet-local function (deliberately NOT a modification to the shared `Organica.traceContours`/`contoursToPathD` — Halide/Komorebi/Warping all depend on those staying byte-exact) that re-walks a Flat-mode channel's own raw traced loops, subdivides every straight tracer segment to ~3px steps (the raw pixel-grid-corner vertices are far too sparse on their own to read as a continuous wobble), and displaces each resulting vertex along its own local normal by `Organica.noise.simplex2` sampled at that vertex's own position — a real per-vertex ink-bleed simulation, not a filter. New per-channel fields `edgeRoughen` (px displacement amount, 0 default/off) and `edgeScale` (the wobble's own wavelength in px), gated to Flat mode only (the only mode with a single crisp boundary path — AM/Noise/Dither's own edges are already organic by construction, being made of many small dots). **The WYSIWYG discipline this project insists on everywhere forced a real architecture decision, not just a new function**: Flat mode's live canvas PREVIEW was a raster `ctx.drawImage(tintOf(ch))`, entirely separate from the vector path only the SVG EXPORT read — so a roughened edge would have shown in the exported file but NOT in the on-screen preview, a real WYSIWYG violation. Fixed by having the canvas preview itself switch to `ctx.fill(new Path2D(roughPathOf(ch)))` whenever `edgeRoughen > 0` (the plain `edgeRoughen=0` case keeps the original fast raster `drawImage` path untouched — zero cost, zero behaviour change for every existing Flat-mode channel). `edgeRoughen:0` verified as an exact, byte-identical no-op (`pathOf(ch) === roughPathOf(ch)`, confirmed via direct string equality, not assumed from the code reading it back). Threaded through the same 5 propagation points every prior screen-mode field needed: `computeBands` defaults, `separate()`'s `keepSettings` merge, `readState`/`applyPreset` (identical defensive backfill: `sc.edgeRoughen != null ? sc.edgeRoughen : ch.edgeRoughen`), `runBatch`'s `cosmeticByIdx`, and all 14 `BUILTIN_PRESETS` channel literals (mechanical addition, `edgeRoughen:0` — none of the current 4 presets' own channels are in Flat mode today, so this ships as an available, verified capability rather than being forced into an existing preset's look it wasn't designed for). **Verified end to end**: edge roughening screenshot-confirmed producing a genuinely organic wavy boundary on a real photo's darkest band, replacing the crisp rectilinear tracer edge; canvas-vs-SVG parity confirmed by exact string containment (the SVG's own `<path d="...">` contains the identical string `roughPathOf` returns to the canvas, not just "similar" geometry); a save→disturb→reload round-trip with `edgeRoughen:5` restored exactly; the pre-existing "Test riso" preset (missing both new fields entirely) still applies cleanly with zero exception; both built-in guards re-fired correctly after another `BUILTIN_PRESETS` edit; 0 of the 2 new controls (`Channel N edge roughening`/`edge wobble size`) unaccessibly-named; zero console errors on a fresh tab. Not committed yet — waiting for an explicit "porta in prod."

- **August 28, 2026 (later same day) — Colornet: 8 gap reali contro Dotraster (stochaster-apps.com/dotraster.html), tutti costruiti in una sessione.** Diego ha chiesto di leggere il manuale di Dotraster e confrontarlo con "Spore" — il confronto reale (entrambi i codici letti a fondo) ha mostrato che le feature di Dotraster corrispondono quasi 1:1 a **Colornet**, non a Spore (che non ha alcuna modalità di screening/preset/batch). Confermato con Diego via AskUserQuestion, incluso un momento di ambiguità genuino ("il tool è un update di Spore, non di Coloraster") risolto esplicitamente: **resta su Colornet**; i pattern custom (item Pattern mode) vivono nella libreria condivisa di Genesis, non in uno store dedicato. **A — Dither: Threshold, +7 kernel, livello Bayer.** `Organica.dither.threshold` guadagna un 4° parametro `level` opzionale (retrocompatibile, verificato: l'unica chiamata di Halide passa 3 argomenti). 7 kernel classici aggiunti (Jarvis-Judice-Ninke/Stucki/Burkes/Sierra/Two-Row Sierra/Sierra Lite/Simple2D), ciascuno verificato sommare esattamente a 1.0 prima di scriverlo nel file. Due campi separati `ditherLevel`(0-255)/`bayerLevel`(0-3→[2,4,8,16]) invece di uno condiviso — un preset salvato sotto Threshold con `ditherLevel:200` sarebbe diventato un Bayer-level fuori range al cambio di algoritmo. **Bug reale trovato e corretto durante la verifica**: il cambio di `ditherAlgo` non ricostruiva i controlli condizionali (Size/Level/Matrix/Pattern) — mancava `buildChannelUI()`, solo `render()`. **B — Noise: Random + Gaussian.** Random pesca dallo STESSO stream `rng` già seedato per canale (nessun secondo RNG necessario); Gaussian usa Box-Muller, nuovo campo `noiseStdDev`. Verificato che la varianza dei raggi aumenti con Std dev più alto. **C — AM Screening: forma Euclidean.** Vera funzione spot PostScript: cerchio pieno che cresce sotto tono 0.5, buco che si restringe sopra — continua esattamente a tono=0.5. Richiede un fill `evenodd` a due sotto-path PER PUNTO (non nello stesso batch fill di circle/square/line, che userebbe nonzero corrompendo il buco) — nuovo ramo dedicato in `drawAmDots` e nel ramo AM di `svgFromPlan`. Verificato su foto reale: alte luci con cerchi, ombre con buchi bianchi visibili sui capelli/giacca; parità canvas/SVG confermata isolando il `<g>` del canale (142 buchi = 142 path evenodd, 116 solidi = 116 cerchi — un primo conteggio grezzo su tutto l'export aveva dato 145 invece di 142 perché contava anche i path Flat-mode di altri canali, non un bug reale). **D — Filtri immagine (tutti e 6).** Nuova sezione pannello "Image" globale (non per-canale), pipeline Grayscale method → Brightness → Contrast → Denoise → Sharpen → Perception dentro `computeBands()` prima della lettura pixel. Color Filter simulato via proiezione del colore sorgente sul colore del filtro (dot product), verificato che hue=0°/240° combaci esattamente con Red/Blue channel puri (matematicamente atteso). **Falso allarme durante la verifica, corretto guardando meglio non assumendo**: tutti i Grayscale method sembravano dare risultati identici sulla prima foto di test — non un bug, quella foto specifica aveva R≈G≈B quasi identici pixel per pixel; con una seconda foto genuinamente colorata (second.png) i 5 metodi hanno dato risultati chiaramente distinti. **Contrast sembrava non cambiare il colore-swatch dei canali** — anche questo non un bug: `keepSettings:true` preserva deliberatamente il colore cosmetico, ma il render (la maschera) risponde correttamente al filtro, confermato visivamente. Path no-op verificato esatto (nessun filtro attivo → colori identici al pre-feature baseline). **E — Export: scale personalizzata + upscale senza interpolazione.** Nuova opzione "Custom…" nel select Scale con input numerico (0.1-20×), verificato che il readout dimensioni combaci esattamente col canvas renderizzato (×2.7 → 1512×1512 in entrambi). Checkbox "No smoothing" → `ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled=false`, verificato che il flag si inverta correttamente. **F — Pattern mode, il più grande.** `ditherAlgo:'pattern'` come sibling di Bayer nella stessa architettura Dither — nuova `Organica.dither.orderedCustom` fattorizza il loop di tiling che `ordered` già aveva. **Storage nella libreria Genesis** (`organica.library.forms`), non uno store Colornet dedicato: ogni pattern salvato come vero SVG (griglia N×N di `<rect>` con `fill-opacity` = livello di grigio della cella), Colornet lo rilegge riparsando gli `fill-opacity` in un `Float32Array` — stessa forma dati di `bayerThresholds`. Editor nuovo (nessun precedente diretto nel file): overlay con griglia 8×8 cliccabile, click sinistro riempie dal nero, click destro dal bianco — due puntatori (`front`/`back`) che si chiudono verso il centro, gestendo correttamente il mix di click sinistro/destro; Undo (stack di azioni); Save abilitato solo a griglia completa (esattamente come nel manuale Dotraster). Verificato end-to-end: 64/64 celle, Undo riporta a 63/64 e ri-disabilita Save, Save scrive nella libreria Genesis e il pattern appare subito nel dropdown del canale, **persistenza confermata con un vero reload di pagina** (non solo in-memory), fallback pulito su Bayer quando il pattern referenziato viene cancellato (zero eccezioni). **Verifica finale**: tutti e 4 i preset built-in ri-applicano correttamente dopo tutte le modifiche; 93 dei 95 controlli del pannello hanno un nome accessibile reale (i 2 senza sono gli `<input type=file>` nascosti preesistenti, mai toccati); zero errori console su tab fresca in ogni fase. Non committato — in attesa di un esplicito "porta in prod".

- **August 28, 2026 (later same day) — Built Radial (`/radial/`), one tool for the whole Book of Shapes "radial" pattern family (`bookofshapes.com/?tag=radial`, 18 patterns), grown out of a first pass at just the `nested_polygons_filled` / "Broken Ring" pattern.** First built `/broken-ring/` alone (Diego: "analizza `bookofshapes.com/patterns/nested_polygons_filled`, cosa possiamo usare di già sviluppato") — a warped concentric-N-gon polar lattice with a noise threshold at each cell centroid deciding which quad cells fill solid; pure vector, one `buildScene()` whose inline-SVG preview string IS the export string. Then Diego pointed at the full radial tag and asked whether the 18 could be **one tool**. Analysis (descriptions + sampled param sets, live): **~16 of 18 reduce to "a polar coordinate field → a render pass over it"** — Group A polar lattice → outlines/mesh/fill (`nested_polygons_filled`, `nested_polygons`, `concentric_noise_rings`, `noise_circle_1`, `modular_circle`, `radial_harmony`, `radial_spokes`, `line_based_circles`, `polar_mesh`), Group B lattice + radius/angle modulation → mesh (`rose_mesh` — confirmed live it exposes Petals/Petal Depth/Rings/Spokes/Inner-Outer Radius **and a Warp Amount**; `spiral_mesh`), Group C polar point placement → mark per point (`phyllotaxis-bloom` golden angle, `spiral_dot_field`, `halftone_sphere`, `chaos_circles`), Group D concentric arc bands (`brockmann_arcs`). Outliers left out: `iso-sphere` (isometric — different projection), `flow_poles` (flow-field streamlines — belongs with a flow family / Pollen flow / the flow-field exploration). Decisions (Diego, via AskUserQuestion): **fold `broken-ring/` (never committed) into `/radial/`** — its warped polar lattice is the core, "Filled cells" (Broken Ring) becomes one Render mode + one preset; **all render modes in v1**. **`/radial/` shape**: `Placement` select (Grid rings×spokes / Phyllotaxis golden-angle / Spiral arms) → `buildPoints()`; `Shape` section (Grid only) — `Modulation` select (None / Rose curve / Spiral shear) applied to `rr`/`a`, plus the always-on domain-warp; `Render` select (Ring outlines / Spokes / Mesh + cell-diagonals toggle / Filled cells / Marks — dot·circle·line, size Uniform/Noise/**radial-gradient limb-darkening curve** shared with Camouflage countershade / Arc bands — `arcPathD` reduced from genesis `wedgePathD`, per-path `stroke-width` ramp). **Reuse (~85%)**: Warping's `warpPoint()`/`NORM` idiom verbatim (2 decorrelated fbm taps offset `(5.2,1.3)`, Amount 0 an **exact** no-op); `Organica.noise.fbm` for warp + fill + mark-size noise; **three decorrelated `Organica.mulberry32` streams** — fill offset (`seed ^ 0x9E3779B9`), jitter (`seed ^ 0x85EBCA6B`), arc-bias (`seed * 2654435761`) — so no field's seed perturbs another (Loom `hexagonal.js` `noiseRng` precedent); `Organica.download`/`stamp`/`sendToFigma`/`createZoomPan`/`presetStore`/`enhanceSliders`/`autoLabelPanel`/`popover`/`status`; shell copied from `warping/index.html` (the living scaffold — `_template.html` is stale). **Genuinely new**: `buildPoints()` (lattice OR flat point list per Placement), the per-mode primitive builders, `sceneSVG()`/`drawSceneCanvas()` over a tagged primitive set (`polygonsStroke`/`polylines`/`lines`/`polygonsFill`/`arcs`/`marks`) — preview string === export string throughout. **16 `BUILTIN_PRESETS`**, one per covered pattern, each a `{placement,modulation,render,…}` snapshot off a `BP()` defaults helper. **One bug caught on the first `/broken-ring/` load** (carried the lesson into Radial): `syncColor()` calling `scheduleRender()` during the init-time `['ink','paper'].forEach(...)` hit a `let renderQueued` TDZ — fixed by dropping the call (the delegated `#panel` input/change listener already covers colour inputs, exactly as Warping). **Verified live** (proper 2×rAF awaits between changes — a synchronous first pass gave false failures against the rAF-debounced DOM): all 16 presets render with sensible primitive counts and **WYSIWYG true** for every one (`currentSVG()` === `sceneSVG(buildScene(P),P)`); Placement/Modulation/Render switches swap the right panel blocks; Warp 0 → ring-vertex radii spread 0.0016px (exact regular no-op), 0.4 → 23.8px; seed reproducible in fill mode AND jitter mode (same seed → byte-identical SVG, ≠ a different seed); PNG a real 234 KB `image/png`; Figma posts `organica-svg` with the `<svg>` payload; 0 unlabeled controls; preset round-trip exact (modulation + petals + render restored); Halftone Sphere's gradient flipped mid-verification to the limb-darkening curve (small marks centre → large at rim) so it reads as a sphere, not a burst; zero console errors on a genuinely fresh tab. Files: `radial/index.html` (was `broken-ring/index.html`, moved + heavily extended), `vercel.json` (`/radial/` rewrite), `index.html` (nav link "Radial" + `.nav__link--radial` `#5a5e82` accent). Shipped to production.

## Moved from CLAUDE.md on 2026-09-28 (Aug 26 – Sep 26, newest-first as they were inline)

- **September 25, 2026 — FVS Symbol: drag column / row borders.** Diego: "in symbol for rectangular grid … make easy to change column and row weights by dragging the col and row size via UI". `fvs/index.html`: `buildTrackHandlesSVG()` adds a preview-only handle (`.sym-track`, wide transparent hit rect + dashed `--tool` line, col-/row-resize cursor) on every inner border of any rect Symbol grid that carries Loom tracks (`symbolTrackGrid`, `trackBorders`); `bindSymbolTrackDrag()` listens in the capture phase so a border drag never starts the marquee/click selection; `moveTrackBorder(axis, i, pos)` trades size between the two neighbouring tracks (total kept, `TRACK_MIN_FRAC` 4% floor), re-resolves the grid with `Organica.loadLoomGrid` so the cells keep their content, and on the Rectangular generator writes `tracksToWeights` (mean 1, 2 decimals) into `grid.params`, the canvas `fvsFrame.params` and the Column/Row weights fields live; releasing clears the (now stale) Suggest gallery. Works on canvas grids and on the legacy square-frame grids (`F.X/F.Y` inverted). Verified with real mouse drags in the Browser pane: col weights `1.6,0.4,1,1`, row weights `1,0.52,1.48,1`, cells and selection untouched, handles absent from the exported SVG, a click still selects a cell, regenerating from the written weights reproduces the tracks within 0.4 units, the legacy 3×3 border follows the pointer (−1 px). Suite 320/320 (`symgrid:drag`). Not committed.

- **September 25, 2026 (still later) — FVS Symbol: the 1px light lines between Components, fixed.** Diego: "fai un debug su symbol tra i diversi componenti vedo delle righe vuote di 1px". Measured, not eyeballed: a Symbol of Components made of solid squares must be solid black inside the margin, so every light pixel is a seam. Three causes, found in order: (1) **the main one** — every nested Component painted its own paper right before its ink; the next cell's paper (a hair wider than the cell after `toFixed` rounding, e.g. 320 × 0.7594 = 243.008) covered the previous cell's ink along the shared border — a full-white (255) 1px line on every row of every border (284/284 rows on the test). (2) Anti-aliasing conflation: two shapes meeting exactly on a border each get half-covered pixels where the border falls inside a pixel (any non-integer zoom), adding up to a light hairline — between Symbol cells and between a nested Component's own cells (e.g. the mid-lines at 151.2/345.6 px). (3) On polygon grids a Fill-fitted Component was centred on the cell's **centroid** but sized to its **bounding box**, so asymmetric cells (hexagons cut by the page margin, triangles) showed an uncovered strip. Fixes in `fvs/index.html`, shared by `symbolSVGFromItems` and `drawSymbolCanvas` (the canvas renderer was restructured into the same two passes): all nested papers are painted first, under all ink; each cell's content and its clip reach `symbolBleed(F)` = max(0.5, 0.1% of the page) past the border (`cellBleed`, `bleedCellOutline`), and a nested Component's own cells do the same in its units (`nestedBleed`); Fill centres on the box (`polygonBoxOffset`, zero for symmetric cells and every Figure lattice; the suggestion engine samples around the same centre). Verified: solid-Component leak count 0 SVG + 0 Canvas on rectangular / bento / wave / hexagonal / triangular / diamond / organic / masonry / radial (annulus) at 500, 537, 811, 1080 px (before: up to 7,696 px); the original arc repro renders without lines; a pixel diff of all 25 catalog Figures vs the previous commit: 20 identical, 5 differ only where the new render is *darker* (126–431 px of 90,000 — the seams filling in), 1 pixel lighter in the whole catalog. Suite 319/319 (new `symbol:seams`; 46 intended hash changes — every SVG that goes through the Symbol renderer), twice in the same page. **Found, not fixed (scope):** the Component tier's own renderer (`buildComponentSVG`/`drawComponentCanvas`, gallery + Component export) has the same conflation seams between its cells, fainter (luma ≤ 64 vs the Symbol's 255) — the same per-item bleed would fix it but changes every Component export hash. Not committed.

- **September 25, 2026 (later) — FVS: Component up to 4×4, Symbol = canvas + complex grids + saved Components + suggested combinations.** Diego: "Component si spinge fino alla generazione di componenti a griglie 4×4, su Symbol invece introduciamo canvas e griglie più complesse, Symbol viene creato dalla generazione di componenti salvati, più l'algoritmo può suggerire come combinarli." Plan-mode analysis first (`~/.claude/plans/component-si-spinge-fino-tender-valiant.md`), decisions via AskUserQuestion: Component 1–4 × 1–4 rectangular, no Loom; Symbol cells = saved Components only; suggestions on all four axes (edge continuity, arrangement rules, visual balance, variant gallery); Canvas = format + margins + physical units/DPI/bleed. Then built in four phases, `fvs/index.html` + `shared/shapes.js` (additive: `frameDims`, `resolveCellPlacement` accepts `natural` as `{w,h}`). **Phase 1** — `#sel-grid-type` → `#sel-grid-cols`/`#sel-grid-rows`; `setComponentGrid(spec)` (accepts the recipes' `'square3x3'` strings, `{cols,rows}` or a saved `entry.grid`); Loom import removed from the Component (legacy Loom entries still load via `state.loomGrid` + `#grid-legacy-hint`); `frameSize` of a rect grid = its long side (letterbox, square grids unchanged); `isCanonical2x2` now requires cols = rows = 2; 1×1 greys out the pattern rules. **Phase 2** — Symbol **Canvas** section (`SYMCANVAS_PRESETS`, Screen/Print, mm/in, DPI, bleed, margin %) stored on the Loom model as `canvas.fvsFrame`; **Generate grid in canvas** runs `loom/js/generators/registry.js` via dynamic `import()` (`symbolGridModel`, 12 generators, gap 0; `kiwi.min.js` now loaded for Bento); `symbolFrame(grid)` → the frame the Symbol draws in (canvas w×h, or the old square with byte-identical coordinate order — `F.X/F.Y` keep `half + v − origin`); `exportSymbolPrint` (SVG mm + bleed + crop marks, PNG at DPI + pHYs); Grid tier tiles a non-square Symbol letterboxed; preview frame fits any proportion. **Phase 3** — `state.symbolPalette` [{name, weight}] + *Components* panel section + the Choose-content overlay in palette mode; Seeds no longer offered as Symbol content (old Seed cells / Figures still render); **Arrange** (`SYMBOL_ARRANGE`, `arrangeCells`) replaces the old 50/50 Seed/Component random fill; rect Components placed by their tight box. **Phase 4** — Suggest engine: `analyseComponent` (64-px ink mask, cached name+savedAt), `suggestGridContext` (rect shared segments; polygon edges bucketed by line so partial/curved borders count; grouped per cell pair, 10 inset samples), `choiceBits`/`pairScore`/`scoreSymbolCandidate` (continuity, balance, surprise), generators = every Arrange rule + `refineTurns`, `beamCandidate` ×3, weighted mix ×2; `suggestSymbols` ranks + dedupes; gallery `#symbol-suggest-gallery` above the canvas; **More like this** re-draws 8–20% of cells; locks kept; deterministic. Rect 6×6 ≈ 45 ms, triangular 118 cells ≈ 70 ms. Suite 282 → 318 (`comp:*`, `symcanvas:*`, `symgrid:gen:*`, `arrange:*`, `suggest:*`), every pre-existing case byte-identical (Figures included). **Two pre-existing problems fixed in the same pass:** (1) the long-standing console `rotate(undefined)` errors came from Symbol cells missing fields (the suite's rename test builds `{source, componentName}` only) — `buildSymbolItems` now completes any partial cell with `withPlacementDefaults` (which also gained `rotation/flipH/flipV/scale` defaults); 0 occurrences over 3 runs. (2) A second suite run in the same page changed `figstrip:hist`/`undoredo`/`nohist`/`play:shuffle:ui`: the Figure history is capped at 50 (full on run 2, so its length stopped growing) and the leftover `state.figureRecipe` was re-applied on entering the Figure tab (the next identical step became a history no-op) — the battery now starts with an empty history and no recipe; 3 consecutive runs 318/318. The app's own undo/redo was correct throughout. `docs/FVS.md` §5/§6/§6a. Not committed.

- **September 25, 2026 — FVS Component: generation-engine debug across grid types.** Diego: debug the Component generation algorithm on several grid types — correct, wide coverage, no bugs, no useless/redundant candidates. Audit battery (every named rule + Random/Exhaustive on Square 2×2/3×3/4×4, Loom rect 4×1/2×2/2×3/4×2, Bento, Hexagonal; structural + raster look-alike checks within and across rules). **Bugs found + fixed (`fvs/index.html`):** (1) the 2×2 literal tables (TL,TR,BL,BR) ran for ANY 4-cell grid — a 4-cell Loom row got 2×2 semantics and Radial was offered on it → new `isCanonical2x2()` (4 cells laid out row-major 2×2) gates the literal path everywhere (`ruleCR`, Checkerboard, Radial, `radialEligible`, FAMILIES). (2) flip H + flip V ≡ a 180° turn for any shape, so Checkerboard's `(0°, H+V)` pair was always a copy of `(0°, 180°)` and Exhaustive listed every such pair twice → `canonicalState`/`componentKey`, `dedupeComponents` compares canonical keys; the pair became `(0°, flip H)`. (3) **Hexagonal Loom grids**: `cellColRow`'s median-based centroid binning (slivers drag the median down) + the hex's offset columns put every full hexagon on one `(col+row)` parity → Checkerboard/Mirror/Diagonal (and parity colour rules) collapsed to Identity; new FVS-local `polygonLatticeColRow` re-bins from full-size cells and folds doubled rows (`componentCellColRow` wrapper — `shared/shapes.js` untouched, so the Symbols tier still has the old binning). (4) Random/Exhaustive fell back to per-cell independent noise on any non-4-cell grid (Exhaustive over the 512 cap on a 3×3 as soon as Rotation was on) → FAMILIES build through a shared `GRID_BUILD` (the same builders the named rules use) on any grid; `exhaustiveTotal()` is now the deduped count (3×3: 24 R / 167 R+F). (5) Random could repeat → skips structural and visual repeats, refills to the requested count. (6) Row mirror on a single row / Column mirror on a single column were Identity in disguise → greyed out (and excluded from FAMILIES). **Redundancy:** `generate()` now hides candidates that PAINT the same with the current Element (`lookAlikeFilter`, the starter gallery's `componentVisualSignature`; RES grows with cell count past 16) and says how many in `#gallery-status` (Circle + Pinwheel → 1 shown, 7 hidden). **Coverage:** beyond 2×2, Mirror adds 3 whole-grid book-matched mirrors, Radial adds 8 *tiled* (rosette per 2×2 block → four circles on a 4×4); both also in FAMILIES. Suite 271 → 282 (`engine:*`); 8 intended baseline changes (Checkerboard pair, 3×3/4×4 Mirror/Checkerboard/Radial lists, one look-alike dropped on the 2×2 Arc Checkerboard, `triangle:3x3:mirror` index shift), re-run clean 282/282. `docs/FVS.md` §5. Not committed.

- **September 21, 2026 — FVS Figure: a workspace to play in (phases 1–4 of the UX plan).** Phase 1 (pipeline strip with a thumbnail per step, starting gallery of the 25 figures, recipe undo/redo) was built in a parallel session and committed as `76cb46f` — two sessions were briefly editing `fvs/index.html` at once; I stopped, waited for that commit, checked it (suite 208/208) and built on it. Mine, in `fvs/index.html`: **persistent active step** (`state.figureStep`, `setFigureStep`, `showFigureStep`; kept across edits, reset only for a new figure) with a **step panel** that follows the card (Seed thumbnails + Style/Ink/Paper; layout thumbnails + rows/rings; Grid types; Rotate/Mirror), the old form folded under **Advanced**; **cell tools** on the Symbol step (`paintCell` writes a Rule: a cell → `when {index}`, Shift → the class via `figureClassWhen`; pointer drag with one history entry per gesture), **rule chips** (`describeRule`; switch off = `off:true`, skipped by `applyClassRules`), the Advanced form keeps rules it cannot express (`isFormRule`); **Mirror / Rotate handles** (`renderFigureHandles` from the per-level frame/box `state.figureShown.metas`, `applyHandle`, `figureHandleAction`; `+ Grid` now selects the new Grid); **play** — `FIGURE_MUTATIONS` in five lockable groups, `figureNeighbours(def, seed)` (deterministic, valid, ≤ 9) and `figureShuffle(def, locks, seed)`, Variations overlay, Lock toggles, Space/V/[ ]/1–6/R/M shortcuts. A bug found on the way: `applyFigureRecipe` stored a recipe before validating it (an invalid JSON stayed "current"); now validated first. Reach measured with real UI events: stepped tree 4 actions, hexagon 5, bow tie 6 (identical to the preset recipes); painting a Shift-click on a down triangle reproduces the Sierpinski and three actions the trapezoid. Suite 208 → 237. Not committed to prod.

- **September 22, 2026 (still later, ×6) — FVS: the Component auto-default starter gallery expanded to 4 variants per rule type.** Diego: "espandi la galleria di default per tipologia, crea 4 varianti." `populateComponentStarterGallery()` now takes each rule's own first `STARTER_VARIANTS_PER_RULE` (4) candidates instead of just `[0]` — 16 total on an eligible grid (4 rules × 4 variants), 12 when Radial is ineligible. Suite: `auto:populates-on-entry` updated `4,true`→`16,true` (the one intentional change; every other case unchanged). Verified live: 16 tiles render in a real 4×4-by-type grid (checkerboard/pinwheel/mirror/radial rows), each visually distinct. Not yet in prod.

- **September 22, 2026 (still later, ×6b) — FVS: the starter gallery can no longer show two look-alike tiles.** Diego, after a debug pass on the feature above found nothing structurally wrong: "fai in modo che nella generazione di default non ci siano componenti uguali." Real gap: each rule's own `dedupeComponents()` only drops **exact cells-array repeats within that one rule** — it can't catch two *different* rotation/flip states, even from two *different* rules, that still **paint** the same picture. Two ways that happens, both confirmed live, not assumed: (1) a **universal identity** — `flipH+flipV` together is always the same transform as a 180° rotation, for *any* shape, so `state(0,true,true)` and `state(180,false,false)` are structurally distinct cells but always render identically (found in the plain Arc default itself: `checkerboard-2`'s pair silently duplicated `checkerboard-0`'s); (2) a **shape's own symmetry** — a left–right-symmetric Seed (an isosceles Triangle with a centred apex, a Circle…) makes `flipH` alone a no-op, so e.g. two of Mirror's 4 named seed-cell arrangements collapse into the same picture, and a fully rotationally-symmetric Circle collapses ALL 16 states into one.
  - Comparing the **cells array** or even the raw SVG markup can't catch this — `rotate(90)` and `rotate(0)` are different text even when a symmetric shape renders them identically. New `componentVisualSignature(comp, grid, seed, size)` rasterises each candidate with the same `drawComponentCanvas()` the PNG export already uses (small offscreen canvas, synchronous Canvas2D fill — no `<img>` load, so `populateComponentStarterGallery()` stays synchronous), then reduces the pixels to a coarse 10×10 alpha-weighted-luma block average. A first attempt hashed the raw RGBA bytes exactly and **failed**: rotating a truly-symmetric shape by 90° still lands each anti-aliased edge pixel on a slightly different sub-pixel offset (float rounding inside the rotation matrix), so a byte-exact digest saw two visually-identical circles as "different" — confirmed by direct hash comparison in the browser. Fixed by comparing the coarse block signatures with a small tolerance (`VISUAL_MATCH_TOLERANCE = 3`, avg per-block luma diff on a 0–255 scale) instead of exact equality — "close enough to be the same picture," not "byte-identical file."
  - `populateComponentStarterGallery()` now walks each rule's **full** candidate list (not just `.slice(0,4)`), skipping any whose signature already matches one already picked, and **backfilling** from the same rule's remaining candidates to still reach `STARTER_VARIANTS_PER_RULE` (4) where the rule has enough non-duplicate variants of its own — Checkerboard/Pinwheel/Radial (6/8/8 raw candidates) reliably backfill back up to 4; Mirror (exactly 4 raw candidates, no pool to draw from) can end up short when it has an internal duplicate.
  - **Verified**: a fully symmetric Circle Seed — previously 16 "distinct" tiles that were actually 16 copies of the same 4-identical-circles picture — now correctly collapses to **1**. The plain default Arc Seed stays at 16 (confirmed both counts and the actual candidate IDs shift — `checkerboard-2`/`-3` are now skipped as duplicates of `-0`/`-1`, replaced by backfilled `-4`/`-5`), all 16 tiles visually distinct on screen. A Triangle-carryover scenario (left–right symmetric, centred apex) drops from 16 to **14** — Mirror contributes only 2 of its own 4 (no backfill pool), Checkerboard/Pinwheel/Radial still reach 4 each — confirmed correct via screenshot, no two tiles look alike. Regression suite: 271 of 272 cases unchanged; `auto:populates-on-entry` intentionally updated `16,true`→`14,true` (its fixture happens to run right after a test that leaves the Element on Triangle). `fvs/index.html` only — no shared-file changes. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 22, 2026 (still later, ×5) — FVS: Element-level Content (Mask / Subtraction mask), Phase 1 — plus the Component tier's auto-default starter gallery.** Diego wanted the two behaviours FVS's Component tier already has via its **Role** feature (`Container`/`Mask` — a whole Component clips/knocks-out against a picked underlying reference) available on a single **Element**, one tier down. Confirmed by direct question, not assumed: Diego's "maschera" = the existing **Mask** (hole inside, shows the reference outside); "maschera a sottrazione" = the existing **Container** (shows the reference only inside). A fourth idea — "Container" as a group/pattern of several *loaded* graphic elements — is a genuinely different, open-ended feature (needs its own data model) and stayed **out of scope**, a disabled dropdown option only. Separately, switching Element→Component used to show a blank "No components generated yet" — Diego wanted an immediate starter gallery mixing several different named rules, staying live (regenerating) until an explicit action locks it.
  - **Element Content, Phase 1** (`fvs/index.html`): new `state.elementContent`/`state.underlyingElementName`, a "Content" panel section (Filled/Mask/Subtraction mask/Container-disabled) between Seed and Appearance, and a second, independent picker overlay (same `LIBRARY` store Role's picker already reads — `resolveUnderlyingComponent` reused verbatim). Two new single-shape boundary helpers, `elementBoundaryClipContent`/`elementBoundaryMaskContent`, siblings of the existing `componentBoundaryClipContent`/`componentBoundaryMaskContent` but for one shape instance instead of a cells array; a new `buildSeedContentSVG` mirrors `buildComponentSVGBody`'s `<clipPath>`/`<mask>` wrapping. `buildSeedPreviewSVG` gets one new branch at the top — delegates to `buildSeedContentSVG` when Content isn't Filled, falling back to plain Filled when nothing's picked yet. **Scoped to the Element tier's own two previews only** (the big canvas + the 6-tile strip); Component/Symbol/Grid/export propagation and the Canvas2D twin are an explicit, later Phase 2.
  - **A real bug, caught live not assumed**: the `<clipPath>`/`<mask>` `id` was keyed only on the underlying reference's name — but `url(#id)` resolves **document-wide**, not scoped per `<svg>`, and the same reference renders simultaneously in 7 places (6 tiles + the big canvas). All 7 computed the identical id, so the browser picked whichever matched first, showing a tiny wrong-scaled clip on most of them (looked like "the clip is broken/tiny" until traced — confirmed correct geometry via an isolated `getBoundingClientRect` + a rasterized pixel-count check before finding the real cause). Fixed by folding `rotation`/`flipH`/`flipV`/`size` into the id, making all 7 simultaneous renders distinct.
  - **Component auto-default starter gallery**: new `state.componentAutoGenerated` (tri-state: `null` = never populated, `true` = still the live untouched starter set, `false` = user did something explicit) + `state.componentAutoGenSignature`. `COMPONENT_STARTER_RULES` (checkerboard/pinwheel/mirror/radial-if-eligible, each rule's own `[0]` candidate) feeds `populateComponentStarterGallery()`. `setTier('component')` auto-populates on a genuinely fresh entry; `renderGallery()` re-checks the signature (seed+appearance+cell count) right after its existing cell-count-invalidation block and regenerates while still live. Every explicit action (`generate()`, `clearGallery()`, `applyLibraryEntryToUI()`, `runBuiltinRecipe()`, `runFigureRecipe()`'s component branch) sets the flag `false`; `pushUndo()`/`undoComponents()` now also snapshot/restore it, so undoing past an explicit action correctly un-locks the starter gallery.
  - **Verified**: live in the Browser pane — Mask/Subtraction mask render correctly (screenshot-confirmed, both directions) once the id bug was fixed; no-reference fallback is byte-identical to Filled; fresh Component entry shows 4 starter candidates (3 on an odd grid, Radial ineligible); changing the Element while live re-renders the new shape (confirmed via the gallery's own SVG `d`, since `renderGallery()` already reads `getSeed()` fresh regardless); Generate/Manual/Clear/a Library load each lock it; Clear stays blank on re-entry; a grid resize while live rebuilds at the new cell count; Undo restores the live flag. Suite 253→272 (19 new cases: `content:*`, `auto:*`). `docs/FVS.md` new §6a. Not yet in prod.

- **September 22, 2026 (still later, ×4) — FVS Element tier: the 6-tile transform preview gets an individual label under each tile.** Diego: "le label posizionale individualmente sotto ogni preview" — the single shared caption under the whole strip is replaced by a per-tile caption (90° / 180° / 270° / Flip H / Flip V / Flip H+V), matching each tile's own transform. `SEED_PREVIEW_STATES` entries gained a 4th element (the label); `renderSeedPreview()` wraps each tile in a new `.fvs-seed-tile` (svg + `.fvs-seed-tile__label`) instead of emitting bare `<svg>`s into `#seed-preview`. CSS: `.fvs-seed-preview-label` (the old shared caption) removed; `.fvs-seed-tile` is a small column (svg on top, label under), `.fvs-seed-tile svg` keeps the `height:auto` fix from the previous note. Suite 253, unchanged. Not yet in prod.

- **September 22, 2026 (still later, ×3) — FVS Element tier: the 7-tile transform preview trimmed to 6, enlarged, labelled.** Diego: add a caption under the strip; check whether the 0° tile means anything new versus the canvas below it, and whether 7 is the right count; enlarge the tiles to line up with the canvas. Checked, not assumed: rasterised `buildSeedPreviewSVG(seed,0,false,false,400)` (the canvas) and `...,48)` (tile 1) to the same resolution and diffed — **0 byte difference**, confirming tile 1 (0°/no-flip) is pixel-identical to the big canvas, just smaller. The count of 7 is mathematically deliberate, not arbitrary — the strip is two short cycles (4 rotations 0/90/180/270°, 4 flip states none/H/V/both) sharing one common origin, 4+4−1=7 — but that shared origin is also exactly what the canvas already shows. Diego's call (asked directly): drop it, keep 6. `SEED_PREVIEW_STATES` now `[90,180,270]-rotation` + the 3 flip states, no `[0,false,false]`. New `.fvs-seed-preview-wrap` sets the strip's width to `min(60vh,70vw)` — the same value `.fvs-element-frame` already used — so the strip's left/right edges line up with the canvas below it (measured: both 555px); each tile is `flex:1` + `aspect-ratio:1` instead of a fixed 48px, so they grow to fill that width (measured 87.5px each, up from 48). New `.fvs-seed-preview-label` caption: "Rotated 90° / 180° / 270°, then flipped horizontal / vertical / both". Suite 253, unchanged (the suite doesn't touch this strip). Not yet in prod.

Slider arrow-key nudging is the native `<input type=range>` behaviour, nothing added. Suite 240→253.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×14) — FVS figure model: the four leftovers closed.** (1) **Bow tie**: `rasterMask` used the white letterbox as the reference's ground, so every silhouette came out solid and the overlap check could not tell shapes apart (~59% for everything, even the hexagon that visibly matched); it now reads the image's own corners. With the fixed check, over 24 candidate constructions the best bow tie is still tier + rotate 90° + mirror over the right edge (78% overlap, proportions ×1.16) and the hexagon (tier + mirror over the bottom edge) is 82% (×0.93) — the residual proportion gap is compatible with the photo's distortion (the two ratios lean opposite ways), so the construction stays. (2) **Per-cell Seed settings**: `cell.seedParams` (geometry uses it when present), Cell properties buttons *Use Element* / *Default*, recipe `seed: 'live'`, a checkbox in the Figure form. (3) **Hexagonal lattices and 60° poses**: `hexLoomModel(rings)` with `ring`/`sector` classes, `snapPose()` (multiples of 30°), `rotate: 'sector'`, three presets; rules gained `ring` and `sector`. (4) **Recursion**: a Grid figure becomes the tile of the next Grid (`promoteFigureToTile`, raw tile entries `{raw:{size,inner,box}}`, placement by the drawn box, `lastFigureMeta`), up to three grid levels with an optional per-level `transform`, a 20 000-shape guard and per-level check statistics (`state.figureLevelStats`); the form edits two grid levels, three presets. Suite 171 → 200 cases; the 187 earlier cases untouched except the intended catalog count. Not committed to prod.

Tested on a real crop of the photo: overlap 62%, ratio 0.82 — the photo is stretched vertically, the check says so honestly. Exports: `exportByTier` routes the Figure tier to the underlying tier's exporter; the Grid PNG size now follows the drawn frame (a Mirror changes it). `tileSelectedInGrid(stay)` no longer forces the Grid tier inside recipes.
