- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×12) — FVS figure model, step 1: Grid corrections.** Start of the plan to generalise the triangle work into a Seed-independent figure model (Seed · lattice · slot classes · class→pose/content rule · composition + transform) with automation (recipe v2, guided panel, reference overlay + checks, image→recipe). Done so far: (1) the Grid can tile the **live Symbol** (`LIVE_SYMBOL` tile, no save step; recipes store it as 'Recipe symbol'); (2) a Grid's tiles share ONE `<defs>` and drop the per-tile `<metadata>` — verified pixel-identical (0 differing pixels over the 12 triangle figures) and string-identical after stripping metadata over all 7 built-in recipes; sizes −7% (recipes) / −24% (triangle grids). This also **corrects my previous note**: identical clip polygons are shareable across tiles (clip units are the referencing element's own space), so the earlier "unique ids per tile" change was unnecessary and is gone; the blank tiles were the Symbol's paper rect; (3) Mirror/Rotate use the tiles' **drawn content box** (`symbolContentBox`, empty cells excluded, mapped through each tile's flip/turn/scale) instead of cell boxes. Suite 136 → 139 cases; 18 baseline entries changed on purpose (metadata/defs only — recipes, variants, plates, tier grids). Next: class context + generic class→pose rule, recipe v2 (`runFigureRecipe`), guided "Figure" panel, reference overlay + report.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×11) — FVS: "Form-based FVS · Triangle Symbol" reconstructed from a photo, with the tools it needed.** Diego's second test image (a red print: 4 columns Component / repeated / mirrored over one axis / over two axes × 3 rows). Study (crops via `sips`): col 1 = triangular lattice with up cells filled and down cells empty (side 2 Sierpinski, a trapezoid = bottom row up-down-up, side 4); col 2 = a **tier** (trapezoid of three macro triangles up-down-up, each holding the col-1 Symbol, down ones turned 180°) stacked twice → widths 3,4,3,4 units; col 4 = the tier mirrored over its bottom edge (hexagon star); col 3 = the tier rotated 90° and mirrored over its right edge (bow tie — the photo's is a little wider than ours). New in `fvs/index.html`: `triangleLoomModel(n)` (exact triangular lattice, bbox-centre placement anchor — the true centroid is h/6 off and made the cells drift), `tierLoomModel(stack)`, `polyOrient()`; Symbol cell source **`empty`** (Choose-content tile, skipped by `buildSymbolItems`, no overflow check); Symbol rule **`orientation`** (up/down → Filled/Empty, a filled down cell turned 180°; `applySymbolRule` now accepts content-level results `{empty, turn}` and passes `orient` in the cell context); Grid types **Triangle 2–4 / Tier / Tier ×2** (`grid.tri`: tile width = cell width, down tiles rotated, the Symbol's own paper rect stripped so overlapping frames don't cover each other, and — CORRECTED in the next note — the per-tile unique `clip-cell-N` ids, which turned out not to be needed: the overlap was the paper rect, not the clips); **Rotate figure + Mirror** (`figureWithMirror`, byte-identical output when both are off). Suite 123 → 136 cases (the 123 unchanged). Pitfall for anyone testing several SVGs in one page: clip ids collide across documents — render each as an `<img>` data URI. Not committed to prod yet.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×10) — FVS: the leftover points closed (A–D).** Phase A: `Tile · <id>` entries (written by *Tile in Grid* and the recipes) are marked `auto`, filtered out of every list by `libraryNames()` and pruned at boot and after cloud pull (`pruneAutoLibraryEntries`); Print keeps a non-square artwork's proportions (`svgBaseDims`, `printComponentDims(baseW, baseH)` — height follows the viewBox ratio only when it differs from the width, so square output is unchanged); colours normalised through `hexKey()` at every entry point (palette, paper, saved entries, recipes) and compared with it in `recolourSVG`/`plateSVG`, with a comment recording why post-processing works on the finished SVG by string. Phase B: Pinwheel, Mirror, Diagonal, Row/Column mirror read each cell's col/row (`ruleCR()`), so they run on any grid; a 4-cell grid keeps the original literal builders so every 2×2 hash is identical; Radial is the only gated rule (`radialEligible()`, even×even, quadrant walk TL,TR,BR,BL); `FOUR_CELL_ONLY_RULES` became `EVEN_GRID_ONLY_RULES`. Phase C: per-cell colour override (`syncCellColourUI`; `cellInk` now `cell.color || paperInk` — an explicit override always wins, legacy pins are still released at load), Choose-content All/Seeds/Components filter, inline Component rename (`renameLibraryEntry` repoints saved Symbols, Container/Mask refs, the live Symbol and the Grid pick), Component-tier Undo (`pushUndo`/`undoComponents`, 20 snapshots of components + selection + tiled Component, ⌘Z outside text fields), a hint under *By quadrant* on odd grids (behaviour unchanged). Phase D: Export → **Figma** button (`Organica.sendToFigma(tierSVG(), 'FVS')`), `docs/FVS.md`, the Safari/Firefox checklist in the Backlog. Regression suite grew 84 → 113 cases (hygiene, N×M rules, colour override, undo, rename, hint); phases A–C were each merged to `main` with the Vercel deployment READY.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×9) — FVS: complete Variants/Plates, built-in recipes, regression suite.** **Variants/Plates** (Export popover): per-row **paper** colour, **SVG or PNG**, PNG at ×1/×2/×4 in one go, **Print mode** wrapping any tier's SVG (`printWrapSVG`) with bleed + crop marks, **registration marks on plates** (need ≥ 8 mm bleed — shared `registrationMarks` withholds them otherwise), PNG in Print mode at exact px with the DPI chunk (`encodeOutput`, one pipeline for variants + plates); a Stroke variant now reaches Components nested in a Symbol (`nestedAppearance` merges `variantAppearance`). **Built-in recipes** (Element tab, "Start from a recipe" → `BUILTIN_RECIPES`, `runBuiltinRecipe`): one click builds Element → Component (cells from the same builders as the rule dropdown) → colours/colour rule → Grid, replacing the current Element/palette/gallery; 7 shipped — circle, leaf block, leaf wave, leaf wave outline, leaf wave two-ink 4×4, triangle pinwheels 3×3, arc kaleidoscope (each checked non-empty, looked at). **Regression suite**: `fvs/_test-regression.html` loads `/fvs/` in a hidden frame, runs 84 cases inside it (every Seed type, all Component rules on 2×2, 3×3 Checkerboard, colour rules × offsets, Symbol Square 4×4 + Checkerboard rule, Grid alt-flip parity, the 7 recipes, variants, plates), FNV-hashes each SVG (timestamp + last-rule name stripped — module state, not geometry) and diffs against `fvs/_regression-baseline.json`. Open it on the dev server and press **Run** → "All 84 cases match" (verified, and a simulated regression in the colour rules flagged exactly the 5 affected cases). When a change is intentional: Run → "Show JSON to record" → paste into the baseline file. Runs are deterministic (3 consecutive identical, including the first on a fresh page — that needed pinning Style to Fill after selecting Segment, which auto-switches to Stroke). Not covered: recipe JSON round-trip, PNG bytes. Not pushed. **Standing rule: Claude runs this suite itself after every change to `fvs/index.html` (or the shared files FVS uses: `shapes.js`, `palette.js`, `print-size*.js`, `plate-export.js`) and before committing** — Browser pane on the dev server, Run, report the result; an intended change updates the baseline in the same commit, an unintended one gets fixed first; new FVS behaviour gets new cases in `battery()`.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×8) — FVS colour rules.** The Palette section gained **Colour by** (cell order · Checkerboard · By row · By column · Diagonal bands · By quadrant) + **Start at** (which palette colour leads), so a multi-ink pattern follows the grid's structure instead of the row-major order. One source of truth in `fvs/index.html`: `COLOR_RULES` / `paletteInk(colors, rule, i, cr)` (`cr` = `Organica.shapes.cellColRow` col/row per cell via `colorRuleCR`, binning so it works on square and Loom grids), used by `buildComponentItems` (live), `cellInk` (Symbol cells, memoised `symbolCR()`) and `entryInkAt(entry)` (saved Component thumbs / nested Components, with the entry's OWN palette + rule). `state.colorRule = {mode, offset}` is saved in Component and Symbol library entries (`colorRule`, default `index` = the old behaviour byte-for-byte), restored by `applyLibraryEntryToUI` / `applySymbolLibraryEntryToUI`, and swapped in/out by `renderedSymbolEntrySVG` + the Symbol-library thumbnails like `colors`. Checkerboard alternates two neighbouring palette colours whatever the palette size. Verified by hand: 3×3 Component with 3 inks under every rule (rows `RRR/GGG/BBB`, diagonal `RGB/GBR/BRG`, offset shifts), Symbol 4×4 checker = 8/8, save→change→reload round trip. Grid tier inherits the Symbol's own rule. Not committed to prod.

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×7) — FVS: rebuilt a printed test image, then 6 of the 7 "create at scale" improvements.** Diego uploaded a red-outline/green-fill print made with FVS; reverse-engineering it gave **Arc (Thickness 100, corner, sweep 90)** → col 1 = Component 2×2 **Radial 180° chir +1** (full circle), col 2 = 2×2 **Checkerboard `[180,0,0,180]`**, col 3 = **Grid tier 2×2 of that block** (no alt-flip); row 1 = Style Stroke ~5, row 2 = Fill. Reproduced from the UI tiers alone. Real bug found and fixed on the way: `buildFvsGridSVG` never added `size/2` to the origin-centred `resolveGridCells` output, so Grid tier on square grids drew only the top-left quarter. Then, `fvs/index.html` only, one commit each: **Symbol Square N×M** grid (2–8, `squareLoomModel`); **Export popover Variants** (Fill/Stroke rows → one SVG each via `tierSVG()` + `renderVariant`, Grid tier through `variantAppearance` merged in `renderedSymbolEntrySVG`), **Plates** (one black-on-transparent SVG per palette colour via `plateSVG` + `Organica.plateExport`), **Recipe** save/load JSON (`buildRecipe`/`applyRecipe`); **Component → Grid** ("Tile selected in Grid" button + saved Components listed in the Grid picker, wrapped on the fly as a 1×1 Symbol by `componentTileEntry`, nothing added to `SYMBOL_LIBRARY`); **Checkerboard on any grid size** (Component rule via `componentCellColRow`, plus reversed pairs `(180,0)`/`(270,90)`; Symbol rule gained Rotate A + Swap — defaults byte-identical); **Symbol cells follow the palette** (`color: null`, `cellInk()`; legacy pinned colours released in `applySymbolLibraryEntryToUI`); **gallery captions** (`componentCaption`: rule + per-cell rotation) and an empty-Element warning in the gallery status (`elementIsEmpty`); Choose-content Component tiles show their name. Deliberately NOT done: per-Symbol-cell `seedSnapshot` (Symbol seed cells use the fixed `SYMBOL_SEED_DEFAULTS`, not the live Element, so there is no live-state dependency to freeze), the "override" indicator for cell colour (there is no per-cell colour control), and generalising Radial/Pinwheel/Mirror to N×M (no meaningful N×M meaning). No automated regression suite exists for FVS; checked by hand in the Browser pane (default 2×2 outputs unchanged). Not pushed — pending "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×6) — Extras for the remaining Seeds (Star · Rounded rect · Chevron · Cross · Lens · Segment · Drop · Blob · Circle) + alphabetical Seed dropdown.** Full per-shape list + conflict check first, then built in one pass. **Star**: Type (Star/Burst/Sparkle/Badge), Rotate, separate Tip and Valley rounding, Corner style, Edge curvature (negative = ✦), Twist, Outline, Angle jitter. **Rounded rect**: Type (Square/Landscape/Portrait/Pill), Corner style (round/chamfer/scoop → ticket), Corner mask (11 options → leaf/tab/tag), Skew, Rotate, Edge curvature, Outline. **Chevron**: Corner radius+style, Edge curvature, Lean, Apex flat, Stack (1–5 + gap), Rotate. **Cross**: Arms (2–12: Y, asterisk), Taper, Tip (flat/point), Corner style, Rotate, Type (Plus/X/Y/Asterisk). **Lens**: Crescent, Petals, Outline, Rotate. **Segment**: Angle, Bend, Wave (+cycles), Dashes (+gap), Lines (+spacing). **Drop**: Tail bend, Neck, Petals, Rotate. **Blob**: Frequency (7 = original), Smoothness (0 = original faceted 32-gon), Outline. **Circle**: Rotate (turns a squircle / lobes; holes + slices follow; overflow-only shrink via the new `circleAdvanced.buildWithBounds`). Rejected: Latin/unequal cross arms, tip rounding for Lens/Drop, irregularity for Rounded rect, Blob rotate. **Architecture**: every `xGeometry(…, opts)` keeps its original path function as the fast path (md5 of default grids for all 13 shapes + the Triangle/Polygon/Arc/Truchet/Wedge/Cross stress grids identical before/after). Engine: `triRing` takes a per-vertex corner array (Star tips vs valleys); `ringPathD` got per-corner `chamfer`/`scoop` styles (scoop = the arc of the circle centred on the corner). Rounded rect uses `ringPathD` with absolute-radius cubic fillets (κ = 4/3·tan(τ/4), correct for skewed corners). **The row definitions live once in `Organica.shapes.EXTRAS` (shared/shapes.js)**: FVS builds its panel rows, `getSeed()` keys, snapshot defaults, listeners and Symbols defaults from that table (`SEED_EXTRAS` in fvs/index.html adds the prefix + derived Type shortcuts; Type is never stored), and Genesis Create builds its Star/Segment/Drop/Blob rows from the same table (`gen-x-*`, keys in `DEFAULT_GEN`, older seeds load at defaults). Genesis Square/Circle and Chevron/Cross/Lens/Rounded rect (not in Genesis) untouched. **Dropdown**: the picker orders rows by the `SEED_ICONS` key order (not the `<select>`), so both were reordered: Arc, Arc truchet, Blob, Chevron, Circle, Cross, Drop, Freehand, Lens, Polygon, Rounded rect, Segment, Star, Triangle, Wedge (+ Custom (uploaded) last); Freehand carries `selected` so the default is unchanged; the Symbols "Choose content" list is alphabetical too, but the seeded `seedTypes` array in `generateSymbolCells` is deliberately NOT reordered (its rng picks by index — reordering would change every existing generated Symbol). Verified: no-NaN stress over ~9,900 combinations, visual sheet of 24 representative results, Type↔Custom sync, Reset seed shape, snapshot round-trip + old snapshot at defaults, Symbols defaults equal the original path functions, Genesis star/drop/blob/segment rows + render, picker order + default. Not click-tested: the Components gallery for every new control. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×5) — Polygon Seed extras (FVS + Genesis Create).** Full list + conflict check first, then built. Added **Type** (Triangle/Square/Pentagon/Hexagon/Octagon/Custom — derived from Sides + Rotate; Square sets Rotate 45° so it stands upright; FVS only), **Rotate** (−180..180°), **Step** (star polygon {n/k}: pentagram, heptagram, compounds like {6/2}; capped at ⌊(n−1)/2⌋ and re-clamped when Sides changes), **Corner style** (Round/Chamfer/Scoop — same pull-back points as Corner radius), **Edge curvature**, **Outline (hollow)** (ignored when Step > 1) and **Angle jitter** (own rng stream, so Irregularity's output is unchanged). Rejected: alternate/inner radius (= the Star Seed), nested rings (Inner seed), squash/fit, >20 sides (Circle; slider shared with Star in Genesis). `shared/shapes.js`: `polygonGeometry(sides,corner,irr,seed,radius,opts)` → untouched `polygonPathD` when every extra is default, else `polygonBuild` (same vertices/rng order, each Step loop through **`triRing`**, outline ring scaled toward the centre and reversed, overflow-only shrink+recentre). `triRing` (Triangle's ring builder) gained optional `centre` (Step loops bow away from the true centre) and `style`; its centroid is now the mean of all points (identical for a triangle). `roundedPolyPathD` was NOT touched (Cross unaffected). Regression gate: md5 of polygon default grid, Triangle stress, Cross, Star and Wedge grids identical before/after. Keys `poly*` (`polyRotate/Step/Style/Curve/Outline/Skew`); `SYMBOL_POLYGON` pins Symbols to a plain hexagon; old snapshots load at defaults. Genesis reuses its Sides/Radius/Corner/Irregularity/Seed rows and adds `gen-poly*` rows + `DEFAULT_GEN` keys (older seeds load at defaults). Verified: 8,640-combo stress (no NaN), 480-combo bbox check (worst overshoot 0.56 of 100), pentagram/heptagram solid, Type↔Custom, Step clamp, Reset seed shape, snapshot round-trip + old snapshot, Genesis rows + heptagram render. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×4) — Wedge Seed extras (FVS only).** Diego asked for the full list + conflict check, then approved the plan. Added **Type** (Quarter/Half/Three-quarter/Ring/Custom — derived from Angle + Inner radius, no stored state), **Corner rounding** (every corner incl. the apex — Arc's pie keeps a sharp apex), **Rotate** (−180..180° about the centre), **Edge curvature** (bows the two radial edges, +outward / −inward, inward clamped so edges never cross) and **Irregularity + Seed**. Rejected: Slices+Gap (= Arc → Segments), Pivot/Start angle (Arc's), Taper/eccentric hole (Arc / leaves the sector), Radius/horizontal squash/outline (Scale/Fit, Width/Length, Inner radius). Genesis and Trellis have no Wedge shape, so nothing to align. `shared/shapes.js`: `truchetBandD` was generalised into **`sectorD`** (opts `sq` y-scale, `rot`, `apexRound`, `curve`; arcs carry `A R,R·sq` + x-axis-rotation, an axis-aligned scale keeps every fillet tangent) — Arc truchet calls it with defaults and its output is byte-identical (regression gate: md5 of the 17,496-combo truchet stress, the wedge default grid and the Arc extras grid all unchanged before/after). `wedgeGeometry(angle,inner,squash,opts)` → untouched `wedgePathD` when every extra is default, else `wedgeBuild`: analytic `sectorD` (apex fillet: tangent points `rc/tan(a/2)` along both edges, clamped against the outer fillet; skipped at Angle ≥ 180), a full-disc/annulus branch with Rotate, and — with Irregularity — the sampled `arcBuild`, extended with optional `squash`/`rotate`/apex corner (per-corner cubic constant `4/3·tan(τ/4)·rc/cut`) and bowed edge samples, so the look does not jump. Wobble can push the rim past the box, so `wedgeGeometry` shrinks+recentres ONLY on overflow (Triangle's pattern); everything else keeps `normScale 1` (480-combo bbox check, worst overshoot 0.16). Keys `wedgeRound/Rotate/Curve/Irregular/Seed`; `SYMBOL_WEDGE` pins Symbols to a plain wedge; old snapshots load at defaults. Verified: Type↔Custom sync, Reset seed shape, snapshot round-trip + old snapshot, rounded ring-segment render. Known limits: at 360° Corner rounding and Edge curvature have no effect (no corners/edges); Irregularity at 360° uses 359.5° so the seam is a hairline. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×3) — Arc truchet Seed extras (FVS only).** Diego asked for the full parameter list + conflict check, then approved the plan. Added **Type** preset (Butterfly/Rainbow/Horseshoe/Halo/Custom — derived over several controls, no stored state), **Fans** (Opposed/Single), **Core** (hollow centre), **Spread** (60–180°, centred on the vertical axis), **Reach** (outer radius), **Thickness ramp**, **Spacing curve**, **End rounding** (100 = semicircular cap) and **Segments + Gap**. Rejected: Twist/start angle/pivot offset (leave the cell), four-way fans (overlapping windings), Irregularity (deferred — needs sampling ~24 rings). `shared/shapes.js`: `arcTruchetGeometry(count, ratio, opts)` — extras all default → the untouched `arcTruchetPathD` (verified string-equal over 180 count×ratio combos, so Trellis and saved Components/Symbols are unchanged); else `truchetBuild` → `truchetBandD`, all true `A` arcs with exact analytic fillets (centre at R−rc / r+rc, angular offset asin(rc/(R−rc)), tangent on the radial line at √((R−rc)²−rc²)), no sampling. Invariants kept: bands never overlap (thickness ≤ 0.95 × local step) → still no fill-rule; radii ≤ 50 and spread centred → still inscribed, no fit/clip (64-combo `getBBox` check in 0–100). Keys are `tru*`-prefixed (`arcCount/arcRatio` stay the truchet's; Arc's extras own `arc*`); `SYMBOL_ARC_TRUCHET` pins Symbols to the plain butterfly; snapshots load old entries at defaults. Trellis and Genesis deliberately untouched (Genesis has no Arc truchet). Verified: 17,496-combo stress (no NaN), Type↔Custom sync, Reset seed shape, snapshot round-trip + old snapshot, Halo/Horseshoe renders. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still ×2) — Arc Seed extras, FVS + Genesis Create.** Diego asked for a full parameter list for the Arc, a conflict check against the other shapes, then "build tier 1 and 2, align with Genesis". Added: **Type** preset (Quarter/Half/Three-quarter/Ring/Custom — derived from Sweep, no stored state), **Start angle**, **End rounding** (100 = full semicircular cap), **Segments + Gap** (dashed ring), **Taper** (−100..100, comet/crescent), **Irregularity + Seed**. Rejected as redundant with existing controls: Squash/inner radius (Wedge / Thickness), outer radius (Scale/Fit), other pivots + direction (per-cell Rotation/Flip), hollow outline (Thickness), concentric bands (Arc truchet / Inner seed). `shared/shapes.js`: `arcGeometry(thickness,pivot,sweep,opts)` — with every extra at 0 it takes the untouched analytic path (byte-identical, so saved Components/Symbols and the Radial 4-arc→circle rule are unchanged); otherwise `arcBuild(cx,cy,outerR,startDeg,sweep,innerR,opts)` draws each segment as a ring sampled at ~1° with cubic-fillet corners (`ringPathD`) and fits it via a bbox taken BEFORE rounding, so End rounding never rescales the shape. `arcBuild` is convention-free (caller supplies pivot/radii), which is how Genesis reuses it in its own 0..200 unfitted box — Genesis keeps its old `wedgePathD` path when no extra is active. FVS keys are `arc*`-prefixed (`arcStart/arcRound/arcSegs/arcGap/arcTaper/arcIrregular/arcSeed`; `arcCount/arcRatio` stay Arc truchet's); `SYMBOL_ARC` pins Symbols to a plain quarter arc; snapshots load old entries at defaults. Genesis reuses its existing Irregularity/Seed rows for Arc; new `gen-arc*` rows, Segment gap only shows when Segments > 1. Known limits: a solid (Thickness 100) pie keeps a sharp apex — only the two outer corners round; the sampled mode's fillet is a cubic approximation of a circle. Verified: default geometry unchanged, 324-combo stress (no NaN), snapshot round-trip + old snapshot, Reset seed shape, Fit to canvas (scale → 1.21 on a 3-segment arc), Genesis Create render. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later still) — FVS Element: Scale / Move / "Fit to canvas" for every Seed, and Inner seed (nested copies of any shape).** Two requests, planned first, then built. **Scale + Move X/Y + ⤢ Fit to canvas** live in the Appearance section, next to Width/Length, and ride the same Element-level transform: `Organica.shapeAppearance.stretchTransformAttr(w,l,c,opts)` (shared) gained an optional `{scale,mx,my}` (absent/default → the exact old string), consumed at the three places that build the stretch — `elementPathMarkup` (all SVG), `applyElementStretchCanvas` (canvas) and the Container/Mask boundary (`componentBoundaryClipContent` + its `DOMMatrix`) — so it reaches Component, Symbol, Grid, previews, PNG and SVG with no per-renderer work, and saved snapshots merge over `DEFAULT_APPEARANCE` (old ones load at 1/0/0). Fit is a **one-shot button** (Diego's choice, Genesis `fitToGrid` maths): it measures the seed as drawn at Scale 1 / Move 0 with Width/Length kept, then `scale = 100/max(bw,bh)`, `move = scale·(50 − bboxCentre)`; it bails only when BOTH bbox dimensions are ~0 (a Segment is a zero-height line, so it still fits by width). Re-tested by clicking it: with Style = Stroke the first version cut the outline at the cell edge (getBBox excludes the stroke and the stroke scales with the shape), so Fit now reserves it — `scale = 100 / (max(bw,bh) + strokeW)`. The header note in `shape-appearance.js` that Scale/Move were deliberately not ported is corrected. **Inner seed** (Count 0–8, Ratio 10–95%) is the Arc Truchet count+ratio generalised: `withInnerCopies` wraps every `SEED_TYPES[k].geometry` once (skipping `freehandraw`), appending `Σ scalePathAbout(d, ratio^k, bboxCentre)` to the shape's own `d`, and returns `fillRule:'evenodd'` **only when Count > 0** — so all 14 shapes at Count 0 are byte-identical (verified by hashing preview-SVG + geometry before/after). The alternating ring/gap look needs evenodd (a reversed-winding trick can't be applied to an arbitrary `d`), plumbed through `elementPathMarkup` (`fill-rule` attr) and a new `paintPath()` for the canvas fill sites; Container/Mask silhouettes deliberately ignore it (the union of copies = the solid outline you want). `scalePathAbout` (new, `shared/shapes.js`) handles the full path command set, absolute and relative; verification caught a real bug in it — a path's very first `m` is an ABSOLUTE start point even when lowercase, and scaling it as a delta shrank the copy toward the origin instead of about its centre (fixed; the uploaded-SVG test with `m`/`h`/`v` is what exposed it). Symbols cells stay plain (`SYMBOL_INNER` = count 0); Reset seed shape also resets Inner. Verified in the browser: Fit fills + centres Circle/Polygon/Wedge/Star/Triangle/Segment (Arc, already a full-cell quadrant, is a no-op); Inner gives 4 sub-paths for Count 3 on every shape (Arc truchet 10 → 40); SVG-vs-canvas raster parity 0.035% with Scale 0.8 + Move + Inner 3 in a Component; snapshot round-trip restores Scale/Move/Inner and an old entry loads at defaults; the Mask/Container clip transform carries the scale. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 (later) — FVS Element step: right panel decluttered, actions moved to the floatbar, rotation/flip strip moved onto the canvas.** Diego's 6-item fix list, planned first (plan mode), then built in `fvs/index.html` only. (1) Removed the "Element · step 1 of 4" `.panel-step-head` (Component/Symbol/Grid keep theirs) and audited the block against `docs/CSS-RULES.md`: two inline-style violations became local classes (`#seedtype-picker{width:100%}`, `.fvs-error{color:var(--danger)}`); the 47 JS-toggled `style="display:none"` blocks are explicitly allowed and stay; `css-lint` clean. (2) **Reset seed shape** — new floatbar icon `#btn-reset-seed`; `resetSeedShape()` restores every control inside the CURRENT shape's own `#seed-<type>-block` to its markup default (`defaultValue` / `defaultChecked` / `defaultSelected`), firing each control's real `input`/`change` event so its own listener refreshes the readout + gallery + preview and core.js's slider-fill delegate follows. Type, Appearance and Palette are untouched (Diego's choice); Freehand runs the existing clear; an uploaded/Creator seed has no block → no-op. It replaces the Circle-only `#btn-circle-reset` (button + handler deleted) — verified the circle markup defaults equal `CIRCLE_PARAMS` defs, so nothing was lost. (3) Upload SVG → floatbar icon (ids `btn-upload-seed`/`file-seed` and handlers unchanged). (4) Creator library → floatbar icon opening an `Organica.popover` (`#creator-library-popover`) that holds the existing empty-state + `#creator-library-tiles`; `toggleCreatorLibraryPicker()` deleted, a tile pick closes the popover via the returned `.close()`. (5) The 7-tile rotation/flip strip (`#seed-preview`, id kept so `renderSeedPreview()` needed no change) now sits centred above the frame in the Element tier-view (`flex-direction: column`), frame shrunk 70→60vh so both fit; Freehand's draw canvas still overlays the frame exactly (326,262,540×540 both). (6) The long hint paragraph deleted. There is **no icon library** in the design system — every floatbar draws inline 16×16 stroke SVGs — so the Upload and Library icons are Genesis's own floatbar SVGs verbatim (consistency), and Reset is one new circular-arrow in the same style. The three actions live in `#fb-element-actions` (`display:contents`, toggled in `setTier()` so they only show on Element; `setTier` also closes the popover). Verified: reset on Triangle/Circle/Arc, Appearance preserved, group visible only on Element, upload opens the picker, popover empty + populated + tier-switch close, console clean bar the env 404. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 20, 2026 — FVS/Genesis Triangle Seed: Corner radius, Edge curvature, Irregularity+Seed, Outline (hollow), Type preset.** Diego asked for a full list of possible Triangle parameters, then an implementation plan with no conflicts against other Seed shapes. Three candidates were rejected as redundant, not missing: **Apex Y** and **Squash** (both equal Height) and **Base tilt** (equals per-cell Rotation) — a triangle has only 2 similarity-DOF and Base+Height+Apex X already reach them. `shared/shapes.js` `triangleGeometry(base,height,apexPct,opts)` gained an optional 4th `opts={corner,curve,irregular,seed,outline}`; with every field off it returns the exact pre-existing path (verified byte-identical against a node dump of 45 triangle param combos + all 14 other shapes taken before the edit), so Genesis's 3-arg call and every saved Element are untouched. **Outline** is real geometry — outer ring + a reverse-wound inner ring scaled toward the incentre (thickness = % of inradius, max 95), so the default nonzero rule cuts the hole with no `fill-rule` attribute (same trick as `wedgePathD`) — and is deliberately separate from the shared Style→Stroke rendering option. Outward **curvature** can overflow the 0..100 box, so the function returns a shrink+recentre `normScale/normTx/normTy` *only on overflow* (a blanket `fitToBox` would make the shape jump in size as the slider crosses 0). All new FVS keys are `tri*`-prefixed (`triCorner/triCurve/triIrregular/triSeed/triOutline`) and ids `rg-tri-*`, none generic, so nothing collides with `polySeed/starSeed/blobSeed`, `*Corner` or `circle*`; `SYMBOL_TRIANGLE` pins them to 0 so Symbols cells stay a plain triangle; the library-load back-compat list gives old snapshots the defaults. The Type dropdown (Isosceles/Right/Equilateral/Custom) is a derived shortcut, not stored state — it writes Base/Height/Apex X and reads Custom after any hand edit. **Genesis Create** reuses its existing `corner`/`irregularity`/`seed` rows for Triangle (corner passed raw 0–50, the same precedent Polygon set) and adds two new keys, `triCurve`/`triOutline`, in `DEFAULT_GEN` so older parametric seeds load with them at 0. Genesis deliberately ignores the geometry's norm layer (a transform there would double Stroke widths), so outward curvature can overhang the artboard slightly, exactly as Drop/Blob already can. Verified on the no-store dev server: FVS Element preview + Components gallery keep the hole and the rounded/curved shape across rotations/flips; library snapshot round-trip restores all 5 params and an old entry lacking them loads at defaults; same seed reproduces the same silhouette; Genesis Create shows the right rows for Triangle only (Square still shows just Corner); console clean bar the pre-existing env 404. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".

- **September 15, 2026 — `shared/canvas.js` shipped: the shared canvas/stage layer starts, first piece is zoom/pan → HUD DOM wiring.** Diego: "facciamo uno sviluppo su un modulo shared 'canvas' ... fai un audit, vedi dove è applicato ora e gli screenshots per l'audit. Successivamente cambieremo la UI." Two-part session: an audit first (no code changes), then — on "vai" — the first real piece of the module the audit's own closing agenda pointed at.
  - **Audit** (plan-mode, no repo files touched): two parallel Explore agents mapped what's already shared (`shell.css`'s `#canvas-wrap`/`.org-stage` skeleton — 25 files; `Organica.createZoomPan` — 13 direct callers; `print-size(-panel).js` — 5 tools) against what every tool still hand-rolls (which element *is* the stage — Canvas2D/WebGL/SVG board/p5 — its resize/DPR strategy, and the zoom→DOM glue), plus a full per-tool surface-type table. Published as an Artifact, **"Canvas Surface Audit"** (`https://claude.ai/artifact/9MpvAMkVyxupcpJz1biAE5`): an adoption matrix for all 22 tools, 7 live captures pulled directly off each tool's own render surface (`canvas.toBlob()` / `XMLSerializer`, POSTed to a throwaway local receiver script rather than hand-copied — a first attempt at pasting a `canvas.toDataURL()` string by hand silently truncated a 29,650-char base64 blob to 1,666 chars, confirming that route isn't viable for binary data) — Halide (main+crop-preview), Pulsar (explicit DPR), Camo Turing (WebGL2/Three.js), Membrane (p5-injected), Blob Boundary (SVG board), Living Path (SVG board + hidden glyph canvas), Colornet (main + N channel thumbnails) — and a documented out-of-scope set: Genesis (fixed artboard, canvas-over-SVG input, not a viewer), FVS ("not a single pannable canvas" — no persistent stage at all), Vortex (a real 3D camera; `createZoomPan`'s CSS transform desyncs p5's own mouse coordinates there, by its own code comment), Rhizome (DOM+SVG node-graph hybrid, already on a bespoke `panAlways` wrapper). Closed with the agenda that became this session's scope: (1) zoom/pan → DOM wiring, (2) DPR-aware sizing, (3) surface mounting, (4) multi-surface patterns.
  - **`shared/canvas.js`** (new) — `Organica.canvasZoomHud(opts)`, built on top of `createZoomPan`, not replacing it. A direct grep confirmed the pattern the audit found was genuinely byte-near-identical across exactly 6 tools (Halide/Spore/Pollen/Colornet/Mote/Membrane) — each independently re-deriving `onChange: ({zoom,zoomed}) => { …textContent…; …classList.toggle('visible',…); …classList.toggle('zoomed',…); }`. The one real variation, caught by diffing all 6 rather than assuming the first one generalizes: Halide/Spore/Pollen gate the HUD's `.visible` on `zoomed && canvas.classList.contains('visible')` (don't show the chip if the image itself isn't loaded), Colornet/Mote/Membrane don't need that guard — so the helper takes an optional `hudVisible(state)` predicate (default `state => state.zoomed`) rather than hard-coding one behaviour. `hud`/`level`/`resetEl` each accept an id string, an element, or `null` to opt out (Trellis-shaped tools with no HUD at all can still get the raw zoom/pan); a custom `onChange` still runs, after the HUD update, for tools with real extra needs on top (Living Path/Apostate syncing an overlay's position) — left unmigrated this pass, flagged as a deliberate follow-on, not attempted here. **Camo Turing's transient auto-hide zoom badge (`showZoomBadge`) is a different UX pattern, not a copy of this one** — confirmed by reading its code before assuming it belonged in the migration; left on raw `createZoomPan`, undocumented as a gap rather than silently folded in.
  - **Migrated**: Halide, Spore, Pollen, Colornet, Mote (single-file tools, `<script canvas.js>` added right after `core.js`) and Membrane (`js/main.js`, an ES module — confirmed its top-level `zoomPan`/`ctrl` aren't on `window`, so verification had to dispatch a real `WheelEvent` at `#canvas-wrap` rather than call `zoomPan.zoomBy()` from outside). Each tool's own ~10–16-line wiring block collapsed to a `canvasZoomHud({...})` call carrying only its real deltas (`hudVisible` override for the 3 that need it, `resetEl` for Colornet's `#zoom-reset` button and Membrane's `#btn-zoom-reset`, `isReady`/`min` passed straight through). Membrane's own unrelated `js/canvas.js` (the Loom-derived Canvas Manager panel) is a real filename collision the audit had already flagged — confirmed it's a different path (`membrane/js/canvas.js` vs `shared/canvas.js`), no actual conflict, just a comment added noting the two are unrelated.
  - **Verified** on the no-store dev server, all 6: `node -c` clean on `shared/canvas.js` and every edited file's extracted script; a real image drop + `zoomBy(1.5)`/wheel-event + reset cycle on each confirmed `#zoom-level` reads "150%"→"100%", `#zoom-hud.visible` toggles correctly (including the 3-tool `hudVisible` guard, tested with the canvas deliberately not-yet-visible), and the canvas's own `.zoomed` class tracks state — Colornet and Membrane's `resetEl`-bound buttons confirmed via a real `.click()`; console clean on all 6 (only the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404 elsewhere in the suite, not present locally). `git status` confirms exactly the 7 intended files touched (6 tools + the new module).
  - **Deferred, per the audit's own agenda, not attempted this pass**: DPR-aware sizing (today only Pulsar/Living Path/Apostate compute it — every other raster tool, Halide/Spore/Pollen included, still skips it); surface mounting (Canvas2D/WebGL/SVG/p5 branching, still hand-rolled per tool); the main+preview / board+glyph-canvas / ephemeral-SVG-export multi-surface patterns; Rhizome/Vortex (real architectural exceptions, not migration candidates at all, per the audit). **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session follow-up ("prossimo?" → "procedi") — item 1 finished across every remaining candidate.** Migrated the 5 tools the first pass had flagged but not touched: **Living Path** (`onChange` passthrough keeps its real extra logic — the `specEdit` pointer-events toggle + `syncSpecEditBox()`/`syncProcLine()` calls — while `canvasZoomHud` now owns the `#zoom-level`/`#zoom-hud`/`.zoomed` part, with the same `hudVisible` guard pattern as Halide/Spore/Pollen); **Apostate** (`hud: null, level: null` — it has no `#zoom-hud` markup at all, confirmed by grep before assuming; `onChange` passthrough keeps the tester-view `syncSpecEditBox()` call); **Trellis, Pulsar, Radial** (all three: `hud: null, level: null`, no other custom logic — Pulsar/Radial weren't even wiring an `onChange` before, Trellis's was a no-op). One correction caught mid-verification: the audit's own surface table had called Radial "Canvas2D (built at runtime)" — live inspection during this pass showed its actual pannable element is `#preview-frame`, a `<div>` wrapping an inline `<svg>`, not a `<canvas>` at all; harmless for `canvasZoomHud` (it only needs `.classList`/`.style`, not a real canvas context) but the audit artifact's table is very slightly wrong on this one row, not corrected retroactively. **`shared/_template.html`** updated too, so a new tool starts from `canvasZoomHud` rather than the raw wiring. **Verified** on the no-store dev server: all 5 syntax-clean (`node -c` on each extracted script); Living Path — `zoomBy(1.5)`→`zoomBy` HUD"150%"+visible+`.zoomed`, `resetZoom()`→"100%"; Apostate — `.zoomed` toggles true→false across `zoomBy`/`reset`, confirmed `#zoom-hud` genuinely absent (`!!document.getElementById('zoom-hud') === false`); Trellis — boots with no throw (`isReady` correctly gated on `cellCount > 0`, no grid loaded by default); Pulsar/Radial — a real dispatched `WheelEvent` on the wrap (not the JS API, since neither assigns `zoomPan` to a variable, matching the pre-migration code) toggles `.zoomed` on `#preview`/`#preview-frame`; console clean on all 5, only the pre-existing env 404 elsewhere. `git status` confirms exactly the 6 intended files (5 tools + `_template.html`) plus this note. **Still open**: DPR-aware sizing, surface mounting, multi-surface patterns (agenda items 2–4) — Rhizome/Vortex remain deliberate exceptions, not gaps. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session follow-up ("procedi con il DPR-aware sizing") — a real scope-narrowing finding, then one tool migrated.** Investigated every tool the audit had flagged as "still skips DPR" (Halide/Spore/Pollen/Colornet/Mote/Komorebi/Warping/Trellis) before touching any of them, since Pulsar/Living Path/Apostate's own pattern (`canvas.width = SIZE * DPR`, `ctx.scale(DPR,DPR)` once per frame, `SIZE` itself never changes) only stays safe when the canvas's pixel dimensions are a pure *display* concern, decoupled from content. **That's not true for most of the list**: Halide/Komorebi/Warping/Spore/Pollen/Colornet/Mote all follow this repo's own standing rule ("a single render function serves the preview AND every export — only the resolution differs," `renderFrame(w,h)`/`computePoints(...)` taking the *exact* work resolution as an argument) — `canvas.width` there **is** the dithered grid size / stipple density / channel-separation resolution the user explicitly controls, not a screen metric. Silently multiplying it by `devicePixelRatio` would make an identical settings panel compute a **different image** on a Retina vs. a non-Retina display — a real correctness bug against the project's own WYSIWYG contract, not a sharpness improvement. **Radial** turned out not to be a candidate either, for an unrelated reason caught by reading its code rather than trusting the audit table: its on-screen preview is `#preview-frame`, a `<div>` wrapping an inline `<svg viewBox="0 0 760 760">` — SVG has no DPR concept at all, it's resolution-independent by construction (a second, smaller correction to the same audit-table row flagged in the entry above). **Membrane/Vortex** (p5.js) weren't flagged by the audit in the first place — p5's own `createCanvas` already manages pixel density internally, decoupled from the logical W/H the tools' own Canvas Manager panel exposes, so there was nothing to add. That leaves exactly **one** real, safe candidate: **Trellis** — a fixed 900×900 preview, redrawn from scratch every frame via `renderFrameAt(tNorm)` (Pulsar's own shape), with its PNG export going through a completely separate offscreen canvas (`exportPNG()` → `document.createElement('canvas')` sized `SIZE*scale`, never reads the live preview's pixels) — the exact precondition that makes DPR-scaling the *display* canvas provably safe.
  - **`trellis/index.html`** — `SIZE` is now read from `canvas.width` *before* the DPR resize (so it stays the logical 900 forever, matching every existing `SIZE`-keyed coordinate in `buildDrawList`/`drawList`/`drawGridOverlay`/`exportPNG`/`updateSizeReadout`); `canvas.width/height` become `SIZE * DPR` (`min(devicePixelRatio||1, 2)`, Pulsar's own cap); `canvas.style.width/height` are pinned to `SIZE + 'px'` explicitly — without this, a `<canvas>`'s intrinsic (replaced-element) sizing would inflate to match the new, larger backing store and the preview would silently render bigger inside the wrap on any HiDPI screen, a real layout regression this pins closed. `renderFrameAt`'s per-frame `pctx.setTransform(1,0,0,1,0,0)` → `pctx.setTransform(DPR,0,0,DPR,0,0)` — one absolute reset per frame (not a `save()`/`scale()`/`restore()` pair), since the code already fully repaints every frame from `buildDrawList` with no persisted canvas state to protect.
  - **Verified** on the no-store dev server (Browser pane's own DPR is 2, a real HiDPI double-check for free): fresh load → `canvas.width/height` 1800×1800, `canvas.style.width/height` "900px"/"900px", `getBoundingClientRect().width` exactly 900 (**zero on-screen size change**, confirming the pinning works); loaded a synthetic 2×2 rect grid (`Organica.loadLoomGrid`-shaped, hand-built since no Loom preset was seeded in this environment) → 4 triangles render sharp and correctly positioned; toggled **"Show grid outline"** — the 1px overlay strokes land exactly on the cell boundaries, confirming `drawList` and `drawGridOverlay`'s independent calls agree under the same per-frame transform; `updateSizeReadout()` still reads "1800 × 1800" at the default ×2 export scale (`SIZE` unchanged at 900 — export resolution provably untouched by the DPR change); zoom/pan (this session's earlier `canvasZoomHud` migration) still works layered on top, `zoomBy`/`reset` producing the expected CSS transform; console clean (no errors). **Scope note for next time**: this narrows agenda item 2 to "done" for the one real candidate that existed — Halide/Spore/Pollen/Colornet/Mote/Komorebi/Warping would need a genuinely different approach (a separate DPR-scaled *display* layer sitting on top of the untouched work-resolution canvas, not a shared helper) if Diego ever wants sharper previews there without touching their WYSIWYG content resolution — a bigger, riskier piece of work, not attempted here. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session follow-up ("procedi") — agenda item 3, "surface mounting," investigated and found to be mostly a non-issue; redirected into a real adjacent duplication instead.** The item as literally stated in the audit ("one call that knows whether the stage is a declared `<canvas>`, a dynamically built one, a WebGL context, or an injected p5 canvas") turned out, on inspection, to have almost nothing worth centralizing: every surface-creation site is already 1–3 lines of genuinely surface-specific code (`ctrl('main-canvas').getContext('2d')`; Camo Turing's `new THREE.WebGLRenderer({canvas, context, antialias:false, preserveDrawingBuffer:true})`, where every option is load-bearing per its own code comment; Membrane/Vortex's `new p5(sketch)`, entirely p5's own lifecycle) — wrapping 4 fundamentally different paradigms behind one call would add an indirection layer to save close to nothing, and would risk quietly losing a tool-specific option in the process. **Went looking for what actually IS duplicated near canvas mounting instead**, and found it: the click-to-open + dragover/dragleave/drop wiring, byte-near-identical (confirmed via `diff`, whitespace-only difference) across exactly **3** tools — Halide, Spore, Pollen. Colornet's own version was checked too and correctly excluded: it supports multi-file batch drop (`runBatch(files)`) and JS-wired (not inline `onchange`) file-input change — a genuinely different shape, not a copy. Mote's is different again (video file, no `.drag-over` toggle, has keyboard a11y the other three lack) — also left alone.
  - **`Organica.canvasDropZone`** added to `shared/canvas.js` — narrow by design: first dropped file only (matches the original `files[0]` + type-check behaviour exactly, not a "find any matching file" upgrade), synthesizes it into the given `fileInput.files` so each tool's existing `loadImage(input)` needed zero changes, `dropIcon` accepts `null` for tools with no click-to-open affordance. Migrated Halide/Spore/Pollen's ~10-line block each down to one `canvasDropZone({...})` call; `shared/_template.html` updated to show the new call as the default starting point. **Verified** on the no-store dev server: Halide — a real `.click()` on `.drop-icon` fires the hidden file input's own click handler; a real `DragEvent('drop')` with a fetched sample image hides `#drop-hint` and shows `#main-canvas`; `dragover`/`dragleave` toggle `.drag-over` on `#canvas-wrap` correctly. Spore/Pollen — the drop path alone re-verified (image loads, `#drop-hint` hides, canvas becomes `.visible`). Console clean on all three. `node -c` clean on `shared/canvas.js` and all 3 tools' extracted scripts. **Left deliberately untouched**: Colornet (batch drop), Mote (video drop) — real behavioural differences, not migration gaps. **Agenda item 4** (multi-surface patterns — main+preview, board+glyph-canvas, ephemeral-SVG-export) still open. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session follow-up ("continua sul 4") — agenda item 4, "multi-surface patterns," investigated per-pattern; two of three framings turned out not to be real, the third pointed at a different, much bigger duplication than expected.** Checked each of the audit's three named patterns directly against the code, same discipline as items 2 and 3: **board+hidden-glyph-canvas** (Living Path/Apostate) — already centralized, not a gap at all: both already share `shared/glyph-editor.js`/`shared/glyph-model.js` (built during the Sep 8 variable-font work, before this session), so there was nothing left to do. **Ephemeral export canvas built from SVG** (Mycel/TuneSutra/FVS) — the audit's own framing was imprecise here: read Mycel's and TuneSutra's `exportPNG()` directly, and neither actually rasterizes its SVG markup through an `Image`/`drawImage` step — each **redraws natively** onto the export canvas with its own `Path2D`/`fillRect`/`arc` calls that mirror (not consume) the SVG-building logic, i.e. the same "one render path, resolution differs only for export" shape as Halide/Komorebi, just without an intermediate SVG step. Nothing SVG-specific to centralize there either.
  - **What WAS real, found while reading those same export functions**: every one of them ends in an identical 4-line `atob(url.split(',')[1])` → `Uint8Array` loop → `new Blob(...)`, immediately before an `Organica.download(...)` call — confirmed via `grep -rl "bin.charCodeAt(i)"` at **11 files**, not the 3 the "ephemeral SVG canvas" framing had pointed at: Warping, Komorebi, Radial, Pulsar, Camo Turing, Loom (`js/main.js`), Trellis, Colornet, Mycel, TuneSutra, plus `shared/_template.html`'s own stub. One real complication caught before writing anything: **Loom's version doesn't feed the loop's output straight to a Blob** — in Print mode it passes the raw `bytes.buffer` into `Organica.printSize.embedPngDpi(...)` first, to splice in a PNG `pHYs` (DPI) chunk *before* the Blob ever gets built. A Blob-only helper would have silently broken that. Every other one of the 11 goes `toDataURL → atob loop → new Blob(..., {type: mime}) → Organica.download`, with `mime` always the exact same variable/literal fed to both `toDataURL()` and the `Blob` constructor (verified per-file, not assumed) — so mime can be read straight back off the dataURL's own header instead of being threaded through separately.
  - **`shared/core.js`** gained two layered primitives, next to `Organica.download`/`downloadText`/`stamp`: **`Organica.dataURLToBytes(dataURL)`** → `Uint8Array` (the bare atob loop, for Loom's DPI-splice case and anything else that needs the raw bytes before Blob-wrapping); **`Organica.dataURLToBlob(dataURL)`** → `Blob`, built on top of it, mime parsed off the dataURL's own `data:<mime>;base64,` header via regex rather than passed in. Placed in `core.js` (not `canvas.js`) since a dataURL is just a string — the primitive itself has nothing canvas-specific about it, unlike `canvasZoomHud`/`canvasDropZone` which take a live DOM element.
  - **Migrated all 10 real consumers** (Loom → `dataURLToBytes`, keeping its own `embedPngDpi`/Blob-wrapping logic; the other 9 → `dataURLToBlob`, each a straight 1-line replacement of their 4-line loop) + `shared/_template.html`'s stub. Colornet's `canvasToBlob()` helper — which *returns* a Blob rather than downloading it directly, the one structurally different call site — collapsed to a 1-line `return Organica.dataURLToBlob(url);`, comment about `toDataURL` vs `toBlob` preserved directly above it. **Verified** on the no-store dev server: `node -c` clean on `shared/core.js`, `loom/js/main.js`, and every one of the other 9 tools' extracted scripts; a synthetic 4×4 canvas confirmed `dataURLToBlob`/`dataURLToBytes` produce byte-identical output (`blob.arrayBuffer()` compared element-by-element against `dataURLToBytes`'s own array) with the correct `image/png` mime read off the header; Mycel's real `exportPNG()` ran with no throw; Colornet — dropped a real sample photo, called `canvasToBlob(renderToCanvas(buildPlan(exportScale())), 'image/png')` directly, got back a real 170,621-byte `image/png` Blob; Loom — a real UI flow (Export popover → PNG button click, not a JS-API call, since `js/main.js` is an ES module with no globals to call into) produced no console error and the header status read "PNG saved"; Warping — `exportPNG()` (which defers its real work in a `setTimeout`) settled to "1800×1200 · PNG saved" with no error. Console clean throughout. `git status` confirms exactly the 11 tool files + `shared/core.js` + `shared/_template.html` touched, nothing else. **The whole 4-item centralization agenda from the original "Canvas Surface Audit" is now closed** — items 1 and 2 shipped as scoped, items 3 and 4 each turned out to have little-to-no real value in their literally-stated form and were redirected into the genuine duplication found while investigating them (`canvasDropZone`, `dataURLToBytes`/`dataURLToBlob`). **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session follow-up — a new shared component, `Organica.canvasPresetGrid`, replacing Loom's Canvas-size `<select>` with a hover-preview card grid.** Diego shared a screenshot of Loom's Canvas panel dropdown next to a mockup — a 2×2 grid of cards, each a rectangle drawn at the preset's own aspect ratio (portrait 4:5 / square 1:1 / landscape 16:9 / widescreen 3:2) with a label below, grey when unselected. Spec, confirmed via AskUserQuestion: hover shows a colour-accent preview border without applying anything; click actually changes the canvas; build it as a **shared component** (not Loom-only), following the same generate-mode polymorphic shape as `seedsPanel`/`printSizePanel`, so a future tool could adopt it without duplicating the UI (Apostate's own separate `shared/canvas-size-panel.js` is untouched — different, simpler component); the applied preset keeps a **persistent** accent border independent of hover on a different card.
  - **Real bug caught in verification, not assumed away**: the first CSS draft set both `width` and `height` explicitly on each swatch `<span>` (with `aspect-ratio` set inline per card) — every swatch rendered as an identical 52×52 square regardless of preset, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` on all 7 before trusting a screenshot. Root cause: CSS only derives a dimension from `aspect-ratio` when that dimension is `auto` — with both `width`/`height` explicit, `aspect-ratio` is silently ignored entirely. Fix attempt 2 (`max-width`/`max-height` only, no `width`/`height`) fixed the *ratios* (confirmed 1.78/0.80/1.50/0.71/1.41 etc., matching each preset exactly) but collapsed every box to ~3-5px — a non-replaced empty `<span>` has no intrinsic content size for the browser to solve `aspect-ratio` + `max-*` from in the first place (unlike `object-fit:contain` on a real `<img>`/`<video>`). Settled on doing the fit-within-a-box math directly in JS (`scale = min(slot/w, slot/h)`, then set `rect.style.width/height` in real px) — simpler and fully deterministic, no fighting CSS's aspect-ratio resolution ambiguity for an empty box. `--preset-card-h` (52px) is a real declared custom property on `.preset-grid` (not just a `var()` fallback), read back via `getComputedStyle` so the JS math and the CSS slot size can never drift apart.
  - **`shared/canvas-preset-grid.js` + `.css`** (new pair) — `Organica.canvasPresetGrid(target, {idPrefix, presets, columns, showCustom, selected, onSelect})` → `{el, getSelected, setSelected, destroy}`. Real `<button>` per card (not `div[role=button]` like `seeds-panel.css`'s `.shape-thumb` — native focus/keyboard for free, worth doing correctly since this is greenfield, not a retrofit). Three CSS states on different box properties specifically so they can't conflict: default (`border-color: var(--mid)`), hover/`:focus-visible` (`border-color: var(--tool)`, transient, that card only), `.preset-active` (`outline: 2px solid var(--tool)`, persistent, independent of hover — hovering the already-active card is a no-op repaint of the same colour, hovering a *different* card while one is active shows both accents simultaneously with zero visual fight). "Custom…" is a dashed text-only card inside the same grid (not a separate control outside it) — one click/hover/active state machine, not two; gets `.preset-active` whenever no preset matches current Width/Height, isn't itself clickable-to-apply (same as the old dropdown's inert "Custom…" option).
  - **Loom integration** (`loom/index.html`, `loom/js/main.js`) — `<select id="sel-canvas-preset">` → `<div id="canvas-preset-grid">`; `<link>`/`<script>` added after `panel.css`/`core.js` respectively. `applyCanvasPreset()` restructured to take `name` as an explicit argument (no more `<select>` to read `.value` from — body otherwise untouched, including its own hard-won Gap/Padding-rescale-vs-Width/Height-must-not-rescale-twice comments from an earlier session). `populateCanvasPresets()` → `buildCanvasPresetGrid()`, constructing the component with `presetNameForCurrentSize()` (matches current Width/Height/unit against `CANVAS_PRESETS`) as the initial `selected`. **New, small behaviour added, flagged explicitly rather than silently bundled in**: the old `<select>` never un-selected itself after a hand-edit of Width/Height (it just kept showing the last-picked label, silently stale) — added `syncCanvasPresetGrid()` (silent `setSelected`, never re-fires `onSelect`) called from the very top of `build()`, the one function every relevant path (hand-edit, Mode switch, Unit change, preset click) already funnels through, so the active card can never go stale regardless of which path changed the canvas.

The fit-math's target box shrinks by `(layers-1)×step` first, so the fanned stack's outermost edge still stays inside the card's slot instead of the front layer alone being sized to fill it and the back layers overflowing.

Console clean throughout. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod".
  - **Same-session correction — "ogni finestra deve essere innestata dentro l'altra": the 4 layers were same-size copies offset diagonally, not real nesting.** Diego's correction was precise: Figma's own layers aren't just staggered — each is a strictly smaller rectangle contained within the one behind it (front `454..478`, back `425..507` — front's whole span sits inside back's). The diagonal-fan approach never had that containment property (every layer was the same size, just translated), so it wasn't "nested" in the geometric sense at all.
  - **`shared/canvas-preset-grid.js`** rebuilt on true concentric nesting: the OUTER (back, `i=STACK_LAYERS-1`) layer is fit to the full slot exactly as before; every layer in front of it shrinks by `STACK_STEP*2` px per step (front, `i=0`, shrinks the most — it's the smallest, a strict subset of every layer behind it), all sharing the same centre point. A genuine bug caught immediately after the first version, by direct `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement rather than trusting the screenshot: the shrink formula had front and back's roles swapped (`shrink = i*STEP*2` instead of `(LAYERS-1-i)*STEP*2`), so front — meant to be smallest — came out full-size identical to every other layer; every idle card rendered as one flat square. Fixed by inverting the index.
  - **A second, real problem this uncovered**: with front now genuinely small (the innermost nested window), the existing hover/active behaviour — "hide the other layers, front becomes the single visible rect" — would show a tiny rectangle instead of the clean full-size swatch every other tool's picker already has. Resolved by decoupling front's *idle* size from its *interactive* size: the idle size is set once (small, nested); a second custom property (`--front-outer-w/h`, the same fit-to-slot size the back layer uses) only takes effect via a `:hover`/`:focus-visible`/`.preset-active` CSS rule, growing front back out to the full swatch and fading the frame layers behind it — so idle shows the true nested stack, interacting with a card still shows one clean coloured rectangle, matching the original approved behaviour.
  - **A second real bug, caught by `getComputedStyle()` immediately rather than trusting the visual**: the very first version of that grow-on-hover rule set the idle size via `rect.style.width/height` directly (a plain inline style) — which unconditionally wins over ANY stylesheet rule regardless of selector specificity, so the hover/active CSS override could never actually apply; a hovered card's front layer measured 22×10px (the small idle size) instead of the expected 52×29px. Fixed by routing BOTH the idle and the grown size through CSS custom properties (`--layer-w`/`--layer-h` read by the base rule, `--front-outer-w`/`--front-outer-h` read only by the hover/active rule) — inline `style.setProperty()` on a custom property doesn't carry the same "always wins" behaviour a direct `style.width` does, so the stylesheet's own cascade order decides correctly again.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×11) — Apostate: moved wheel-zoom to the tester (canvas-with-text) view + gave every view mode a real, shareable URL.** Diego: "lo scroll va messo sulla modalità canvas con testo poi diamogli un nome/url a queste diverse modalità ad esempio quando vado su full family." Two changes.
  - **Zoom relocated**: `zoomPan`'s `isReady` switched from `VIEW==='family'` to `VIEW==='tester'`; the zoom-hud now shows only in tester. Since `zoomPan` owns one shared CSS transform on `#board` (the same SVG element every view renders into), leaving tester now explicitly calls `zoomPan.reset()` in the dispatcher — otherwise a leftover tester zoom would visibly carry over and incorrectly scale the Full Family single-glyph view, which has no zoom concept of its own. `renderTester()`'s old `board.style.transform=''` reset (added when zoom was family-only, to keep tester visually static) was removed, since the transform is now tester's own to keep. `onChange` also re-calls `syncSpecEditBox()` on every zoom tick so the transparent `contenteditable` caret overlay keeps tracking `#board`'s real (now-scaled) bounding rect.
  - **Named, shareable URLs per mode** — new `setView(v, push)` / `readViewFromURL()` / a `popstate` listener: every mode switch now writes `?view=tester|family|glyph` (plus `&char=X` for glyph) via `history.pushState`, so Full Family really is literally the URL `?view=family`, browser back/forward moves between modes, and a direct link like `?view=glyph&char=R` deep-links straight into the node editor on a specific letter at page load (`readViewFromURL()` runs once at boot, before the first font-load render, feeding `loadFontBuffer`'s own existing charset-validation so an invalid `char` param falls back safely). All 4 previous direct `VIEW='...'; render();` call sites (`enterGlyphEdit`, the `tgFamily` click handler, `btn-edit-points`, the Escape-key handler) now route through `setView()`; the `#tgFamily` button's `aria-pressed` sync moved into the `render()` dispatcher itself so it stays correct regardless of which path changed `VIEW` (click, popstate, or a deep-link boot).
  - **Verified live**: a genuine OS-level wheel scroll (via the computer-use `scroll` action) over the tester now changes `zoomPan.zoom` (1→1.15, screenshot-confirmed larger "Apostate" specimen text with the HUD reading "1.15x"); switching to Full Family correctly reset `zoom` back to `1` (`board.style.transform` back to `translate(0px,0px) scale(1)`); clicking "Full Family" → URL became `.../apostate/?view=family`; entering glyph-edit on "A" → URL became `.../apostate/?view=glyph&char=A`; browser **back** correctly restored `?view=family` and `VIEW==='family'` live (via the new `popstate` listener); a **fresh page load** at `?view=glyph&char=R` landed directly in the node editor on "R" with the glyph canvas visible and "R" highlighted in the charset list, confirming true deep-linking. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×10) — Apostate: mouse-wheel zoom on the "Full Family" glyph stage.** Diego: "implementa lo zoom allo scroll del mouse." Replaced the old manual `zoom` variable + additive `zoomBy(d)` (only reachable via the ±HUD buttons) with **`Organica.createZoomPan`** — the shared utility already used by Spore/Pollen/Halide (`shared/core.js`), reused rather than hand-rolled: wheel-zoom toward the cursor, drag-to-pan once zoomed, dblclick reset, ⌘/Ctrl +/-/0, all for free. Bound to `#board`/`#stage`, gated with `isReady: () => VIEW==='family'` so scrolling over the stage does nothing in the tester or glyph-edit views (both checked live: `zoomPan.zoomBy()` correctly no-ops outside `family`). The old `renderFamily()` no longer sets `board.style.transform` itself — `createZoomPan` now owns that transform exclusively, re-applied automatically on every `onChange`; the HUD's ±buttons call `zoomPan.zoomBy(1.2)`/`zoomPan.zoomBy(1/1.2)` (multiplicative, matching the utility's own contract) instead of the old additive ±0.1. Added local `#board.zoomed`/`#board.panning` cursor CSS (grab/grabbing), matching the convention already documented in `shared/shell.css` for every other `createZoomPan` consumer (Apostate doesn't link `shell.css`, so the two rules are local, mirroring the existing "own canvas surface" tools like Genesis/Rhizome/FVS). **Verified**: a genuine OS-level mouse-wheel scroll (via the computer-use `scroll` action, not JS injection) over the stage in `family` view changed `zoomPan.zoom` from 1 → 1.3225 (exactly 1.15² — two real wheel ticks), screenshot-confirmed the glyph rendering at the new scale; the same action while `VIEW==='tester'` correctly left zoom unchanged. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×9) — Apostate: ported Living Path's UI/UX — tester-as-default, a real floatbar, "Full Family" behind a button, and a node/point editor for the current letter.** Diego: "portiamo in apostate alcuni elementi della UI di Living Path e alcune parti di UX" — (1) Apostate's default landing view becomes an editable text specimen like Living Path's tester, with a floatbar carrying the needed controls; (2) what is TODAY Apostate's whole screen (charset list + one big glyph + FX panel) moves behind a "Full Family" floatbar button — deliberately **not** a Fontra-style grid (a confirmed divergence from what "Full Family" means in Living Path); (3) an optional node/point editor for the currently-displayed letter, reusing Living Path's own `shared/glyph-model.js`/`shared/glyph-editor.js` (both already font-agnostic, zero changes needed to either), in addition to — not instead of — the existing global-slider editing.
  - **New 3-state view dispatcher** (`VIEW: 'tester' | 'family' | 'glyph'`) — naming is deliberately the *inverse* of Living Path's own tester/family split (there, "family" = a grid of every glyph; here, per Diego's explicit choice, "family" = today's existing single-glyph screen), documented in a code comment to head off future confusion. `render()` is now this dispatcher; the old single-glyph `render()` body was renamed `renderFamily()` verbatim (plus one new `editedGlyph()` check, see below) — confirmed byte-identical output to the pre-refactor code via direct `dFromSubs` string comparison across identity/`bubbles=100`/`groviera=60`/default-`griddler` on A/O/8/R. `#panel` (the FX sliders) is structurally never hidden — it sits in the grid's last column in every state, untouched by the dispatcher.
  - **New floatbar** (`.org-floatbar`, previously entirely unused in Apostate bar one dead stylesheet `<link>`) — markup/classes copied from Living Path's own (`org-floatbar__btn`, `org-popover-wrap`), holding: open-font, the `#fb-tester` group (SIZE/TRACK sliders, L/C/R align seg-ctrl, CAPS), "Full Family" toggle, Reset (`resetAll()`, already covers every FX/GRID/ECHO/POLY/JITTER/WOBBLE/SCATTER/BUBBLE key), and Export (moved out of the header into a floatbar popover — `Organica.popover($('btn-export'), $('export-popover'))` needed zero code change, only the trigger button's location moved; confirmed working live via a real click).
  - **New tester** (`buildSpecimenSubs`/`renderTester`) — runs `applyPipeline` **per character** (each still normalised to its own 1000-box internally, so every effect's calibration — incl. `bubbleSubpath`'s size/radius math — is untouched regardless of how many letters are on screen), then `denormGlyph(subs,k,ox,oy)` — the exact function the OTF export already used and nothing else — converts each glyph back to real font-em-unit coordinates, laid out left-to-right by real `advanceWidth` (scaled to the chosen SIZE) plus TRACK tracking, word-wrapped, aligned, optionally uppercased. Verified: typing "HELLO" showed real (non-equal-width) character spacing; enabling `bubbles` showed the point count jump from 172→6812 across the specimen, confirming the per-glyph pipeline genuinely runs on typed text, not a cached single-glyph shape.
  - **Node/point editor** (Phase B) — a minimal per-glyph "bake" layer was the real engineering gap, since `applyPipeline` is stateless per render with zero per-glyph storage anywhere in this file (unlike Living Path, which already has `glyph-model.js`-backed state for this). New `_gmodel`/`gmodel()`/`editedGlyph(glyph)`/`stackIsIdentity()`/`glyphAdvance(gl)` mirror Living Path's own `_gmodel`/`editedGlyph`/`glyphSubs`/`glyphAdvance` pattern exactly. Entering edit mode (`enterGlyphEdit`→`mountGlyphEditor`) bakes the CURRENT `applyPipeline` output for the selected letter into `Organica.glyphModel`'s canonical-contour shape (`g.contours`/`g.advance`/`g.baked`/`g.edited`) — skipped only when the whole stack is at true identity (rare in practice, since `griddler` boots non-zero by default) — then mounts `Organica.glyphEditor` on a new `#glyphCanvas`, with a minimal `#glyphTools` (Undo · Reset glyph, 2-click armed · +pt — no Edit⇄Preview seg needed, since the sliders already apply live to whichever letter is selected, satisfying that half of the requirement for free). Every read site that previously called `applyPipeline`/`gl.advanceWidth` directly now checks `editedGlyph(gl)` first: `renderFamily()`, `buildSpecimenSubs()`'s per-character loop, `exportOTF()`, `exportSVG()` — a baked glyph's frozen shape flows through all four without ever re-running the effect stack.
  - **Verified live** (real DOM clicks + direct state checks, not just JS injection): default boot lands on `VIEW==='tester'` with `#charset` `display:none` and a 2-column grid; "Full Family" toggle restores the 3-column grid + `renderFamily()`; entering glyph-edit on "A" bakes it (`g.baked/edited === true`, contour count 2 matching A's known outer+counter structure) and the editor renders the melted (bubbled/griddler'd) shape, not the raw glyph; simulating a node move (`g.contours[0].pts[0].x += 500`) showed up immediately in `renderFamily()`'s output; tweaking an unrelated slider (`twist`) afterward left that exact `d` string **unchanged** (frozen) while a **different**, never-edited letter ("B") **did** respond to the same slider — confirming per-glyph isolation; "Reset glyph" (2-click armed) correctly un-baked back to the original raw point, then re-baked through the current live stack (`griddler`'s non-zero default) exactly as documented; Escape exits glyph mode back to family; OTF/SVG export path-building both succeed against a baked override, using `glyphAdvance()`/`Organica.glyphModel.toSubs()` instead of the raw pipeline. Zero regressions found on any never-edited glyph across the session's own established multi-density test set (I/A/O/8/R/J/L/U).
  - **CSS promotion, per Diego's confirmation**: `.fb-tester`/`.fb-field`/`.fb-val` (previously a byte-identical local `<style>` block that only existed in `livingpath/index.html`) promoted to `shared/floatbar.css` — this repo's own "extract at the second consumer" rule — with Living Path's local copy deleted and re-verified rendering identically off the shared rule (screenshot-confirmed, console clean).
  - `node -c` on the extracted inline script clean throughout; console clean in every one of the 3 views (bar the pre-existing, this-session-only stale-tab console history unrelated to this change). **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×8) — Apostate: `bubbles` — replaced the angle-threshold taper with a curvature-aware one, then fixed the real remaining leak with a fill-rule change Diego proposed directly.** Two follow-ups in one pass.
  - **Part 1 — the ×7 taper over-flattened.** Diego sent a screenshot of "J" (`size=160`) circling sharp horn-like cusps at the top and a flattened wedge at the hook — "devi equilibrare per evitare che le curve siano poi troppo piatte e che non sia più un bubble." Root cause of ×7's fix: a fixed turn-ANGLE threshold ignores bubble size entirely (the same gentle curve is harmless at a small bubble but overlap-prone at a large one), so it either under-reacted (still overlapping) or over-reacted on curves that never risked overlapping, and — since each segment computed its own safety factor independently — a hard-clamped segment sitting right next to an untouched full one created a visible seam/cusp. Replaced with: (1) a **radius-vs-local-curvature** comparison (`circumradius(a,b,c)`, the standard 3-point discrete curvature estimate) — only reduces depth once a bump's own radius genuinely risks outrunning the base path's actual curvature there, scale-aware rather than a fixed angle; (2) a **3-tap smoothing pass** over neighbouring segments' safety factors, so a clamped bump blends gradually into a full one instead of meeting it at a hard seam.
  - **Part 2 — even the improved taper left one residual leak** (confirmed live at 2.2× zoom on "J" at `size=160`): a small self-intersecting sliver right at the hook's tightest point, because the curvature estimate — built from the *resampled* bubble points — under-samples a curl that occurs entirely within one large resample step (a step of 160 units can span a tight curl that a 3-point estimate off the coarse resampled points simply doesn't see). Diego, looking at the same screenshot, proposed the actual fix directly: **"credo che si possa semplicemente fare un merge delle due e riempirle di nero?"** — right instinct, simpler and more robust than chasing perfect curvature detection.
    - Traced the "leaf" artifact to `fill-rule="evenodd"`, hardcoded everywhere in this file — evenodd reads any self-crossing of the SAME subpath as an alternating hole, exactly the sliver in the screenshot. Confirmed by re-deriving the winding-direction work from two sessions ago: every outer contour has *positive* shoelace area, every counter/hole *negative*, consistently — which means **`nonzero`** (winding-number fill) punches a hole exactly as correctly as evenodd does for a real counter (opposite-wound regions cancel to 0 either way), while ALSO correctly merging same-winding self-overlaps (like two crossing bubbles) into solid black instead of alternating them into a hole — literally Diego's own "merge them and fill with black," achieved as a one-line fill-rule swap, no boolean/union library needed. The one exception: `echo` (a `damnation` effect, unrelated to bubbles) deliberately depends on evenodd's alternating behaviour for its own nested-ring look (own case comment, confirmed still correct) — so fill-rule is now `currentFillRule() → STATE.echo ? 'evenodd' : 'nonzero'`, applied in both the live preview (`render()`) and `exportSVG()` (the OTF export path, `subsToOTPath`, has no fill-rule concept at all — TrueType/OpenType rasterizers use nonzero-equivalent winding by convention, so no export-side change was needed there).
  - **Verified**: re-created the exact "J" `size=160` repro — no more sharp horns (part 1) AND the residual hook sliver is gone (part 2), confirmed at 2.2× zoom; `echo` re-checked on "O" — still renders its classic alternating concentric rings correctly (fill-rule reads back as `evenodd` when echo is active); "A"'s counter re-checked with echo OFF — still a clean hole under `nonzero` (fill-rule reads back `nonzero`), no regression on ordinary hole rendering; identity (Amount=0) unchanged across all 8 test glyphs; a combined stack (`bubbles` + `twist` + `groviera`) still produces valid, non-empty path data. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×7) — Apostate: `bubbles` — tapered bulge depth on sharp local curvature to stop neighbouring bumps overlapping.** Diego sent a screenshot of "U" (`bubbles=100, size=128`) with small self-intersecting "leaf" slivers circled at the tight curve at the bottom of the U (and, more subtly, near the valley between the two tallest bumps) — asked "come evitare queste sovrapposizioni." Different failure mode from the earlier "J" hook bug (that one was a wrong DIRECTION on a concave curve; this one is a genuine geometric overlap between two adjacent full-size bumps when the underlying path curves away faster than a large, fixed-radius bump can follow — the bump's own circle simply crosses its neighbour's).
  - **Fix**: new `turnAngle(a,b,c)` helper (unsigned angle 0..π between the incoming and outgoing direction at a point) measures how sharply the ORIGINAL resampled path turns around each segment's two endpoints. Each segment's bulge **depth** (not its geometry, count, or spacing — the arc-length resample and bump placement are untouched) is scaled by a `safety` factor — `Math.max(0.15, 1 - maxTurn/(π/2))` — so a segment sitting on a near-straight run (turn≈0) keeps its full bulge, while one straddling a tight curve (turn approaching 90°+) tapers toward flat, shrinking the bump just enough that it stops reaching into its neighbour. Deliberately a depth taper, not a radius/spacing change — keeps bump density visually consistent (no sudden gaps or extra bumps appearing near the fix), just softens the ones that would otherwise cross.
  - **Verified**: re-created Diego's exact repro (`bubbles=100, size=128, bubblesInner=-30, sizeInner=24` on "U") — the leaf self-intersections are gone, confirmed at 2.5× zoom (clean scallop through the tight bottom curve, slightly flatter there than the sides — the taper is visible but not jarring); re-checked "O" at a normal bubble size (24, gentle uniform curvature) — byte-for-byte the same visual result as before this fix, confirming straight/gentle curves are genuinely unaffected; identity (Amount=0) unchanged on all 8 test glyphs. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×6) — Apostate: fixed `bubbles` self-intersecting into a tangled mess on concave curves (e.g. "J"'s hook).** Diego tested on "J"/"L" and sent a screenshot with a hand-drawn annotation circling a chaotic, loopy self-intersecting tangle right at the hook's tight concave curl, with everything else on the letter scalloping cleanly. Root cause, confirmed by inspecting the exact geometry: the "outward" reference used since `bubbles` first shipped was "away from this subpath's own bbox centre" (`bboxOf([s])` dot product) — this works for a roughly-convex shape (O's ring, A's triangle) but breaks down on a **concave** curve: a point sitting on a tight inward curl (like the inside of J's hook) can be closer to — or even past — the subpath's own bbox centre, so "away from centre" no longer means "away from the glyph" there, and the sign flips mid-curve, producing exactly the self-intersecting tangle in the screenshot. This was already flagged as a known caveat in the original design plan for this effect ("can occasionally pick the wrong local direction at a concave inflection") — "J"'s hook is exactly the concave-enough case that caveat warned about.
  - **Fix**: replaced the per-point bbox-centre heuristic with the subpath's own **winding direction** (shoelace signed area, computed once per subpath, not per segment) as a single, constant sign for the whole subpath.
