This is the standard, mathematically robust solution — for a simple (non-self-intersecting) closed contour with consistent winding, "outward" relative to the direction of travel is a FIXED rotation (always the same 90° turn) regardless of local convexity or concavity, unlike a distance-to-centre reference which is inherently local and blind to the glyph's actual boundary shape. Confirmed empirically first (not assumed): every outer contour across "O"/"A"/"8" has a positive shoelace area and every counter/hole negative, consistently — so this swap also automatically preserves the already-verified outer-vs-hole behaviour (a hole still bulges into its own open space) with zero extra logic.
  - **Verified**: re-created Diego's exact repro settings (`bubbles=-78, size=90, bubblesInner=-18, sizeInner=95` on "J") — the tangled mess is gone, the hook now scallops cleanly and consistently with the rest of the letter; re-checked "O" (still bulges outward correctly, unchanged from before), "A" (outer + inner both correct simultaneously), and "R" (its own concave leg/bowl junction, also clean, no tangle) — no regressions on any previously-working case; identity (Amount=0) unaffected on all 7 test glyphs including "J"/"L"; OTF export with the exact repro settings on "J" succeeds. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×5) — Apostate: fixed a real "all outer" bug — it could silently zero the whole bubble effect.** Diego: "all outer non funziona verifica" / "testalo sulla J." Re-verified "J"/"L" first (correct — single subpath, toggling `allOuter` produces byte-identical output either way, confirmed via real UI clicks this time, not just JS state). The real bug only showed on a glyph with a counter (e.g. "A") in one specific sequence, reproduced live: set `bubblesInner=100` with `bubbles` (outer) still at its default 0, then check "all outer" — the ENTIRE effect vanished, letter back to flat identity. Root cause: `bubblesInner`'s case early-returns `if(BUBBLE.allOuter) return subs;` (correct — outer now owns everything), but `bubbles`' own case is still gated by the generic top-of-function guard `if(!v...) return subs`, keyed on `STATE.bubbles` alone — which was still 0. Checking "all outer" doesn't transfer Inner's amount anywhere, so with Outer still at 0 the whole thing silently disappears with no visual explanation — indistinguishable from "broken."
  - **Fix**: rather than silently absorbing the Inner value (a surprising/magic transfer), made the now-dead control **visibly disabled**. New `syncBubbleInnerDisabled()` greys out (`disabled` + `opacity:0.4` on the row) `bubblesInner`'s main slider AND its own "bubble size" sub-slider whenever `BUBBLE.allOuter` is on, called from the "all outer" checkbox's own `onchange`, from `syncPanelFromState()` (covers `resetAll()`/`randomizeAll()`), and once after the initial `buildPanel()` call at boot. Unchecking "all outer" re-enables both controls and **restores their preserved value** (never cleared, just made inert) — confirmed live: Inner's 100 was still there and immediately active again the moment the box was unchecked, both sliders then bulging the letter together as originally designed.
  - **Verified** via real UI clicks (End-key on a focused range input to jump to max, real checkbox clicks — not JS state injection): "J" unaffected by the toggle either way; "A" with the exact repro sequence now shows the Inner row visibly greyed the instant "all outer" is checked (so the disappearing effect is now self-explanatory, not silent); dragging Outer to 100 while "all outer" is checked correctly bulges both the outer ring and the inner triangle from the one slider; unchecking restores Inner's interactivity and its old value. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still ×4) — Apostate: tested `bubbles` on single-contour letters ("J"/"L") + new "all outer" override.** Diego: "facciamo un test sulla J e L in teoria questo tipo di lettere solo outer bubble, rendiamole outer ma visto che c'è l'abbiamo mettiamo un'opzione per tenere quello che abbiamo ora ossia che il 'dentro' è inner." Checked live: "J" and "L" are each a single subpath in Archivo Black (confirmed via `fromOTCommands`, 70 and 6 points respectively, no counter) — the existing outer/inner classification (largest-bbox-area subpath = outer, everything else = inner) already puts 100% of a single-subpath glyph through `bubbles` (outer) for free, since the lone subpath is trivially its own max area; `bubblesInner` was already correctly inert on both (nothing to classify as inner). Screenshot-confirmed "J" scallops cleanly end-to-end with only the outer slider.
  - **New option added anyway, as requested**: `BUBBLE.allOuter` (default `false` — keeps the current per-subpath split) — a checkbox "all outer (ignore inner split)" in the `bubbles` sub-panel. When on, `bubbles` (outer) treats **every** subpath as outer regardless of area (so a multi-contour glyph like "A"/"O" can be bulged as one uniform shape through a single slider+size, useful when the smaller subpath isn't really acting as a "hole" for the current look the user wants), and `bubblesInner` becomes fully inert (early `return subs` at the top of its case) since outer already claims everything.
  - **Verified**: "J"/"L" produce byte-identical output whether `allOuter` is on or off (single subpath, no behavioural difference — confirmed via point-array diff); on "A", `allOuter=true` makes the inner triangle counter go through the SAME outer bubble treatment (bigger point count, screenshot shows both outer ring and inner counter scalloped identically by one control) instead of staying untouched; `bubblesInner`'s own slider is confirmed inert (no effect on point count) while `allOuter` is on, even at Amount 100. `resetAll()` restores `allOuter:false`. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still, later still, later) — Apostate: `bubblesInner` gets its own independent "bubble size" sub-slider.** Diego: "per i bubble inner aggiungi lo slider per la bubble size solo per la inner." `BUBBLE.size` was shared between `bubbles` (outer) and `bubblesInner`, so the two couldn't have differently-sized bulges at once. `bubbleSubpath(s, rawAmt, amt)` gained a 4th `size` param (removed its own `BUBBLE.size` read); the `bubbles` case passes `BUBBLE.size`, `bubblesInner` passes a new `BUBBLE.sizeInner` (own default 24). New "bubble size" sub-slider (`#rg-bi-size`, 8–300, same range as the outer one) added to `bubblesInner`'s own sub-panel in `buildPanel()`, wired through `syncPanelFromState()`/`resetAll()`. `faceted` stays a single shared toggle (not split — Diego only asked for size). **Verified**: point-array diff on "O" with `bubbles`/`bubblesInner` both at Amount 100 but `size=24` (outer) / `sizeInner=150` — outer produced 1000 pts (fine, dense bumps) and inner 216 pts (few, large bumps) in the same render, confirming the two sizes are read independently; screenshot shows exactly that — a ring of small tight bulges on the outer edge and a handful of large star-like points on the inner hole, simultaneously, at genuinely different scales; `resetAll()` restores `BUBBLE.sizeInner=24`. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still, later still) — Apostate: `bubbles` gets a real smooth-curve mode + a distinct faceted mode.** Diego: "le bubble sono segmenti e non linee curve, io vorrei linee curve ma visto che l'abbiamo facciamo anche una con linee a segmenti." Root cause: `bubbleSubpath` sampled every semicircle at a FIXED `K=8` regardless of the bulge's own radius — fine for small bubbles, but once bubble size could go up to 300 (this session's own earlier fix), a K=8 semicircle at radius ~150 has ~58-unit straight sub-chords, visibly faceted rather than a smooth curve. New `BUBBLE.faceted` boolean (default `false` = smooth, per Diego's stated preference) drives `K` inside `bubbleSubpath`: **smooth** scales `K` with the bulge's own arc length (`Math.round(radius*Math.PI/8)`, clamped 10–48) so every straight sub-chord stays ~8 units regardless of bubble size — reads as a genuine curve at any zoom (verified at 3.5× UI zoom, no visible faceting); **faceted** keeps a small fixed `K=5` — a deliberate, distinctly angular low-poly/cut-gem look, kept as an explicit alternative exactly as asked ("facciamo anche una con linee a segmenti"), not removed. New checkbox "faceted (segments, not curves)" in the `bubbles` sub-panel, wired through `syncPanelFromState()`/`resetAll()` the same way `ck-g-rand` (griddler's own checkbox) already is. **Verified**: point-count diff confirms smooth scales adaptively with size (1000→464 pts as size goes 24→150) while faceted stays low and flatter (500→80 pts over the same range) — the two modes are genuinely different sampling strategies, not cosmetic; screenshots at matching zoom show smooth giving clean round bumps and faceted giving visibly angular spiked teeth on the same "O" outline; identity (Amount=0) unaffected on all 5 test glyphs; OTF export succeeds in both modes; `resetAll()` restores `faceted:false`. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still, later) — Apostate: fixed `bubbles`' "bubble size" — increasing it past ~80 silently did nothing.** Diego: "prova ad aumentare le bubble size." Root cause: `bubbleSubpath` fed `BUBBLE.size` into the existing `resamplePolyline(pts, closed, step)` — a function built for a different contract (`pixelate`/`groviera`/`spikes` need "at least this dense, never sparser than the original"), which loops over each ORIGINAL segment independently and guarantees `Math.max(1, ...)` output points per original segment — so the resampled count can never drop below the glyph's own raw point count (64 for "O"'s outer ring) no matter how large `step` gets; confirmed numerically, `resamplePolyline` returned exactly 64 points at step 80, 150, and 300 alike. Fixed with a **new dedicated function, `resampleArcLength(pts, closed, step)`** (`resamplePolyline` itself left untouched, since its three existing callers rely on its specific floor-at-original-count behaviour) — walks the polyline's TOTAL cumulative arc length as one continuous line and places points evenly at `~step` apart, correctly consolidating multiple original segments into fewer/longer ones when `step` is large. Also raised the "bubble size" slider's max from 80 → **300** now that larger values actually do something. Verified: `resampleArcLength` on "O"'s 64-point outer ring now genuinely produces monotonically fewer points as size grows (100 → 30 → 16 → 8 pts at size 24/80/150/300); screenshots confirm size 80 gives ~30 clean, evenly-sized round-tipped teeth (a real gear look, previously indistinguishable from size 24) and size 150 gives ~16 much larger bulges that touch at their bases into a spiky sun/star shape — both a genuine, correct response to the slider, not a regression; identity (Amount=0) unaffected on all 5 test glyphs; OTF export with a large size + both outer/inner sliders active still succeeds. `node -c` clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later still) — Apostate: two new "Texture (extra)" sliders — `bubbles` (outer) and `bubblesInner` — perfect semicircular bulges between resampled contour points.** Diego: "tra due punti/nodi presenti sul caratteri si creassero delle bolle curve e semirotonde perfette" — clarified via AskUserQuestion (Apostate, bubble replaces the segment rather than decorating it), then via direct feedback after the first plan draft: the bulge must be able to go **either outward or inward** (not fixed), and a closed glyph's **inner counters/holes need an independent control from the outer contour** (e.g. "A"'s triangle, "O"'s ring, "8"'s two bowls).
  - **No reusable "scallop the polyline" utility existed anywhere in the repo** (grepped for scallop/bubble/semicircle/cloud — nothing). Constraint discovered by reading the code: both `dFromSubs` (preview) and `subsToOTPath` (OTF export) deliberately draw only straight `L`/`lineTo` segments — a documented WYSIWYG rule (a Catmull-Rom curve there once rounded real corners unexpectedly) — so a "perfect semicircle" can't be an SVG arc/bezier command; it has to be built by inserting new points along a **true circular arc** (cos/sin, exact — not a bezier approximation), exactly like the file already flattens the font's own bezier curves into polylines (`cubicPts`/`quadPts`, `FL=8`).
  - **Two sliders, not one, both signed `-100..100`** (same "direction+amount in one slider" convention already used by `twist`/`wave`/`noise`/`curl` in this file) — `bubbles` ("bubbles · outer (new)") acts only on the subpath with the largest bbox area (the outer contour), `bubblesInner` ("bubbles · inner (new)") acts on every other subpath (counters/holes) — same outer-vs-inner classification already used elsewhere in this file by `pinch`/`refraction`/`flip`/`crush`. Sign picks direction (positive = outward, negative = inward), magnitude is strength; ±100 gives the literal "perfect" full semicircle requested.
  - **New shared helper `bubbleSubpath(s, rawAmt, amt, K)`** (next to `angularHarmonic`/`squareify`): resamples the subpath at `BUBBLE.size` spacing (`resamplePolyline`, the same helper `pixelate`/`groviera`/`spikes` already use to decouple feature size from the glyph's raw point density — so a dense contour like "O", 64-72 raw points, doesn't produce dozens of meaningless micro-bulges), then for each resampled segment A→B builds a true semicircle (centre = midpoint, radius = half the chord — an exact geometric fact, not approximated) sampled at `K=8` interior points (matching the file's own `FL=8` precedent for "how many samples read as smooth"), blended between the flat chord (Amount 0) and the full arc (Amount 100). Outward direction is picked per-subpath via a dot product against that subpath's own bbox centre (`bboxOf([s])`) — the same "each subpath owns its own reference point" trick already validated in this file for `pinch`/`pull`/`refraction`/`groviera`, robust for both the outer ring and a hole without needing to detect winding direction (CW/CCW) at all; the slider's sign then flips which of the two arc sides gets swept, giving a true mirrored semicircle rather than a linear reflection.
  - **Verified**: point-array diffing across "I"(4pt)/"A"(8+4pt)/"O"(64/72pt)/"8"(146/64/64pt)/"R"(48/35pt) confirmed Amount=0 is a structural no-op on both sliders; `bubbles` at +100 vs -100 on "O" produces genuinely different, non-trivial displacement (real outward gear-like scallop vs. real inward scalloped notch, both screenshot-confirmed as clean round bumps); `bubblesInner` at +100 on "A" correctly bulges only the inner triangle counter (a toothed zigzag into the hole) while leaving the outer "A" outline byte-identical; combining `bubbles=60` + `bubblesInner=-60` simultaneously on "O" showed both effects active independently with no interference (outer gear-bumps outward, inner hole notched inward, at the same time); a combined stack with `twist` rendered without error; `resetAll()`/`randomizeAll()` correctly reset/randomize both sliders and leave `BUBBLE.size` untouched (matching `JITTER.seed`/`WOBBLE.freq`/`SCATTER.seed`'s existing behaviour); OTF export path generation (`subsToOTPath`) and SVG `d` string generation both succeeded with the effect active, confirming WYSIWYG (no separate export-path change needed — both already consume `applyPipeline`'s output). **One accepted, non-blocking limitation found and documented, not fixed**: a `bubble size` large relative to a glyph's own short edges (e.g. size 80 on "I", whose top/bottom edges are much shorter than that) produces overlapping oversized semicircles near corners that read as sharp spikes rather than clean round bumps — the same class of "extreme parameter combos can look odd" caveat already accepted elsewhere in this file for `spikes`/`scatter` at their own extremes, not something this session's scope calls for auto-clamping.
  - `node -c` on the extracted inline script clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 (later) — Apostate: debug pass on the new "Texture (extra)" group found and fixed two real bugs.** Diego: "fai un debug su questi nuovi." Drove each of the 3 new effects live at extreme values across glyphs of very different point density ("I" 4pts, "A" 8+4pts, "8" 146/64/64pts, "O" 64/72pts, "R" 48/35pts) instead of only the single mid-density glyph used when they first shipped.
  - **`jitter` — self-intersecting shards on any dense contour, at ANY Amount.** A flat per-point independent random offset (same class of bug this file's own `noise` effect had already hit and fixed once, per its own comment) breaks as soon as the offset magnitude approaches the LOCAL spacing between neighbouring points — on "O" (~32 units between points) even Living Path's own "safe" ceiling of 40 was already enough to make points leapfrog past their neighbours, screenshotting as a torn, self-intersecting spiky mess rather than a grain texture. Fixed by capping each point's displacement to `Math.max(2, localSpacing*0.45)` (spacing = average distance to its two neighbours) — dense contours now get a safe, contained grain at Amount 100; a sparse contour like "I" has wide local spacing and keeps the full dramatic corner-jitter, unclamped, matching the original intent.
  - **`wobble` — a complete, silent no-op at the DEFAULT Frequency (6), on any low-point-count glyph.** Root cause: the sine's phase was `i/n * Frequency * 2π` — raw POINT INDEX normalised by point count, not position along the contour. Whenever `(2×Frequency) mod n === 0` (n = the subpath's own point count), every single sample lands on an exact half-integer multiple of π, so `sin()` is bit-for-bit zero at every point simultaneously and the whole subpath goes dead. Confirmed live and numerically: "I" (4 points) showed literally zero displacement at Frequency 6, 8, 10, 12, 14 (all the even values — half the slider's entire range) and only moved at odd values (7, 9…) — meaning the tool looked completely broken on any simple/rectilinear glyph at its own shipped default. Fixed the same way `spikes`/`groviera` already fixed an unrelated but same-shaped bug in this file: parametrise the sine by actual **arc length** along the contour instead of raw point index, so the phase varies smoothly regardless of how few or unevenly-spaced the source points are. Verified: all previously-dead frequencies (6/8/10/12) now produce real, non-zero displacement on "I"; "O" (dense, was never affected) re-checked byte-for-byte unchanged in visual character (same clean 6-lobe scallop).
  - **`scatter`** — investigated an apparent "no visible effect" on "8" that turned out to be a testing artifact, not a bug: the math is a faithful 1:1 port of Living Path's own formula (confirmed identical), and it does displace points substantially (bbox growth up to 10%, individual points moving 50+ units) — the visual result on a vertically-symmetric glyph like "8" just reads as a subtle uniform "balloon outward" rather than a dramatic fragment-scatter, because both the outer contour and each counter grow outward together. Confirmed via direct bbox/point measurement, not screenshot alone (a quick screenshot glance is exactly how the false alarm first happened). No fix applied — this characteristic already exists in Living Path's own upstream implementation, not something this port introduced.
  - **Method note**: the false alarm on `scatter` was only caught by cross-checking bbox/point-level numbers against the screenshot, not by re-screenshotting alone — a repeat of a method already flagged elsewhere in this file (Apostate's earlier `polygonize`/`pixelate` false alarms). Kept as a reminder for this session: a visually-subtle-but-real effect and a genuinely broken one can look identical in a single screenshot; only per-point numeric diffing tells them apart.
  - **Verified**: `node -c` on the extracted inline script clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404; regression battery across 5 glyphs of very different point density confirms both fixes move points and stay deterministic (same seed → identical result); a combined stack (twist + jitter + wobble + scatter together) still renders without error. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 12, 2026 — Apostate: new "Texture (extra)" group — jitter, wobble, scatter, ported concept-only from Living Path's `shared/pathfx.js` `FX` registry.** A comparative analysis of Apostate vs Living Path (requested by Diego) flagged three vector effects in Living Path/Sinew's shared engine — `jitter` (per-point noise), `wobble` (sine displacement along each point's local contour normal), `scatter` (per-subpath outward explode) — that have no equivalent in Apostate's 27-slider typoclast reproduction and would add a complementary organic/textural family alongside the existing clean geometric distortions. Diego: "procedi con jitter+wobble+scatter come nuovo gruppo." Per Apostate's own "zero shared code with pathfx.js, start from zero" rule, all three were **reimplemented locally** in `apostate/index.html` on Apostate's own point model (`{closed,pts:[{x,y}]}`), not imported — the same 1000-unit normalisation box (`normGlyph`) meant Living Path's own default magnitudes could be used directly as a calibration reference. New 4th panel category **"Texture (extra)"** (explicitly labelled non-typoclast, unlike the 3 typoclast-faithful groups), added as a final pass in `applyPipeline()` after `damnation` — so it's automatically WYSIWYG in both the live preview and the OTF/SVG export (both already route through `applyPipeline`, no separate export-path change needed). Each effect follows the existing griddler/echo/polygonize pattern: one main "Amount" slider (0–100, in `FX`) + its own sub-parameter mini-panel — `jitter` → seed (fixed-seed `mulberry32`, so the noise pattern itself doesn't reshuffle as Amount is dragged, same convention as `groviera`/`spikes`), `wobble` → frequency + phase, `scatter` → seed (one random per-subpath drift direction, fragmenting multi-contour glyphs like "R"/"A"/"8" independently per contour). `resetAll()`/`syncPanelFromState()`/`randomizeAll()`/`buildPanel()` extended to cover the 3 new sliders + their sub-params, following the exact same structure as the existing multi-param effects. **Verified** on the no-store dev server: the new group renders correctly positioned after "Eternal damnation"; direct pipeline calls confirmed all three genuinely displace points (nonzero bbox/point deltas, each shape distinct) and `jitter` is bit-for-bit deterministic across repeated calls at the same seed (no flicker); a combined stack (twist + jitter + wobble + scatter) rendered without error on "A", visibly showing the added organic texture on top of the existing geometric twist; `resetAll()` correctly zeroes the 3 amounts and restores `JITTER.seed=7`/`WOBBLE.freq=6,phase=0`/`SCATTER.seed=5`; `randomizeAll()` randomizes the 3 amounts (sub-params untouched, matching `ECHO`/`POLY`'s existing behaviour); SVG path generation with all 3 active produced a valid `d` string via the same `applyPipeline` the exporter uses. `node -c` on the extracted inline script clean; console clean bar the pre-existing `_vercel/insights` 404. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 11, 2026 (later still, still) — Apostate: `polygonize` and `groviera` finished, the two gaps left open by the group re-review above.** Diego: "continua con polygonize e groviera."
  - **`polygonize` — turned out NOT to be broken.** Re-tested carefully this time (step-by-step typoclast interaction with an explicit slider-value read between each step, not a combined reset+select+set batch) — the earlier "genuinely SHATTERED, detached triangular shards" finding was a **stale/glitched typoclast render**, the exact same class of false alarm the group re-review had already caught once on `pixelate`. A slow, verified re-test showed Amount 60 alone (facets default 2, chamfer default 50%) is legitimately near-invisible on both tools — a gentle default, not a bug. Scrolling to the real `facets` sub-slider and driving it directly (0–100 range) confirmed the underlying mechanism — decimate to an N-sided polygon, chamfer-blend the corners — is correct: facets 8 produces a clean 8-sided polygon on the live reference, facets 2 (default) stays close to round. **The one real bug**: the `facets → N` mapping had the wrong SHAPE. `N=Math.max(5, Math.round(40-facets*5))` is linearly inverse and lands on N=0→clamped-5 (a pentagon) at facets=8, when the reference shows a clean **octagon** there — and a straight `N=facets` fixes facets=8 but then makes facets=2 a stark triangle, which the reference does not show at its own default. The real relationship is a **decay**, not a line: low facets barely decimates (near the original ~64-point smoothness), and N drops off toward facets' own value as it rises. Fixed to `N=Math.max(3, Math.round(60/Math.max(1,facets)))` — verified against both live data points (facets=2 → N=30, stays smooth; facets=8 → N=7.5≈8, clean octagon, matching the reference at both).
  - **`groviera` — the real bug, found after 4 iterations.** The corner-threshold theory from the group re-review (raising `findCorners` 35°→55°) was a dead end — both thresholds found the identical 8 real corners on "R," while the live reference shows many more bite sites than that, densely packed around the bowl's own curve (gear teeth) and only a couple of small nicks on the straight leg/stem — not a corner-only effect at all, the same finding `spikes` already needed. Rebuilt on the same even-arc-length-resample pattern as `spikes` (`resamplePolyline` first, so bite density follows `amount` and the glyph's own curve length, not its uneven raw point spacing) — but the FIRST rebuild silently no-op'd on every straight run: bite direction was derived from `midpoint(qa,qb) - p`, which is exactly `(0,0)` whenever a point sits on a dead-straight segment (qa and qb land symmetrically either side of p), so the leg/stem kept coming back completely unbitten while the curved bowl (never exactly collinear) looked fine. Switched direction to the tangent's own perpendicular (well-defined on a straight run) — but a SECOND bug then bit the counter/hole outward instead of inward: signing that perpendicular by "toward the glyph's global centre" is backwards for a hole, whose own material lies *away* from the glyph centre, not toward it. A THIRD attempt (an even-odd inside/outside probe per candidate point) still somehow read as outward teeth everywhere despite a hand-traced example showing the probe picking the correct direction — abandoned as too fragile to reason about per-point. Replaced with something structurally simpler: each subpath gets its **own** centre (`bboxOf([s])`), bite **toward** it for the largest-area subpath (the outer contour — biting toward your own centre removes material) and **away** from it for any smaller subpath (a counter/hole — its material sits outside its own boundary, so biting away from *its own* centre is what removes material there). No winding, no per-point sign flips, and it was finally direction-correct — but still read as a dense saw-blade of sharp triangular teeth blanketing the WHOLE boundary uniformly, not the reference's sparser, rounder gear-look concentrated more on the curve. Two final tweaks: capped the bite depth well under the spacing between sites (a real flat boundary survives between notches now, instead of every notch touching its neighbour and reading as one continuous zigzag), and added a seeded 35% skip on candidate sites plus doubled the arc's interior sample count (4→8) for a rounder scoop instead of a sharp triangular point. **Verified**: "R" at Amount 60 now shows a mild rough texture on the outer contour and clean, clearly rounded bites forming a scalloped/gear pattern on the inner counter, matching the reference's dominant character; "A" stays fully legible under both fixed effects (no self-destruction, corners intact); a fresh regression check on `polygonize`'s own default (facets 2) confirmed still near-invisible after the groviera rewrite (independent code paths, as expected).
  - **Method note, reinforced**: hit the SAME stale-browser-cache trap twice more this session, on the `apostate/index.html` side this time, not typoclast's — editing the file and re-navigating (even with a fresh URL) sometimes left the OLD script running in the tab (confirmed by reading `applyEffect.toString()` and finding the old comment text still present). `location.reload(true)` plus an explicit post-reload read of the live function source (checking for a string unique to the new edit) before trusting any screenshot is now the reliable pattern — a plain re-navigate is not enough on its own.
  - **Verified**: `node -c` on the extracted inline script + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean. Both fixes checked live against typoclast.com; "A" regression-checked on both. Tool reset to a clean baseline state (Archivo Black, "A", identity + griddler's own default-60) at the end. **Not committed** — same "porta in prod" convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 11, 2026 (later still, group re-review) — Apostate: a group-by-group re-debug of Total blasphemy + Eternal damnation against typoclast, on 2–3 letters each (O/A/R).** Diego: "rivedi per gruppi e fai un debug comparando i risultati su 2/3 lettere." Drove both tools side by side again (typoclast via dispatched `input`/`change` events on its real sliders; Apostate via direct `STATE`/`render()` calls for speed), this time comparing the RESULT SHAPE's overall character (lobe count, which subpath moves, magnitude) rather than just presence/absence of an effect. **Found and fixed real regressions/bugs in ~15 of the 27 effects**, all in `apostate/index.html`'s `applyEffect`:
  - **A genuine base-rendering bug at identity**, caught testing "O" before touching any slider: Archivo Black's inner counter has a real `Q Q L L Q Q` pattern in its own font data (a tangent-continuous curve split by a short straight "L" bridge, common in font curve-fitting) that `dFromSubs`'s straight-chord renderer drew as a visible flat notch. Fixed in `fromOTCommands`: dedupe zero-length consecutive points, then detect "false corners" (a segment far longer than the subpath's own median spacing, with a small turn angle at both ends — i.e. not a real vertex) and re-curve just that one gap with a Catmull-Rom fill using the flanking points as tangent references. Real corners (large turn angle, e.g. "A"'s vertices) are untouched. Residual: the outer contour still shows some low-amplitude waviness beyond this one bridge — not chased further (would need switching the whole pipeline off flattened-polyline chords onto true curve segments, out of scope this pass).
  - **`twist`**: 3/5/7-harmonic sum read as a bumpy multi-lobed "flower" next to typoclast's own single clean asymmetric lean on "O" — dropped to a dominant n=1 term + weak n=2.
  - **`pinch`**: the `(r/R)^power` law used a single bbox-derived `R`, which only matches a round contour's true radius at the bbox extremes — everywhere else (e.g. 45° on a circle) the high power exaggerated the gap into a lumpy scalloped OUTER ring, when typoclast's outer stays a clean circle. Fixed: only the smaller (counter) subpath(s) pinch now; the largest-area subpath is left alone.
  - **`pull`**: exponent 3.2 barely bulged the diagonal 14% past the circle radius — nowhere near typoclast's near-flat-sided stadium. Raised to exponent 9.
  - **`perspective`**: an unclamped `±(v/100)` coefficient reached literal zero width at max, reading as a sharp cone vs typoclast's much gentler trapezoid at the same slider value — capped to a 0.4 max coefficient.
  - **`wave`**: 4 lobes/amp 0.5 → a sharp 4-pointed cross vs the reference's soft rounded triangular (3-lobe) bulge — switched to 3 lobes, amp 0.3.
  - **`refraction`**: the two-harmonic wobble distorted the OUTER contour into a deep bowtie/hourglass the reference never shows (outer stays close to round; only the inner counter turns into a rounded near-square with a slight cusp) — reworked on the same "largest subpath untouched" pattern as pinch, squareify-style pull + a small cusp term on the smaller subpath(s).
  - **`bulge`**: amplitude 0.6 → a sharp 8-pointed throwing star vs typoclast's gentle soft scallop — dropped to 0.08.
  - **`noise`**: the 0.006 noise-coordinate scale was HIGH frequency relative to point spacing (adjacent points sampling near-uncorrelated noise → genuinely torn/self-intersecting chaos), when typoclast's own maxed noise is a coherent wavy cloud edge — dropped to 0.0016 (lower frequency, coherent) + gentler magnitude.
  - **`inflate`**: push range read as a sharp gear/cog next to typoclast's soft scallop — softened base amount + harmonic weight.
  - **`sharpen`**: the OLD "confirmed live" call (a clean decimated polygon) doesn't hold up on direct re-comparison — typoclast's own sharpen is a soft 7-petal floral bulge, essentially in the bulge/inflate family, not a faceted polygon (which is already `polygonize`'s job, a separate slider with its own facets/chamfer sub-controls — duplicating that mechanism here would be redundant). Rewritten as a 7-lobe angular-harmonic bulge.
  - **`ripple`**: the 2-lobe hourglass model doesn't match either — typoclast's own maxed ripple turns the OUTER contour into a faceted octagon-ish polygon while the INNER counter elongates into a vertical stadium. Rewritten: outer decimates (straight facets read fine now that `dFromSubs` draws plain lines, no corner-clamp trick needed), inner stretches vertically.
  - **`flip`**: the outer-circularise half was already correct; the inner counter was ALSO circularising when the reference shows it faceting into a hexagon-ish polygon instead — inner now decimates.
  - **`wind`**: freq 6/amp 0.12 read as chaotic torn zigzagging over the whole ring (inner counter included) vs typoclast's contained, mild ripple on a still-recognisable O — dropped to freq 3/amp 0.05.
  - **`curl`**: uniform amplitude 0.55 on both contours produced a thin sharp pinwheel star vs the reference's soft-wobble outer + sharp toothy-star inner — split so the outer gets amplitude 0.12, inner 0.5.
  - **`spikes`**: corner-only targeting (`findCorners`) clustered a dense fuzz of micro-spikes on "R"'s bowl curve alone (a real curve, just sparse enough there to read as a >35° kink) while the reference shows long thorns distributed all around the WHOLE letter, including from partway along curves, not just from real vertices. Rewritten: resample the contour at even arc length first (point density along a font contour is very uneven — curves carry far more raw samples than straight runs, so picking spike sites off raw point indices/gaps clusters them on curves and skips straight edges), then push a seeded ~50% subset outward along their local normal.
  - **`slice`**: on a low-point-count glyph like "A", shifting only the sparse EXISTING points per band was invisible — most of a band's few points sit far from its own boundary, so the shift just slid an already-straight edge sideways with nothing to reveal the seam. Densified via `resamplePolyline` first (same helper pixelate already uses) so a real jagged multi-band seam appears, matching the reference's torn-strip glitch look.
  - **`groviera`**: raised the `findCorners` threshold 35°→55° to match `spikes`, though direct testing showed the real remaining gap is elsewhere — `bite` is capped by each corner's own adjacent segment length, and several of "R"'s real corners sit between short segments, shrinking their bite to near-invisible while the inner counter's two corners (longer segments) bite normally and read as the only visible notch. Documented as still-open, not silently left as "fixed."
  - **Confirmed already correct, no change needed**: `rotate`, `slant` (a false alarm at first — 12° looked unslanted at a glance in a small screenshot, but a pixel-level dark-row-center measurement on both tools confirmed matching direction and magnitude; re-verified visually at 45°), `stretchX`/`stretchY` (the "wavy O" concern traced entirely to the identity bridge bug above, not to stretch itself), `crush` (squareify exponent 7 still reads as a clean rounded square, matching), `echo` (mechanism — literal nested concentric rings via evenodd — confirmed matching), `pixelate` (an initial "looks totally different" read on "A" turned out to be a STALE typoclast screenshot from a combined reset+select+set-slider batch call racing the framework's re-render; a careful step-by-step re-test showed pixelate was already correct, clean Minecraft-style blocky "A" matching Apostate's own output almost exactly).
  - **Confirmed still broken, not fixed this pass (documented, not papered over)**: `polygonize` — at Amount 60 typoclast's own reference isn't a decimated polygon at all, it's a genuinely SHATTERED letter with detached triangular shard fragments (closer to a Voronoi/triangle-shatter break than any point-space decimation); Apostate's current decimation+chamfer model produces an almost invisible result at the same value. Flagged as a confirmed gap in a new code comment, not just "low confidence" as before — a real rebuild (a different mechanism entirely) is out of scope for this pass.
  - **Method note, worth remembering**: a combined `reset+select+setSlider` call against typoclast, immediately followed by a screenshot in the SAME batched turn, produced one clearly stale/glitched render (the pixelate false alarm above) — likely a Vue re-render not settled before the screenshot fired. Splitting state-changes and the screenshot into separate tool calls (with an explicit slider-value read as a sanity check in between) reliably avoided this; re-verifying `twist` the same careful way reproduced the exact same result as the original combined-batch test, so this wasn't a systemic problem with every earlier finding this session — just an intermittent risk worth guarding against on the highest-stakes checks.
  - **Verified**: `node -c` on the extracted inline script + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean throughout, after every edit. All fixes checked live against the real typoclast.com on the affected letter(s); no regression check found on "A"'s sharp corners (rotate/slant/twist/pinch/wave/bulge/noise/inflate/sharpen/ripple/crush all re-screenshotted clean on "A" too during the pass). **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod", same convention as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 11, 2026 (later) — Apostate shipped: a standalone, from-scratch reimplementation of typoclast.com.** Diego, hypothetically: "facciamo un percorso inverso, facciamo finta che non abbiamo sviluppato Living Path e che voglio copiare typoclast.com così com'è. è fattibile?" Plan-mode feasibility pass first, decisions via AskUserQuestion: Organica's own visual chrome, not a pixel clone of typoclast's dark UI (would violate the mandatory design-token rules); a **new standalone tool**, not a Living Path mode; the **effects engine written from zero**, ignoring `shared/pathfx.js` entirely (the explicit point of the exercise — repeat the derivation work independently, not reuse last week's); font-loading/OTF-export **plumbing pattern** reused (opentype.js, the glyph→point-space bridge, Web Worker export — generic infrastructure, not "the filters"); a **fixed 27-slider pipeline** (not Living Path's flexible layer stack) — faithful to how typoclast itself works.
  - **Live verification against typoclast.com before building** (fetched and grepped the real production bundle, then drove the actual `input[type=range]` sliders via dispatched events, isolating each parameter) caught two real mischaracterizations from memory alone: (1) typoclast's primary view is **not** a live-updating full-charset grid (my first assumption, carried over from the earlier comparative session) — it's a **character-picker list + one big glyph**, corrected via a second AskUserQuestion before any code was written; (2) **`griddler` has two independent layers** — an Amount slider that alone draws a clean grid-cut, plus non-zero **rows/cols/tile rotation/tile scale** sub-params present at typoclast's true default boot state (Amount 60) that shatter the glyph into an independently-rotated mosaic — "reset all" zeroes both, which is why an Amount-only retest looked like a plain cut, reconciling the two observations. Also confirmed **`groviera` renders real round "cheese-bite" notches** at each corner (not a point nudge), and **no animation feature exists anywhere** in typoclast (bundle-level: 0 keyframes/`.animate(`/Lottie/video-export hits beyond generic UI-library `requestAnimationFrame`; live: no play/timeline control; two screenshots 3 s apart with zero interaction came back byte-identical).
  - **`apostate/index.html`** (new, single-file) — a **local, independent** geometry/effects module: `cubicPts`/`quadPts`/`bboxOf`/`dFromSubs`/`fromOTCommands`/`normGlyph`/`denormGlyph` (generic point-space plumbing, deliberately re-typed rather than imported from `shared/pathfx.js`), a local `smoothNoise2`/`mulberry32`/`findCorners` (real direction-change corner detection, not every-Nth-point). **`FX`** — 27 entries in typoclast's own exact order and **exact min/max ranges** (read directly off the live site's own range inputs), split `mild`/`blasphemy`/`damnation`; `applyPipeline()` runs the combined mild-heresy affine first, then every blasphemy effect, then every damnation effect, in fixed order, no stacking/reordering. `griddler` implements both confirmed layers: a point-space grid-line "crimp" (pull points toward the nearest grid line — the vector analogue of Living Path's raster `griddlerField`, no raster round-trip needed since nothing here is raster) plus a genuine per-tile rotate/scale transform (each point rotates/scales around the centre of its own grid cell — approximates the real per-tile mosaic without needing a polygon-clip dependency). `groviera` bites each real corner with a concave arc, **capped to a fraction of the corner's own adjacent edge length** — a real bug caught live: the first version sized the bite off the whole glyph's diagonal, which on Archivo Black's low-point-count (8-anchor) "A" swallowed entire short edges even at a tiny amount; the second bug (same effect) was arcing the corner's *full* neighbour-to-neighbour span rather than a local reach along each edge, which meant every edge got redrawn as a zigzag from *both* its endpoint corners regardless of bite depth on a glyph where literally every vertex is a real corner — fixed by capping the notch to 28% along each adjacent edge so two neighbouring corners' notches never double-cover the same stretch.
  - **A real, unrelated rendering bug caught and fixed early**: `fromOTCommands` only set a subpath's `closed` flag on an explicit trailing `Z`, and separately, font contours routinely carry a **coincident duplicate point** where the path closes back onto its own start; left in, that duplicate anchor sits exactly where `dFromSubs`'s closed-loop Catmull-Rom wraps around, spiking the tangent into a visible stray spur on *every* glyph regardless of which effect was active (font contours are always closed loops, and the duplicate is dropped by proximity check) — fixed both, verified clean on the "A" at identity.
  - **Layout**: typoclast's real 3-pane structure (character list · glyph · controls) doesn't fit the standard 2-column canvas+panel shell (`shared/shell.css` assumes 2), so `#app` is a local 3-column grid — a deliberate, documented divergence, not an oversight. `--tool: #9c2b3a` (blood-crimson) — an open hue distinct from every existing warm-red/coral/vermilion/rose accent already claimed, fitting the heresy/blasphemy/damnation framing.
  - **Verified**: representative live spot-checks (not all 27, per the plan's own stated scope) — perspective (trapezoidal skew), slice (multi-band glitch), groviera (legible per-corner bites, post-fix), griddler both layers (clean grid-cut + shattered mosaic, post-fix) all screenshot-confirmed on Archivo Black; a combined-effects OTF export re-parses as a valid 95-glyph font (`opentype.parse` round-trip); "reset all" correctly zeroes every slider including griddler's own default-60 boot value; `node -c` on the inline script + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean (after adding the required `.mobile-gate` div). Hub nav entry ("Tracing & vectorization" group, next to Living Path/Sinew), `vercel.json` rewrite, `shared/tokens.json` accent entry. **`twist` and `echo`'s first-guess implementations (swirl / segment-seam pinch) were both wrong — see the full 1:1 debug pass below, same day, which replaced most of the 27.** Deferred, not built: `.ttf` export (opentype.js only builds CFF-flavoured OTF fonts easily; a true glyf-table TTF is a materially different code path, out of scope this pass — typoclast's own `.ttf` dropdown option is unimplemented here, `.otf`/`.svg` only).

- **September 11, 2026 (later still) — Apostate: a full 1:1 debug pass against typoclast, effect by effect.** Diego: "fai un debug 1 a 1 con typoclast sui parametri." Went through all 27 sliders live — typoclast in one tab, Apostate in the other, same glyph ("O" for the radial-family effects, "R"/"A" for corner-dependent ones), each parameter driven to its max via dispatched `input`/`change` events on the real `input[type=range]` elements, screenshots compared side by side. **Result: ~18 of the 27 first-guess implementations were wrong** — not subtly off, but built on the wrong mechanism entirely, because the initial pass (same day, earlier) leaned on assumption/naming rather than driving typoclast's own sliders one at a time.
  - **Found and fixed one bug before any effect testing could even start**: `resetAll()` zeroed every `STATE` value including `stretchX`/`stretchY`, whose real identity default is `1` — a `0` scale collapses the glyph to a single point. Confirmed live: typoclast's own "reset all" shows "stretch x 1.00 / stretch y 1.00" afterward, not 0. Fixed to reset each slider to its own `f.def` (griddler still resets to 0, per its own confirmed behaviour).
  - **Two new shared helper functions**, both confirmed as real recurring families across multiple effects: `angularHarmonic(subs,cx,cy,lobes,amp,phaseDeg)` — `r' = r·(1+amp·cos(lobes·θ+phase))` around a shared centre — and `squareify(subs,cx,cy,amt,nExp)` — blends each point's radius toward its own subpath's superellipse/Chebyshev radius, **normalised per-subpath** (a first version shared the whole glyph's bbox between outer and inner contours, which pulled the small inner counter out to nearly the SAME absolute radius as the outer ring, ballooning it until it nearly swallowed the ring gap — caught live on `crush`, fixed by giving each subpath its own half-extent).
  - **Rewritten from a wrong mechanism to the confirmed real one** (old → new, all in `apostate/index.html`'s `applyEffect`): **twist** (radial swirl → a sum of 3 odd angular harmonics at descending amplitude — confirmed live: a maxed twist barely rotates an "R" at all, and reads as an organic lumpy wobble on "O", not a pinwheel); **pinch** (angular sine bump → radial power-law `r'=R·(r/R)^(1+amt·5)` — confirmed live: the inner counter crushes toward a point while the outer contour barely moves, no lobed pattern); **pull** (directional drag → `squareify` soft end, exponent 3.2 — confirmed live: a wide rounded stadium, not an asymmetric shift); **wave** (spatial x-axis sine → `angularHarmonic` 4 lobes at 45°phase — confirmed live: the outer skews into a diamond, the smaller inner counter reads as a tighter scallop at the same frequency); **bulge** (radial sine bump → `angularHarmonic` 8 lobes — confirmed live: a genuine 8-petal flower); **noise** (5%-of-height wobble → 22%-of-diagonal chaotic multi-point noise — confirmed live: a maxed noise is barely-recognisable torn chaos, not a subtle jitter); **inflate** (uniform push → `angularHarmonic`-modulated push, 8 lobes on both contours — confirms the Sep 8 Living Path/pathfx.js finding independently); **sharpen** (neighbour-deviation jitter → real decimation to N facets, **plus a corner-clamp twin-point trick** so the shared Catmull-Rom renderer doesn't smooth the sparse decimated points back into a rounded blob — confirmed live: a clean straight-edged nonagon); **ripple** (concentric-by-distance sine → `angularHarmonic` 2 lobes — confirmed live: a genuine hourglass, top/bottom bulge and waist pinch, on both contours); **flip** (horizontal mirror blend → per-subpath circularise-toward-average-radius — confirmed live: the outer contour becomes a near-perfect circle, the inner counter shrinks toward its own average radius, no mirroring at all); **crush** (vertical squash → `squareify` hard end, exponent 7 — confirmed live: a genuine rounded-square ring on both contours); **wind** (asymmetric x-weighted zigzag → a symmetric y-keyed horizontal zigzag, equal amplitude both sides — confirmed live); **curl** (edge-anchored progressive rotation → `angularHarmonic` 7 lobes, signed by direction — confirmed live: a many-pointed star/gear burst on both contours, not a spiral); **spikes** (every-Nth-point nudge → real corners only via `findCorners`, large displacement — confirmed live: long thorns from each actual vertex of an "R", correctly a no-op on the corner-less "O"; first attempt at 0.45×diagonal way overshot into a self-crossing mess, retuned to 0.15×); **echo** (segment-seam centroid pinch → **literal nested concentric copies** of the whole glyph, scaled + rotated per ring, new `ECHO.repeats`/`ECHO.angle` sub-sliders — confirmed live as a completely different mechanism: `fill-rule=evenodd` turns the stacked copies into alternating rings automatically, exactly matching the live reference); **pixelate** (raw grid-snap → grid-snap + consecutive-duplicate-point removal for a clean staircase, not a noisy stutter); **polygonize** (plain decimation → decimation + the same corner-clamp trick as sharpen, chamfer-driven inset blend, new `POLY.facets`/`POLY.chamfer` sub-sliders exposed since typoclast reveals them live once Amount > 0); **perspective** (clamped skew `[0.25,2.5]` → an unclamped true vanishing-point convergence, confirmed live the far edge collapses to a genuine point at max value).
  - **Left as lower-confidence best-effort** (flagged in code comments, not fully pinned down live): `refraction` (organic wobble + a pronounced cusp/heart-shape on the inner counter — approximated as two summed harmonics; the exact cusp mechanism wasn't reverse-engineered) and `polygonize`'s exact `facets`→N mapping (typoclast's own slider read as barely-visible at every tested facets value, so the approximation couldn't be checked against a strong reference signal).
  - **Untouched, already correct from the first pass**: `rotate`/`slant`/`stretchX`/`stretchY` (simple affine, confirmed live pixel-for-pixel in direction and magnitude), `tracking` (confirmed a genuine no-op on typoclast's own single-glyph view — it only matters in a word-level specimen, which neither tool's single-glyph view has), `slice`, `groviera`, `griddler` (all three already fixed in the same-day earlier pass).
  - **Re-verified after every fix, live against typoclast on the same glyph**: crush, bulge, ripple, echo, flip, pinch, wave, perspective, inflate, twist (amplitude retuned down after an initial too-dramatic pass), wind, curl, spikes (magnitude retuned down after an initial self-intersecting overshoot), noise, sharpen — all screenshot-compared post-fix and read as the same family/character as the live reference (exact pixel match not claimed or expected, given no source access). A combined-effects OTF export re-parsed clean after the full rewrite (95 glyphs). `node -c` + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean throughout. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod", same as the rest of Apostate.

- **September 11, 2026 (still later) — the actual root cause of "sembra davvero diverso": a rendering bug present even at IDENTITY, before any effect.** Diego: "fai un test su typoclast con lo stesso font mi sembra davvero diverso." Loaded the identical font (Archivo Black) into both tools with every slider at 0 and screenshotted the same glyph side by side — the real regression test the per-effect debug pass hadn't covered, since every earlier comparison was effect-vs-effect, never "does the REST state even match." It didn't: Apostate's "A" had its **top peak and both leg corners visibly rounded**, while typoclast's stayed razor-sharp — a real, structural bug, not a tuning issue.
  - **Root cause**: `dFromSubs` re-emitted every subpath as an unconditional Catmull-Rom spline (`C` commands, `(p2-p0)/6` control points) with no notion of "this vertex was actually a straight corner in the source glyph." Archivo Black's "A" is built from plain `L` (line) commands with real 90°-ish corners; smoothing every point uniformly rounded them regardless of whether any effect was even active — so EVERY glyph, at rest, read softer/rounder than the real typoclast, independent of and compounding on top of the 27-effect fidelity work already done.
  - **Fix**: `dFromSubs` now emits plain straight `L` segments between consecutive points instead of a spline. This works cleanly because `fromOTCommands` already flattens curves densely (`FL=8` samples per Bézier) — dense enough that straight chords between samples still read as smooth curves to the eye, while a real corner (an `L`-to-`L` vertex with no curve samples between) now has nothing smoothing it away. Verified: the "A" at identity is now corner-for-corner sharp like the reference; the "O" stays visibly smooth (140 points around the ring, straight segments imperceptible at any reasonable zoom); re-ran `bulge`/`groviera` post-fix — both effects still read correctly, `groviera` still correctly no-ops on the corner-less "O" and still bites cleanly on the "A".
  - **The export path (`subsToOTPath`) had the exact same bug**, independently — its own Catmull-Rom-derived `curveTo` calls, unrelated code but the same unconditional-smoothing mistake. Fixed to `lineTo` as well, both because it shares the bug and because the project's own standing rule ("a single render path serves the preview AND every export — if they can disagree, the tool is broken") requires it. Side benefit, not the point of the fix: the exported font is now also meaningfully smaller (a combined-effects "A" export: 50 KB → 12.7 KB for the same 95-glyph charset), since `lineTo` commands are far more compact than `curveTo`.
  - **Verified**: identity "A"/"O" pixel-family-matched against typoclast on the same font; `bulge`(100), `groviera`(60) re-checked live post-fix, no regression; a full-charset OTF export re-parses clean (95 glyphs, 12,672 bytes). `node -c` + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod", same as the rest of Apostate.
  - **Follow-up, same session** — Diego: "ingrandisci i punti nodi." The `.handle` overlay dots (`show handles`) were `r="4"` on a 1000-unit viewBox, much smaller than typoclast's own reference nodes; bumped to `r="9"`, verified visibly larger and still legible against the letterform.
  - **Follow-up, same session** — Diego: "fai un check su pixelate tra i due tool." Isolated `pixelate` on the same "O" at the same value (60) in both tools: typoclast's own result is a clean, fully orthogonal Minecraft-style staircase on every contour. Apostate's **inner counter** matched (its corners happened to land axis-aligned already after snapping), but the **outer contour** showed real diagonal cuts across cell corners instead of right-angle steps — a bug invisible from the counter alone, only caught by comparing both contours of the same glyph side by side. Root cause: grid-snap + dedupe (the identity/`dFromSubs` fix's own logic, reapplied here) guarantees points land on the grid but not that consecutive kept points differ on only one axis — wherever the original curve crossed a cell diagonally, the snapped points stayed diagonal too. Fixed: a second pass inserts a real axis-aligned corner point `{x:b.x, y:a.y}` between any two consecutive points that still differ on both axes, forcing every step into a genuine L-shape. Verified: the "O"'s outer contour now shows clean right-angle stairs matching the reference exactly, matching the counter's own (already-correct) behaviour.
  - **Follow-up, same session** — Diego: "rivedi." Re-checked pixelate again, this time on "A" instead of "O" (low point-count glyph, 8 outer anchors, vs. "O"'s 140) — and found a second, more serious bug the "O" test hadn't surfaced: quantizing the EXISTING sparse anchor points directly collapsed the "A" into a crude, unrecognisable rectangular blob (the far-apart original corners left nothing for the grid-snap to trace the true edge through), while typoclast's own pixelate stayed legible as the letter at the same Amount. New `resamplePolyline(pts, closed, step)` helper densely resamples the ORIGINAL contour (linear interpolation along each existing edge at ~0.4×cell spacing) before the grid-snap/dedupe/corner-insert pipeline runs — so even a glyph with very few source anchors gets pixelated against its true silhouette, not just the polygon between its far-apart corners. Verified: the "A" now stays legible and closely matches the reference at Amount 60; re-checked "O" afterward, still correct, no regression from the added resample step.

- **September 11, 2026 — Living Path: filter-by-filter comparative test against typoclast, full realignment including the 4 flagged "complex" ones.** Diego: *"fai un test comparativo tra typoclast filtro per filtro e living path, allinea dove trovi discordanze living path a quello che fa typoclat"* — then, on being asked cheap-fixes-only vs. everything: **"Tutto, inclusi i 4 algoritmi complessi."** Continuation of the Sep 8 Distort/Deform/Break work (same session arc) — this pass tuned the math of nearly every shared-named effect rather than just exposing it, since a side-by-side drive of both tools on the same loaded font (Archivo Black, CORS-fetched real `.ttf` via the Google Fonts CSS2 API into typoclast's hidden file input — the OS file-picker had blocked this earlier in the session) showed most effects diverged in *behaviour*, not just presence.
  - **Unifying math families found**: pinch/bulge/wave/ripple all became one **angular-harmonic radial warp** (`r' = r·(1+amp·cos(n·θ))` from the glyph's own centroid, `n`=lobe count differentiating the four); crush/pull became one **superellipse/Chebyshev "squareify"** family (sharp Chebyshev radius vs. a softer squircle blend). `shared/pathfx.js`: twist→shear, pinch→saddle squeeze+grow, wave/ripplev→angular-harmonic (dropped `freq`/`wavelength` for `lobes`/`angle`), refraction→continuous sine, sharpen→decimate-to-facets, flip→circularize blend (`axis`→`amount`), crush→sharp-square blend (dropped `axis`), wind retuned (amplitude 0.18→0.3, freq 2.5→4), curl→radial spiral (adopts twist's old math), spikesvec→real-corners-only via new **`findCorners(pts,angleDeg)`** helper (direction-change dot-product threshold, distinguishes genuine polygon vertices from smooth-curve points — dropped `every`), perspective clamp removed (range widened), slice→signed multi-band glitch (one signed `amount` -100..100, dropped `at`/`angle`).
  - **The 4 flagged "complex algorithms"** (Diego explicitly asked for all of them, not just the cheap fixes): **`griddler`** moved vector→raster — new `griddlerField(g,cells,gutterPx)` zeroes the raster field along an N×N grid of gutter bands so marching-squares re-traces real axis-aligned gaps through the solid body (typoclast's own "vector griddler" turned out to be a real grid-clip with visible gutters, not a mesh warp — a true polygon-clip needs a dependency this project doesn't have, so it reuses the existing raster round-trip instead — the cheapest path to a *real* cut). **`groviera`** moved raster→vector — corner-bite notch via `findCorners`, first pass too subtle (`bite=R*0.05*amount`, read as a faint rounding) → strengthened to `R*0.16*amount` after a live visual check showed it needed to read as a genuine cut at each vertex, not a chamfer. **`echo`** was a near-invisible duplicate-copy pinch (max ~25-unit displacement on a 700-unit "O", and a **complete no-op** on Archivo Black's 9-point straight-line "A" — below its own `n<count*3` guard, confirmed numerically) → rewritten as a real centroid-pull pinch at each segment seam (guard lowered to `n<6`, pull strength `*0.5`→`*1` toward centroid, window widened `0.18`→`0.22`) — now moves points 200+ units and never silently no-ops on simple glyphs. **`slice`** was already redone as the signed multi-band glitch in the batch above (an explicit **approximation**, documented — no true polygon-boolean cut in this pipeline).
  - The engine swap (`griddler` vector→raster, `groviera` raster→vector) required matching updates in `livingpath/index.html`: `LP_CATEGORIES` (raster `texture` types list / vector `break` types list swapped), `LP_PRIMARY` (`griddler:'cells'` now raster-side, `groviera:'amount'` now vector-side), `SEEDED_FX` (dropped `griddler`, added `groviera`), the OTF-export Web Worker's `shared=[...]` embed list (added `griddlerField`) + a new `applyLayerW` dispatch case for `griddler`.
  - **`inflate`** — the one item flagged in the comparison table but not yet realigned when the Sep 8 pass ended: typoclast shows a high-frequency petal ripple on **both** contours (outer bulge and inner counter alike), Living Path still used a plain uniform normal-push. Added **Ripple** + **Lobes** params — the base `Distance` push is now modulated by `1+ripple·cos(lobes·θ)` around the glyph's own centre (shared centre, so outer and inner contours' petals stay in register), backward-compatible defaults (`ripple:40, lobes:6`) so the effect is visible without a slider touch.
  - **Verified**: echo numerically (max delta 25→225+ on "O", 0→322 on "A", confirmed via direct `FX.echo.apply` calls against `glyphSubs`); groviera + griddler both visually on single-glyph view (screenshots — groviera shows real corner notches on "R", griddler shows genuine grid-cut gutters through an entire specimen phrase); a combined vector(echo)+raster(griddler) stack exported through the real Worker OTF path and **re-parsed as a valid, installable font** (28 glyphs, "R" 186 path commands); Sinew regression — `FX`/`RFX` registry counts (26/20) and engine placement (`groviera` in `FX`, `griddler` in `RFX`) confirmed correct, console clean; `node -c` (both `shared/pathfx.js` and the inline script) + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean throughout. Docs: `docs/LIVINGPATH.md` new subsection ("Filter-by-filter realignment against typoclast"), this note. **Not committed** — pending Diego's "porta in prod" (same still-uncommitted batch as the Sep 8 Distort/Deform/Break feature this continues).

- **September 8, 2026 (later still) — Living Path: "full family" rebuilt as a Fontra-style font overview.** Diego: *"sviluppo della font-family — usa la stessa struttura di Fontra … ogni lettera è in un box con il rispettivo nome e segno."* The old view merged every charset glyph into **one flat `<path>`** on the fixed 1400×900 stage — bbox-fit per cell, no box, no name, capped at 120, no scroll. Decisions (AskUserQuestion): **whole font, scrollable** · **Fontra-style glyph fit** (shared per-row baseline, true em-relative size — `x` short, `H` tall, `g`/`p` descenders hang) · **caption = name + `U+XXXX`** · **export = the overview as shown** (a specimen sheet). `livingpath/index.html` only.
  - **`familyGlyphs()`** — `.slice(0,120)` → `MAX_FAMILY=1000`; drop the `unicode===32` skip (**space is shown**, empty cell + `space / U+0020` caption); new filter `u!==32 && (u<0x20 || (u>=0x7f && u<=0xa0))` drops C0/C1 controls (`nonmarkingreturn` etc. leaked once uncapped).
  - **`_familyCache = {sig, glyphs:[{name,uni,adv,subsEm}]}`** — per-glyph **em-unit** geometry cache. `famSig()` = `JSON.stringify(GROUPS.raster[0].layers.map(l=>[l.type,l.params])) + '|' + getContourSmooth() + '|' + fontSig()` — **not** `STACK_REV` (which `applyPreset` doesn't bump — a real bug caught in verification: the first `famSig` used `STACK_REV` and a preset applied in family mode left the grid un-effected; switched to the layer-serialization shape already used by the OTF-worker cache key ~line 1244). `ensureFamilyGlyphCache()` is **async**, runs `processGlyphEm` per glyph, yields every 12 (`await new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,0))`, `building family… n/total` status), aborts on a newer `_familyToken`. Cold build of Archivo Black (420 glyphs) through **Avulsion** ≈ 5.9 s; re-open with the same stack ≈ 290 ms (cache hit → relayout + re-emit only).
  - **`buildFamilyLayout(cols)`** (pure, replaces `buildFamilySubs`): `FAM_U=180` board-units/cell; glyph area = top `U*0.74`, `em = glyphArea*0.62`, baseline `by = y0 + glyphArea*0.72` (constant per row), `bx` centres on the advance width. `familyCols()` = `clamp(round(wrapW()/150), 4, 12)`; `wrapW()` = `canvasWrap.clientWidth - 48`.
  - **`familySVGMarkup(L, ink, bg)`** — shared by render + export: one `<path>` of dividers (`stroke-opacity 0.14`), per cell a glyph `<path fill-rule="evenodd">` (skipped for space) + two `<text>` (`fill-opacity` 0.62 / 0.4; name font-size drops 13→9 when `name.length>13`). Resolved hex + `fill-opacity`, **no CSS vars / `color-mix`** so the exported standalone `.svg` renders anywhere. Outline rings deliberately **not** applied in family mode.
  - **`renderFamily()`** async, token-guarded: sets `#board` `viewBox="0 0 ${GRID_W} ${GRID_H}"`, `style.width='100%'`, `style.height = rows*(wrapW()/cols)+'px'` (real px → the wrap scrolls). `exitFamilyView()` restores `viewBox 0 0 1400 900` + clears the inline w/h. `#canvas-wrap.family{overflow-y:auto; overflow-x:hidden; align-items:flex-start}` + `#canvas-wrap.family #board{max-width:none;max-height:none;box-shadow:none}` (local CSS). `render()` toggles the `.family` class + calls `exitFamilyView()` when leaving; `repaint()` (the swatch `onChange` path) early-returns into `renderFamily()` when `showFamily` — the family `CACHE` is a **layout object**, not subs, so `dFromSubs(CACHE)` would throw. `#tgFamily` handler resets zoom + hides `#zoom-hud` on entry; `createZoomPan` `isReady` → `board.classList.contains('show') && !showFamily` (family mode scrolls, doesn't zoom — `isReady` gates the wheel handler in `shared/core.js`). `ResizeObserver` on `#canvas-wrap` re-runs `renderFamily` (debounced 120 ms, cache hit) while the view is open.
  - **`currentSVGString()`** family branch → `viewBox 0 0 GRID_W GRID_H` + bg `<rect>` + `familySVGMarkup(...)`; **`#exPng`** reads the SVG's own W/H (not the hardcoded 2000²) so a tall sheet isn't squashed. `.lvp` format untouched (`showFamily` is transient, never persisted).
  - **Verified** on the no-store dev server (Archivo Black, 420 glyphs): overview renders as a scrollable boxed grid, glyphs on a shared baseline at real relative size, `name` + `U+XXXX` captions, `space`/`U+0020` cell present, no control-char cells; **Avulsion** re-processes every glyph with a live `n/420` counter, second open instant; colour change repaints the grid via `repaint→renderFamily`; SVG export = a full specimen sheet (`viewBox 0 0 1080 12600`, 420 `<path>`, 840 `<text>`, bg `<rect>`, carries the current ink); toggle **full family** off fully restores the tester (`viewBox 1400×900`, styles cleared, `.family` gone, overflow `hidden`, specEdit/testbar/phrase-dots back, no transform); 0 console errors. `node -c` + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean. Docs: `docs/LIVINGPATH.md` §7, this note + Tools row.
  - **Follow-up same session (Diego):** the `full family` text button moved out of the stage's top-right `.gridtoggle` into the **floatbar** as a grid icon (`aria-label="Full Family View"`), between Reset and Export. Active state via `aria-pressed="true"` → local `.org-floatbar__btn[aria-pressed="true"]{background:var(--ink);color:var(--paper)}` (matches the `[aria-expanded="true"]` popover-open look; `shared/floatbar.css` has no `aria-pressed` rule). `.gridtoggle` + `.mini.on` CSS removed (`.mini` kept — still used by the preset-delete `×`). **Then** (Diego: "aggiungi gli altri comandi nella floatbar…") the whole `.lp-testbar` (the bottom-of-stage control bar) was moved into the floatbar as `#fb-tester` (SIZE / TRACK sliders + align seg + CAPS, same ids so `wireTbSlider`/`syncTestbar` are untouched; `hidden` toggled with `#specEdit`/`#phrases` in `render()`); the testbar's own **RESET** dropped (the floatbar `↻` already resets the stack — the tester RESET's SIZE/TRACK/align/CAPS-to-defaults is now only reachable via a fresh `.lvp` or reload) and the **◐ night-mode** button dropped with it (`#tbNight` handler + `nightOn` gone — Ink/Paper still edit in the right-panel Colour section, just no one-click swap). `.lp-testbar*` CSS → `.fb-tester*`; the specimen now uses the full stage height. `docs/LIVINGPATH.md` §7 updated.
  - **Then** (Diego, two rounds): (a) processing text off the header → a `#procOverlay` scrim over `#board`, shown by `busy()` / hidden by `idle()`, gated on `heavy = family || (isFont && !stackIsIdentity())` after a double-`rAF`; (b) **the whole `.org-header__status` element removed** ("font · Archivo Black" was still persisting there) — `statusEl` is now a shim (`{get/set textContent}`) routing every call site to `toast(msg)`, a transient pill top-centre of the stage (`#toast`, auto-hide 2.4 s / 4.2 s for `✗`) used for `✓`/`✗` results + errors only; (c) `#procOverlay` keeps the grey-out **and the centred `#procMsg` pill** (`applying effects…` / `building family… n/total` / `exporting glyphs i/n…`, as before), plus a **5 px solid loading bar that hugs the CANVAS (`#board`) top & bottom edge** — `#procOverlay::before/::after` (`background:var(--ink)`, no gradient; `@keyframes procFill` sweeps `width` 0→100% left-anchored once over ~0.55 s then holds via `animation-fill-mode:forwards`, re-triggered each `display:none`→`flex`) positioned via `--line-top` / `--line-bot` CSS vars set by `syncProcLine()` (`board.getBoundingClientRect()` vs the stage), re-run from `busy()` / the `ResizeObserver` / `zoomPan.onChange` / `renderFamily` after the board grows. `render()` guards the overlay with a `_renderSeq` token (a newer render owns it) and clears it on the identity path so a stale scrim can't stick. **(d)** the overlay only appears once work runs **longer than `PROC_GRACE` (250 ms)** — async jobs (family / OTF export) get a real `setTimeout(busy, GRACE)` that `clearTimeout`s if they finish first (`ensureFamilyGlyphCache` also gates its own `busy()` on `performance.now()-t0 > GRACE`); a blocking tester render can't be watched mid-flight, so it's predicted "slow" from `_lastHeavyMs` (the previous heavy render's measured duration) — first slow one is silent, repeats show it. `renderFamily` now `idle()`s on every exit for the non-`render()` callers.   - **Then** (Diego: "nella versione EDIT dobbiamo recuperare l'editor del glyph che avevamo precedentemente sviluppato" — full scope, straight into the editor, propagate everywhere; **shipping phased — Phase 1 only**): the dormant `shared/glyph-model.js` (`Organica.glyphModel`) + `shared/glyph-editor.js` (`Organica.glyphEditor`) — never wired into Living Path since they were built — are now **live**. Loaded after `pathfx.js`. `gmodel()` lazily builds `Organica.glyphModel.createModel(window.__FONT)` (`_gmodel`, invalidated + `_editRev++` in `seedFontSource`). Clicking a Full Family cell → `openGlyphOne` sets `showGlyph` + `glyphEditMode=true` → `mountGlyphEditor()` shows a `<canvas id="glyphCanvas">` (sized to the stage × DPR, `_ged.resize()` on the `ResizeObserver`) running `Organica.glyphEditor` on `masters[0]` (`opts.ink/paper` = the swatches, `opts.metrics` from `window.__FONT` os2, `opts.addMode` = `_addPt`, `opts.onChange` bumps `_editRev` + clears `_familyCache.sig`). In-stage `#glyphTools` bar: `[Edit|Preview]` seg (`renderGlyphOne`→`renderGlyphPreview` is the Preview render), Undo, **Reset glyph** (2-click armed), **+ pt**. **The hook:** `glyphSubs(glyph)` / `glyphAdvance(glyph)` now return `editedGlyph(glyph)`'s canonical contours/advance when a glyph is `.edited` in the model, else `fromOTCommands` unchanged — so an edit flows through `processGlyphEm` (tester / family / PNG-SVG / main-thread OTF) **and** the Worker OTF path's own `glyphSubs(gl)` call; only edited glyphs deviate, zero regression for the rest. `famSig()` gains `'|e'+_editRev` so Full Family rebuilds. `.lvp` **v4→v5**: `serializeEdits()` → `edits:{ [glyphKey]: {advance,contours} }`; `loadProject` stashes `_pendingEdits` (a `.lvp` has no font) → `applyPendingEdits()` seeds `masters[0]._cache` on the next `seedFontSource` (or immediately if a font is loaded); old v4 files load unchanged. Esc leaves glyph mode unless the editor has a node selected (defer to its own deselect). **Verified** on the no-store dev server: modules load; click `Q`/`H`/`A` → editor mounts with nodes + metric guides + advance/sidebearing lines; a real synthetic node-drag deforms the outline (gaussian soft-drag, corners crisp) and bumps `_editRev`; Undo / armed Reset glyph work; a committed edit reroutes `glyphSubs`/`glyphAdvance`, shows in the tester ("Q Q Q"), the Full Family grid (Q visibly shifted), the Preview render, the SVG export, and the exact `glyphSubs(gl)` call the OTF Worker makes; `.lvp` v5 round-trips (save → fresh page → `loadProject` → H restored, editor + tester reflect it); every exit path (Esc → tester, Full Family btn → grid) hides `#glyphCanvas`; `node -c` + `python3 scripts/css-lint.py` clean; console clean. **Phase 2** (axes / masters / design-space slider / `checkCompat` / `bakePresetMaster` — `normGlyph`/`denormGlyph`/`applyStackNorm` are already the exact signatures it wants) and **Phase 3** (`shared/ufo-export.js` + `shared/zip.js` → "⬇ UFO + designspace (.zip)") are scoped in the plan file as follow-on passes.

Now `mountGlyphEditor()`, when `!stackIsIdentity()` and the model glyph isn't already `.baked`, **bakes** `processGlyphEm(gl, upm)` (the current em-unit effect result) into `masters[0].glyph(key)` (`subsToLoose`, `g.baked = g.edited = true`, `_editRev++`, `_familyCache.sig=''`) **before** `glyphEd().setGlyph` — so the editor's nodes sit on the effected contour, Edit ≙ Preview. `editedGlyph()` now returns a glyph for `(c.edited || c.baked)`; `processGlyphEm()` **short-circuits** `if(ed && ed.baked) return toSubs(ed.contours)` (no re-apply → no double effects) — so tester / Full Family / PNG-SVG / main-thread OTF all render the baked shape as-is. The **Worker OTF** path detects `bk.baked` in the glyph-list loop → pushes `{baked:true, norm:normGlyph(subs).norm}` and the worker `onmessage` passes `gd.norm` straight through (skips `processGlyphW`); main-thread `denormGlyph` maps it back to em. `#gtReset` un-bakes (`_ged.resetGlyph()` → rebuild raw canonical, then `g.baked=g.edited=false`, `render()` → `mountGlyphEditor` re-bakes through the *live* stack → "Reset = discard my node edits, keep the current effect"). `serializeEdits()` stores `baked:!!g.baked`; `applyPendingEdits()` restores it → `.lvp` v5 round-trips baked. Identity stack → no bake (edit the raw outline, Phase-1 behaviour). **Trade-off (Diego's call):** a baked glyph is frozen — a later effect-stack change is not reflected in it until Reset glyph. **Verified** on the no-store dev server (Archivo Black + **Avulsion**): open `Q` from Full Family → editor shows the melted `Q`, baked contours **byte-match** `processGlyphEm` (359 pts, identical bbox); Edit ⇄ Preview identical (screenshots); a real node-drag (soft-drag, `_editRev++`, `baked` preserved) shows in tester ("QxQ") + Full Family + Preview; Worker OTF export re-parses clean (`Q` 71 cmds vs raw 33, non-edited `A` 56 = normal Avulsion, 28 glyphs); `.lvp` v5 round-trips `baked:true` + geometry exactly; Reset glyph 831→705.45 (raw effect y), re-baked; identity stack → `W` opens raw (155 pts, no bake); `node -c` + css-lint clean; console clean (only a synthetic-pointer test artifact). Shipped `028a3dc` → merge `74904eb` on `main`.
  - **Then (Diego: "procedi phase 2") — the variable-font design-space layer.** `livingpath/index.html` only. New right-panel `.panel-section` **#var-section "Variable · Masters"** (shown once a font is loaded): **Axes** (`+ Axis` inline tag/name/min/def/max form → `model.addAxis`; `×` → `removeAxis`), a **Location** `<input type=range>` per axis writing `model.loc` (step 1 for ranges ≤400, else `(max-min)/300`), **Masters** (`+ Master here` = `model.addMaster(masterLabel(loc), {...loc})` + auto-sets `editMasterIdx`; a radio per master picks the edit target; `×` removes a non-base master; a `◂ here` tag on whichever master the current `loc` sits on), a **⚠ compat** strip (`model.checkCompat(key)` while the editor's open), and **Bake stack → master** (`model.bakePresetMaster(editMasterIdx, {normGlyph, denormGlyph, apply: applyStackNorm, glyphKeys: glyphOrder, presetRef, onProgress})` — freezes the live stack into the selected master, topology re-projected onto base point structure; topology-changed glyphs skipped + counted in the toast). **The hook:** new `_varInst(glyph)` — returns `model.instanceGlyph(key, loc)` (interpolated) or a master's own contours when `loc` sits exactly on a non-base master, else `null` (≤1 master / no axes / on base). `glyphSubs`/`glyphAdvance` consult it **before** `editedGlyph` → tester, Full Family (`famSig` gains `'|v'+varSig()`), and both OTF exports all render the current design-space instance; the family cache's per-glyph `adv` switched `gl.advanceWidth` → `glyphAdvance(gl)`. `mountGlyphEditor()` is master-aware: `masters.length===1` keeps the Phase-1 bake-on-entry; otherwise `setGlyph(m, editMasterIdx, key)` and, when `loc` isn't a master, `previewInstance(instanceGlyph(...))` (read-only between masters), auto-setting `editMasterIdx` to the master under `loc` when there is one. **`.lvp` v5** gains a `variable` block — `{axes, loc, editMasterIdx, masters:[{name,location,source,edits}]}` (non-base masters only; base edits stay in `edits`); `serializeVar()` / the `_pendingVar` path in `applyPendingEdits()` / `loadProject` now does `_gmodel=null; _editRev++` first so a prior session's axes/masters/edits don't linger (**pre-existing Phase-1 gap** — an `edits`-carrying `.lvp` loaded after another never cleared the first's base edits either; fixed by the same reset).
